## Collective-guard scan of the `.pyx` sources (prototype, 2026-11-14)

A review tool for this week, as the dev hub asked. Next week it becomes a test_0052 PR. The code and logs are kept on the review workstation in the run directory `runs/2026-11-14-pyxscan/`:
- `pyx2py.py`: a line-preserving Cython-to-Python pre-pass;
- `pyxscan.py`: test_0052's classifier plus PETSc collectives and the early-exit rule;
- `dev-c2aa36b3.pyx.txt` and `dev-c2aa36b3.all.txt`: the raw hits.

- **What it adds to test_0052:**
  1. `.pyx` files: all 12 parse, 418 functions.
  2. PETSc collectives: petsc4py method names, and C names such as DMGetGlobalSection, MatAssembly* and SNESSolve.
  3. A `return`, `break` or `continue` under a rank-local guard, followed later in the same function by a collective. test_0052 sees this only inside loops. Raises are listed separately.
- **Validation:** it flags #1156's `_constrain_interior_multipliers_in_section` at baf11592 (the return at line 11845, before a collective at 11880). It does not flag it at 7f1c201f.
- **Development c2aa36b3, `.pyx`: 29 hits** (11 exits, 8 inside a guard, 10 raises). Triaged by reading:
  - **Every exit and inside hit is uniform:**
    - `_same_pattern` is an allreduce;
    - field names and `natural_bcs` are the same on every rank;
    - `n_ext_total` comes from an allgather;
    - the attributes are set on every rank.
  - **Latent, not a defect:** `_setup_discretisation` returns early on an xxhash of the rank's own `mesh.X.coords` (three solver classes). Probed at np 2 with a deform that moved only rank 1's nodes: Poisson, the vector projection and Stokes all re-solve. It is in the geometry inventory, under Charter §12.
  - **Defect: #1179.** The raise list led to `_build_velocity_nullspace_vector`. On a rank with no velocity nodes the mode reshape fails on that rank only. At np 8 with two empty ranks, MPICH aborts on mismatched Allreduces. The default `nullspace_rotations` reaches it on an annulus or sphere.
- **`.py` tree, the new rules:** 84 exits and 107 raises, not yet triaged. That pass comes before the exit rule joins test_0052.

## Is a solved value refreshed in `mesh.lvec` before another solver reads it? (2026-11-14, development 4b782b21, with #1170)

**Verdict: refreshed. The answers agree.**

**Case.**
- **Mesh and solver 1:** a 2-D simplex box (cellSize 1/16). Stokes solves (v, p) with no slip on all walls, a pressure nullspace and tol 1e-10.
- **Solver 2:** a Poisson solver for T, whose source reads both fields, `1000·v_y + 10·p`.
- **Sequence:** Stokes solves for body force A, then solver 2. Then Stokes solves for body force B, then solver 2 again, so a stale aux vector would carry A's v and p into B.
- **Variants:**
  - creation order, default (V, P, T) or scalar first (T, P, V);
  - with or without an explicit `mesh.update_lvec()` before solver 2;
  - solver 2 built before or after the first Stokes solve.
  - Each at np 1 and np 4.

**Result.**
- **Within a rank count,** all 8 variants give the same ∫T² and ∫T·x after A and after B, to every printed digit (13 at np 1; 12 to 13 at np 4).
- **B never carries A's values:** ∫T² is 2.308e-3 after A and 2.644e-2 after B, in every variant.
- **Across rank counts,** the raw runs differ: ∫T² after A is 2.308079505e-3 at np 1 and 2.292246076e-3 at np 4. That is the pressure gauge: mean p is +1.27e-4 at np 1 and −4.97e-4 at np 4, and `10·p` is in the source.
- **With the mean pressure taken out of the source,** np 1 (default order) and np 4 (scalar first) agree to 11 digits:
  - after A: ∫T² 2.304846709437e-3 and 2.304846709393e-3;
  - after B: 2.644322904938e-2 and 2.644322904935e-2.

**So:** the value a Stokes solve writes reaches the aux vector a second solver reads, in either creation order and without an explicit refresh. There is no missing refresh to report. A coefficient that reads a gauge-free pressure needs the gauge fixed, or the mean removed, to compare across rank counts.

**Scripts and logs** are kept on the review workstation, in `runs/2026-11-14-lvec/` (`lvec_probe.py`, `lvec_gauge.py`, `logs/`).

### #348: the pin where constant pressure is not a null mode

The per-rank part stays fixed. `tests/parallel/test_1156_rotated_lu_pressure_pin_parallel.py` at np 2: 5 passed.

The second part is live. The probe uses a unit box (`UnstructuredSimplexBox`, cellSize 0.1, P2/P1, unit viscosity, body force (0, cos(pi x) sin(pi y))). It has rotated free slip on Bottom, Left and Right, and an open Top, with `petsc_use_pressure_nullspace = False`. The reference is the same problem with the axis-aligned free slip written as component Dirichlet conditions (no rotation, no pin), solved directly with MUMPS. `lu` is `solver._rotated_use_lu = True`; `it` is the default iterative rotated path.

```
np1 open top, rotated free slip on Bottom/Left/Right, vs axis-aligned reference:
   lu: pin=True  |dv|/|v| 5.190e-03  |dp|/|p| 1.844e-01
   it: pin=None  |dv|/|v| 6.204e-09  |dp|/|p| 2.191e-08
np2   lu: pin=True  |dv|/|v| 5.162e-03  |dp|/|p| 1.839e-01
      it: pin=None  |dv|/|v| 6.349e-09  |dp|/|p| 2.189e-08
np4   lu: pin=True  |dv|/|v| 5.124e-03  |dp|/|p| 1.801e-01
      it: pin=None  |dv|/|v| 6.332e-09  |dp|/|p| 2.175e-08
```

With an open top, constant pressure is not a null mode, so the pin replaces a real equation. The direct solve is then a different problem: 0.5 % off in velocity and 18 % off in pressure. It also varies a little with rank count (5.19e-3, 5.16e-3, 5.12e-3), because the lowest global pressure row depends on the partition. `_global_pressure_pin` still pins unless there is a pressure Dirichlet condition. Its docstring names this case as open (read).

### #408: boundary_flux 3-D assembly memory

Read: the 3-D branch of `_desmear` (`utilities/boundary_flux.py`) no longer allgathers. It `comm.gather`s the coordinate-keyed reactions and trace elements to rank 0, which assembles and solves, and `scatter`s each rank's values back (commit 732831f1, "Avoid replicated 3D boundary-flux recovery", via #593). Three things remain:
- Rank 0 still holds the whole boundary as Python objects.
- The full-traction path still repeats the gather once per component.
- The 2-D branch still `allgather`s both dicts on every rank. That is cheap for a 1-D boundary.

Measured with a unit-cube P2 Poisson problem (`regular=True`, flux -1 on Bottom). `tracemalloc` takes each rank's Python peak inside `poisson.boundary_flux("Bottom")`:

```
np1 h=0.025 boundary nodes 7601, max|flux+1| 3.7e-10
   rank 0: local boundary nodes 7601  peak 25.87 MiB
np2 h=0.025 boundary nodes (with copies) 7692, max|flux+1| 1.1e-10
   rank 0: local boundary nodes 3232  peak 19.44 MiB
   rank 1: local boundary nodes 4460  peak  9.84 MiB
np4 h=0.025 boundary nodes (with copies) 7700, max|flux+1| 1.1e-10
   rank 0: local boundary nodes    0  peak 15.13 MiB
   rank 1: local boundary nodes    0  peak  0.80 MiB
   rank 2: local boundary nodes 4329  peak  8.94 MiB
   rank 3: local boundary nodes 3371  peak  5.93 MiB
(h=0.04: np1 10.6 MiB; np4 rank 0 8.0, a rank with no boundary 0.21 MiB)
```

As filed (every rank materialises the full boundary), this is fixed: a rank's peak follows its own share, and a rank with none uses under 1 MiB. Rank 0 still carries the global assembly, 15 MiB at np 4 with no boundary nodes of its own, and that grows with the global boundary. The issue's suggested fix (a distributed, reduce-scatter assembly) was not done. Close or re-scope to "rank 0 holds the boundary".

### #604: located point interpolating to NaN

No reproducer exists, and there is still no counter on the recovery path (`_global_fallback_indices` in `function/_function.pyx` silently folds non-finite located points into the fallback). The probe wraps that selector to count non-finite values whose mask says located. It runs `global_evaluate` on a P2 vector, a P1 scalar, the vector's Jacobian and a scalar derivative (simplex box, cellSize 0.05). The points: 4000 random points, mesh vertices, vertices jittered by 1e-9, and midpoints of vertex pairs.

```
np3: fallback selector calls 48, non-finite but located 0, non-finite returned 0
np4: fallback selector calls 64, non-finite but located 0, non-finite returned 0
np4, random partition: fallback selector calls 64, non-finite but located 0, non-finite returned 0
```

Nothing found, as in the 2026-11-13 pass. CAN'T TELL: closing it needs a case, or a counter left in the code.

### #999: global_evaluate of a derivative, point outside the domain

The probe: `V = (x^2, x y)` as P2 on a simplex box (cellSize 0.2). It calls `global_evaluate(V.sym.jacobian(mesh.X), pts)` and `global_evaluate(V.sym[0].diff(x), pts)` with the default mode and no flags. The near point is (1.05, 0.5), 0.05 outside; the far point is (1.5, 0.5).

```
            in-domain only         + near point (1.05, 0.5)                 + far point (1.5, 0.5)
np1 gradV   (2,2,2) [0.6 1.2]      (3,2,2) dVx/dx 1.932                     ValueError: 1 of 3 query points lie outside the mesh ...
np1 dVx/dx  (2,1,1) [0.6 1.2]      (3,1,1) 1.932                            ValueError (same)
np2 gradV   (2,2,2) [0.6 1.2]      NotImplementedError on ranks 0, 1        ValueError: 2 of 6 query points ... (ranks 0, 1)
np2 dVx/dx  (2,1,1) [0.6 1.2]      (3,1,1) 1.7864                           ValueError (same)
np3 gradV   (2,2,2) [0.6 1.2]      NotImplementedError on ranks 0, 1, 2     ValueError: 3 of 9 query points ...
np3 dVx/dx  (2,1,1) [0.6 1.2]      (3,1,1) 1.8011                           ValueError (same)
```

The NotImplementedError reads "A matrix-valued expression with derivatives cannot be evaluated with rbf=True (mode='fast') ... Use the default mode", although the caller used the default mode. Every rank raises, so nothing hangs.

- **Far point:** #1182 now refuses it at every rank count, so it no longer differs between serial and parallel. One detail: the count in the message is summed over ranks ("2 of 6" at np 2 for one point of three).
- **Near point:** still live, as in the earlier pass:
  - the matrix gradient raises at np 2 and 3, and returns at np 1;
  - the scalar derivative depends on np (1.932, 1.786, 1.801);
  - the in-domain values agree.

At np 2 and 3, the near-point scalar call also prints the #606 "evaluate() is rank-local: 26 of 26 query points are not located on this rank ... EXTRAPOLATED and are wrong" warning on every rank. That comes from the fallback's internal rank-local evaluate, not the caller's, so it is misleading here.

### #1166: partition dependence of the gradient density metric

The probe uses #1158's test mesh (`UnstructuredSimplexBox`, cellSize 0.07, 303 nodes) and T = 0.5 (1 - tanh((y - 0.5 - 0.1 cos 6x)/0.05)). `metric_density_from_gradient` uses its own projected gradient at the default tolerance. The owned values are gathered, sorted by coordinate, and compared with np 1. The random and empty-rank partitions come from a shell-partition hook.

```
np2 cells [260, 284]
  front-following  nodes differing 206/303, max diff 0.41
  percentile       nodes differing 113/303, max diff 0.000636
  raw              nodes differing 303/303, max diff 0.00539
np4 cells [139, 130, 137, 138]
  front-following  200/303, 0.303     percentile 112/303, 0.000513    raw 303/303, 0.00515
np4 random partition cells [128, 142, 127, 147]
  front-following  204/303, 0.391     percentile 112/303, 0.000521    raw 303/303, 0.00488
np4 one empty rank cells [195, 165, 184, 0]
  front-following  202/303, 0.363     percentile 112/303, 0.000442    raw 303/303, 0.00572
```

Matches the issue's numbers (0.41 at np 2, 0.30 at np 4). No flat-region floor and no tighter metric projection have landed in `meshing/smoothing/metrics.py` (read).

```
  10 passed, 2 skipped on each of the 8 ranks   (the skips: the fixture test, "not measured to hold the case at np 8")
mpirun -n 8 python -m pytest --with-mpi tests/parallel/test_0786_stokes_rank_without_cells_parallel.py
  5 passed on each of the 8 ranks
```

The solve on cell-less ranks completes and matches serial. This comes from development's two-call assembly. PETSc's `DMPlexComputeJacobianByKey` still tests the local flags upstream. Under the issue's own condition, it stays open until PETSc is fixed and the workaround is removed. On the probe rule, the defect as users see it is fixed on development.

### #874: LIVE (finding 4 of the #1165 review)

The probe takes the call that `test_0874_provenance_and_parent_collective_parallel` captures. It hands `provenance_transfer_parallel` a record whose `_addon_parent` is cut to one entry on the last rank only. The statement `parent = pre._addon_parent[pcell]` lies between the `n_out` allreduce and the SF setup, outside both guards:

```python
par = np.asarray(fine._addon_parent)
short = par[:1] if comm.rank == comm.size - 1 else par
hidden = T._Hidden(fine, _addon_parent=short)
try:
    fault_mg.provenance_transfer_parallel(coarse, fine, hidden, *args[3:], **kwargs)
except Exception as e:
    print(f"rank {comm.rank}: raised {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
comm.Barrier()
```

```
mpirun -n 2 python p874_f4.py
rank 0: record length 640 of 640
rank 1: record length 1 of 628
rank 1: raised IndexError: index 53 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 1
(supervisor, 90 s of silence)
  native: rank 0: ... MPIR_Allreduce_intra_recursive_doubling < MPIR_Allreduce_impl
  native: rank 1: ... MPIR_Barrier_impl
```

Wanted: the same `CollectiveBuildError` on both ranks. The parts fixed by #1130 and #1165 hold. The four `tests/parallel/test_0874_*` files give 16 passed at np 2 and at np 4.

The second open item, `add_fault`'s split, was not probed. The comment in `fault_mg.split_tail_levels` still describes a rank-local raise inside the split, before its own collectives, as a hang (read).

## Second issue probe sweep: 18 issues on development 0189b21f (2026-11-16)

Summary: 0 FIXED, 15 LIVE, 3 CAN'T TELL (#755 skipped for time, #922 needs Open MPI, #941 not reproduced). Every probe ran on a fresh build of 0189b21f. Probes and logs are kept by the reviewer.

All probes ran on development **0189b21f** (the merge of #1206), built fresh from that commit. No existing build's compiled sources matched it: the nearest was 6 lines short in `petsc_generic_snes_solvers.pyx`. The machine is a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH 5.0.1, conda-forge PETSc 3.25.3). Every run used at most 4 ranks, and parallel runs went under the project's MPI supervisor (300 s silence budget) inside an outer timeout. The probe scripts, logs and partition hook are kept by the reviewer. Nothing was posted.

| issue | verdict | one-line evidence |
|---|---|---|
| #348 | LIVE (second part, unchanged) | Open top, rotated free slip, direct-LU path: still pins a pressure row. Off the axis-aligned reference by 0.52 % in velocity and 18 % in pressure at np 1, 2 and 4; the iterative path agrees to 6e-9. #1211 is not merged. |
| #883 | LIVE | Datum-only breathing sphere (w = 0.1, cellSize 0.25): `rnorm_history` [0, 1.79e-8, 7.88e-13], so two increments where one suffices. |
| #884 | LIVE | Annulus, bodyforce (-y, x): the first solve starts at rnorm0 1.213e-1, its re-solve at 7.026e-2. Both end at 1.217e-11. |
| #939 | LIVE (warns since #1127) | Left (None, 0.05): the corner v is (0, 0.05), with n.v = 0.04 where it should be 0. Left (0.05, None): n.v = 0.03. Both now print the #1127 warning. |
| #941 | CAN'T TELL | Not reproduced. The geometric normal matches the analytic normal exactly at np 1, 2 and 4, on default, band and random partitions. The code is unchanged: constrained components are still not summed across ranks. No partition was confirmed to leave the corner on a rank that holds none of its facets. |
| #969 | LIVE | Annulus with its levels moved by delta = 1e-3: the rotation is `rejected_by: constraint`, and the solve reports converged with a rotation about (delta, 0) of 1.0e-2 x v_rms (FMG) and 9.5e-2 (GAMG). At delta = 0 it is 1e-17. |

### #348: the pin where constant pressure is not a null mode

The probe is the one from sweep B, unchanged. It uses a unit box (`UnstructuredSimplexBox`, cellSize 0.1, P2/P1, unit viscosity, body force (0, cos(pi x) sin(pi y))). Rotated free slip is on Bottom, Left and Right, Top is open, and `petsc_use_pressure_nullspace = False`. The reference is the same problem with axis-aligned free slip written as component Dirichlet conditions, solved directly with MUMPS. `lu` is `solver._rotated_use_lu = True`; `it` is the default iterative path.

```
np1 open top, rotated free slip on Bottom/Left/Right, vs axis-aligned reference:
   lu: pin=True  |dv|/|v| 5.190e-03  |dp|/|p| 1.844e-01
   it: pin=None  |dv|/|v| 6.204e-09  |dp|/|p| 2.191e-08
np2   lu: pin=True  |dv|/|v| 5.162e-03  |dp|/|p| 1.839e-01
      it: pin=None  |dv|/|v| 6.349e-09  |dp|/|p| 2.189e-08
np4   lu: pin=True  |dv|/|v| 5.124e-03  |dp|/|p| 1.801e-01
      it: pin=None  |dv|/|v| 6.332e-09  |dp|/|p| 2.175e-08
```

These numbers match sweep B (development 176958a9) to every digit. `_global_pressure_pin` still pins unless there is a pressure Dirichlet condition. #1211 is still open, so its change is not in this build.

### #883: the cold-start datum lift and the convergence reference

The breathing sphere of test_1092 at w = 0.1: `SphericalManifold(radius=1, cellSize=0.25)`, `ThinSheet`, unit viscosity, tolerance 1e-9, `normal_velocity = 0.1`.

```
converged True increments 2
rnorm_history ['0', '1.79e-08', '7.88e-13']
```

The reference is still the lifted iterate's residual (`r0 is None or (lift_datum and iters == 1 and r0 == 0.0)` in `solve_rotated_freeslip`). The 1.79e-8 therefore cannot pass its own relative test, and a second increment follows.

### #884: the rotation-gauge projection on an uncached solve

`Annulus(1, 0.5, cellSize=0.2)`, rotated free slip on both walls, bodyforce (-y, x), pressure null space on, tolerance 1e-8. The probe calls `solve(zero_init_guess=True)` twice.

```
solve 0: rnorm0 1.213233e-01  last 1.217e-11  passes 2  max|v| 5.043952e-03
solve 1: rnorm0 7.025761e-02  last 1.217e-11  passes 2  max|v| 5.043952e-03
```

The first solve's r0 still includes the rotation component, 1.7 times the re-solve's. Here both stop at the same point, but their relative tests measure against different references.

### #939: a nonzero component pin at a rotated corner

The issue's repro: unit box, cellSize 0.25, body force (0, cos(pi x)). Rotated free slip is on Top (normal (0.6, 0.8)), Bottom (0, -1) and Right (1, 0); the component Dirichlet condition is on Left. The probe reads the Top-Left corner.

```
Left (None, 0.05): corner v (+0.0000, +0.0500)  n.v +0.0400  max|v| 0.050
Left (0.05, None): corner v (+0.0500, +0.0000)  n.v +0.0300  max|v| 0.094
Left (None, 0.0):  corner v (+0.0000, +0.0000)  n.v +0.0000  max|v| 0.038
```

The #1127 warning fires in the two nonzero cases ("... has a component of 0.05 / 0.03 along the wall normal ... reduced as if the essential value were zero"). The constraint itself is unchanged.

### #941: the geometric normal at a partly pinned node, across ranks

The probe is a unit box (cellSize 0.125) sheared by x += 0.25 y, so the Right wall is tilted. Right has rotated free slip with `normal=None`. Bottom and Top pin v_y = 0, Left is no-slip, and the body force is (sin(pi y), cos(pi x)). The control is the same run with the wall's analytic normal (1, -0.25)/|.|. The probe reads sigma_nn at the two Right-wall corners and `boundary_normal_traction_integral` (mean removed) for fn = 1, y and y(1 - y).

```
                      int y sigma_nn     int y(1-y) sigma_nn   corner (1,0)  corner (1.25,1)
np1 geometric         -2.5277217220e-02  +1.0946633330e-02     -0.25998638   -0.36998752
np1 analytic          -2.5277217220e-02  +1.0946633330e-02     -0.25998638   -0.36998752
np2 geometric         -2.5277217220e-02  +1.0947602268e-02     -0.26010582   -0.37010696
np2 analytic          (identical to np2 geometric)
np2 rand1 geometric   -2.5277217220e-02  +1.0947741142e-02     -0.26012294   -0.37012408
np2 rand1 analytic    (identical)
np4 rand1 geometric   -2.5277217220e-02  +1.0946033854e-02     -0.25991249   -0.36991363
np4 rand1 analytic    (identical)
```

The geometric normal gives exactly the analytic normal's numbers at every rank count and partition. The issue's mechanism does not show here: a corner whose owner holds none of its facets would make the two differ. The code is unchanged: `_sum_node_vectors_across_ranks` still keeps a rank's own contribution for constrained components. No partition was confirmed to place a corner that way, so this is CAN'T TELL rather than FIXED.

The small spread across partitions (1e-4 relative in the corner values and the y(1 - y) integral) is the same for both normals, so it has another cause.

### #969: an exact rotation off the origin

`Annulus(1, 0.5, cellSize=0.2, refinement=1)`. The probe moves the fine mesh and its coarse levels by (delta, 0) with `deform`. Rotated free slip (facet-averaged normal) is on both walls, the body force is cos(3 theta) radial about the moved centre, the tolerance is 1e-8, and the run is serial. "fmg" registers `custom_mg.set_custom_fmg` on the levels.

```
delta 0     fmg  (PC mg):   converged True  rotation about (delta,0) x R / v_rms 9.5e-18  gauge accepted
delta 0     gamg:           converged True  rotation ... 1.7e-18                          gauge accepted
delta 1e-3  fmg  (PC mg):   converged True  rotation ... 1.02e-02  viol 3.48e-4, rejected_by: constraint
delta 1e-3  gamg:           converged True  rotation ... 9.46e-02  viol 3.48e-4, rejected_by: constraint
```

The declared mode is still the rotation about the origin. On the moved mesh it is rejected, and the solve reports converged while carrying an arbitrary rotation about the true centre.

The velocity cap is printed, but the pressure sub-KSP at its 200 cap (DIVERGED_ITS) is not reported anywhere, and the solve reports converged. By reading, the rotated path still checks only `vel_ksp` (`_warn_if_ksp_diverged`), and only its last application. The cap of 200 is still hard-coded in the rotated `cfg`.

### #817: Stokes_Constrained on a nonlinear flux

The test_1061 contrast annulus (cellSize 0.1, lateral viscosity ramp 1 to 1e3, multiplier free slip on Upper with `augmentation_base=1e2`, pressure null space, basic line search, tolerance 1e-8), serial:

```
linear (eta):                        reason 2   its 1   fnorm 4.39e-10
eta (1 + 1e-6 edot_II), default:     reason -9  its 1   fnorm 8.55e-02   (DIVERGED_DTOL)
  the same, picard=-1:               reason 4   its 12  fnorm 1.06e-05   (CONVERGED_SNORM_RELATIVE)
  the same, consistent_jacobian=False: reason 4 its 12  fnorm 1.07e-05
SNES function norms, picard=-1: 3.28, 8.38e-7, 8.55e-2, 7.66e-7, 1.44e-2, 1.11e-6, 2.41e-3, ...
```

The second Newton iteration still blows up, and the iteration then oscillates. It now stops on a small step (reason 4), so it reports converged at 1.06e-5, 300 times above its 3.3e-8 target. The field-1 cap warning fires on the way.

## Fix brief: IntegrationPointVariable on a rank with no cells (2026-11-17, development 35ac1397)

For a desk to fix directly, as a PR without an issue (found in the #1221 re-check).

### Summary
A SwarmVariable with proxy_location="integration_points" (or any
IntegrationPointVariable) fails on a rank that holds no cells:
`_BaseIntegrationPointVariable._create_petsc_fe` passes
`dm.getCellType(cStart)` for an empty cell range, so DMPlexCreateReferenceCell
gets DM_POLYTOPE_UNKNOWN. The ranks with cells carry on into the next
collective and wait there. Reproduced at np 2 and np 3 on development 35ac1397.
"cells" and "nodes" proxies are fine.

### Environment
- A 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH), with
  PETSc 3.25.3 from the pixi dev environment.
- underworld3 development 35ac1397 (merge of #1219). The compiled modules come
  from a build at aa3c49df. That build has no differences from 35ac1397 in
  *.pyx, *.pxi, *.pxd, *.h, *.c or setup.py. Development's .py files were laid
  over a copy of that install, and `underworld3.__file__` was checked to point
  at the overlay.
- Every parallel run went through `scripts/mpi_supervisor.py --silence 60`.

### Reproducer (repro.py)
Put it next to a copy of tests/parallel/empty_rank_fixtures.py.

    """A SwarmVariable with proxy_location="integration_points" on a rank with no cells.

        mpirun -n 2 python repro.py [proxy_location] [full]

    Needs tests/parallel/empty_rank_fixtures.py on the path. "full" gives every rank
    cells (the control)."""
    import sys
    import underworld3 as uw
    from empty_rank_fixtures import right_triangle_of_cells, cells_per_rank

    location = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "integration_points"
    full = sys.argv[2:] == ["full"]
    size = uw.mpi.size
    # At np 2 only a one-cell mesh keeps a rank empty (see the fixture's docstring).
    n = 4 if (full or size > 2) else 1
    dm = right_triangle_of_cells(n, size if full else max(size - 1, 1))
    mesh = uw.discretisation.Mesh(dm, degree=1, qdegree=2)
    print(f"rank {uw.mpi.rank}: cells per rank {cells_per_rank(mesh)}", flush=True)

    swarm = uw.swarm.Swarm(mesh)
    s = uw.swarm.SwarmVariable("s", swarm, 1, proxy_location=location)
    uw.mpi.barrier()
    print(f"rank {uw.mpi.rank}: {location} proxy created", flush=True)

### Failing run on development 35ac1397
    python scripts/mpi_supervisor.py --silence 60 -- mpirun -n 2 python repro.py

    rank 0: cells per rank [0, 1]
    rank 1: cells per rank [0, 1]
    [0]PETSC ERROR: Invalid argument
    [0]PETSC ERROR: Cannot create reference cell for cell type unknown
    [0]PETSC ERROR: #1 DMPlexCreateReferenceCell_Internal() at src/dm/impls/plex/plexcreate.c:4621
    [0]PETSC ERROR: #2 DMPlexCreateReferenceCell() at src/dm/impls/plex/plexcreate.c:4658
    Traceback (most recent call last):
      File "repro.py", line 21, in <module>
        s = uw.swarm.SwarmVariable("s", swarm, 1, proxy_location=location)
      File "underworld3/swarm.py", line 577, in __init__
        self._create_proxy_variable()
      File "underworld3/swarm.py", line 1362, in _create_proxy_variable
        self._meshVar = uw.discretisation.IntegrationPointVariable(
      File "underworld3/discretisation/enhanced_variables.py", line 985, in __init__
      File "underworld3/discretisation/enhanced_variables.py", line 135, in __init__
      File "underworld3/discretisation/discretisation_mesh_variables.py", line 4286, in __init__
      File "underworld3/discretisation/discretisation_mesh_variables.py", line 595, in __init__
        self._setup_ds()
      File "underworld3/discretisation/discretisation_mesh_variables.py", line 2416, in _setup_ds
        petsc_fe = self._create_petsc_fe(dim, name0 + "_")
      File "underworld3/discretisation/discretisation_mesh_variables.py", line 4300, in _create_petsc_fe
        return create_delta_fe(
      File "src/underworld3/cython/petsc_quadrature_fe.pyx", line 149, in create_delta_fe
    petsc4py.PETSc.Error: error code 62
    [0]PETSC ERROR: 103760801873152 is too big for PetscInt ...
    [0]PETSC ERROR: #2 PetscGarbageKeySortedIntersect() at src/sys/objects/garbage.c:129
    Abort(63) on node 0 ...
    Abort(...) on node 1: Fatal error in internal_Allreduce_c: Message truncated
      MPI_Allreduce_c(count=1, MPI_LOR, ...) failed
      Message from rank 0 and tag 14 truncated; 1 bytes received but buffer size is 64

    exit code 63

At np 3 (`mpirun -n 3 python repro.py`), the cells per rank are [8, 8, 0].
Rank 2 shows the same error and traceback, and the exit code is 142 (abort).

### Where the other ranks wait
In the plain reproducer the failing rank exits. On its way out, PETSc's garbage
cleanup does an allreduce. That allreduce meets the allreduce the other ranks
are already in, the message sizes differ, and MPICH aborts ("Message
truncated"). So this run aborts instead of hanging.

To see the wait itself, a diagnostic variant catches the error on the empty
rank and sleeps. It ran at np 3. The supervisor then reported a hang (exit
124):

    no rank moved in 20 s: this is stuck, not slow
    rank 2 is in <module>()          (the failing rank, sleeping)
    the other 2 are in _set_vec()
    native, ranks 0 and 1: MPIR_Allreduce < PMPI_Allreduce_c < DMClone
                           < DMCreateSubDM_Plex < DMCreateSubDM

The Python stack on ranks 0 and 1:

    discretisation_mesh_variables.py:621  __init__   (`_ = self.data`, the eager canonical array)
    discretisation_mesh_variables.py:3531 data
    discretisation_mesh_variables.py:3549 _create_canonical_data_array
    discretisation_mesh_variables.py:2525 _set_vec   (`self.mesh.dm.createSubDM(self.field_id)`)

This is the same MPI_LOR, count-1 allreduce named in the abort message above.

### Controls (same script, same build)
- np 1, one cell: exit 0, "integration_points proxy created".
- np 2, no empty rank ("full"; cells [8, 8]): exit 0, created on both ranks.
- np 3, no empty rank ("full"; cells [6, 5, 5]): exit 0, created on all ranks.

### Cause
- The failing call is in
  src/underworld3/discretisation/discretisation_mesh_variables.py,
  `_BaseIntegrationPointVariable._create_petsc_fe`:

      cStart, _ = self.mesh.dm.getHeightStratum(0)
      return create_delta_fe(self.mesh.integration_rule,
                             self.mesh.dm.getCellType(cStart), ...)

- The value goes to src/underworld3/cython/petsc_quadrature_fe.pyx,
  `create_delta_fe`, which calls
  `DMPlexCreateReferenceCell(PETSC_COMM_SELF, polytope, ...)`.
- Why it fails with no cells: on an empty rank the height-0 stratum is empty
  (cStart == cEnd). `getCellType(cStart)` is then asking about a cell that does
  not exist, and it returns DM_POLYTOPE_UNKNOWN. PETSc has no reference cell for
  that type. The cell type is a property of the local plex, and an empty rank
  has none to ask.
- The cell type is the only per-cell input to the element. The quadrature rule
  (`mesh.integration_rule`) is already built from the reconciled
  `mesh.isSimplex`, so it is the same on every rank.
- The collective the others wait in: the ranks with cells finish
  `_setup_ds` and reach the eager `_ = self.data` in
  `_BaseMeshVariable.__init__`. There, `_set_vec` calls `dm.createSubDM`, and
  its DMClone does an MPI_Allreduce (LOR). That is the first collective they
  block in, and the empty rank never arrives. On a mesh that already has fields,
  `_setup_ds` takes its `dm_old.clone()` path, so the wait would probably come
  earlier, in that clone.

### Suggested fix
Get the polytope from information every rank agrees on, not from local cell 0.
Either:
- map (`mesh.dim`, reconciled `mesh.isSimplex`) to TRIANGLE, QUADRILATERAL,
  TETRAHEDRON or HEXAHEDRON; or
- allgather the local first-cell type (populated ranks only), as `Mesh.__init__`
  already does for isSimplex, and use that.

Either way the empty rank builds the same element and enters the same
collectives. After the fix, check that `integration_points`, `data` and
`cell_data` give zero-length arrays on the empty rank. Also check that
`IntegrationPointVariable` created directly, and the DDt and materials callers,
work with an empty rank.

### Other proxy locations
- proxy_location="cells": no problem. Exit 0 at np 2 (cells [0, 1]) and at
  np 3 (cells [8, 8, 0]). It is a plain discontinuous MeshVariable, built with
  `PETSc.FE().createDefault` from `mesh.isSimplex`.
- proxy_location="nodes" (the default): no problem. Exit 0 at np 2 and np 3
  with the empty rank. Same path as "cells".

Files (kept by the reviewer): repro.py, the diagnostic hold variant, the
outputs of every run above, and the supervisor's Python and native stacks.

### #742: psi_star[0] after solve(), by solver family

Probe `p742.py`: the Maxwell shear box from test_1059's `_maxwell_shear`, ETD-1, dt 0.1, 5 solves, with `psi_star[0][0,1]` and `constitutive_model.flux[0,1]` read at the origin.

```
Stokes            semi_lagrangian    SemiLagrangian                  psi*[0]=0.393469 flux=0.451188 exact(k)=0.393469 exact(k-1)=0.329680 -> k
Stokes            integration_point  IntegrationPointSemiLagrangian  psi*[0]=0.393469 flux=0.451188 -> k
NavierStokes      semi_lagrangian    SemiLagrangian                  psi*[0]=0.393469 flux=0.451187 -> k
NavierStokes      integration_point  IntegrationPointSemiLagrangian  psi*[0]=0.393469 flux=0.451187 -> k
NavierStokesSLCN  semi_lagrangian    SemiLagrangian                  psi*[0]=0.329681 flux=0.393469 -> k-1
NavierStokesSLCN  integration_point  SemiLagrangian                  psi*[0]=0.329681 flux=0.393469 -> k-1
```

The convention still differs: SLCN's level 0 is one step behind. test_1059 still reads the flux on SLCN, with its #742 pointer.

A side finding: `NavierStokesSLCN` accepts `stress_transport="integration_point"` but builds a `SemiLagrangian` history all the same (`SNES_NavierStokes` always constructs `SemiLagrangian` for DFDt). The setting is ignored silently.

`dfg_cylinder.py` is not on this host. The nearest equivalent is the cylinder proxy from the #735 review (`probe_cyl.py`): the DFG geometry built with gmsh with the cylinder hole, Wi 1, `uw.systems.NavierStokes` (SUPG), DEVSS nu/4, ramped parabolic inflow and dt 1/120. The `p738.py` variant prints the SNES result every solve and puts a `Lagrangian_Swarm` on `ns.Unknowns.DFDt`. The swarm is advected before each solve, with `population_control = dict()` and BDF-1, for 7 steps.

```
CYL_INT=bdf CYL_LS=cells [CYL_FILL=8] [CYL_REF=0] python p738.py lagrangian 10 out.json 7
```

| history | mesh | solves converged | first solve |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lagrangian_Swarm, cells, fill 3 | base 5, refined once | 7/7 | reason 2, 7 its, fnorm 4.3e-9 |
| Lagrangian_Swarm, cells, fill 3 | base 5, unrefined (the issue's case) | 7/7 | reason 2 |
| Lagrangian_Swarm, cells, fill 8 | base 5, refined once | 7/7 | reason 2 |
| Lagrangian_Swarm, nodes, fill 3 | base 5, refined once | 7/7 | reason 2 |
| stress_transport="lagrangian" (solver-owned swarm) | base 5, refined once | 7/7 | reason 2, 7 its, fnorm 4.3e-9 |

No DIVERGED_FUNCTION_NANORINF. tau L2 grows smoothly from 4e-5 to 9.7e-4 over steps 1 to 7.

Fixing PR, believed and not bisected: **#833** ("Stress transport on the grid, forward from launch points, and on particles", merged 2026-10-08). It carries b13b6b1b, which fixed the particle history reading a half-updated proxy during its flux evaluation and added population control for open boundaries, and 6287c09c, the degree-1 swarm proxy. Both landed after the issue was filed on 2026-09-14.

Caveats: this is a proxy driver, not the issue's script, and the SLCN-momentum combination was not repeated.

```
negative at: [[0.9914, 0.0029], [0.9889, 0.0011], [0.4497, 0.6767]] values [-0.00193014 -0.00295697 -0.00083974]
200x200 interior grid: min -0.005002, negative 26
most negative at: [[0.9995, 0.0005], [0.0005, 0.0005], [0.9945, 0.0005], [0.9995, 0.0055], [0.0055, 0.0005]] values [-0.00500194 ...]
```

These are the same numbers as on 176958a9. The issue's points are fine. The small negatives sit where Einv2 tends to zero: the two bottom corners and the vortex centre (0.45, 0.68). The locator changes since 176958a9 (#1213, #1215, #1235) did not move them. The remainder is undershoot in the recovered derivative, as the 2026-11-13 comment says, not location.

### #1178: double claims at a partition seam

The probe is the sweep A probe. #1123's sheared 3-D box (cellSize 1/6), a P1 field linear in x, y, z, 292 points at offsets 1e-8 to 1e-4 from interior edges. Every rank asks for every point. "Claimed" counts the ranks whose `_robust_owning_cells` returns a cell.

```
np1  WORST 1.78e-15
np2  offset 1e-05: max err 2.10e-04, wrong per asking rank [19, 21], claimed by >1 rank 42, by none 0   WORST 2.10e-04
np3  offset 1e-05: max err 1.00e-04, wrong per asking rank [24, 29, 27], claimed by >1 rank 57             WORST 1.00e-04
np4  offset 1e-04: max err 4.07e-04, wrong per asking rank [3, 3, 5, 4], claimed by >1 rank 10;
     offset 1e-05: max err 2.79e-04, claimed by >1 rank 79                                                 WORST 4.07e-04
```

#1213's exact locator is used by `points_in_domain`. It does not reach this `global_evaluate` path: the face-tolerance claim (`_EVAL_FACE_TOL`) is unchanged. The counts are 42 to 79 points with two or more claimants, against 42 to 107 at np 2 to 8 before. The worst error is unchanged at 4.1e-4.

### #940: unzeroed pooled reads at a constrained pair node

`fault_contact._nodal_slip` and `nodal_normal_traction` still read through `dm.getLocalVec()` and `globalToLocal` with no zeroing first. `node_index` still does not skip constrained pair nodes.

The issue's route is closed on development: a fault at a Dirichlet wall. `add_fault` with a trace reaching the Right wall raises "fault_split: the fault touches the domain boundary. Only strictly interior faults ... are supported". So the probe constrains the pair DOFs another way. It uses a strictly interior fault (0.30, 0.45)-(0.70, 0.55) in a unit box (cellSize 1/8) with simple shear on the four walls. It adds a viscous law `add_fault_bc(1.0, "Flt")` and an essential condition `add_dirichlet_bc((0, 0), "FltMinus")`. It solves, fills the pooled local vectors with 0 and then with 1e3, and reads both.

```
rank 0: pair nodes 11, on a constrained wall 11
   constrained pair at [0.375 0.4688]: sigma_nn pool=0 +2.573231e-10  pool=1e3 +2.117647e+04;  slip pool=0 +4.410814e-02  pool=1e3 -1.212634e+03
   constrained pair at [0.6   0.525 ]: sigma_nn pool=0 -6.162461e-10  pool=1e3 +1.694118e+04;  slip pool=0 +8.234143e-02  pool=1e3 -1.212596e+03
   ...
control (no FltMinus condition): pair nodes 11, constrained 0; max change sigma 0.00e+00, slip 0.00e+00
```

This configuration does not survive the solve reliably:
- **Serial:** 8 of 10 runs died in the solve (`malloc(): unaligned tcache chunk detected`, `free(): invalid pointer`, `corrupted size vs. prev_size`, SEGV).
- **np 2:** both ranks failed in `residual_add` with `petsc4py.PETSc.Error: error code 63` (argument out of range) at `Fh.setValues(self._rows...)`, and then an MPI_Allreduce message-truncation abort.

The slip rows of a constrained pair come from the local-to-global map of constrained DOFs, so `residual_add` writes to invalid indices. That is the issue's "node_index does not skip pair nodes that have constrained components", with a worse effect than a stale value. The configuration is artificial, but no check refuses it.

### #941: the geometric normal at a stranded corner

The sweep 2 probe is used again. A unit box (cellSize 0.125) is sheared by x += 0.25 y. Right has rotated free slip with `normal=None`. Top and Bottom pin v_y, Left is no-slip, and the body force is (sin(pi y), cos(pi x)). The probe now also prints each rank's normal at the corners. A copy of the partition hook adds two layouts. Both start from a band split at y = 0.5. Layout c941a puts the cell holding the Right facet at corner (1, 1) (undeformed) on rank 0 and the corner's other cell on rank 1. Layout c941b is the reverse.

```
np1 geometric        int y sigma_nn -2.5277217220e-02  int y(1-y) sigma_nn +1.0946633330e-02  corner (1.25,1) -0.36998752
np2 c941a analytic   int y sigma_nn -2.5277217220e-02  int y(1-y) sigma_nn +1.0947312504e-02  corner (1.25,1) -0.37007124
np2 c941a geometric  int y sigma_nn -2.5298176598e-02  int y(1-y) sigma_nn +1.0954298963e-02  corner (1.25,1) -0.37190844
    exact normal [ 0.970143 -0.242536]
    rank 0: corner (1.25, 1.0) owned False labelled Right facets here 1 normal [ 0.970143 -0.242536]
    rank 1: corner (1.25, 1.0) owned True  labelled Right facets here 0 normal [1. 0.]
np2 c941b geometric  (identical to c941a analytic); rank 0 (not owner, no facet) has [1. 0.], the owner has the exact normal
```

In layout c941a the corner's owner holds none of its Right facets. Because v_y is constrained there, it gets the normal (1, 0). The geometric normal then departs from the analytic one on the same partition:
- the y-weighted integral moves by 2.1e-5 (8e-4 relative);
- the corner sigma_nn moves by 0.5%.

In layout c941b the wrong normal sits on a non-owner that does not report. So the answer depends on the partition, as the issue says. The code is unchanged: `_sum_node_vectors_across_ranks` still keeps a rank's own contribution for constrained components.

## UNFILED 20: a point on a box wall and a partition seam is claimed by two ranks

Found while reviewing #1252 (reviewed at 264dbce0; the review comment is on the PR). For H6, as the follow-up to #1258.

**What happens.** `mesh.points_in_domain` returns True on two ranks for a point that lies exactly on a box wall where a partition seam meets that wall. `Swarm.add_particles_with_coordinates` filters a rank-identical array by that claim, so the point is added once on each rank. `migrate()` keeps both copies.

**Reproducer** (unit `UnstructuredSimplexBox`, cellSize 0.1, 242 cells; run with `mpirun -n 2`):

```python
import numpy as np
import underworld3 as uw
comm = uw.mpi.comm
mesh = uw.meshing.UnstructuredSimplexBox(minCoords=(0.0, 0.0), maxCoords=(1.0, 1.0), cellSize=0.1)
pts = np.array([[0, .5], [1, .5], [.5, 0], [.5, 1], [0, 0], [1, 1], [0, 1], [1, 0], [0, .37], [.37, 1]], float)
claims = comm.allreduce(np.asarray(mesh.points_in_domain(pts)).astype(int).ravel(), op=uw.MPI.SUM)
sw = uw.swarm.Swarm(mesh)
sw.add_particles_with_coordinates(pts)
n_add = comm.allreduce(sw.local_size, op=uw.MPI.SUM)
sw.migrate()
n_mig = comm.allreduce(sw.local_size, op=uw.MPI.SUM)
if uw.mpi.rank == 0:
    print("ranks claiming each point:", claims.tolist())
    print(f"{len(pts)} points -> {n_add} after add, {n_mig} after migrate")
```

**Results.** "Exact" means the ten points as written. "Clamped" means each point first pushed 1e-15 outside its wall and returned by `mesh.return_coords_to_bounds`.

| build | np | layout | claims (exact) | swarm count (exact) | swarm count (clamped) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| development 1901a469 | 1 | – | all 1 | 10 | 10 |
| development 1901a469 | 2 | PETSc default (cells 119/123) | point 3 (0.5, 0) claimed twice | 11 | 10 |
| development 1901a469 | 2 | horizontal bands (121/121) | points 1, 2 (x = 0 and 1 at y = 0.5) claimed twice | 12 | 10 |
| #1252 at 264dbce0 | 2 | PETSc default | point 3 twice | 11 | **11** |
| #1252 at 264dbce0 | 2 | horizontal bands | points 1, 2 twice | 12 | **12** |
| #1252 at 264dbce0 | 3 | PETSc default (78/83/81) | point 4 (0.5, 1) twice | 11 | 11 |
| either | 3 | horizontal bands | all 1 (no seam on these points) | 10 | 10 |

- With the default PETSc partition, the plain script above gives 10 → 11 at np 2. The 10 → 12 case uses a test-only band partition hook, which I can share.
- On development, clamped points sat 1e-3 inside the wall and were claimed once. #1252's snap puts them exactly on the wall, so they now duplicate like exact on-wall input.
- Advection is unaffected: a 1452-particle swarm driven into the left wall or the lower-left corner kept 1452 at np 1, 2 and 3 on both builds. Migration routes existing particles to one rank.
- Suggested fix: the lowest-containing-rank tie-break from #1258, applied in `points_in_domain` or in `add_particles_with_coordinates`.

## #941: the stranded-corner partition, as a reusable hook and probe

For reviewing #1265 (node normals summed through the point SF). These are the hook and probe behind the sweep 3 #941 entry above, which shows the results on development c2f8b4f6.

**How to run.** Put `sitecustomize.py` in its own directory, put that directory first on `PYTHONPATH`, set `LAYOUT`, and run under MPI from the uw3 tree:

```
PYTHONPATH=<hookdir>:$PYTHONPATH LAYOUT=c941a python scripts/mpi_supervisor.py --silence 300 -- mpirun -n 2 python p941.py            # geometric normal
PYTHONPATH=<hookdir>:$PYTHONPATH LAYOUT=c941a python scripts/mpi_supervisor.py --silence 300 -- mpirun -n 2 python p941.py analytic   # control
```

- The hook acts only in MPI ranks (`PMI_RANK` is set: MPICH and Hydra). For Open MPI, test `OMPI_COMM_WORLD_RANK` as well.
- It skips the JIT build subprocess.
- It replaces the partition of every gmsh-built mesh (it wraps `discretisation_mesh._from_plexh5`), so it is one mesh per process.
- The c941 layouts are band splits at y = 0.5 that strand the corner nearest (`C941X`, `C941Y`), default (1, 1) in the undeformed box:
  - **c941a:** the corner cell that holds the Right facet goes to rank 0, and the corner's other cells go to rank 1. The owner ends up with no Right facet.
  - **c941b:** the reverse.
- The hook prints the corner's cells and where they went. On development, c941a gives `cells [114, 120] right-facet cell [120] -> rank 0, others -> rank 1`.
- `LAYOUT=band`, `emptyband` and `randN` are the general layouts used elsewhere in this file.

**What to look for.**
- On development, the c941a geometric run prints `rank 1: ... owned True labelled Right facets here 0 normal [1. 0.]`. The exact normal is [0.970143 -0.242536].
- The integral of y sigma_nn is -2.5298176598e-02, against -2.5277217220e-02 for the analytic control on the same partition.
- A fixed build should give the exact normal on every rank holding the corner, and should match the control to solver tolerance.

`sitecustomize.py`:

```python
# #1158 review (copied from #1145 review, + emptyband): shell partition of every gmsh mesh, only in MPI ranks.
#   LAYOUT=band   cells by centroid height into equal bands, rank 0 at the bottom
#                 (at np >= 3 the middle ranks hold no Top or Bottom facet)
#   LAYOUT=randN  random assignment, seed N
import os
import sys
# 2026-11-16 copy: skip in the JIT build subprocess (setup.py), which inherits PMI_RANK
if os.environ.get("LAYOUT") and os.environ.get("PMI_RANK") is not None and not sys.argv[0].endswith("setup.py"):
    import numpy as np
    from petsc4py import PETSc
    import underworld3 as uw
    dmod = uw.discretisation.discretisation_mesh
    _orig = dmod._from_plexh5
    def _hook(*a, **k):
        res = _orig(*a, **k)
        if not isinstance(res, tuple):
            return res
        sf0, dm = res
        cS, cE = dm.getHeightStratum(0); n = cE - cS
        size = uw.mpi.size
        lay = os.environ["LAYOUT"]
        if lay == "band":
            vS, vE = dm.getDepthStratum(0)
            X = np.asarray(dm.getCoordinatesLocal().array).reshape(-1, dm.getCoordinateDim())
            ymid = np.array([X[[p - vS for p in dm.getTransitiveClosure(c)[0] if vS <= p < vE], 1].mean()
                             for c in range(cS, cE)]) if n else np.zeros(0)
            yn = (ymid - ymid.min()) / max(np.ptp(ymid), 1e-300) if n else ymid
            assign = np.minimum((yn * size).astype(int), size - 1) if n else np.zeros(0, int)
        elif lay == "emptyband":
            # bands over size-1 ranks; the LAST rank gets no cells
            vS, vE = dm.getDepthStratum(0)
            X = np.asarray(dm.getCoordinatesLocal().array).reshape(-1, dm.getCoordinateDim())
            ymid = np.array([X[[p - vS for p in dm.getTransitiveClosure(c)[0] if vS <= p < vE], 1].mean()
                             for c in range(cS, cE)]) if n else np.zeros(0)
            m = max(size - 1, 1)
            yn = (ymid - ymid.min()) / max(np.ptp(ymid), 1e-300) if n else ymid
            assign = np.minimum((yn * m).astype(int), m - 1) if n else np.zeros(0, int)
        elif lay.startswith("c941"):
            # 2026-11-20 sweep3 B, #941: band split at y = 0.5, then STRAND the corner (1,1):
            # c941a: the cell holding the Right facet at the corner -> rank 0, the corner's other cells -> rank 1
            # c941b: the reverse. Prints the corner's cells and where they went.
            vS, vE = dm.getDepthStratum(0)
            X = np.asarray(dm.getCoordinatesLocal().array).reshape(-1, dm.getCoordinateDim())
            cv = [[p - vS for p in dm.getTransitiveClosure(c)[0] if vS <= p < vE] for c in range(cS, cE)]
            ymid = np.array([X[v, 1].mean() for v in cv]) if n else np.zeros(0)
            assign = (ymid >= 0.5).astype(int) if n else np.zeros(0, int)
            if n:
                cx, cy = float(os.environ.get("C941X", 1.0)), float(os.environ.get("C941Y", 1.0))
                kc = int(np.argmin(np.hypot(X[:, 0] - cx, X[:, 1] - cy)))
                corner_cells = [i for i, v in enumerate(cv) if kc in v]
                fcell = [i for i in corner_cells if sum(abs(X[w, 0] - cx) < 1e-9 for w in cv[i]) >= 2]
                rf, ro = (0, 1) if lay == "c941a" else (1, 0)
                for i in corner_cells:
                    assign[i] = rf if i in fcell else ro
                print(f"[hook {lay}] corner {X[kc]} cells {corner_cells} right-facet cell {fcell} -> rank {rf}, others -> rank {ro}", flush=True)
        else:
            assign = np.random.default_rng(int(lay[4:] or 0)).integers(0, size, n)
        part = dm.getPartitioner(); part.setType(PETSc.Partitioner.Type.SHELL)
        part.setShellPartition(size, sizes=np.bincount(assign, minlength=size).astype(np.int32),
                               points=np.argsort(assign, kind="stable").astype(np.int32))
        return sf0, dm
    dmod._from_plexh5 = _hook
```

`p941.py`. It uses `rotated_bc` internals (`_boundary_velocity_nodes`, `_boundary_stratum_is`, `_point_coord`) for the per-rank corner printout. If #1265 renames them, adapt that last block. The traction figures above it don't depend on them.

```python
# #941: a tilted wall with the geometric normal (normal=None) meets a wall that pins one
# component (v_y = 0 on Bottom and Top). Traction readings at the shared corners, np 1 vs np > 1.
import os, sys, numpy as np, sympy, underworld3 as uw
mesh = uw.meshing.UnstructuredSimplexBox(minCoords=(0., 0.), maxCoords=(1., 1.), cellSize=0.125, qdegree=3)
X = np.array(mesh.X.coords); X[:, 0] += 0.25 * X[:, 1]; mesh.deform(X)   # Left/Right walls tilted
v = uw.discretisation.MeshVariable("V", mesh, 2, degree=2)
p = uw.discretisation.MeshVariable("P", mesh, 1, degree=1)
s = uw.systems.Stokes(mesh, velocityField=v, pressureField=p)
s.constitutive_model = uw.constitutive_models.ViscousFlowModel
s.constitutive_model.Parameters.shear_viscosity_0 = 1.0
x, y = mesh.X
s.bodyforce = sympy.Matrix([[sympy.sin(sympy.pi * y), sympy.cos(sympy.pi * x)]])
ANALYTIC = len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] == "analytic"
s.add_rotated_freeslip_bc(0, "Right", normal=sympy.Matrix([[1.0, -0.25]]) / sympy.sqrt(1.0625) if ANALYTIC else None)
s.add_dirichlet_bc((None, 0.0), "Bottom"); s.add_dirichlet_bc((None, 0.0), "Top")
s.add_dirichlet_bc((0.0, 0.0), "Left")
s.tolerance = 1e-10
s.solve(zero_init_guess=True)
xs, sig = s.boundary_normal_traction("Right")
xs = np.asarray(xs).reshape(-1, 2); sig = np.asarray(sig).ravel()
I1 = s.boundary_normal_traction_integral("Right", 1.0, remove_mean=True)
Iy = s.boundary_normal_traction_integral("Right", y, remove_mean=True)
Ib = s.boundary_normal_traction_integral("Right", y * (1 - y), remove_mean=True)
vmax = uw.mpi.comm.allreduce(float(np.abs(np.asarray(v.array)).max()), op=__import__("mpi4py").MPI.MAX)
allx = uw.mpi.comm.gather(xs, root=0); alls = uw.mpi.comm.gather(sig, root=0)
if uw.mpi.rank == 0:
    xs = np.concatenate(allx); sig = np.concatenate(alls)
    out = []
    for c in ((1.0, 0.0), (1.25, 1.0)):
        k = np.hypot(xs[:, 0] - c[0], xs[:, 1] - c[1]) < 1e-9
        out.append(f"corner {c}: sigma_nn {np.unique(np.round(sig[k], 10))}")
    print(f"np{uw.mpi.size} {os.environ.get('LAYOUT', 'default')} {'analytic' if ANALYTIC else 'geometric'} normal: int sigma_nn {float(np.ravel(I1)[0]):+.10e}  int y sigma_nn {float(np.ravel(Iy)[0]):+.10e}  int y(1-y) sigma_nn {float(np.ravel(Ib)[0]):+.10e}  |v|max {vmax:.6e}", flush=True)
    for o in out: print("   ", o, flush=True)
# 2026-11-20: each rank's geometric normal at the corners, and who owns the corner vertex
from underworld3.utilities import rotated_bc as _rb
dm = s.dm; dim = 2
csec = dm.getCoordinateSection(); cvec = np.asarray(dm.getCoordinatesLocal().array).reshape(-1, dim)
v0, v1 = dm.getDepthStratum(0); gsec = dm.getGlobalSection()
nodes = _rb._boundary_velocity_nodes(s, "Right", None if not ANALYTIC else sympy.Matrix([[1.0, -0.25]]) / sympy.sqrt(1.0625))
sis = _rb._boundary_stratum_is(dm, s.mesh, "Right")
lab = set(int(z) for z in sis.getIndices()) if (sis and sis.getSize() > 0) else set()
lines = []
for q, nv in nodes:
    if not (v0 <= q < v1):
        continue
    c = _rb._point_coord(dm, dim, cvec, csec, v0, v1, q)
    for cc in ((1.0, 0.0), (1.25, 1.0)):
        if np.hypot(c[0] - cc[0], c[1] - cc[1]) < 1e-9:
            supp = set(int(e) for e in dm.getSupport(q))
            lines.append(f"rank {uw.mpi.rank}: corner {cc} owned {gsec.getOffset(q) >= 0} labelled Right facets here {len(supp & lab)} normal {np.round(nv, 6)}")
allL = uw.mpi.comm.gather(lines, root=0)
if uw.mpi.rank == 0:
    print(f"   exact normal {np.round(np.array([1.0, -0.25]) / np.sqrt(1.0625), 6)}")
    for L in allL:
        for l in L: print("   ", l, flush=True)
```

| 109 | 01b06c91 | #1100 | build | 0.5265065397 | 0.00801 | fail |
| 122 | 8a388dd7 | #1040 | build | 0.5265065397 | 0.00801 | fail |
| 178 | 176958a9 | #1195 | build | 0.5265065397 | 0.00801 | fail |
| 205 | 4ab522a4 | #1230 | build | 0.5265065397 | 0.00801 | fail |
| 214 | c902390f | #1237 (head) | CI and the caller's run | 0.5265065 | 0.00801 | fail |

- **The bad value is stable:** every bad commit gives 0.5265065397 to 12 digits.
- **The early time agrees:** u(1) = 0.9534678129 on both sides, and good and bad runs match to the last digit until t = 2.0.
- **Not the stress-history PRs.** #1222 and #1231 come after the first bad commit, and #1232 was not on development when this was run.
- **Inside #1014:** the change is its first code commit, 2ac631ac ("Convergence reference, stage 1: native solves share one target"). This is from reading the code; the commits inside the PR were not built.

**Mechanism.** The forward history commits each step's new flux through `SNES_MultiComponent_Projection` (rtol 1e-3, warm-started from the previous step's projected stress).
- **Before #1014:** the projection used PETSc's relative test, so it always took one Newton step (reason 3, one iteration, every step).
- **After #1014:** every native solve stops at T = rtol · max(‖F(x0)‖, ‖F(0)‖). The projection has no history of its own, so it is classed "stationary". Its reference is ‖F(0)‖, the norm of the whole stress field.
- **Step 160 (t = 2.0):** the warm guess's residual is 9.41e-5 against a target of 9.33e-5, so the projection takes one step.
- **From step 161:** the residual is 8.34e-5 against 9.34e-5, so the projection returns after 0 iterations (reason 2). The stored flux is then the previous step's projection, not this step's flux.
- **Velocity:** the centre velocity departs at step 162 (0.716688 against 0.716672). The gap grows to 0.0074 by t 6.5.
- **The Navier-Stokes solve is not the cause.** Forcing it back to the old "stationary" reference still gives 0.5265065397.

**Diagnostic.** Setting `uw.systems.solvers.SNES_MultiComponent_Projection._convergence_reference_rule = "increment"` (class-wide, diagnostic only; in a pytest plugin) restores u(6.5) = 0.5191441704 exactly, at #1014 and at the development head c902390f. So the fix belongs in the history's commit projection: it should measure its increment, or solve from a zero guess. Re-pinning the test is the wrong fix.

**Wider worry.** Any warm-started projection held to #1014's max-rule target can return after 0 iterations once its field changes slowly between steps. When it does, it silently keeps the previous value. Candidates not checked:
- the nodal and integration-point histories' commit projections;
- any other warm-started `Projection` or `Vector_Projection` with a loose rtol in a time loop.

A cheap screen: log iterations and the converged reason of each projection per step in a long run, and look for reason 2 with 0 iterations.

**Pin drift (separate).** Even the good value, 0.51914, is 0.0006 from the pin's comment value, 0.51851. That drift predates the regression and is well inside the tolerance.

Logs, per-step projection logs (steps 150 to 166, good and first-bad) and plugins are kept by H1 on hyperion (runs/2026-11-20-bisect1061/).

## Reviews held during the GitHub pause (from 2026-11-25)

GitHub writes to underworldcode/underworld3 are paused. Each review below is the full comment text, ready to post as written when the pause lifts. Each records the PR head it was run at. Check that head has not moved before posting.

### #1197, fourth re-check, at 199955d2: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Fourth recheck at 199955d2: changes requested.** All four findings from the third recheck are fixed:
- A viscosity floor, cap or layer written with `Min`, `Max`, `Abs` or `Piecewise` keeps its units. It evaluates to the hand value, and it sets and solves to development's numbers.
- A dimensionless constant in any form beside a dimensional term is read in model units. Development gives the same numbers.
- A sum made mismatched by a later reassignment is refused at the solve.
- No session codes in the PR description or the tests.

The `test_1059` change corrects the test. Development's `evaluate` returned the viscosity as a bare model-unit number (0.5), labelled dimensionless. The right answer is 2.91665e19 Pa s, and the PR returns that. Every value a solver uses is bit-for-bit the same on both builds (section below).

One new false refusal blocks the merge, and it comes from the same fix. `Min` now compares its branches' units. The strain-rate invariant of a velocity field without units carries no units, so a user-written plastic viscosity `Min(eta, tau_y/(2*edot_II))` reads its plastic branch as a stress, in Pa, and is refused when set (new finding 1). The Spiegelman benchmark in the docs writes its viscosity this way. It now stops when the plastic viscosity is set. Development, and the PR at 5bac99b5, run it.

The other new findings are Low.

The PR's compiled sources differ from the merge base and from every existing build, so the PR was built fresh at 199955d2. Its installed `.py` files match the source tree. The baseline is development at the new merge base, 1e10c63f. Its `.py` files were laid over a copy of a build whose compiled sources and environment files match 1e10c63f exactly. Every run printed which `underworld3` it loaded.

## Against the third recheck (5bac99b5)

| finding | status at 199955d2 | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1. High: a sum holding `Min`, `Max`, `Abs` or `Piecewise` of dimensional terms refused | fixed | Each case below evaluates to its hand value at x = 18 km, T = 1100 K: `eta_min + Min(eta1*exp(T0/T - 1), eta_max)` and `Max(eta0, eta1*exp(T0/T - 1)) + eta_min` give 9.14101e23 Pa s; `eta0 + Piecewise((eta1, x > W/2), (eta0, True))` gives 1.1e24; `eta0 + Abs(eta1 - eta0)` gives 1e24; `T + Abs(T)`, `T + Max(T, T0)` and `T + Piecewise(...)` give 2200 K. As arguments, `exp(Max(T, T0)/T0 - 1)`, `exp(Min(T, T0)/T0)`, `exp(-Abs(x - W/2)/W)`, `exp(Abs(T - T0)/T0)` and `exp(Piecewise((T/T0, x > W/2), (1, True)) - 1)` give 1.10517, 2.71828, 0.904837, 1.10517 and 1.10517, all equal to the hand values. The four viscosities pass the setter and solve to development's \|v\|max to 10 digits. Mismatched branches are refused at evaluate and at the setter, naming the function: `Max(T, x)`, `Min(eta0, T)`, `Piecewise((eta0, x > W/2), (T, True))`, `Min(eta0, W)`, `eta0 + Max(T, T0)`, `Abs(T) + x`, and `exp(Max(T, x)/T0)`. Development accepts all of these silently: for example, `Min(eta0, T)` solves as a viscosity. Every rank gives the same verdicts at np 2, np 3, and np 3 with an empty rank (cells 20 / 22 / 0) |
| 2. Medium: dimensionless quantity beside a dimensional term refused | fixed | `x - uw.non_dimensionalise(uw.quantity(200, "km"))` gives 300000 m, as on development. `x - uw.quantity(0.5)`, `x - uw.quantity(0.5, "dimensionless")`, the same inside `uw.expression`, and a plain named 0.5 all give 0 m (hand: 500 km - 0.5 × 1000 km). `T - uw.quantity(1, "dimensionless")` gives 200 K, as `T - 1` does. A dimensionless term that depends on the mesh is still refused beside kelvin (`T + 0.5*x/W`). The hint now names the units: "give N.x*0.5/W_d units of kelvin" |
| 3. Medium: sum made mismatched by a reassignment not refused at the solve | fixed | With `eta0*exp(T/T0 - 1) + a` set as the viscosity, solving after `a.sym = uw.quantity(1000, "K")` is refused, naming `a` and its units. Setting `a` back to Pa s solves again to the original \|v\|max. Every rank agrees at np 3 with an empty rank. Development solves the mismatched viscosity (\|v\|max 8.35e-05) |
| 4. Low: session codes in the PR description and a `test_0760` comment | fixed | None in the PR description, in the diff against the merge base, or in the tests. Four older commit messages still carry one (new finding 2) |
| earlier fixes (stale cache, atan2 and θ, body force and integrals, hints, the 512 bound) | still hold | The recheck probe's output is identical to its runs at 5bac99b5, on both builds. At np 3 with an empty rank every rank prints the same lines, apart from the per-rank cell integrals |
````
