````markdown
| per-solve check cost | unchanged | Same 40-term probe. The check alone takes 4.9 to 5.0 ms, and 23.8 to 24.3 ms after a named value changes (third recheck: 4.4 to 4.6 ms and 21 ms). A repeat solve takes 0.031 to 0.033 s, against 0.028 to 0.029 s on development. The machine was busier this time (other jobs, plus a test slice alongside). Not blocking |

## test_1059

**The test was corrected. Nothing the solver uses changed.**

The test builds a `ViscoPlasticFlowModel` in pure shear, with `yield_mode = "min"`. Its effective viscosity is `Min(eta_0, tau_y/(2*edot))`. The floor case puts `smooth_max(tau_y, tau_y_min, 0)` in place of `tau_y`. That is `(tau_y + tau_y_min + Abs(tau_y - tau_y_min))/2`: a sum with `Abs`, not a `Max`. The test evaluates the viscosity at the centre, and projects it onto a field without units.

The old test took `float(evaluate(viscosity))`. The new one takes `evaluate(viscosity).to("Pa*s")` and divides by eta_0.

By hand, with R = 29.1 MPa / eta_0 and eta_0 = 5.8333e19 Pa s:
- yield stress 29.1 MPa: eta = tau_y/(2R) = eta_0/2 = 2.91665e19 Pa s;
- yield stress 0.2 MPa with a 0.35 MPa floor: eta = 0.35 MPa/(2R) = 3.507998e17 Pa s, which is 0.0060137 eta_0.

| | development (1e10c63f) | PR (199955d2) |
|---|---|---|
| `get_units(viscosity)` | dimensionless | pascal * second |
| `get_units` of the `Min` node | dimensionless | pascal * second |
| `evaluate`, yield stress 29.1 MPa (4 floor spellings) | 0.5, a plain array with no units | 2.91665e19 Pa s |
| `evaluate`, yield stress 0.2 MPa (3 floor spellings) | 0.006013745704, no units | 3.507998282e17 Pa s |
| projected field, both cases | 0.5 and 0.00601374570447 | identical, bit for bit |

Development's value was in the wrong units. A viscosity in Pa s came back as a model-unit number, labelled dimensionless, because `Min` (and the `Abs` in the floor) lost their units. The number happens to equal eta/eta_0, and the old test relied on that. At 5bac99b5 these functions still lost their units, so the old test passed in the third recheck's slice. The PR's value is the dimensional answer that `evaluate` gives for every other viscosity.

The solver side:
- The projected field is identical, bit for bit, in all 7 cases.
- I also ran a Stokes solve with each of the 7 models, from a zero initial guess, with the pure-shear velocity on the walls. The velocity and pressure arrays are identical, bit for bit, on the two builds:
  - with a 29.1 MPa yield stress: 13 Newton iterations, \|v\|max 3.67955256554, \|p\|max 90.1244742136;
  - with a 0.2 MPa yield stress, where the whole box yields: diverged after 6 iterations (DIVERGED_LINEAR_SOLVE). It does the same on both builds.

Test runs:
- development's `test_1059` on development: 11 passed;
- development's `test_1059` on the PR: 9 failed. It compares 2.9e19 or 3.5e17 with 0.5 or 0.006;
- the PR's `test_1059` on the PR: passes;
- the PR's `test_1059` on development: 9 failed, because a plain array has no `.to`.

The PR description and the commit message say the bare number "came from `Max` dropping the units". In this test it came from `Min` and `Abs` (new finding 3).

## Examples and docs (changes only)

The 26 files were run again on both builds, from fresh copies of each tree: the PR at 199955d2 and development at 1e10c63f. The copies had the same three edits, and the same 900 s cap. One file changed against development:

| file | development | PR |
|---|---|---|
| examples/WIP/Benchmark/Ex_VP_Spiegelman_Benchmark | runs; timed out at 900 s, as in every recheck (its first nonlinear solves converge) | **refused at line 621, after 120 s**: setting the plastic viscosity `eta_b + Min(eta_p, eta_bg)` gives "a Min of terms with different dimensions: \eta_{bg} is in pascal * second and Max(0, ...)/(2*\varepsilon + 2*sqrt(...)) is in kilogram * kilometer / meter ** 2 / second ** 2" (new finding 1) |

Three files behave differently from the third recheck, on both builds, because development changed them: `Tutorial_Thermal_Convection_Units` and `Ex_Stokes_Cartesian_SolNL` now run to the end, and `fault_friction` now runs until it times out at 900 s. These three and the other 22 files have the same exit status on both builds, with the same output apart from paths, object ids, timestamps, timings and line numbers. The PR edits `Ex_Geographic_Meshes` and `Ex_Stokes_Cartesian_SolNL`, which shifts some line numbers. In the annulus convection example, every timestep matches to the printed digits. Each file that fails stops at the same statement with the same error on both builds. The geographic tutorial prints development's velocities.

## The sums of constants only

The PR description says "A sum of constants only is folded into one constant when it is set, on development too, so it never follows its parts." Both builds give the same result:
- `eta0 + a`, with both named viscosities, becomes the single value 1.1e24 Pa s as soon as the `+` is written, not at the setter.
- The setter stores that value, in Pa s. The solve does not follow a later `a.sym = 1e25 Pa s`: \|v\|max stays 1.598e-04. It does not refuse `a.sym = 1000 K` either.
- `eta0 + W` and `eta0 + T0` (Pa s plus km, Pa s plus K) raise pint's `DimensionalityError` at the `+`.
- `eta0 + 1` stays symbolic and solves.

So, for a user:
- a constant sum has the right units at the setter;
- a mismatched constant sum is refused when it is written, with pint's message;
- reassigning one of its parts later has no effect, silently.

The last point is unchanged from development and not part of this PR. The description now states it. "When it is set" would be more exact as "when it is written".

## Findings

1. High: a plastic viscosity written by the user, with a velocity field without units, is refused when set.

   In a units model whose velocity `MeshVariable` has no units (the Spiegelman benchmark's `v_soln`, and most of the examples):
   - `get_units(stokes.Unknowns.Einv2)` is None on both builds.
   - `C/(2*edot_II)` therefore reads as a stress, in Pa, and not as a viscosity.
   - With `Min` now comparing its branches, these are refused at the setter on the PR:
     - `eta_b + Min(eta, Max(C - rho*g*y/2, 0)/(2*edot_II))`;
     - `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))`.
   - Development sets the first and solves it: 4 Newton iterations, \|v\|max 2.75637835.
   - The Spiegelman benchmark writes this form, and it stops at the setter (examples table).
   - At 5bac99b5 it ran, because `Min` then carried no units.
   - The built-in `ViscoPlasticFlowModel` is not refused, because its soft minimum is plain arithmetic. But its `get_units(viscosity)` reads "pascal" for the same reason.

   The message blames the plastic term ("give ... units of pascal * second"). It does not say that the cause is a field without units inside the strain rate. A user cannot follow it without giving the velocity units.

   The PR's own sum rule treats a field without units as a pure number in model units. A product that holds one cannot have a known dimension, though: `C` times an unknown is not "Pa". Suggested fix:
   - in `_common_units`, treat a term whose units come partly from a field without units as unknown, and skip it, as a sum skips a term that carries no units;
   - or keep the refusal, have the message name the field without units, and update the Spiegelman example to give its velocity and pressure units;
   - add a test: `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))` with a velocity without units, at the setter.

2. Low: the mismatch hint can tell the user to change the wrong term.

   The hint names the first term with units, in sympy's argument order, as the reference. It then asks for the other term to be given those units:
   - `eta0 + Max(T, T0)` set as a viscosity: "give \eta_{0m} units of kelvin";
   - `Min(eta0, W)`: "give \eta_{0m} units of kilometer";
   - `T + x`: "give {T_d}(N.x, N.y) units of kilometer".

   The refusal is right in each case, and both units are named. Only the advice can point the wrong way. Possible fixes:
   - say "these terms need the same dimension" and name both units, without picking one;
   - or, at a setter, take the reference from the parameter's own dimension.

3. Low: four commit messages on the branch name the reviewer by session code: a43b7090, e0326d2b, c29e11f5 and 1f3cb36e. They reach development's history unless the merge is a squash. Reword them, or write a clean squash message.

4. Low: wording in the PR description.
   - `test_1059`'s bare number came from `Min` and `Abs` losing their units, not from `Max`.
   - The "0.9 ms per repeat solve" is still not reproduced here: 4.9 to 5.0 ms, and 24 ms after a change.
   - Six of the eight new `test_0760` items pass on development. They guard against the third recheck's regression inside this PR, and all but one fail at 5bac99b5.
   - The "floor and cap" item has no field, and only checks that the solve runs.
   - The `Max` item reads `exp(Max(T, T0)/T0 - 1)` with T below T0. The result is 1, whichever way the units are read.

   A value check at T above T0 would pin it down (the probe here: 1.10517 at T = 1100 K, T0 = 1000 K).

Also noted, unchanged from development:
- A constant ratio beside a dimensional term is read in model units. For example, `T + eta1/eta0` (eta1/eta0 = 3) gives 4200 K, the same rule as `T + 3`.
- Two outputs differ from development, and are more correct:
  - `evaluate(Max(eta0, 1))` now gives 1e24 Pa s, where development gave a bare 1;
  - `Max(T, 0)` gives 1100 K, where development gave a bare 1.1.

  Solves are unchanged.
- The empty rank's local `evaluate` at a point it does not own prints a different number. That is a probe-side effect, as before.

## What was run

- **Builds:**
  - the PR, built fresh at 199955d2;
  - development at the merge base 1e10c63f: its `.py` files over a copy of a build whose compiled sources and environment files match.
- **Diff read:** deb68cb1 and 199955d2, the merge of development excluded.
- **Probes, serial, PR and development:**
  - `Min`, `Max`, `Abs` and `Piecewise` in sums, arguments, the setter and the solve, with mismatched branches and a viscoplastic model;
  - dimensionless constants in seven forms, and the hint text;
  - sums of constants only, at the setter and the solve, with reassignment;
  - a reassignment after the setter, through `get_units`, evaluate and the solve;
  - the `test_1059` model: evaluate, projection and Stokes, compared array by array;
  - the recheck probe of the earlier fixes;
  - the 40-term cost.
- **Parallel, PR:**
  - the `Min`/`Max` probe at np 2, np 3, and np 3 with an empty rank (cells 20 / 22 / 0);
  - the constant-sum, reassignment-at-solve, setter-and-solve and recheck probes at np 3 with an empty rank;
  - accept and refuse verdicts are identical on every rank.
- **Examples and docs:** the 26 files, on both builds, from fresh copies of each tree, with a 900 s cap.
- **The strain-rate probe, PR and development:** units of the strain-rate invariant with and without velocity units, and a user-written plastic viscosity at the setter and the solve. The second user viscosity and the built-in viscoplastic solve did not finish within 900 s, on either build.
- **Serial slice, PR, the same 82 files as before:** 717 passed, 7 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed. That is the third recheck's 709 plus the 8 new items.
- **np 2, PR:** `test_0762` and the parallel `test_0035`: 13 passed.
- **New tests against other builds:**
  - development: the 8 new `test_0760` items give 6 passed and 2 failed; `test_1059` as above;
  - the 5bac99b5 build: 7 failed and 1 passed (the plain-number form).

**Not run:**
- the full suites;
- `test_1086`: it launches up to 5 ranks, beyond this run's core budget, and the PR does not touch it;
- anything above np 3;
- the Spiegelman benchmark to completion on development;
- the docs' Markdown code blocks.

The runs shared the machine with other jobs.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1304 at 3d9ea0be: ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at 3d9ea0be: ready to merge on green from this review.** Both fixes do what the body says. A derivative sum gives the same solve as the `s.sym` form, bit for bit. A mesh gets the same coordinates, bit for bit, whether its lengths are expressions or quantities. Nothing regressed in the 05-08 serial bands, the parallel units files or the 26 docs units files. One claim in the body is wrong: tutorial 12 does not depend on #1293. #1293 is already in the merge base, and tutorial 12 now stops on a different gap (finding 1). The other findings are edge cases and earlier behaviour, listed for follow-up.

## What users see differently

- `s.diff(x) + s.diff(y)`, `s.diff(x) - ...`, `-s.diff(x)`, `2 * s.diff(x)`, `s.diff(x) / 2`, `s.diff(x) + 1`, and a field, coordinate, symbol, `uw.expression` or quantity times or plus a derivative now work. They used to raise TypeError. The result is a plain sympy Matrix, equal to the `s.sym.diff(x)` form.
- `uw.non_dimensionalise` takes a sympy expression or Matrix: fields, coordinates, numbers and bare symbols. It returns `uw.unwrap(expr, keep_constants=False)`. These all raised TypeError before.
- `uw.scaling.non_dimensionalise`, which the mesh constructors use, takes a `uw.expression` holding a quantity or a number. So `Annulus(radiusOuter=uw.expression(...quantity...))`, and the same for a box, work.
- `uw.non_dimensionalise(named_expression)` for an expression with a symbolic value now returns the model-unit form (for example `0.5*T` for `T / ΔT` with ΔT = 3000 K and a 1500 K scale). It used to return the raw value, `T/ΔT`. The `.data` property itself is unchanged: `es.data` still returns `T/ΔT` on head. What changed is what `uw.non_dimensionalise` returns, not `.data`.
- Tutorial 15 runs to the end. Tutorial 3 gets past the derivative sum.

## Method

- Head 3d9ea0be against merge base da35d141 (development). The change is Python only. Both builds are copies of the merge base's compiled install (a clean worktree at da35d141), with each commit's `.py` laid over it. Every run printed `underworld3.__file__`, and every log shows the intended overlay.
- Cores 0-3 only. Every mpirun ran through `scripts/mpi_supervisor.py --silence 60` inside an outer `timeout`. Other jobs on the machine used other cores.
- Probes, written for this review, each run on both builds:
  - derivative arithmetic (scalar and vector fields; with a units model and without);
  - Poisson and projection solves built from derivative arithmetic;
  - `non_dimensionalise` through both entry points:
    - with reference quantities;
    - with quantities but no reference quantities;
    - with no units;
  - mesh constructors with lengths given as expressions and as quantities, compared by a hash of the coordinates;
  - an np 2 probe.
- Docs units files: the 26 files from the earlier docs units sweep, plus tutorial 3. All were copied fresh from the merge base's `docs/`, which this PR does not touch; two have since moved to `WIP/needs_update/`. Each ran serially with a 900 s cap. The notebooks ran twice: as a plain script, and under `ipython`, so that `display` and `%%timeit` cells work.
- Serial: `test_05*` to `test_08*`, plus `test_0004`, `test_0023_field_realness_and_derivatives` and `test_1064_stress_history_units`. Run with 4 xdist workers on each build.
- Parallel, np 2, each build: `test_0770_mesh_move_in_a_units_model_parallel`, `test_0992_semi_lagrangian_record_units_parallel`, `test_0785_evaluated_values_reach_storage_parallel` and `test_0750_global_statistics`. The two new test files were also run under mpirun at np 2.

## Results

**New tests.** Head: 9 passed. Base: 7 failed, 2 passed. The two that pass are the `uw` entry point for a quantity expression, and the round trip, as the body says.

**UNFILED 40 (derivative arithmetic).**
- Every operation listed above equals the `s.sym` form as a sympy Matrix. This holds for a scalar field (1x1) and a vector field (1x2), with and without a units model.
- `get_units` gives these results:
  - sum of two derivatives of T: K/km;
  - product `s.diff(x) * s.diff(x)`: K²/km², which is correct;
  - derivative times T: K²/km;
  - derivative divided by T: 1/km;
  - vector field `v.diff(x) * v.diff(x).T`: cm²/(km² yr²).
- Without a units model, `get_units` gives None for sums and "dimensionless" for products and negation. That mirrors how the base build treats plain sympy forms.
- A sum with mismatched units, `s.diff(x) + T`, is accepted silently, and `get_units` reports K. This is the same as the plain `s.sym.diff(x) + s.sym` on both builds. It is neither refused nor new (finding 3).
- Solves:
  - Poisson with source `3*(w.diff(x)+1) - w.diff(y)/2` against the `s.sym` form: bitwise equal, same SHA-1.
  - Projection of `w.diff(x)+w.diff(y)`: bitwise equal.
  - In a units model, a projection of `T.diff(x)+T.diff(y)` and of `2*T.diff(x)-T.diff(y)` against the `s.sym` forms: bitwise equal.

**UNFILED 41 (`non_dimensionalise`).**

With reference quantities (length 1000 km, temperature 1500 K):

| input | `uw.non_dimensionalise` | `uw.scaling.non_dimensionalise` |
|---|---|---|
| expression holding 6370 km | 6.37 | 6.369999999999857 (same as the quantity itself on this path) |
| number expression | 2.5 | 2.5 (base: NoUnitsError) |
| `T/ΔT` named expression | `0.5*T` | `0.5*T` |
| `T/V[0]` | `T/V_0` (fields are stored in model units) | same |
| `x*ΔT` | `2.0*x` | `2.0*x` (base returned `x*ΔT` unscaled, silently) |

- The round trip through `uw.dimensionalise` gives 6370 km. Through `uw.scaling.dimensionalise` it gives 6370 km; on base it raised DimensionalityError.
- With no reference quantities, or no units at all, nothing that worked on base changed. The new inputs follow the same conventions as each entry point already did: `uw.non_dimensionalise` returns the magnitude, and `uw.scaling` returns SI.

Meshes, coordinate hashes, head against head:

| mesh | expression lengths | quantity lengths | base |
|---|---|---|---|
| Annulus, reference model | same as quantities, bitwise | reference | expression raised |
| UnstructuredSimplexBox, reference model | same as quantities, bitwise | reference | expression raised |
| StructuredQuadBox, reference model | same as quantities, bitwise | reference | expression raised |
| Annulus, no reference quantities | same as quantities, bitwise | reference | expression raised |
| StructuredQuadBox, no reference quantities | same as quantities, bitwise | reference | expression raised |
| Annulus, plain numbers | number expressions bitwise equal to plain numbers | not applicable | same as head, bitwise |

- Every quantity-length mesh is bitwise the same on head and base.
- SphericalShell refuses quantities on both builds (DimensionalityError), so it has no expression path to compare. It is not covered by this PR.

**Behaviour change, callers.**
- The source calls `uw.non_dimensionalise` in about 40 places. They guard on `UWQuantity`, a `magnitude` attribute or a units array before the call.
- A `uw.expression` is a sympy Basic, and `mathematical_mixin` sends it down its sympy branch first.
- `ddt._as_float` and the smoothing length can receive a symbolic named expression. Before, they took a symbolic `.data` that `float()` could not convert. Now they take the model-unit form, which converts when the expression reduces to constants.
- No test changed answer: see the serial and parallel rows below.

**Tutorials** (run on both builds):

| tutorial | head | base |
|---|---|---|
| 3 | stops at line 298, `b.sym`: `uw.function.derivative(..., evaluate=False)` returns a sympy `Derivative`, as the body says | stops at `s.diff(x) + s.diff(y)` |
| 15 | runs to the end, 25 timesteps, writes `html5/annulus_convection_scaled.html` | stops at the Annulus with expression radii (DimensionalityError km to dimensionless) |
| 12, under ipython | gets past `uw.non_dimensionalise(temperature / velocity[0])`, then stops at the next cell, `(temperature/velocity[0]).units`: AttributeError, a Matrix has no `.units` (finding 1) | stops at `non_dimensionalise` |
| 12, as a plain script | stops at a `%%timeit` cell (`get_ipython`) | same |

**Docs units files** (26 files, plus tutorial 3):
- No new failures. Every file has the same exit code on both builds, except t3, t15 and t12-ipython as above.
- Head and base give the same final error, file for file:
  - sttr: plain array into a units variable.
  - annfs: broadcast error.
  - madapt: missing `project_variables`.
  - step4: `mesh.adapt(metric)` meaning changed.
  - sink: `setPointCoordinates` error 56.
  - sb2: no `eta_0`.
  - sb2a and sb3a: no metric remesher in this PETSc.
  - vfing: recycling not implemented.
  - t14 as a plain script: `display`.
- ff and spieg hit the 900 s cap on both builds.
- The remaining 11 exit 0 on both builds, as do t13, t14 and t16 under ipython.

**Serial bands, 4 workers, 0 failed on either build.**
- Head: 1687 passed, 23 skipped, 19 xfailed, 1 xpassed.
- Base: 1678 passed, 23 skipped, 19 xfailed, 1 xpassed.
- The difference of 9 is the new tests.

**Parallel, np 2.**
- The four units files pass on both builds with the same counts: 2, 4, 16 plus 1 skipped, and 13.
- The new tests pass under mpirun at np 2: 9 passed on each rank.
- The np 2 probe ran `non_dimensionalise` on field expressions, a named symbolic expression, a Matrix, a quantity expression and a derivative sum. Results hash the same on both ranks. `unwrap` makes no MPI calls, so the result is rank-local and the same on every rank.
- An Annulus with expression radii is bitwise equal, on each rank's local coordinates, to one with quantity radii.
- A projection of a derivative sum completes on both ranks.
- Base fails the same probe at its first call, with TypeError on a sympy Mul.

## Findings

1. Medium: the body's line on tutorial 12 is out of date. "Tutorial 12 stops on its own unit error, which #1293 fixes": #1293 is already in the merge base, da35d141. Run as a notebook on head, tutorial 12 gets past `uw.non_dimensionalise(temperature / velocity[0])`, then stops at the next cell, `(temperature/velocity[0]).units`. A sympy Matrix built from fields has no `.units`, and `uw.get_units(...)` is the working form. This is a separate gap, in the library or in the notebook. Please correct the body and file it, so that tutorial 12 is not counted as fixed.
2. Medium: a nested named expression takes a different path from a direct one when the model has no reference quantities. `uw.expression("r_o", uw.expression("r", uw.quantity(6370, "km")))` goes through `unwrap` and gives 6370, the km magnitude. A direct quantity expression, or the quantity itself, gives 6370000.0 m through `uw.scaling`. In `Annulus(radiusOuter=nested, radiusInner=uw.quantity(3480, "km"))` this builds a mesh with outer radius 6370 and inner radius 3.48e6, silently. With reference quantities the nested form gives 6.37 against 6.369999999999857, so the coordinates differ in the last bits from the quantity mesh. Suggest: in `scaling.non_dimensionalise`, follow a UWexpression whose `_sym` is itself a UWexpression down to its value before choosing a path. A test for a nested length would cover it.
3. Low: the arithmetic accepts dimensionally inconsistent sums without a word. `s.diff(x) + T` reports K, `s.diff(x) + x` reports km, `s.diff(x) + 1` reports K/km, and `v.diff(x) + uw.quantity(1, "K/km")` reports K/km. This matches the plain `s.sym` forms on both builds, so it is earlier behaviour that now applies to derivatives too. Not a blocker. Also earlier behaviour: `s.diff(x)[0] + s.diff(y)[0]` (indexed elements) reports K, while `(s.diff(x) + s.diff(y))[0]` reports K/km. A single element reports K/km correctly. Worth an issue.
4. Low: the arithmetic is not complete. Each of these raises TypeError on head:
   - a sympy Matrix on the left times a derivative (`M * s.diff(x)`). Sympy tries to rebuild a `UnitAwareDerivativeMatrix` (missing `with_units_func`); `__rmul__` is never reached.
   - `s.diff(x) ** 2`.
   - `s.diff(x) @ ...`.
   - `1 / s.diff(x)`.

   The result is a plain Matrix, so a scalar applies to every entry only in the first operation: `3*s.diff(x) + 1` raises, while `3*(s.diff(x) + 1)` works. All of this matches sympy, but "adds, subtracts, multiplies, divides" reads wider than it is. Suggest one line in the docstring.
5. Low: two entry points disagree on a bare `UnitAwareDerivativeMatrix`, with the same result on both builds. `uw.non_dimensionalise(v.diff(x))` raises TypeError. `uw.scaling.non_dimensionalise(v.diff(x))` returns an ndarray of `200.000000000003*v_{0,0}`, a magnitude conversion on a symbolic object. `uw.non_dimensionalise(v.diff(x) + v.diff(y))` now gives the unscaled model form, so `+ 0` changes the answer through `uw.scaling`. This is outside the diff, but the new arithmetic makes the mismatch easy to reach.
6. Low: `uw.non_dimensionalise` now accepts a bare `sympy.Symbol`, a sympy number or a coordinate and returns it unchanged. Before, it raised TypeError. This is consistent with plain numbers passing through. `uw.scaling.non_dimensionalise(Matrix)` now returns an ImmutableDenseMatrix; base returned the input Matrix. No caller in `src` mutates the result.
7. Low, observed, not caused by this PR: tutorial 15 is not repeatable on one rank. Three head runs agree to timestep 2 and part at timestep 3 (dt 946.61, 944.60 and 947.64 Myr). Base cannot run it, so it gives no comparison. Worth knowing before tutorial 15 output is used as a reference.
8. Low, tests: `test_an_annulus_takes_its_radii_as_expressions` compares the largest and smallest radius with `approx`. The coordinates are in fact bitwise equal (above), so `np.array_equal` on `_coords` would be the stronger guard.

## Not run

- np 3 and above.
- The full `scripts/test.sh --parallel-only` run: four units files at np 2 instead.
- The test bands outside 05-08, apart from the three extra files named above.
- Spherical meshes with expression lengths: they refuse quantities on both builds.
- `docs/` notebooks other than tutorials 3 and 12 to 16.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1298 at 99948a53: ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at 99948a53: ready to merge on green from this review.**

The change does what it says. With the projections measured against their own first residual, test_1061's forward stress history passes again. u(6.5) = 0.5191441704, the value it had before #1014, and the flux projection takes no zero-iteration step in 521 solves. On 42 time-loop test files, the warm projections that returned the previous answer without iterating go from 152 to 0. Every test outcome is the same as on development. Solved fields move by at most 1.2e-6 relative, outside one already-failing order-2 case. The cost is +8 % nonlinear and +11 % linear projection iterations, and +1.2 % wall time. It composes cleanly with #1300 (next section).

## Together with #1300

- **Merge:** #1298 and #1300 merge without conflict. Git auto-merges `systems/solvers.py`, where the two change different classes: the projections here, `SNES_NavierStokes` there. Each also merges cleanly with development (c1691428).
- **test_1061 on the merged build:** passes. u(6.5) = 0.5191441704 (error 6.4e-4 against the 3e-3 tolerance), the same to 3e-16 as #1298 alone. The flux projection takes one iteration in 519 of its 521 solves and two in one. The other one is the initial projection of a zero field, a cold solve with nothing to do. No solve is skipped. Wall time 68.7 min on one core.
- **Tests on the merged build:** test_1014_a, test_1297, test_1059, test_1247 and 18 other SLCN, Navier-Stokes and viscoelastic files: the same outcomes as #1300 alone, all passing (table C).
- **Does the increment rule change #1300's numbers?** No. The heterogeneous probe (8 cases, below) is bit-identical between #1300 alone and the merge: every printed v, p and stress value at every step. On the 22 test files, the largest change in any solver's solution norm between #1300 alone and the merge is 1.2e-6 (test_1059), and 9.3e-6 on test_1053's variable-Maxwell-time case.
- **Parallel on the merged build:** the viscoelastic probe at np 2 (quads) and at np 3 (a gmsh simplex mesh whose last rank holds no cells) agrees with serial to the same level as before these PRs. Details are in the #1300 review.

## Method

- **Build.** Both PRs change only Python files. #1300's merge base with development is a22c4b17. #1298's is 6fc3fc5d, which is older and lacks #1256 and two compiled changes. All four sides used one clean compiled build whose compiled sources match a22c4b17 exactly. Its installed package was copied four times, and each side's `src/underworld3` Python files were laid over a copy:
  - **base:** a22c4b17.
  - **1298:** a22c4b17 with #1298 merged (clean).
  - **1300:** #1300's head.
  - **both:** #1300's head with #1298 merged (clean).

  Every run prints `underworld3.__file__` to confirm which copy it loaded. Tests ran from one copy of the merged test tree, which holds both new test files. The other test files are identical across the sides.
- **test_1061:** run serially, one core each, on 1298 and on both at once. It used the bisect's plugin to print u(1) and u(6.5), and a logger that prints every projection solve's iterations and converged reason.
- **Skip screen:** 42 test files that run time loops: stress histories and transport, SLCN, Navier-Stokes, viscoelastic, semi-Lagrangian, free surface, Darcy and Richards. They ran serially on base and 1298, with tier C deselected and a 900 s cap per file.
  - **The logger:** a pytest plugin logs each projection call: class, iterations, converged reason, and the norm of the projected field before and after. It also logs each outer solver call's solution norm, in order, so the two builds' answers can be matched solve by solve.
  - **What counts as a skip:** a solve that returns "converged" at 0 iterations from a nonzero starting field.
- **Reading the rule.** `_resolved_reference_rule`, `_reference_residual` and `_apply_convergence_reference`, which #1298 does not change. Under "increment" the reference residual is 0, so `F_ref = ||F(x0)||` and the SNES stops at `max(rtol * ||F(x0)||, atol)`. Under "stationary" it is `max(||F(x0)||, ||F(0)||)`. So the increment target is never looser than the stationary one, and the two are equal for a cold start. The linear floor follows the target.

## Results

### A. test_1061 (forward stress history, Waters and King, 521 projection solves)

| build | u(1) | u(6.5) | error vs pin 0.5185 (tol 3e-3) | projection solves at 0 iterations | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| development (from the bisect) | 0.9534678129 | 0.5265065397 | 8.0e-3 | 11 of 280 flux solves from step 161 | fail |
| #1298 | 0.9534678129 | 0.5191441704 | 6.4e-4 | 1 (the initial cold solve of a zero field) | pass, 68.6 min |
| #1298 + #1300 | 0.9534678129 | 0.5191441704 | 6.4e-4 | 1 (the same) | pass, 68.7 min |

0.5191441704 is the value the bisect recorded on every commit before #1014.

### B. Skip screen, base against #1298 (42 files, serial)

| | base (a22c4b17) | #1298 |

| projection calls | 2750 | 2756 |
| warm 0-iteration "converged" solves of a changed field | **152** | **0** |
| projection Newton iterations | 2726 | 2947 (+8.1 %) |
| projection linear iterations | 4799 | 5335 (+11.2 %) |
| summed wall time | 5130 s | 5194 s (+1.2 %; each includes one 900 s timeout) |
| negative converged reasons | 0 | 0 |
| test outcomes | same | same |

Where the skips were, and what changed (only the files with skips or a nonzero change are listed):

- **Outcomes.** All 42 files have the same outcome on both builds: 39 pass and 1 is the new test, failing on base and passing on #1298. test_1297 fails on both, since it needs #1300. test_1065_log_conformation hit the 900 s cap on every build, before its first test reported.
- **Toward the references.** Only test_1061 moves by more than its tolerance, and it moves toward its pin (table A). Everywhere else the change is 1e-5 relative or less, far inside each test's own tolerance, so the tests cannot say which way it went. I did not extract per-assertion errors.
- **The 3.4e-3 in test_1297.** Base's order-2 SLCN diverges, so any difference in a projection grows. With #1300 in place the same file changes by 4e-16 under #1298 (table C).
- **The 0-iteration solves still on #1298.** 242 of the projection solves remain at 0 iterations. Every one of them starts and ends with a zero field: a cold projection of zero, which is correct. 24 solves converge by step size (reason 4) after one or two iterations. These are the "unchanged field" case the PR describes, and nothing stalls.

### C. SLCN, Navier-Stokes and viscoelastic files, #1300 alone against the merge (22 files)

### D. New test, base against head

`test_1014_a_warm_projection_iterates_on_a_changed_field.py`:
- **Base:** all 3 cases fail. The scalar, vector and multi-component warm re-solves each take 0 iterations and leave the field unchanged.
- **#1298 and the merge:** all 3 pass. Each warm re-solve takes one Newton step with 3 linear iterations.

## Partition dependence (UNFILED 45)

The question is whether the increment rule changes the np 1 against np 2 difference of viscoelastic Stokes. It does not. The case:
- **Mesh and discretisation:** gmsh simplex box at cell size 1/12, P2/P1.
- **Model:** Maxwell, η = 1 + 99 exp(-r²/0.02) at (0.2, 0), G = 1.
- **Time stepping and solve:** BDF-1 with dt 0.1, solve tolerance 1e-10, 3 steps.
- **Comparison:** v and the level-0 stress at 77 points. The tables give max |Δv| / max |v| and the same ratio for the stress. Both np use the same cached mesh.

| build | step 1 v / stress | step 2 v / stress | step 3 v / stress |
|---|---|---|---|
| base (a22c4b17) | 2.0e-11 / 2.0e-3 | 1.5e-4 / 4.4e-4 | 1.5e-5 / 2.1e-4 |
| #1298 | identical to base | identical | identical |
| #1298 + #1300 | identical to base | identical | identical |
| #1298, every projection at rtol 1e-12 (diagnostic) | 2.0e-11 / 4.9e-11 | 1.8e-11 / 9.3e-12 | 6.7e-13 / 5.7e-12 |

- **Same on every build.** The serial runs of the three builds are bit-identical, and so are their np 2 runs. In this case no projection solve is skipped, so the stopping reference never decides anything. The rule changes the reference, not the preconditioner, so this is as expected.
- **The cause.** The step-1 solve agrees to 2e-11. The stress it commits to the history then differs by 2e-3 between partitions, and that difference carries into the velocity of step 2. With the projections at rtol 1e-12 the dependence goes away (bottom row). This matches the diagnosis that the default projection solve (GMRES with GAMG, rtol 1e-4) leaves a residual that depends on the partition.
- **Against the run tolerance.** The momentum solve is held to 1e-10. The step-2 difference of 1.5e-4 in v is about 10^6 times that, and about 20 times the error the default projection makes in serial: np 1 at the default against np 1 at 1e-12 is 6.7e-6 at step 2 and 8.3e-6 at step 3. So it is not "the same answer within the run's tolerance" for a run that asks for 1e-10. It is within the history projection's own 1e-4. This is a question of the projection defaults (rtol, or CG with Jacobi), outside this PR.

## Findings

1. Low: the branch predates #1256. #1298's merge base, 6fc3fc5d, is older than #1256's SLCN commit. A two-dot diff of the branch against development therefore shows `NavierStokesSLCN.stress_transport` and the post-solve stress commit as removed. The three-way merge keeps them: my merge with a22c4b17 and with #1300 both contain them, and so does a merge with development c1691428. Merging development into the branch before it goes in would make the diff on the PR page read true. CI would then also test #1298 against the current SLCN code.

2. Low: where "increment" could be wrong, I found nothing.
   - **Its target is never looser than the stationary one.** `F_ref` is `||F(x0)||` instead of `max(||F(x0)||, ||F(0)||)`.
   - **Cold solve and large first change:** a cold solve is unchanged. A warm solve whose first change is large gets the same target as before.
   - **Unchanged or round-off field:** this costs one iteration. Step-size convergence (reason 4) ends it, and the screen shows no divergence.
   - **Projections re-solved inside a nonlinear loop:** each call now costs one iteration where it could cost none. That is a cost, not an error.

   No negative converged reason appeared in 2756 projection calls. The one place the rule does no work is a field below `atol`, as before.

3. Low: cost. Measured over the 42 files: +8.1 % projection Newton iterations, +11.2 % linear iterations, +1.2 % wall time. This agrees with the PR's estimate.

4. Low (existed before this PR; no action here): test_1061 now passes at 0.51914, against a pin of 0.5185. The pin's comment value drifted by 6e-4 before #1014, as the bisect noted. That drift is well inside the 3e-3 tolerance.

## What was run and not run

**Run:**
- **test_1061:** in full on #1298 and on the merge, serial.
- **The new test:** on base and on #1298.
- **The skip screen:** 42 files on base and #1298, and 22 SLCN, Navier-Stokes and viscoelastic files on #1300 and on the merge.
- **Probes:** the heterogeneous viscoelastic probe in 8 cases on all four builds, the Maxwell-box probe for #742, and a viscoelastic Stokes np 1 against np 2 probe on three builds (Partition dependence).
- **Code read:** the reference-rule code.

**Not run:**
- **Parallel test files.** The parallel checks were the probes, reported in the #1300 review.
- **The full 166-file projection set the PR body cites.** The screen covered the time-loop files.
- **test_1065_log_conformation** to completion. It hit the 900 s cap on every build.
- **Per-assertion error extraction** for the test files.
- **#1298 against its own merge base 6fc3fc5d.** It was tested merged into a22c4b17 (Method).
````

### #1300 at 4ab700ce: ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at 4ab700ce: ready to merge on green from this review.**

The change does what it says. With a viscoelastic model, `NavierStokesSLCN` now uses the model's stress as its momentum flux, so order-2 BDF no longer diverges. On the shear box and the stiff-inclusion box over 30 steps it follows Stokes and the composed `NavierStokes` at the level of the time discretisation. Before the PR it was off by 100 to 600. Order 1 moves closer to the references too. The history is built at the model's order. A used history refuses an order change, with the same error on every rank, an empty rank included. A restart with the same version continues the unbroken run to 2e-6. The findings are all Low. It composes with #1298 without interaction (next section).

## Together with #1298

- **Merge:** the two merge without conflict. They touch different classes in `systems/solvers.py`. Each also merges cleanly with development (c1691428).
- **#1298 does not change this PR's numbers.** The heterogeneous probe below is bit-identical between this PR alone and the merge, in all 8 cases: every printed value at every step. The #742 value is also unchanged (0.393424). So is the restart (identical to the last digit).
- **Tests on the merged build:** all three test_1297 tests pass. test_1059, test_1247, test_1014_a and 18 other SLCN, Navier-Stokes and viscoelastic files pass too. The largest change of any solver's solution norm against this PR alone is 9.3e-6 (test_1053) and 4e-16 on test_1297.
- **test_1061** (the forward stress history, about 69 min serial) passes on the merge: u(6.5) = 0.5191441704.
- **Parallel on the merged build:** see table E.

## Method

- **Build.** Python-only change. This PR's merge base with development is a22c4b17. One clean compiled build whose compiled sources match a22c4b17 exactly was copied four times. The Python files of each side were laid over a copy:
  - **base:** a22c4b17.
  - **#1298 merged into the base.**
  - **this PR's head.**
  - **this PR with #1298 merged.**

  Every run prints `underworld3.__file__`.
- **Heterogeneous probe** (the one from the #1256 review):
  - **Setup:** a Maxwell shear box on 16x8 quads, P2/P1, η = 1 + 9 exp(-r²/0.04) at (0.2, 0) ("inclusion") or η = 1 ("shear box"), G = 1, solver tolerance 1e-8, ρ = 1e-6 for the Navier-Stokes solvers.
  - **Cases:** ETD-1 and BDF-2, a fixed dt of 0.1 and a changing dt of 0.1(1 + 0.4 sin 1.3k), 30 steps.
  - **Runs:** SLCN on all four builds, and Stokes and `NavierStokes` on base and on the merge.
  - **Output:** v, p, level-0 stress and the model's flux at 5 points after every step.
  - **Metric:** the tables give the largest relative difference over the 30 steps, max|a-b| / max|a| over the points.
- **The #742 probe:** the Maxwell box with Stokes, `NavierStokes` and SLCN, reading `psi_star[0]` after 5 ETD-1 solves.
- **An order-handling probe**, at np 1 and 2 on quads and at np 3 on a simplex mesh with an empty rank. Each step prints on every rank:
  - an order-1 solver given an order-2 model;
  - after a solve, an order-1 model (expect the refusal), then the same order again;
  - an unused history taken 2 → 1 → 2 before the first solve;
  - a model class rather than an instance;
  - a viscous model after a viscoelastic run.
- **Restart:** `save_state` after step 6, then a fresh process that rebuilds the model, takes one solve and calls `load_state`. It continues to step 12 and is compared with the unbroken run.
- **Parallel:** through the MPI supervisor inside a 300 s `timeout`, on 4 cores. np 3 used a gmsh simplex mesh partitioned so that the last rank holds no cells (checked: 200, 200 and 0 cells).

## Results

### A. SLCN against Stokes, 30 steps (velocity / level-0 stress)

| case | base | this PR | merge | `NavierStokes` vs Stokes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| inclusion, BDF-2, fixed dt | 259 / 11.1 (diverging) | 1.4e-2 / 3.2e-2 | identical to PR | 2.9e-6 / – |
| inclusion, BDF-2, changing dt | 602 / 56 (diverging) | 1.8e-2 / 3.2e-2 | identical | 1.6e-6 |
| shear box, BDF-2, fixed dt | 129 / 5.3 (diverging) | 6.5e-5 / 6.0e-4 at step 1, 5.3e-5 at step 30 | identical | 2.9e-6 |
| shear box, BDF-2, changing dt | 501 / 33 (diverging) | 6.8e-5 / 6.0e-4 at step 1 | identical | 1.6e-6 |
| inclusion, ETD-1, fixed dt | 1.6e-2 / 2.8e-2 | 1.1e-2 / 3.1e-2 | identical | 3.3e-3 |
| inclusion, ETD-1, changing dt | 4.0e-2 / 2.8e-2 | 1.3e-2 / 3.0e-2 | identical | 2.6e-2 |
| shear box, ETD-1, fixed dt | 1.5e-4 / 6.0e-4 | 5.0e-5 / 6.0e-4 | identical | 1.0e-5 |
| shear box, ETD-1, changing dt | 3.1e-4 / 6.0e-4 | 6.0e-5 / 6.0e-4 | identical | 9.2e-6 |

- **Stokes and `NavierStokes`:** bit-identical between base and the merge in every case.
- **Shear-box stress:** the 6.0e-4 at step 1 is the same on every build. It is the degree-2 SLCN history against Stokes's degree-1 one, plus the commit projection's tolerance. By step 30 it falls to 4e-5 to 5e-5.
- **Against the PR body's figures:** they are of the same size. The body reports the stress at the origin relative to its peak, and velocity at 5 other points, so the figures differ in detail.

### B. #742 probe, `psi_star[0]` after 5 ETD-1 solves (exact 0.393469)

| build | SLCN | Stokes, `NavierStokes` |
|---|---|---|
| base | 0.393421 | 0.393469, 0.393469 |
| #1298 | 0.393421 | same |
| this PR | 0.393424 | same |
| merge, np 1 | 0.393424 | same |
| merge, np 2 | 0.393536 | same |

The `integration_point` refusal from #1256 still fires on every rank.

### C. Order handling

| step | base | this PR (np 1, 2; np 3 with an empty rank) |
|---|---|---|
| `NavierStokesSLCN(order=1)` given an order-2 BDF model | history 1, model forced to 1 | history 2, model 2, solver 1 (its momentum history) |
| after a solve, an order-1 model | accepted silently | `ValueError` on every rank: "the stress history is order 2 and has been used…" |
| after a solve, the same order again | accepted | accepted; solves |
| unused history, 2 → 1 → 2 | model forced to the solver's 2 | history rebuilt each time; ends at 2; effective order 2 after 2 solves |
| model class on an order-2 solver | 2 | 2 |
| viscous model after a viscoelastic run | runs | runs |

Nothing hangs at np 2 or np 3.

### D. Restart, same version (save after step 6, continue to 12; v / stress / p, max over steps 6 to 12)

| build | ETD-1 | BDF-2 |
|---|---|---|
| this PR | 5.6e-7 / 2.3e-6 / 1.6e-5 | 1.1e-6 / 2.3e-6 / 2.2e-5 |
| merge | identical to the PR | identical to the PR |

For comparison, the #1256 review measured 1.2e-6 / 2.3e-6 (ETD-1) and 1.2e-5 / 4.4e-6 (BDF-2) on its head. A checkpoint written before this PR will not restart identically after it. The PR body says so, and I did not re-measure that direction.

### E. Parallel against serial, merged build (v / stress, max over steps)

| case | SLCN | `NavierStokes` |
|---|---|---|
| np 2, quads, inclusion, ETD-1, 12 steps | 2.3e-4 / 4.3e-4 | not run |
| np 2, quads, inclusion, BDF-2, 30 steps | 3.7e-4 / 5.3e-4 | 2.9e-5 / 5.5e-5 |
| np 3, simplex, last rank empty, ETD-1, 12 steps | 4.4e-5 / 5.5e-4 | not run |
| np 3, simplex, last rank empty, BDF-2, 12 steps | 5.8e-5 / 5.7e-4 | not run |

These match what the #1256 review measured on its head: 4.1e-4 / 4.3e-4 at np 2 and 9.8e-5 / 5.5e-4 at np 3. They sit at the level of the stress history's commit projection, whose default tolerance is 1e-4. That is within the projection's own tolerance, but far outside the momentum solve's 1e-8, so it is not the same answer for a run that asks for 1e-8. The #1298 review traces the cause to the projections' default solver and tolerance; it is outside this PR. SLCN's spread is about 10 times that of `NavierStokes`, as before this PR (finding 3). BDF-2 at np 2 is now stable: the #1256 review measured 4.1e-2 there on the diverging scheme.

### F. Tests

| file | base | this PR | merge |
|---|---|---|---|
| test_1297 (new, 3 tests) | 3 fail | 3 pass | 3 pass |
| test_1059_stress_transport (28) | pass | pass | pass |
| test_1247 (4) | pass | pass | pass |
| 18 other SLCN, NS, VE files (0020, 0066, 0070, 0610, 1052 VEP, 1053, 1054, 1056 x2, 1060, 1063, 1071, 1094, 1106, 1107, 1148, 1164) | pass | pass | pass |
| test_1014_a (#1298's test) | fail | fail | pass |
| test_1065_log_conformation | 900 s cap, on every build | same | same |

## Findings

1. Low: a rebuilt history leaves its first set of variables on the mesh. When the setter rebuilds the flux history, the variables of the history it replaces stay registered on the mesh. Saved state carries them too. This happens for `NavierStokesSLCN(order=1)` given an order-2 model, which is the case this PR is for.
   - **Example:** on the 2 → 1 → 2 sequence, the mesh carries the original order-2 history (`psi_star_sl_270_0/1` and its flat copy) and the order-1 one (`psi_star_sl_319_0` and its flat copy). Only the final history is used.
   - **Effect:** harmless to the answer, and a restart rebuilds the same sequence. It costs memory and checkpoint size.
   - **Possible fixes:** build the flux history when the model is set, or after it, instead of in `__init__`. Or drop the replaced history's variables from the mesh.

2. Low: at order 1 with the inclusion, the stress moves slightly away from Stokes while the velocity moves closer. With ETD-1 and a fixed dt, v goes from 1.6e-2 to 1.1e-2 and the level-0 stress from 2.8e-2 to 3.1e-2. With a changing dt, v goes from 4.0e-2 to 1.3e-2 and the stress from 2.8e-2 to 3.0e-2. All of these are at the level of `NavierStokes` against Stokes on the same box (3.3e-3 to 2.6e-2), so I read them as time-discretisation differences between the families, not a defect. The PR body could say that the order-1 stress is not uniformly closer.

3. Low (existed before this PR; separate decision): SLCN's partition spread is set by the commit projection's tolerance. It is about 5e-4 in stress at np 2 and np 3 (table E), against 5e-5 for `NavierStokes`. This PR does not change it, and the #1298 body defers a tighter history-projection default to a separate decision.

4. Low: the cross-version checkpoint break is named in the PR body. Release notes should carry it, since a viscoelastic SLCN run checkpointed on development will continue with a different momentum flux.

The diff was read in full. The `requires_stress_history` branch gives F1 = `constitutive_model.flux` minus pressure plus the penalty term, as in Stokes. The viscous path keeps the Adams-Moulton average: its code is unchanged by this PR, though I did not rerun a viscous-only SLCN probe. Stokes and `NavierStokes` are bit-identical between base and the merge.

## What was run and not run

**Run:**
- **The heterogeneous probe:** 8 cases, 30 steps, SLCN on four builds, Stokes and `NavierStokes` on two.
- **The #742 probe:** on four builds at np 1, and on the merge at np 2.
- **The order probe:** at np 1 on three builds, and on the merge at np 2 (quads) and np 3 (simplex, empty rank).
- **Restart, same version:** ETD-1 and BDF-2, on this PR and on the merge.
- **Parallel probe on the merge:** np 2 on quads, np 3 with an empty rank.
- **22 test files:** on this PR and on the merge, with base and #1298 results from the 42-file screen in the #1298 review.
- **test_1061:** on the merge.

**Not run:**
- **Restart across versions.** The PR body already names it.
- **Restart at np 2 or np 3.**
- **Parallel test files** (tests/parallel).
- **ETD order 2 on SLCN.** It is refused on both sides, as before.
- **A mesh-refinement study.**
- **test_1065** to completion (900 s cap).

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1310 at a3ccfa0c: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

## Review of #1310 at a3ccfa0c

**Verdict at a3ccfa0c: changes requested.**

The design is right, and where it engages it does exactly what the body says. We ran every read path on every mesh relationship at np 1, 2, 3, 4, np 3 with an empty rank, and two random layouts, with P1, P2, vector, discontinuous P1 and a combined file. With an absolute mesh-cache path the branch reads exactly or refuses on every rank. The refusal carries the same message on every rank. The one exception is `load_from_h5_plex_vector` (finding 2). The reads that were wrong on development become exact: `read_checkpoint` on the writing mesh, and `load_from_h5_plex_vector` on a rebuilt mesh in parallel. The payload-only `read_timestep` no longer hangs. A refined mesh's writer, which raised different errors on different ranks on development, now reads exactly.

But the check does not engage when the mesh was loaded through a relative path, and the default mesh cache, `.meshes`, is relative (finding 1). In the default configuration the branch gives the same wrong field as development, with no error. That covers every user who has not set `UW_MESH_CACHE_DIR` to an absolute path, and CI. The payload-only `read_timestep` changes from a hang to a silently wrong field. Under CI's conditions the PR's own test fails 4 of its 7 cases at np 2, np 3 and np 3 with an empty rank; it passes all 7 only with an absolute cache. The fix is two lines. `load_from_h5_plex_vector` also stays silently wrong at np 1 on files written in parallel, and for discontinuous fields at every np (finding 2). Both of those are inherited from development, and the fix is one line.

### What users see differently

Numbers are the largest error at the nodes, for fields of size up to about 2 (probe) or 0.3 (restart driver).

- **Restart through the documented route** (rebuild the mesh from `<name>.mesh.00000.h5`, then `read_checkpoint`, `read_timestep` or a payload-only file): exact on development and on the branch, 2→3, 3→2, 1→3, 4→2, random layout 4→3, empty rank 3→3, 3→2 and 2→3. A restart driver (a Poisson iteration writing `write_timestep(..., petsc_reload=True)` every 2 steps) restarted at np 3 from np 2 and at np 2 from np 3 gives the step-2 field exactly. After two more steps it differs from the uninterrupted run by 8.4e-15. Same on both builds.
- **Restart by re-running the same script** (a freshly built gmsh mesh, then `read_checkpoint`), np 2 → 3:
  - development: wrong, 0.276 for a field of size 0.300, with no error. After two more steps it is still 5.4e-3 off.
  - branch, absolute mesh cache: refused on every rank, with the same message.
  - branch, default relative cache: **wrong, 0.276, the same as development** (finding 1).
- **Reading back on the mesh that wrote the file** (a second variable, or the same one, at np ≥ 2): development wrong (1.1 to 2.8); branch exact with an absolute cache, **wrong (1.59) with the default cache**.
- **Payload-only file through `read_timestep`**: development hangs at np 2 and 3 (exit 124). On the branch:
  - the writing mesh and a rebuilt mesh read it exactly;
  - another mesh is refused;
  - with the default cache, the writing mesh gets **the wrong field (1.59)**.
- **New refusals:** a freshly built mesh at np ≥ 2 is now refused by `read_checkpoint` and `load_from_h5_plex_vector`. In one process at the same np, development's `load_from_h5_plex_vector` happened to be right there for continuous fields (same deterministic partition). The refusal is the safe call, but scripts that relied on it will now stop with the error.
- **`read_timestep` of a file with `/fields`:** the coordinate remap is unchanged and exact in every case, onto any mesh at any np. It is the safe restart onto a freshly built mesh today.
- **Cost:** none measurable (see Method).

### Method

- **Builds.** Head: a3ccfa0c. Base: the merge base bf022b14 (development has moved on since). The PR is Python-only: no `.pyx`, `.pxd`, `.pxi`, `.h`, `.c` or `setup.py` change. No existing build matched bf022b14's compiled sources, so we built bf022b14 fresh. We copied its installed package twice and laid each commit's `src/underworld3` `.py` files over a copy. The two overlays differ in exactly the two changed modules. Every run prints `underworld3.__file__`, and every run used the intended overlay.
- **Runs.** MPICH, at most 4 ranks on 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine. Every parallel run went through the repository's MPI supervisor (silence 60 to 120 s) inside an outer timeout. A hang shows as exit 124, with every rank's stacks.
- **Layouts.** A test-only shell partition on every gmsh load: `emptyband` (bands over np - 1 ranks, the last rank empty: cells 175 / 171 / 0), and `rand1` and `rand2` (random cell assignment). The default partitioner is used otherwise.
- **Probe.** A 2-D simplex box (cellSize 1/12, 346 cells) with four fields, each set from an analytic function of the node coordinates:
  - T: P2 scalar
  - V: P2 vector
  - P: P1
  - D: discontinuous P1
- **Files.** Three file families:
  - `write_timestep(petsc_reload=True)`, with `/fields`;
  - the same with `create_xdmf=False` (payload only);
  - `write_checkpoint(separate_variable_files=False)`, all four fields in one file.
- **Reads.** Every read is checked against the analytic field at the reading variable's own node coordinates. Exact means a largest deviation below 1e-12. Refused means a `RuntimeError` on every rank, and the messages are compared across ranks. Anything else is reported as found.
- **Mesh relationships.** The writing mesh, into a new variable (which also adds a variable and rebuilds the DM) and into the same variable; the writing mesh after `mesh.deform`; another mesh built the same way in the same process; a mesh rebuilt from each family's mesh file; and in a new process at another np, a rebuilt mesh and a freshly built gmsh mesh. Also a refined mesh (`refinement=1`, 1384 cells) at np 1 and 3, and 3 → 2.
- **Edge cases.**
  - a relative mesh cache (`UW_MESH_CACHE_DIR=.meshes`, the default) and a relative `outputPath`;
  - a rebuilt mesh that rewrites its own source file and reads it back;
  - files written by development, read by the branch (2→3, 3→2, 4→1).
- **Cost.** A box of cellSize 1/200, 279,426 points, written at np 4. `read_checkpoint` of a P2 field on the rebuilt mesh, 5 reads per run, two runs each. Median times: np 1, 0.268 / 0.275 s development and 0.267 / 0.280 s branch; np 4, 0.077 / 0.076 s and 0.079 / 0.079 s. The extra rank-0 read of two point-order arrays costs about 3 % at np 4 and nothing measurable at np 1.

### Read path × mesh × np

Exact = every field (P1, P2, vector, DG1) and every file family that applies. The np column covers 1, 2, 3, 4, 3 with an empty rank, and the random layouts unless a row says otherwise. "Default cache" = branch with the default relative mesh cache, measured at np 2 for in-process rows and at np 3 for the restart.

| read | mesh | development | branch, absolute cache | branch, default cache |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `read_checkpoint` | writing mesh (new or same variable, after adding variables, after `deform`) | exact at np 1; wrong at np ≥ 2 (1.13 to 2.83) | exact everywhere, refined mesh included | **wrong, 1.59** |
| `read_checkpoint` | writing mesh, refined, np 3 | raises, different messages on different ranks, then the job dies | exact | not run |
| `read_checkpoint` | another mesh, same gmsh file, same process | exact at np 1; wrong at np ≥ 2 | exact at np 1; refused at np ≥ 2 | **wrong, 1.59** |
| `read_checkpoint` | rebuilt from the checkpoint (same np and cross-np) | exact | exact | exact |
| `read_checkpoint` | fresh gmsh mesh, new process, 2→3, 3→2, 4→2, 4r→3r, 3→1 | wrong (1.13 to 2.83), np 1 included | refused on every rank | **wrong (restart driver 0.276 of 0.300)** |
| `read_checkpoint` | fresh gmsh mesh, file written at np 1 → np 3 | exact | exact | not run |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | writing mesh | exact; **DG1 wrong 0.0605 at every np** | exact; **DG1 wrong 0.0605 at every np** | not run |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | rebuilt mesh, np ≥ 2 | wrong (1.06 to 2.94) | exact | not run |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | rebuilt mesh, np 1, file written at np 3 or 4 | wrong: T, V 1.58; DG1 0.0605 | **wrong: T, V 1.57 to 1.58; DG1 0.0605** (P1 exact) | not run |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | fresh gmsh mesh, np ≥ 2 | wrong; in-process at the same np exact for continuous fields | refused | not run |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | fresh gmsh mesh, np 1, file written at np 3 or 4 | wrong (1.13 to 2.58) | **wrong (1.13 to 2.58)** | not run |
| `read_timestep`, file with `/fields` | any | exact | exact | exact |
| `read_timestep`, payload-only file | writing mesh | hang (np 2, 3) | exact | **wrong, 1.59** |
| `read_timestep`, payload-only file | rebuilt mesh, any np | hang | exact | not run |
| `read_timestep`, payload-only file | another or fresh mesh, np ≥ 2 | hang | refused | not run |
| `read_checkpoint` | rebuilt mesh that rewrote its own source file, np 3 | wrong, 1.55 | exact | not run |
| `read_checkpoint` | writing mesh, relative `outputPath` | wrong, 1.59 | refused its own file | n/a |
| files written by development, read on the branch | rebuilt / fresh | n/a | as the branch's own files: rebuilt exact, fresh refused, np 1 `load_from_h5_plex_vector` wrong | not run |

Refusals were always on every rank, with one message. No branch run hung. Swarm checkpoints do not use this point map: swarm `read_timestep` reads its own per-particle files. The disk snapshot (`Model.load_state`) passes `same_layout=True` and is untouched by the PR. Its tests pass on both builds (see Neighbours).

**The layout token.** It hashes each rank's point-SF graph: the leaves together with their remote owners. Two layouts that differ only in which rank owns a shared point have different leaf sets, so they get different tokens. `deform` and adding variables keep the token, and the reads stay exact. We found no way to repartition an existing mesh in place in this build (`remesh` needs mmg), so a file written and then read after an in-place repartition is not tested.

### 1. High: the check is off for any mesh loaded through a relative path, including the default mesh cache

`_from_plexh5` records `os.path.realpath(filename)` at line 264. That call is inside `with _short_io_path(filename)` (line 255), which has already changed into the file's directory. A relative `filename` then resolves against the wrong directory. The default gmsh cache path is `.meshes/uw_simplexbox_...msh.h5`, so the recorded source becomes `<cwd>/.meshes/.meshes/uw_...msh.h5`. That file does not exist, so `_point_map_verdict` returns `None` and the read takes the old unchecked path.

The same happens in `_write_petsc_reload_file`: the key at line 6749 is computed inside the `_short_io_path` opened at line 6726. With a relative `outputPath`, the writing mesh's own file is no longer recognised. It is refused rather than read wrong.

Measured at np 2 with `UW_MESH_CACHE_DIR=.meshes` (unset behaves the same):

| read | result |
|---|---|
| `read_checkpoint` on the writing mesh | wrong, 1.59 |
| `read_checkpoint` on another mesh | wrong, 1.59 (absolute cache: refused) |
| `read_timestep` of a payload-only file on the writing mesh | wrong, 1.59 (development: hang) |
| restart np 2 → 3 on a fresh mesh, `read_checkpoint` | wrong, 0.276 of 0.300, identical to development |
| the new test under CI's conditions (run from the source tree, no `UW_MESH_CACHE_DIR`) | 4 failed, 3 passed at np 2, np 3 and np 3 with an empty rank: the writing-mesh case, the another-mesh case and both payload cases. With an absolute cache: 7 passed at np 2 and 3. |

**Fix:**
- take the real path before entering `_short_io_path`, in both places:
  - `source = os.path.realpath(filename)` above line 255;
  - `key = os.path.realpath(checkpoint_file)` above line 6726.
- add a case to the test that runs with a relative cache directory, so it cannot pass only because of the environment.

### 2. High: `load_from_h5_plex_vector` is still silently wrong in two cases

- **At np 1, on a file written in parallel.** The new route through `read_checkpoint` is behind `if uw.mpi.size > 1` (line 2107). At np 1 the vector is loaded as stored, in the writer's parallel order:
  - on the mesh rebuilt from the checkpoint, files written at np 3 and 4: T and V wrong by 1.57 to 1.58, DG1 by 0.0605;
  - on a fresh mesh: 1.13 to 2.58.
  This is the serial post-processing of a parallel run, the use the docs recommend for metrics-from-checkpoint jobs.
- **For a discontinuous field, on every mesh at every np, the writing mesh included: 0.0605.** The direct path loads a DM-associated vector. That reads `/fields/<name>`, not the restart payload, and for DG1 `/fields` is not stored in the vector's order. A payload-only file has no `/fields` at all, so the direct path fails there with PETSc error 79 on every rank.

Both cases are wrong on development too, so this PR does not cause them. But the PR presents this function as fixed, and the fix is one line: let `load_from_h5_plex_vector` always return `self.read_checkpoint(filename, data_name=data_name)`. The token check gives the writing mesh `same_layout`, which reads the restart payload; that read was exact for DG1 in every run. Every other mesh goes through the point map or is refused, at np 1 as well.

### 3. Medium: when the check cannot be made, the read falls back to the path that was silently wrong

A mesh loaded from a file has an SF, and the checkpoint has a saved point order. If the source file cannot be read, `_point_map_verdict` returns `None` and the read goes ahead unchecked. In parallel, that is the old silently-wrong path. Finding 1 is one way to get there. Others are:
- the cache directory was cleaned between the run and the restart;
- the restart runs on another machine;
- the cache file was regenerated with another numbering.

Suggestion: at np > 1, when the mesh has a file SF and the variable file has a saved order but the source cannot be read, take the token check, and refuse if it fails. Keep "unchanged" only for files with no saved order (legacy) and for meshes with no file SF.

### 4. Low: the refusal message's first suggestion does not help for payload-only files

The message says "Read it with read_timestep (the coordinate remap)". For a file written with `create_xdmf=False`, `read_timestep` now hands back to `read_checkpoint` and gives the same refusal; we saw this on the fresh mesh and on the other-mesh cases. Suggestion: name the rebuilt-mesh route first, and say that `read_timestep` needs a file written with `create_xdmf=True`.

### 5. Low: an unreadable source file hangs the job

`_point_map_verdict` opens the source file with `h5py` on rank 0 alone, before the `bcast`, with no `try`. A source file that exists but cannot be opened raises on rank 0 only (locked, truncated, or being rewritten by another job sharing the cache), and the other ranks wait in the `bcast`. Catching `OSError` there would avoid that (with finding 3, a failed check then refuses on every rank).

### Neighbours

| set | development | branch |
|---|---|---|
| Serial, 18 files that use `read_checkpoint`, `read_timestep`, `write_timestep`, `petsc_reload`, `load_from_h5_plex_vector`, `write_checkpoint` or swarm checkpoints (4 workers) | 202 passed, 1 skipped | 202 passed, 1 skipped |
| Serial, 6 snapshot and restart files (test_0010, test_0020 ×2, test_1063, test_1074, test_1079) | 50 passed, 1 xfailed | 50 passed, 1 xfailed |
| Parallel at np 2 and 3: ptest_0762, ptest_0864, test_0005_xdmf_viz_topology_mpi, test_0690, test_0757, test_0790, test_0795 | 17 passed at each np | 17 passed at each np |
| Parallel at np 2: test_0020 (swarm values), test_1155, test_0079 | 5 passed | 5 passed |
| ptest_0010 as a script at np 4; ptest_1079 as a script at np 2 | PASS; exit 0 | PASS; exit 0 |
| The new test, CI's conditions (relative cache) | the first cases fail and the payload cases hang (exit 124), at np 2 and 3, relative or absolute cache | 4 failed, 3 passed at np 2, 3 and 3 with an empty rank |
| The new test, absolute cache | — | 7 passed at np 2 and 3 |

Every outcome other than the new test's is the same on both builds. Under pytest, ptest_0010 is not collected and ptest_1079 errors at collection, on both builds. So both were run as scripts, the way their headers say.

### Not run

- 3-D meshes, manifold meshes, and meshes built from a DMPlex (`adapt` children, `SphericalManifold`): the PR leaves their path unchanged.
- In-place repartition, which needs `remesh` and mmg (not in this build).
- np above 4.
- The `/uw_checkpoint` legacy group beyond test_0005_xdmf_compat (serial).
- Faulted (split) meshes beyond ptest_0864 and test_0864.
- With the default cache, only the np 2 in-process cases and the np 3 restart were run, not the full matrix.

Logs kept by the reviewer.
````
