### #1312 at f9438460: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**At f9438460: changes requested.**

The refusal works for the cases the PR names. Plain ints, floats, bools, numpy scalars and sympy `Float`/`Integer`/`Rational` on a field with units are refused. This holds for essential and natural conditions, alone or in a tuple, and the message names the condition, the component and the `uw.quantity(v, "<units>")` form. Every rank refuses identically, including an empty rank. Quantities give the same model values as on development in all 124 + 79 cases of the #1264 probes. Fields without units and models without reference quantities are unchanged. The three edited tests record the same 54 values as before. None of the 26 docs files hits a new refusal.

One problem needs fixing before merge (finding 1). A number that sympy does not class as a `Number` (`sympy.pi`, `sympy.sqrt(2)`, `sympy.E`, `2*sympy.pi`) gets past the refusal. Development divided such a value by the field's scaling coefficient. This PR removes that division, so the value is now silently read in model units instead. Two medium findings should be fixed with it or right after.

## What users see differently

- In a model with reference quantities, a nonzero plain number on a field with units is now refused, where development accepted it. The message suggests the quantity form. Development read such a number as SI and divided it by the field's scaling coefficient. For example, `(0.5, 0.0)` on the solver's velocity gave 1.58e9, a plain traction of 1e6 gave 3.2e15, and 1300.0 on a K field gave 1.0.
- A lone `uw.expression` or sympy expression is now accepted as a value. Development raised "Unknown quantity type" or "Unsupported BC conds". The same holds for a lone sympy number, `sympy.oo` and `sympy.Integer(0)`.
- With reference quantities set and `uw.use_nondimensional_scaling(False)`, a quantity now records its SI magnitude: 1300 K records 1300.0, where development recorded 1.0. This now matches the `uw.expression` route and development's plain-number route in that mode.
- `SNES_Stokes.add_basal_traction([uw.quantity(1, "MPa"), 0], ...)` on the solver's own fields is now refused (finding 2). Development accepted it but gave -9.96e7.
- A level-set solver whose level-set field has length units, with `far_field=1.0`, is now refused.

## Method

- **Builds.** Head f9438460 was built fresh. Its compiled modules are newer than the build start, and the new message strings are in the `.so`. The base is the merge base 7091a93c (development). It is an existing build whose compiled sources are identical to 7091a93c, with its installed Python checked file by file against 7091a93c's. Every run prints `underworld3.__file__`.
- **Reference quantities.** 10 km, 1e21 Pa s and 10 mm/yr, plus 1300 K where a temperature is used. So 5 mm/yr is 0.5, 1 MPa is 0.031558, and 1300 K is 1.0.
- **New probes.**
  - A matrix of 761 cases on the recorded value, in a units model and without reference quantities. Fields: T in K, T in degC, u in km, u without units, u dimensionless, the solver's velocity, a passed velocity without units, and a passed velocity in m/s. Every value kind listed above, essential and natural, alone and in tuples.
  - Solves with scaling switched off.
  - Lone expressions through a solve, with the expression updated between solves.
  - The library paths that set conditions themselves: Nitsche, a rotated free-slip datum, basal traction and drag, and the level set.
  - A parallel probe at np 1, np 2 (horizontal bands) and np 3 with an empty rank (cells 121/121/0, shell partition of a gmsh box).
- **Earlier probes.** The #1264 probes were rerun on both builds.
- **Recorded values.** In the edited tests and in the docs runs, every `add_condition` call was recorded through a wrapper, on both builds, and the records compared.
- **Load.** 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine.

## Results

**(a) Refusal, serial (units model).**

| value | field with units (K, degC, km, m/s; essential and natural) | field without units or dimensionless |
|---|---|---|
| 2, 0.5, True, nan, `np.float64(0.5)`, `sympy.Float/Integer/Rational` | refused, alone and in a tuple | accepted, unchanged |
| `np.float32`, `np.int64` | refused in a tuple; alone: "Unsupported BC conds" (as on development) | same |
| 0, 0.0, -0.0, `sympy.Integer(0)`, `sympy.Float(0)`, `np.float64(0)` | accepted, records 0.0 | accepted |
| inf, `np.inf`, `sympy.oo`, `-sympy.oo`, None | accepted, free | accepted |
| `y*0.5`, `sin(x)` | accepted, symbolic | accepted |
| `sympy.pi`, `sympy.sqrt(2)`, `sympy.E` | **accepted, recorded as 3.14159 / 1.41421 / 2.71828** | accepted |
| `uw.expression` holding a plain 0.5 | accepted, 0.5 | accepted |
| `sympy.Matrix([0.5])`, `[0.5]` | refused | accepted |

The message reads, for example: `essential (Dirichlet) condition on 'Left' component 0: the plain number 0.5 on the field 'V6', which is in meter / second, in a model with reference quantities, is ambiguous: 0.5 of what? Give it units, e.g. uw.quantity(0.5, "meter / second"). Zero needs none.`

**Parallel.** Four refusals: `(0.5, None)`, `(None, np.float64(2))`, natural `(0, 1e6)` and `(Rational(1,2), 0)`. All ranks raised the same message, at np 2 band and np 3 with an empty rank. No condition was recorded, and an allreduce right after reached every rank. The accepted values were the same on every rank: `(q 5 mm/yr, 0)` → [0.5, 0.0], `(0, -0.0)`, `(None, Integer(0))`, and natural `(0, q 0.1 MPa)` → 0.0031558. The solve after the refusals gave a left-wall vx of 0.5 at np 1, 2 and 3. The integral of v·v was 1.9156022e-20 at np 1 and 1.9155884e-20 at np 2 and 3. The same pattern held on a degC Poisson field: the integral of T was 513.42497 at np 1 and 513.42501 at np 2 and 3. As in #1264's runs, np 2 and np 3 agree to all digits, and np 1 differs at the 1e-5 level.

**Fields without units, and models without reference quantities.** In the 761-case probe without reference quantities, the two builds agree in every case except the new lone-expression and lone-sympy acceptances. On fields without units in the units model, all plain-number cases are the same digits on both builds. In the #1264 probe without reference quantities, all 79 cases are identical except two lone `uw.expression` cases, which development refused.

**(b) Quantities.**

- In the #1264 probes (79 + 124 + 52 cases), every case that both builds accept gives the same digits. The differences are only plain numbers on fields with units, now refused, and lone expressions, now accepted.
- On development the lone expressions raised. On head, the Poisson wall with a lone `uw.expression` of 5 km reads 0.5, the same as the quantity.

**With `use_nondimensional_scaling(False)` and reference quantities set** (T field in K, 1300 K reference):

| value | development: recorded / field data | head: recorded / field data |
|---|---|---|
| `uw.quantity(1300, "K")` | 1.0 / 1 | 1300.0 / 1300 |
| `uw.quantity(1026.85, "degC")` | 1.0 / 1 | 1300.0 / 1300 |
| plain 1300.0 | 1300.0 / 1300 | refused |
| `uw.expression` of 1300 K | refused (lone) | sym / 1300 |

So on head a quantity agrees with the expression route and with development's plain-number route in this mode. On both builds, `evaluate()` then reads that field back as 1.69e6 K, so the mode is not self-consistent either way.

It is a behaviour change for anyone who sets reference quantities, switches scaling off, and gives boundary values as quantities. `test_0817` is the only user of that combination in the repository. The Stokes variant of the toggle could not be compared, because with a 1e21 Pa s viscosity and scaling off the pressure preconditioner hits a zero pivot on both builds.

**(c) Lone expressions.** These cases are on head; development refused all of them.

- On a T field in K with a 1300 K reference, a lone `uw.expression` of 300 K records `300 [kelvin]`, which unwraps to 0.230769.
- Updated between solves to 300 K, then 650 K, then 1026.85 degC, the wall reads 300, 650 and 1300 K.
- A lone `sin(y)` stays symbolic.
- Without reference quantities, a lone expression of 2.0 gives a wall of 2.

**Library paths, in a units model with the solver's own fields:**

| path | development | head |
|---|---|---|
| `add_nitsche_bc(0.5, …)` / `(uw.quantity(5 mm/yr))` | accepted / accepted | same (does not go through `add_condition`) |
| `add_rotated_freeslip_bc(0.5, …)` datum | stored 0.5 | same |
| `add_rotated_freeslip_bc(uw.quantity(5, "mm/yr"), …)` | SympifyError | same |
| `add_basal_traction([q 1 MPa, 0])`, solver's fields | -9.96e7 (wrong) | **refused** |
| `add_basal_traction([q 1 MPa, 0])`, passed fields | -0.0315576 | -0.0315576 |
| `add_basal_traction([0.5, 0])` | -1.58e9 | refused |
| `add_basal_drag(1.0, ref [q 5 mm/yr, 0])` | `u - 0.5` | same |
| level set `far_field=1.0`, level-set field without units / in m | accepted / accepted | accepted / refused |

These library paths add their own conditions and keep working in a units model, because each passes zeros, symbolic values or fields without units:
- the free surface's derived solves (their velocity fields carry no units);
- the dual projection (zeros, on a field without units);
- the stress-history inflow condition and the cylindrical walls (symbolic).

"Outside library-internal work" in the code means: not inside the `_internal_resampling()` context. No library call of `add_condition` runs inside that context today, so the exemption is unused for now.

**Edited tests.** `test_0817`, `test_0818` and `test_1450` record the same 54 values on both builds, digit for digit. Examples:
- `test_0817`, scaling off: 1000.0, and 1.0 with scaling on;
- `test_0818`: (1.0, 0.0) on both settings;
- `test_1450`: 300.0 and 1600.0.

So the quantities express the same physics as the old plain numbers. Head's `test_0774` run against the development build fails exactly the two new tests (the refusal, and the lone expression), and passes the rest (15).

**Docs (26 files, 900 s cap each, both builds).**
- Exit status and final error are the same on both builds for all 26.
- The recorded values of every boundary condition set (88 calls) are identical on both builds, and none was refused.
- `Ex_Convection_Annulus_Recorded` and tutorials 13, 14 and 16 run to the end on both builds.
- Tutorials 12 and 15, the taylor_test transcript and five more examples fail the same way on both builds, for reasons unrelated to this PR: a mesh built from a quantity, `mesh.adapt` renamed, the remesher missing from PETSc, a parameter renamed, a missing module, and error code 56 in the sinking block.
- fault_friction and the Spiegelman benchmark reach the 900 s cap on both builds. Their conditions up to then are identical.
- Two listed files (AnnulusFS, Ex_viscousFingering) are no longer in the docs tree.

## Regression runs

- **Serial, head, at most 4 workers.** 214 files: tests 0500–0899, 1450, the free-surface (1070), rotated free-slip (1018, 0883, 0884, 1066), constrained-solver (0022, 0062), Nitsche (1060), flux and body-force tests, and the fault files (0845–0866, 1029, 1077, 1087, 1107, 1120, 1174). Result: 2007 passed, 25 skipped, 19 xfailed, 1 xpassed, no failures (68 min).
- **Edited and units-BC tests, serial, both builds** (0774, 0817, 0818, 1450): head 29 passed; development 26 passed with its own copy of 0774.
- **Parallel, head, np 2, file by file.** These 14 files pass: 0765, 0766, 0768, 0770, 0774 empty rank, 0856, 0992, 1065, 1066 datum, both 1069 files, 1074 free surface on an empty rank, 1086 slip classification, and 1103 split fault slip. Result: 55 passed, 3 skipped. `test_1064_rotated_freeslip_parallel` passes its first eight tests and then reaches the time limit, on head (600 s) and on development (900 s) alike.
- **Under mpirun at np 2, both builds** (0774, 0810, 0812, 0817, 0818, 1450): the same two failures on each, `test_0812::test_poisson_check_bc_values` and `test_0817::test_poisson_scaling_improves_conditioning`. Both tests are serial by nature, and the results do not depend on this PR.

## Findings

1. **High: a number that is not a sympy `Number` bypasses the refusal, and its meaning changed silently.**

   The check is `isinstance(val, sympy.Basic) and val.is_Number`. `sympy.pi`, `sympy.sqrt(2)`, `sympy.E` and products such as `2*sympy.pi` are numbers but not `Number` atoms, so they pass. The removed scaling path treated any sympy value without free symbols as numeric and divided it by the coefficient. With that path gone, these values are now read in model units:

   | value, field | development | head |
   |---|---|---|
   | `(sympy.pi,)` on a T field in K (1300 K reference) | 0.0024166 (π K) | 3.14159 (about 4084 K) |
   | `(sympy.pi, 0)` on a velocity | 9.9e9 | 3.14159 |

   No error is raised. Using `val.is_number` (true for these, false for a `uw.expression` and for anything with a coordinate) would refuse them like any other plain number. Please add `sympy.pi` and `sympy.sqrt(2)` to the refusal test.

2. **Medium: `SNES_Stokes.add_basal_traction` with a quantity is refused on the solver's own fields.**

   `_nondimensional_row` turns `uw.quantity(1, "MPa")` into the model number 0.0316 and passes it to `add_natural_bc`. That call now refuses it as a plain number, and tells the user to write `uw.quantity(-0.0315576, <the flux's units>)`, which is what they did. On passed fields without units, the same call gives the right -0.0315576.

   Development accepted the call but divided the value again by the velocity scale (-9.96e7), so this is a refusal replacing a wrong answer. Still, the documented quantity route of this method no longer works on the default fields. Passing the quantity through to `add_natural_bc`, which converts it, would fix it. A test with a quantity traction on the solver's fields would cover it.

3. **Medium: plain 0 on an offset-unit field is not "the same in any units".**

   On a T field in degC, a plain 0 is accepted ("Zero needs none") and records 0.0. That is 0 K: the field reads back 3e-16 K, which is -273.15 °C. Meanwhile `uw.quantity(0, "degC")` records 0.2101 (273.15 K). Development recorded the same 0.0, so the value is not new.

   But the PR now states, in the message and in both docstrings, that zero needs no units. For degC and degF that is false, and a user writing `add_dirichlet_bc(0, "Top")` on a degC field means 0 °C. Refusing plain 0 on an offset-unit field, or narrowing the wording to "zero on a field whose unit has no offset", would close this.

4. **Low: a lone `uw.expression` is accepted without a dimension check.**
   - An expression of 5 mm/yr on a T field records 0.5, and an expression of 300 K on a km field records 0.23. Quantities get the dimension check from #1264; expressions do not. Expressions in tuples were not checked on development either.
   - An expression holding a plain 0.5 on a K field is accepted as 0.5 model units (650 K on the wall). That is a route around the refusal.

   Neither needs to block, but the dimension check could look at the expression's units when it has them.

5. **Low: a lone value on a vector field is one entry.** A lone `uw.expression` (or a lone 0.5, as on development) on the Stokes velocity records one entry and constrains x only. With expressions now accepted alone, `add_essential_bc(V_wall, "Left")` on a 2-D velocity sets vx and silently leaves vy free. A message asking for one entry per component, as #1129 did for short values, would be safer.

6. **Low: the rule covers `add_condition` only.**
   - `add_nitsche_bc` accepts a plain 0.5, and a quantity, without conversion or check.
   - A rotated free-slip datum accepts a plain 0.5, and fails on a quantity with a SympifyError.
   - A level-set solver whose field has length units now refuses its own `far_field` float, with a message that points at `add_dirichlet_bc`, which the user never called.

   All of these except the level set behave as on development. This is worth an issue that lists these paths.

7. **Low: the scaling-off change (b) is a behaviour change, and the PR body should say so.**
   - A quantity now records its SI magnitude with `use_nondimensional_scaling(False)`. For 1300 K with a 1300 K reference, development recorded 1.0 and head records 1300.0.
   - It is consistent with the expression route and with development's plain numbers.
   - `evaluate()` reads the field back as 1.69e6 K on both builds in this mode, so the mode itself needs a look (separate issue).

8. **Low: without reference quantities, a lone expression and a quantity disagree.** A lone `uw.expression` of 5 km gives a wall of 5, while `uw.quantity(5, "km")` gives 5000 (SI). Development refused the lone expression, so this is newly reachable. The same expression in a tuple gave 5 on development too.

9. **Low, cosmetic.**
   - A lone `sympy.oo` is now accepted as a condition with no constrained component, where development said "Unsupported BC conds". The open PR that refuses an all-free condition will catch it.
   - A lone `np.float32` or `np.int64` still gets "Unsupported BC conds", while the same value in a tuple gets the units message.
   - The natural-condition message shows the literal text `<the flux's units>`, where it could name the flux's units.

10. **Low, existing before this PR.**
    - `test_1064_rotated_freeslip_parallel` reaches the time limit at np 2 on both builds.
    - `test_0812::test_poisson_check_bc_values` and `test_0817::test_poisson_scaling_improves_conditioning` fail under mpirun at np 2 on both builds.

## Not run

- The serial run on the development build (head had no failures). The full test suite. Parallel runs at np 4.
- Tensor-valued conditions, and pressure-field (f_id 1) conditions through `add_condition` directly.
- `add_fault_bc` with a nonzero coefficient in a units model. Its `conds` is a viscosity passed to the fault-contact code, not through `add_condition`. The fault test files pass.
- The Stokes solve with scaling switched off: both builds stop at a zero pivot.
- The docs examples past their 900 s cap (fault_friction, the Spiegelman benchmark), and those that fail on both builds before any condition is set.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1306 at 75cb427b: ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at 75cb427b: ready to merge on green from this review.** The three entry points the PR names now take a raw pint quantity and record the same value as the `uw.quantity` form, and solves are bitwise identical. One more place a `uw.quantity` is taken still refuses the raw form: the Poisson source term `f` (finding 2). It can be a follow-up, but the title says "wherever", so either fix it here (one line) or narrow the wording.

## Method

- The PR changes Python only. I built the merge base (development 657d984e) fresh, then made two overlays on that install. The base overlay is the install as built; its four changed `.py` files match 657d984e byte for byte. The head overlay replaces those four files with the 75cb427b versions. Every run printed `underworld3.__file__` to confirm which overlay it used.
- A probe script set each value in the raw form and in the `uw.quantity` form and compared the recorded value (`uw.unwrap(..., keep_constants=False)` and `.units`). I ran it in three units modes:
  - reference quantities with `use_nondimensional_scaling(True)`;
  - reference quantities with scaling off (the default);
  - no reference quantities.
- Solve check: a Stokes problem (viscosity and a two-component body force) and a Poisson diffusion problem, each solved once with raw inputs and once with `uw.quantity` inputs. I compared velocity, pressure and temperature bitwise, serial in all three modes and at np 2.
- The tutorial `docs/examples/Tutorial_Thermal_Convection_Units.py` ran as committed and in a variant that sets the diffusivity and viscosity as raw pint (`1e-6 * uw.units.m**2 / uw.units.s`, `1e21 * uw.units.Pa * uw.units.s`), on both builds.
- Neighbours:
  - Serial bands `test_00*` and `test_05*` to `test_08*` with 4 xdist workers, on both builds.
  - Parallel units files at np 2, on both builds: `test_0750`, `test_0755`, `test_0770`, `test_0774`, `test_0785`, `test_0992`.
- All runs were pinned to 4 cores, alongside other jobs on the machine. Logs kept by the reviewer.

## Results

- **New test `test_0778_raw_pint_quantities.py`:** 5 passed on head. On base, 3 failed (parameter, body force, expression) and 2 passed (`non_dimensionalise`, `.array`), as the body says.
- **Recorded values, raw against `uw.quantity`, identical in every mode:**
  - Parameters: `DiffusionModel.diffusivity`, `ViscousFlowModel.shear_viscosity_0` (including `1e18 kPa*s`, which is recorded with its own units and the same reduced value), `ViscoPlasticFlowModel.yield_stress`, `DarcyFlowModel.permeability`.
  - Expressions: `uw.expression(...)` with and without `units=`, and `.sym =`.
  - Body force as a tuple, as a list, mixed raw + `uw.quantity`, raw + a plain number, and raw + a sympy term.
  - Through the `.sym` setter, `NavierStokesSLCN.rho` and `Stokes.penalty` also take the raw form now; on base they raised.
  - On base every raw case raised `DimensionalityError: Cannot convert from '...' to 'dimensionless'`.
- **Solves bitwise:**
  - Velocity, pressure and temperature hashes match for raw and quantity inputs in all three modes. The reference-quantity runs had max |v| about 73 model units (scaling on) and 7.3e-7 (scaling off).
  - At np 2 the result was bitwise identical on both ranks.
  - On base the raw-input solve raises at the parameter setter.
- **Pint variants:**
  - From a separate `pint.UnitRegistry()`: converted, with the same values as the `uw.units` form. The helper goes through `str(value.units)`. A unit defined only in the other registry would fail with pint's undefined-unit error, which is a clean refusal.
  - Offset units: `Quantity(1300, degC)` as an expression or a `.sym` value is recorded as 1573.15 K, the same as `uw.quantity(1300, "degC")`.
  - Dimensionless: the raw form now records `units=dimensionless` and the same value as the quantity form. On base it was taken as a bare sympy number with `units=None`.
  - Numpy-array pint:
    - As a parameter, stored as the same array as the quantity form.
    - As `uw.expression`, both forms raise the same `TypeError`.
    - As a body force, `np.array([0, -3e4]) * Pa/m` works because it is iterated element by element. `uw.quantity(np.array(...), "Pa/m")` raises `'UWQuantity' object is not iterable`, so here the raw form is more permissive than the quantity form (Low, pre-existing).
  - pint Measurement: not testable, because `uncertainties` is not in the environment.
- **Wrong dimensions:** the two forms are consistent: neither is refused. `diffusivity = 1 Pa` and a body force in `Pa` are taken in both raw and `uw.quantity` form, on head and on base. The PR keeps that consistency and does not add a dimension check, so this is not a regression.
- **Places outside the diff:**
  - Already took raw pint on base, and still do:
    - `uw.non_dimensionalise`;
    - mesh-variable and swarm-variable `.array`;
    - `add_dirichlet_bc`;
    - `UnstructuredSimplexBox` coordinates and cell size;
    - `Annulus` radii and cell size;
    - `delta_t`.
  - Still refuse raw pint: `Poisson.f` (finding 2), and arithmetic of `MeshVariable.sym` or a `uw.expression` with a raw pint quantity.
  - Refuse both forms: `AdvDiffusionSLCN.f` (`SympifyError` for the quantity form) and `NavierStokesSLCN.bodyforce` as a tuple. Both are outside this PR.
- **Tutorial:**
  - As committed, the output is identical on head and base.
  - The raw-pint variant fails on base at the viscosity setter (`DimensionalityError`). On head it runs to the end with step-by-step output identical to the committed version (mean T 0.634, same velocities). The only differences are in how the quantities print.
- **Serial bands:**
  - Head: 2392 passed, 1 failed, 25 skipped, 19 xfailed, 1 xpassed.
  - Base: 2388 passed, 0 failed, same skips and xfails.
  - The one failure is `test_0849_plot_default_colormap::test_save_colorbar_with_its_default_colormap` (`module 'matplotlib' has no attribute 'backend_bases'`, inside a shared xdist worker). It passes alone on head, and after `test_0778` in the same process. This PR does not touch the visualisation code; the failure depends on how tests are spread across workers (head has 5 more tests).
- **Parallel units files at np 2:** all passed on both builds (13, 11, 2, 7, 16 + 1 skipped, 4).

## Findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium: `Poisson.f` (the source term) takes `uw.quantity(1e-12, "K/s")` but refuses `1e-12 * uw.units("K/s")` with the same `DimensionalityError` this PR removes elsewhere. This holds in all three units modes, on head. The cause is the setter's duck test `hasattr(value, "value")`, which a pint Quantity does not pass. A `from_raw_pint(value)` call at the top of that setter would close it, together with a raw case in `test_0778`. The alternative is to scope the title and body to "a constitutive parameter, an expression and the Stokes body force".
3. Low:
   - The `not isinstance(value, UWQuantity)` guard in `from_raw_pint` never fires, because `UWQuantity` is not a pint subclass and nothing in `src/` subclasses pint Quantity. It is harmless, and it would protect against a future subclass.
   - Body force from a numpy-array quantity: the raw form now works, but `uw.quantity(np.array(...))` still raises (pre-existing). This is a small asymmetry worth a line in a follow-up.
   - Aside, not this PR: in the committed thermal convection tutorial, `print(f"Ra = {Ra.to_base_units().magnitude:.2e}")` prints `1.26e+06 [second / meter ** 2]` when the diffusivity is a `uw.quantity`, and a plain `1.26e+06` when it is raw pint. The magnitude is right, but a pint-by-`UWQuantity` mix leaves stray units on a quantity that should be dimensionless. It deserves its own issue.

## Not run

- pint Measurement (`uncertainties` is not installed).
- The full `scripts/test.sh --parallel-only` sweep: only the parallel units files listed above were run.
- Notebook tutorials (`12-Units_System`, `13-Scaling...`): they set parameters with `uw.quantity`, and their raw-pint lines feed `set_reference_quantities` and arithmetic only.
- np greater than 2.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1310 re-check at f2ed5b16 (local head, to be pushed): changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

## Re-check of #1310 at f2ed5b16

**Verdict at f2ed5b16 (the branch head as it will be pushed): changes requested.**

The five findings from the review at a3ccfa0c are fixed. With the default relative mesh cache, an absolute cache, or a relative `outputPath`, every read in the full matrix is exact or refused on every rank: 4,824 cases, no wrong value, no hang. The restart driver is exact or refused on every route. `load_from_h5_plex_vector` is now exact at np 1 on files written in parallel, and exact for discontinuous P1 fields. The PR's test passes all 9 cases at np 1, 2, 3 and 3 with an empty rank, under CI's conditions. The neighbour tests give the same results as development.

One silent wrong read remains (new finding 1). At np 1, when the mesh's source file cannot be read at read time, the point map is trusted without a check. A freshly built mesh then reads a file written in parallel wrong, by 1.58 for a P2 field and 2.53 for a discontinuous field. The same read with the source present is refused. The fix is to drop one condition. A missing variable file still hangs a parallel job, as it does on development (new finding 2).

### The five findings, then and now

| # | at a3ccfa0c | at f2ed5b16 |
|---|---|---|
| 1. High: check off with a relative path / the default cache | wrong 1.59 on the writing mesh and on another mesh. The payload-only `read_timestep` was wrong. A fresh-mesh restart np 2 → 3 was wrong by 0.276 of 0.300. The PR's test failed 4 of 7 under CI's conditions. | **Fixed.** The real path is taken before `_short_io_path` in both places. Results are identical with the default cache, an absolute cache and a relative `outputPath` / relative read paths: the per-case outcome tables are the same in all 26 runs of each mode. The restart np 2 → 3 and 3 → 2 on a fresh mesh is refused on every rank. Test: 9/9 at np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty. |
| 2. High: `load_from_h5_plex_vector` wrong at np 1 on parallel files, and for DG P1 | T, V 1.57 to 1.58 and DG1 0.0605 on the rebuilt mesh at np 1. Fresh mesh 1.13 to 2.58. DG1 0.0605 at every np, writing mesh included. | **Fixed.** It now returns `read_checkpoint`. It is exact on the writing mesh (DG P1 included) and on the rebuilt mesh at np 1 from files written at np 2, 3, 4 and random layouts. It is refused on a fresh mesh, at every np. Restart `rebuilt_lp` is exact at 2→3, 3→2 and 3→1. |
| 3. Medium: unverifiable map fell back to the unchecked path in parallel | the old wrong path | **Fixed at np ≥ 2.** Source renamed or unreadable after loading: the writing mesh reads its own files exactly, and every other mesh is refused on every rank with one message. **Not fixed at np 1:** see new finding 1. |
| 4. Low: refusal advice loops for payload-only files | named `read_timestep` first | **Fixed.** The message names the rebuilt mesh first. It offers `read_timestep` only "if the file holds fields/ (written with create_xdmf=True)". |
| 5. Low: rank 0 opens the source with no `try` | possible hang | **Fixed.** `OSError` gives "unknown" on rank 0, and the verdict is broadcast. Source mode 000 at np 2, 3 and 3-empty: refused on every rank, no hang. |

### Read path × mesh × np, default relative cache

The branch at f2ed5b16. `UW_MESH_CACHE_DIR` is unset, and runs start in a directory where `.meshes` is relative. Exact = all of P2 scalar, P2 vector, P1 and DG P1, and every file family that applies. The np column covers 1, 2, 3, 4, np 3 with an empty rank, and two random layouts. Cross-np reads are 2→3, 3→2, 1→3, 4→2, 3→1, 4→1, 2→1, random 4→3, 2→3-empty, 3-empty→3-empty, 3-empty→2 and random 3→1.

| read | mesh | f2ed5b16, default cache |
|---|---|---|
| `read_checkpoint` | writing mesh: new or same variable, after adding variables, after `deform` | exact |
| `read_checkpoint` | another mesh, same gmsh file, same process | exact at np 1; refused at np ≥ 2 |
| `read_checkpoint` | rebuilt from the checkpoint (`ck`, `po` and `wc` mesh files), same np and cross-np | exact |
| `read_checkpoint` | fresh gmsh mesh, new process, file written at np ≥ 2 (np 1 readers included) | refused on every rank |
| `read_checkpoint` | fresh gmsh mesh, file written at np 1, read at np 3 | exact (the saved order is the cache file's); refined mesh: refused |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | writing mesh, DG P1 included, every np | exact |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | rebuilt mesh, np 1 on files written at np 2, 3, 4 and random layouts | exact |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | another or fresh mesh | as `read_checkpoint`: exact at np 1 in-process, refused otherwise |
| `read_timestep`, file with `/fields` | any mesh, any np | exact |
| `read_timestep`, payload-only file | writing mesh, rebuilt mesh | exact |
| `read_timestep`, payload-only file | another mesh (np ≥ 2) or fresh mesh | refused |
| refined mesh (`refinement=1`), written at np 1, 2, 3; read 3→2, 1→3, 2→3, 3→1 | writing, rebuilt (storage-v3 `write_checkpoint` `wc` mesh included), fresh | writing and rebuilt exact; another mesh refused, np 1 included (as at a3ccfa0c); fresh refused |
| files written by development (bf022b14), read 2→3, 3→2, 4→1, 1→3, refined 3→2, 3→1 | rebuilt, fresh | rebuilt exact (np 1 `load_from_h5_plex_vector` included, which was wrong at a3ccfa0c); fresh refused, except a file written at np 1, which is exact everywhere |

Absolute cache, and relative `outputPath` with relative read paths: the same outcome in every case. No refusal had different messages on different ranks, and no matrix run hung.

### Runs

- **Builds.** f2ed5b16 is two commits on from a3ccfa0c and changes only `discretisation_mesh.py`, `discretisation_mesh_variables.py` and the test. It is Python-only. We copied the development build's (bf022b14) installed package and laid f2ed5b16's `src/underworld3` `.py` files over the copy. It differs from the a3ccfa0c overlay in exactly the two changed modules. Development and a3ccfa0c are the review's overlays. Every run printed `underworld3.__file__` and used the intended overlay.
- **Conditions.** MPICH, at most 4 ranks on 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine. Every parallel run went through the repository's MPI supervisor (silence 60 to 120 s) inside an outer timeout.
- **Matrix.** The review's probe in three modes: default relative cache, absolute cache, and relative `outputPath` with every read path relative. Each mode had 26 runs: 10 writes and 16 cross-np reads, refined meshes included. That makes 1,608 cases per mode, 4,824 in all: no wrong value, all exit 0.
- **Restart driver** (Poisson, `write_timestep(..., petsc_reload=True)` every 2 steps), default cache. Runs at np 2 and 3. Restarts 2→3 and 3→2 on 8 routes each, and 3→1 on 5 routes:
  - Rebuilt mesh with `read_checkpoint`, `read_timestep`, payload-only `read_checkpoint` or `load_from_h5_plex_vector`: step 2 exact. Step 4 is within 8.5e-15 of the uninterrupted run (1.4e-14 at np 1), for a field of size 0.300.
  - Fresh mesh with `read_timestep`: exact.
  - Fresh mesh with `read_checkpoint`, payload `read_checkpoint` or `load_from_h5_plex_vector`: refused on every rank, one message.
  - With the cache pointed at a new, empty directory (the cache cleaned, or another machine), the fresh-mesh restarts 2→3 and 3→1 regenerate the cache file and are refused.
- **Unverifiable map.** Each run started in its own new directory with its own `.meshes`. After the mesh was loaded, rank 0 renamed the mesh's source file, or set its mode to 000. Only files the run had created itself were touched: its own cache file and its own copies of the checkpoint mesh files. Runs at np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty, P2 and DG P1, 12 reads each:
  - **np ≥ 2:** the writing mesh is exact on `read_checkpoint`, `load_from_h5_plex_vector` and payload-only `read_timestep`. Another mesh, a fresh mesh reading a file from another np, and rebuilt copies are refused on every rank with one message. `read_timestep` of a `/fields` file is exact. No hang.
  - **np 1:** see new finding 1.
- **test_1302** (9 cases), run from the source tree:

  | build, cache | np 1 | np 2 | np 3 | np 3, empty rank |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | f2ed5b16, default cache | 9 passed | 9 passed | 9 passed | 9 passed |
  | f2ed5b16, absolute cache | — | 9 passed | 9 passed | — |
  | a3ccfa0c, same test file, default cache | 8 passed, 1 failed | 3 passed, 6 failed | 3 passed, 6 failed | 3 passed, 6 failed |
  | a3ccfa0c, same test file, absolute cache | — | 7 passed, 2 failed | — | — |

  The a3ccfa0c failures:
  - np 1: the DG case through `load_from_h5_plex_vector`.
  - np ≥ 2, default cache:
    - the writing-mesh case;
    - the another-mesh case (it also asserts the new message wording);
    - both payload-only cases;
    - both DG cases.
  - Absolute cache: only the another-mesh case (wording) and the `load_from_h5_plex_vector` DG case.

  So the relative-cache cases are the ones the fix turns.
- **Neighbours,** f2ed5b16 against development (the review's development logs; that build has not changed):

  | set | development | f2ed5b16 |
  |---|---|---|
  | Serial, the 18 files (4 workers) | 202 passed, 1 skipped | 202 passed, 1 skipped |
  | Serial, the 6 snapshot and restart files | 50 passed, 1 xfailed | 50 passed, 1 xfailed |
  | Parallel at np 2 and 3: ptest_0762, ptest_0864, test_0005_xdmf_viz_topology_mpi, test_0690, test_0757, test_0790, test_0795 | 17 passed at each np | 17 passed at each np |
  | test_0690 at np 3 with an empty rank | — | 4 passed |
  | Parallel at np 2: test_0020 (swarm values), test_1155, test_0079 | 5 passed | 5 passed |
  | ptest_0010 as a script at np 4; ptest_1079 as a script at np 2 | PASS; exit 0 | PASS; exit 0 |

  test_0690 is the refined storage-v3 reload, which uses the new `dms/<name>/order` fallback. With the probe's refined runs, that fallback reads exactly or refuses at np 1, 2 and 3, written at np A and read at np B. Under pytest, ptest_0010 is not collected, on both builds, as in the review.

### New findings

**1. High: at np 1, a map that cannot be checked is trusted, and a fresh mesh reads a parallel-written file wrong**

`read_checkpoint` sets `unverified = point_map is None and uw.mpi.size > 1`. Its comment says "Serially every numbering is the file's own, so the map stands." That holds only for a file written at np 1. A file written in parallel saves the writer's point order. On a fresh gmsh mesh at np 1, that order does not match the cache file's. With the source readable, the branch finds the mismatch and refuses (matrix rows 3→1, 4→1, 2→1, random 3→1).

With the source unreadable at read time, the check returns `None`. At np 1 the read then goes ahead. File written at np 3, source renamed or mode 000 after the mesh was loaded:

| read at np 1, fresh gmsh mesh | P2 scalar | DG P1 |
|---|---|---|
| `read_checkpoint` | **wrong, 1.58** | **wrong, 2.53** |
| `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | **wrong, 1.58** | **wrong, 2.53** |
| `read_checkpoint` of the payload-only file | **wrong, 1.58** | **wrong, 2.53** |
| source present (same reads) | refused | refused |

The fields are of size about 2, and there is no error or warning. To get here, the source must disappear or become unreadable between loading the mesh and the read. Ways that can happen:
- a cache cleaned during a run;
- another job sharing the cache directory regenerating the file;
- a rebuilt mesh whose checkpoint mesh file is moved or overwritten.

This is serial post-processing of a parallel run, the use finding 2 of the review was about. On a3ccfa0c every `None` verdict was unchecked at every np, so this is the remainder of finding 3, now confined to np 1.

**Fix:** remove the np condition, so that "unverified" applies at every np. Keep "unchanged" for legacy files with no saved order. That is, `unverified = point_map is None and saved_order is not None and source file set`; the verdict can be broadcast together with a flag saying the file has a saved order. Then at np 1:
- the writing mesh still reads its own files through its layout token;
- a mesh whose source has gone is refused, as it is now at np ≥ 2;
- legacy files with no saved order keep today's behaviour.

Add a test case: written at np ≥ 2, read at np 1 on a fresh mesh after the source file has been renamed. Expect a refusal.

**2. Low: a missing or unreadable variable file hangs a parallel job (inherited)**

`read_checkpoint` opens `filename` with `h5py` on rank 0 alone, before the `bcast`, with no `try`. The PR's finding-5 fix guards the source file but not this open. If the variable file is missing, or is set to mode 000, at np 2 and 3:
- rank 0 raises `FileNotFoundError` or `PermissionError`;
- the other ranks wait in the `bcast` (exit 124 under the supervisor, stacks: rank 0 in the caller's `allgather`, rank 1 in `MPIR_Bcast`).

Development and a3ccfa0c hang the same way at np 2. At np 1 the error is raised cleanly. This is not caused by the PR, but the PR rewrites this block. The fix is to catch `OSError` around the rank-0 read, broadcast the error, and raise it on every rank.

**3. Low: the test's module docstring describes the old `load_from_h5_plex_vector`**

The docstring of `test_1302_checkpoint_reads_get_the_right_field_parallel.py` still says `load_from_h5_plex_vector` "goes through `read_checkpoint` unless this mesh wrote the file in this layout". It now always does. The test still has no case for an explicitly relative cache. CI's default cache is relative, so CI covers it: a3ccfa0c fails 6 of the 9 cases under it.

### Behaviour change to note in the body

In parallel, a mesh rebuilt from a checkpoint whose mesh file has since been moved or deleted is now refused, where a3ccfa0c read it exactly through the map. That is the safe call under finding 3. The message tells the user to rebuild the mesh from the checkpoint file, and a user can't do that if the file is gone. In that case `read_timestep` of a `/fields` file still works, and the message names it.

### Not run

- 3-D meshes, manifold meshes, and meshes built from a DMPlex: their path is unchanged.
- In-place repartition (needs `remesh` and mmg, not in this build).
- np above 4.
- The `/uw_checkpoint` legacy group beyond the serial test_0005_xdmf_compat.
- The unverifiable case on a3ccfa0c at np 1. Under the default cache its check was off anyway, and its code leaves every `None` verdict unchecked.
- The cost timing was not repeated: the read path differs from a3ccfa0c only in the `OSError` guard and the storage-v3 fallback.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1305 at e111957a: ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**At e111957a: ready to merge on green from this review.**

The diff does what the body says. The extent is a buffer `Allreduce` (MIN and MAX) over every rank, a rank with no nodes adds `+inf`/`-inf`, and both probes go through `global_evaluate`. Every rank reaches both `Allreduce` calls and both `global_evaluate` calls. The only branch before them (`if len(coords)`) picks a value and contains no collective. The assertions, the 300 K and 1600 K targets and the 1 K tolerance are unchanged, and the top probe keeps its one-part-in-1e9 inset.

## Results

Build: current `development` (fbef72fa, which includes the `global_evaluate` neighbour exchange from #1290). The PR changes only a Python test file, so the head's test file was run against that install. The file on `development` is identical to the one at the PR's merge base, so the merged file will be the head's. The mesh is `StructuredQuadBox`, which is built through gmsh, so the empty-rank partition hook applies to it.

`test_0812_poisson_with_units.py`, whole file:

| | serial | np 2 | np 3 | np 4 | np 4, ranks in horizontal bands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| development | 4 passed | `check_bc_values` fails on every rank | fails on every rank | fails on every rank | not run |
| e111957a | 4 passed | 4 passed on every rank | 4 passed on every rank | 4 passed on every rank | 4 passed on every rank |

The development failures match the body. At np 2 both probes returned 949.98 K. At np 3 they returned 1209.997 and 1210.002 K on one rank and 1145.008 K on another. At np 4 they returned 949.99 K.

`test_poisson_check_bc_values` alone, with the last rank holding no cells:

| | np 2 | np 3 | np 4 |
|---|---|---|---|
| development | not run | hangs: two ranks wait in the rank-local `evaluate`'s `update_lvec`, and the empty rank has already left it | not run |
| e111957a | passed on every rank | passed on every rank | passed on every rank |

The test still catches a real error. Each change below was made in a scratch copy and run serially and at np 3:

- Expected top value moved by 1.5 K: fails ("Top BC ... 1599.9999987 != 1600"), on every rank at np 3.
- Bottom BC applied as 298.5 K: fails ("Bottom BC ... 298.5 != 300"), on every rank at np 3.
- Bottom probe moved 1 % of the height into the domain (a wrong extent): fails at 313.0 K, on every rank at np 3.

The failure is the same on every rank because `global_evaluate` gives every rank the same value. A wrong BC therefore cannot pass on some ranks and fail on others.

**CI:** `scripts/test.sh` does not run this file under `mpirun`. The mpirun phases cover `tests/parallel/test_*.py`, the `ptest_*` list and a fixed list of named files, and `test_0812` is not among them. CI runs it serially only, so CI does not exercise this fix and will not catch a regression of it.

## Findings

1. Low: the two gradient tests in the same file (`test_poisson_linear_gradient_with_*`, through `_check_linear_gradient`) still take the centre from the rank's own `mesh._coords` and probe with the rank-local `evaluate`. They pass at np 2, 3 and 4 only because the gradient is uniform. With an empty rank the whole file hangs inside them, on `development` and on this branch alike: the ranks that hold cells wait in `update_lvec` and the empty rank does not. That is outside this PR. A follow-up could give them the same `Allreduce` and `global_evaluate` treatment. The hang suggests that the rank-local `evaluate` is not collective-safe on an empty rank, which is worth a separate look.
2. Low: CI does not run this file under `mpirun` (see above). Adding it to one of the named mpirun lists would keep the fix covered. This is optional.

## Not run

- The other tests' behaviour with an empty rank beyond the hang in finding 1.
- A build from before #1290. The runs used current `development` only.
- CI. No GitHub action was taken.

Logs kept by the reviewer.
````

### #1307 at 2f7fca41: ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**At 2f7fca41: ready to merge on green from this review.** The change does what it says for a refined base, and where development already worked the answers are unchanged, bit for bit. One sibling gap remains, below as Medium. It is the same cause in `Mesh.adapt`'s child, so a mesh that is adapted and then faulted still refuses when every cell sits on one rank. A one-line fix for it is measured below. It can go in this PR or in a follow-up.

## Method

- The change touches Python only (`discretisation_mesh.py`, `fault_split.py`, one new test). I copied the install from a build whose compiled sources match the merge base a22c4b17 exactly. Over that copy I laid head's `.py` files for one overlay and the merge base's for the other. Every run printed `underworld3.__file__` to confirm which overlay it loaded.
- The partition hook puts a shell partition on each loaded gmsh DM. I added an "every cell on rank K" layout to it, and also used its existing layouts: last rank empty (`emptyband`), height bands, random, and the default partitioner.
- The probe is `UnstructuredSimplexBox` (cellSize 0.25, `refinement=1`) with `add_fault` on (0.3, 0.45)–(0.7, 0.55), followed by a Stokes simple-shear solve with a frictionless fault. For each run it records:
  - cells per rank on every level and on the wraps;
  - fault pairs;
  - peak slip;
  - a SHA-1 of the gathered, coordinate-sorted velocity and pressure.
- Variants: two faults in one call; a second `add_fault` on a faulted mesh; adapt then fault (the shared `faulted_2d(2)` fixture); two 3-D disc sheets (cellSize 0.15, `refinement=1`); and a user-built DMPlex with every cell on rank 0.
- To test whether each flag is needed, I removed one flag at a time in a scratch overlay, with a spy on `Mesh()` constructions of DMs that are not distributed.
- All runs were on 4 cores, through the MPI supervisor. Other jobs on the machine were running at the same time.

## Results

Reproducer: refined base, then `add_fault`, then solve.

| np | layout (cells per rank on the finest base level) | development | head |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | — | slip 0.1657957111 | identical (same SHA for v and p) |
| 2 | all on rank 0 (168/0) | refuses: "84 fine cell(s) have no local parent cell…" on both ranks | works; faulted 174/0, 9 pairs, slip 0.1657957111, v/p SHA equal to np 1 |
| 2 | all on rank 1 (0/168) | refuses (84) | works, same as np 1 |
| 3 | all on rank 0 (two ranks empty) | refuses (112) | works, same as np 1 |
| 4 | all on rank 0 (three empty) | refuses (128) | works, same as np 1 |
| 3 | last rank empty (80/88/0) | works | identical to development (174 cells as 34/140/0, same SHA) |
| 4 | last rank empty | works | identical |
| 2 | default, band, random | works | identical (cells per rank per level, pairs, slip, SHA) |
| 3 | random | works | identical |
| 4 | band | works | equal to round-off on the first pair of runs (v 4e-16, p 2e-14); bitwise equal on a rerun. This is run-to-run variation at np 4, not the change. |

In development the wrap splits a one-rank level (42/0 becomes 21/21, or 14/14/14 at np 3), and that is the refusal. On head the wrap keeps 42/0. Across layouts the slip agrees to about 2e-8, well inside the 1e-6 solver tolerance.

Second fault and split child paths, head against development:

- **Two traces in one call:** at np 1, 2 (default) and 3 (last rank empty) the results are identical, with 13+13 pairs and slips 0.15537373 / 0.1330985436. With every cell on rank 0 at np 2 and np 3, development refuses (132 and 176 cells) and head works, bitwise equal to np 1.
- **A second `add_fault` on a faulted mesh:** the same pattern. Head and development are identical wherever development works, and head works, equal to np 1, where development refuses.
- **3-D, two sheets:** at np 1 head and development are identical (33+33 pairs, slips 0.1714683311 / 0.1265024892). With every cell on rank 0 at np 2, development refuses (6336 cells) and head works, bitwise equal to np 1.

Each flag is needed:

- Without the `_add_fault_sheets` flag, the spy catches the placed child (12610 cells) being distributed afresh, and 3-D `add_fault` refuses (9578 cells).
- Without the `split_fault` flag, the split child (174 cells) is distributed afresh to 89/85, and the solve fails with "paired point 211 carries velocity DOFs but is not in the 'FltPlus' facet closure".

Is `_preserve_partition=True` ever wrong here? I found no case.

- Every caller of `_wrap_coarse_level` passes either a level of `dm_hierarchy` or one of `adapt`'s `level_dms`. Both kinds were built after the constructor's `distribute()`: `_build_refined_hierarchy`, `_build_coarsened_hierarchy` and the sidecar splice each distribute the coarsest level before they refine.
- The three `fault_split` DMs are cut from an existing mesh's DM.
- A DMPlex built by the user with every cell on rank 0 (72/0, not distributed) is distributed by its own `Mesh()` constructor (36/36) before any wrap or cut. `add_fault` on it then gives identical results on head and development, both with `refinement=1` and with no refinement.

Load balance: with every cell on rank 0, the faulted mesh and its multigrid tail stay on rank 0 (174/0, tail 42/0). `partition_addon` keeps the add-on co-partitioned with its base, so this is the base's own partition. That is acceptable: the user chose that partition. Ranks that hold no cells take part in the solve without trouble.

Tests:

- **New test, np 2, 3 and 4:** head passes 3/3; development fails 3/3.
- **`test_0949`:** passes on both head and development here (4 passed). Its exact-`['Lower']` assertion depends on the platform's gmsh mesh, and development has already relaxed it (#1308, merged after this PR's merge base). It has nothing to do with this PR.
- **Serial neighbours** (the 107 files that use `add_fault`, `fault_mg`, `custom_mg` or `refinement=`, run with 4 workers): head and development both give 997 passed, 5 skipped, 1 xpassed.
- **Parallel neighbours at np 2** (the 58 existing files that use those, plus the new one): 392 passed and 18 skipped on head, the same outcome per file as development except the new file.
- **Empty-rank and move files at np 3** (1103, 1189, 1173, 1108, 1111, 1176): the same outcomes on head and development.
- **Merge:** the PR merges cleanly with current development.

## Findings

1. High: none.

2. Medium: the same failure remains for an adapted mesh. `Mesh.adapt`'s child (`_adapt_nested`, the `child = Mesh(current_dm.clone(), …)` whose comment says "on-rank; no redistribute") does not pass `_preserve_partition=True`.
   - **The failure:** `faulted_2d(2)` (cellSize 0.1, `refinement=1`, two adapt levels, then `add_fault`) on a base with every cell on rank 0 at np 2 still refuses on head, with "678 fine cell(s) have no local parent cell" (211 on development, which fails earlier, at the wrap). The spy shows the adapt child (1360 cells, not distributed) being distributed afresh.
   - **The fix, measured:** adding the flag to that one construction in a scratch overlay makes the same run work. The faulted mesh is 1470/0 with tail 242/0, 968/0 and 1268/0, 131 pairs, and slip 0.1595809403 (np 1: 0.159580936).
   - **Why it matters:** adapt then fault is the usual fault workflow, so it is worth adding here, with a fourth case in the new test, or filing as a follow-up.

3. Low:
   - **The same pattern elsewhere:** `addon_partition._moved_wrap` and the `moved` Mesh in `custom_mg` (near line 3417) wrap a DM that has just been redistributed by assignment, also without the flag. If an assignment ever puts every cell on one rank, they would distribute it again. Not reached in these runs (`partition_addon` left the one-rank meshes in place). Not tested further.
   - **PR body wording:** it calls the `_add_fault_sheets` construction the "second-fault child". It is the placed child of every 3-D sheet, the first one included, which is why one-sheet 3-D faulting needed it too.
   - **Not from this PR:** a "Mean of empty slice" RuntimeWarning (`discretisation_mesh.py`, the centroid mean near line 7486) prints on a rank with no cells, on development and head alike. It is cosmetic.

## Not run

- np > 4, and 3-D at np 3 or 4.
- 3-D sheets with the last rank empty.
- The `_moved_wrap` and `custom_mg` one-rank assignment case.
- Adapt then fault at np 3 or 4 with the scratch fix.
- macOS or CI builds.

Logs kept by the reviewer.
````

### #1309 at 8460d6d5: ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

## #1309 at 8460d6d5: refuse a condition that leaves every component free

**Verdict at 8460d6d5: ready to merge on green from this review.** The refusal works as described: on every path tried, at every rank count, it fires at the call that adds the condition. Nothing that constrains at least one component changes. There are two small points about the wording of the message (findings 2 and 3), and neither blocks the merge.

### Method

- The change is in a `.pyx` file, so I built two fresh environments: the head (8460d6d5) and its merge base with development (c1691428). Every run prints `underworld3.__file__`, and each log shows the build it used.
- I read the diff and the code around it in `add_condition`.
- A probe script tries 31 cases on both builds. Each case runs serially, at np 2, and at np 3 on a gmsh box whose cells are split into bands, with the last rank given no cells (the empty rank shows up as `n=0` in the outcomes). Each rank reports its own outcome: refused at add, solved (with SNES reason and a SHA-1 of its local solution array), or the error raised.
- Parallel runs used the MPI supervisor (silence limit 60 s) inside an outer timeout, on 4 cores of the machine, alongside other jobs on the machine.
- Neighbour tests:
  - all 216 serial test files that call `add_dirichlet_bc`, `add_essential_bc`, `add_natural_bc` or `add_condition`, with 4 workers;
  - all 66 such files under `tests/parallel/`, one file at a time at np 2 with `--with-mpi`;
  - the PR's own test on both builds.

### Results

**All-free conditions** (np 1, 2 and 3 with an empty rank). On development, every case below fails at the solve with `IndexError: Out of bounds on buffer access (axis 0)`, raised in `_setup_discretisation` (Stokes saddle point, `SNES_Scalar` or `SNES_Vector`). On the branch, every rank refuses at the add with the new `ValueError`, and no rank goes on to the solve:

| case | development | this branch |
|---|---|---|
| Stokes `(None, None)` tuple, `add_dirichlet_bc`, plain wall | IndexError at solve | refused at add |
| Stokes `[None, None]` list | IndexError | refused |
| Stokes `(None, None)`, `add_essential_bc` | IndexError | refused |
| Stokes `(None, None)`, `add_condition(0, "dirichlet", ...)` directly | IndexError | refused |
| Stokes `sympy.Matrix([oo, oo])` | IndexError | refused |
| Stokes `(0.0, 0.0)` with `components=[]` | IndexError | refused |
| Stokes 3-D `(None, None, None)` | IndexError | refused |
| Stokes natural `(None, None)`, plain wall | IndexError | refused |
| Stokes `(None, None)` on a fault side (`FltMinus`, fault law on `Flt`), essential and natural | IndexError | refused |
| Poisson `(None,)`, essential and natural | IndexError (`SNES_Scalar`) | refused |
| Vector projection `(None, None)` | IndexError (`SNES_Vector`) | refused |
| AdvDiffusion `(None,)` | IndexError (`SNES_Scalar`) | refused |

**At least one component constrained.** The cases below give per-rank hashes on the branch identical to development at np 1, 2 and 3 (empty rank), with the same SNES reason:
- `(0.0, None)`, `(None, 0.0)`, `(None, x*y)`, `(None, uw.quantity(0.0, "m/s"))`, `sympy.Matrix([oo, 0.0])` and 3-D `(None, None, 0.0)`;
- the deprecated forms `components=[1]` with `(None, 1.0)`, and `components=1` with `(0.0, 1.0)`;
- natural `(None, 1.0)`;
- Nitsche (`add_nitsche_bc`), `add_fault_bc` alone, Poisson, vector projection and AdvDiffusion with a set value.

`(None, None)` with `components=[1]` is not refused, on either build (see finding 2). Rotated free slip (`add_rotated_freeslip_bc`) matches at np 2 and 3. At np 1 it gives one of two hashes from run to run on development as well as on the branch (development gave both in three runs, the branch gave one hash in three runs), so it is not used as a bitwise check here. This variation predates the PR.

**Neighbour tests.**
- Serial, 216 files: 1790 passed, 6 skipped, 1 xfailed, 1 xpassed on both builds. The per-test outcome lists are identical, 1797 entries each.
- Parallel at np 2, 66 files: every file has the same outcome on both builds, 0 failures. (Counted across both ranks' output: 278 vs 279 passed lines, 13 skipped. The one difference is two ranks' output lines interleaved in test_1169; that file passes 11 of 11 on both builds.)
- The PR's test: 4 of 4 pass on the branch. On development the three refusal tests fail (tuple, list, natural) and the solve test passes.

### Findings

1. High: none.

2. Medium: for a natural condition, the message suggests a fix that does not work. It says to write `(None, 0.0)` "to set only the second". For an essential condition that works. For a natural condition it does not. `add_natural_bc((None, 1.0), "Left")` puts `sympy.oo` into the flux, and the solve ends with SNES reason -4 (a NaN residual), on development and on the branch alike. `(0.0, 1.0)` converges (reason 2). So someone following the advice for `add_natural_bc((None, None), ...)` trades a clear refusal for a NaN solve.
   - Suggestion: for `c_type == 'neumann'`, say that a natural condition sets every component, with `0.0` for no traction, and that dropping the condition leaves the boundary traction-free.
   - Related gap, also present on development: the check looks only at `components.size`, so a named component whose value is None is still accepted. `(None, None)` with `components=[1]` (natural) and `(None, 0.0)` with `components=[0]` (essential) are both accepted, and both solves end with reason -4. A check that every named component has a value other than `oo` would catch these. It could go in this PR or as a follow-up.

3. Low: the message prints the processed value, not what the caller wrote. The PR body quotes `((None, None))`, but the message prints `([oo, oo])`, because `conds` has already been converted when the error is raised. Keeping `repr` of the original argument before conversion would match the description. Separately, `self.is_setup = False` is set before the raise. That is harmless (it only forces a re-setup), but the refusal is not entirely free of side effects.

Outside this PR: on development, an essential condition with one component set (`(None, 0.0)`) on a fault side that also carries `add_fault_bc(1.0, ...)` aborts at np 1 with heap corruption (`corrupted size vs. prev_size`, signal 6). At np 2 and 3 it stalls until the outer timeout. The branch behaves the same. #1285, still open, refuses this combination, so it is not a finding against #1309.

### Not run

- The neighbour tests at np 3, and the full neighbour tests with an empty rank. Only the probes ran at np 3 with an empty rank.
- Free-surface and curved-wall drivers beyond the tests already listed.
- CI: GitHub writes are paused, so nothing was triggered.
- The rotated free-slip np 1 variation was not chased beyond confirming that development shows it too.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1197, fifth re-check, at cace7153 (local head, to be pushed): ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Fifth recheck at cace7153: ready to merge on green from this review.** This head is one commit on 199955d2 and has not been pushed yet. The verdict is for cace7153 as it stands. CI has not run on it.

The commit fixes the fourth recheck's High finding. A term that holds a field without units, or a derivative of one, is no longer compared with the other terms of a sum, `Min`, `Max` or `Piecewise`. With a velocity field without units, `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))` and `eta_b + Min(eta, Max(C - rho*g*y/2, 0)/(2*edot_II))` now pass the setter. The second one solves to development's numbers. The Spiegelman benchmark now runs into its solves, with development's residuals, until the 900 s cap. The mismatch hint now names both terms and their units, and no longer says which one to change.

The fix gives something up. A sum that is truly mismatched is no longer refused at the setter or the solve if one of its terms holds a field without units. Development accepts these sums too, and solves them to the same numbers. `evaluate` still refuses them on every build, through a check that development already has. So these sums go back to development's behaviour. I judge this acceptable (section below). The new findings are all Low.

Only `.py` files changed between 199955d2 and cace7153: `src/underworld3/function/unit_conversion.py` and `tests/test_0760_evaluate_units_contract.py`. So the head was not rebuilt. Its `src/underworld3` `.py` files were laid over a copy of the 199955d2 build. After the overlay, `unit_conversion.py` is the only installed file that differs from that build. The baseline is development at the merge base 1e10c63f, the same overlay as in the fourth recheck. Every run printed which `underworld3` it loaded.

## Against the fourth recheck (199955d2)

| finding | status at cace7153 | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1. High: a user-written plastic viscosity refused at the setter when the velocity has no units | fixed | Velocity without units, so `get_units(Einv2)` is None. `eta_b + Min(eta, Max(C - rho*g*y/2, 0)/(2*edot_II))` sets, with `get_units` pascal * second, and solves in 4 Newton iterations to \|v\|max 2.75637835. Development gives the same. `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))` sets, with `get_units` pascal * second. Its solve did not finish within 900 s, as on development. The Spiegelman benchmark gives no refusal. Its 13 nonlinear residuals match development's line for line (0.00662074 ... 0.00641563), and it reached a 14th before the 900 s cap. The built-in `ViscoPlasticFlowModel` is unchanged |
| 2. Low: the hint could tell the user to change the wrong term | fixed | The message is now "... W_g is in kilometer and \eta_{0g} is in pascal * second. These terms need the same dimension." Each of `Max(T, x)`, `Min(eta0, T)`, `Piecewise((eta0, x > W/2), (T, True))`, `eta0 + Max(T, T0)`, `Abs(T) + x`, `Min(eta0, W)` and the reassignment at the solve names both terms with their units, and picks neither |
| 3. Low: four commit messages name the reviewer by session code | open, left for the squash | a43b7090, e0326d2b, c29e11f5 and 1f3cb36e still carry it. The new commit, its test comment and the diff carry no session code |
| 4. Low: description wording | not checked | The description is edited on GitHub, and nothing has been pushed since the fourth recheck |
| earlier results (`Min`/`Max`/`Abs`/`Piecewise` hand values and refusals with every field in units, dimensionless constants, constant sums, reassignment refused at the solve, `test_1059`) | unchanged | The fourth recheck's probes were run again at cace7153 and compared line by line with their output at 199955d2. Only the hint wording differs, in `Min`/`Max`/`Piecewise`, dimensionless constants, constant sums, strain-rate units, the reassignment probes and the earlier-fixes probe. The `test_1059` model's evaluate, projection and Stokes arrays are bit-for-bit those of 199955d2 and of development (21 arrays) |
| every rank the same | holds | The `Min`/`Max` probe at np 2, np 3, and np 3 with an empty rank (cells 20 / 22 / 0). The constant-sum, reassignment-at-solve, setter-and-solve and earlier-fixes probes at np 3 with an empty rank. Once the ranks are sorted, the output matches 199955d2's apart from the hint wording. The new probe below gives the same verdict on every rank. The empty rank's local `evaluate` at a point it does not own prints a different number, a probe-side effect seen in earlier rechecks too |
| per-solve check cost | unchanged | Same 40-term probe. The check alone takes 4.5 to 4.8 ms, and 22.4 to 22.7 ms after a named value changes (fourth recheck: 4.9 to 5.0 ms and 24 ms). A repeat solve takes 0.030 to 0.031 s. The new walk over each term costs nothing measurable here |

## What the change gives up

A term with a field without units anywhere inside it, a field's derivative included, is skipped when a sum, `Min`, `Max` or `Piecewise` is checked. The probe uses `T` in K, a field `U` without units (value 2), `x` in km, and `eta0` in Pa s, at x = 18 km and T = 1100 K. It was run on all three builds.

**Refused at 199955d2, accepted at the setter and the solve at cace7153.** Development accepts every one of these, with the same \|v\|max to 10 digits:

| viscosity | development | 199955d2 | cace7153 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `eta0 + T*U` | solves, 7.643748114e-05 | refused at the setter | solves, 7.643748114e-05 |
| `Min(eta0, T*U)` | solves, 0.001758062066 | refused | solves, same |
| `Piecewise((eta0, x > W/2), (T*U, True))` | solves, 0.001169469727 | refused | solves, same |
| `eta0*U + T` | solves, 0.0001352355436 | refused | solves, same |
| `eta0 + T*exp(U)` | solves, 2.136692085e-05 | refused | solves, same |
| `eta0 + T*(1 + U)` | solves, 5.170770783e-05 | refused | solves, same |
| `eta0 + Ts*Us` (swarm variables: Ts in K, Us without units) | solves, 7.643748114e-05 | refused | solves, same |
| `eta0*exp(T/T0 - 1) + a*U`, then `a.sym = 1000 K` | solves, 8.330006296e-05 | refused at the solve | solves, same |

Also accepted now by `get_units`, where 199955d2 raised: `Max(T, x*U)`, `T + x*U`, `eta0 + T*dU/dx` and `eta0 + T*Us`.

**`evaluate` still refuses all of these, on all three builds.** One exception is `Max(T, x*U)` (finding 2). The refusal comes from development's existing check: "this expression puts fields without units (U_g) beside factors with units ... Give the field units". So a user who evaluates one of these sums is still told. Only the setter and the solve accept them silently, as on development.

**Still refused at cace7153:**
- `eta0 + T*U + W`: "W_g is in kilometer and \eta_{0g} is in pascal * second". The terms that carry units are still compared.
- `eta0 + T*U/U`: sympy cancels U.
- `eta0 + Ts`, a swarm variable in K. A swarm variable's proxy carries its units, so a swarm field with units is still compared.
- `exp(T*U)`: refused by the argument rule, not the sum rule.
- `eta0*exp(T/T0 - 1) + a` with `a.sym = 1000 K`: refused at the solve.

Every refusal of a mismatch in which all the fields carry units is unchanged (table above).

**Judgement: acceptable.**
- Each sum that is accepted again is one that development accepts, and it gives development's numbers.
- No sum that development refuses is now accepted.
- The PR never refused these sums on development's behalf. 199955d2 began to refuse them only through the same rule that refused the Spiegelman viscosity. The rule cannot tell `C/(2*edot_II)` (units really unknown) from `T*U` (a genuine mismatch, if `U` is a pure number).
- A field without units is opaque in a units model. `evaluate` already says so, and it now sets the limit for the setter as well.

Two of the skipped cases have fully known units: `T*exp(U)`, where U sits only inside a dimensionless function, and `T*(1 + U)`, where the PR's own sum rule makes `1 + U` a pure number. Both are kelvin, so a narrower walk could still refuse them (finding 1). That is a possible refinement, not a regression against development.

## Examples and docs

The 26 files were run at cace7153 from fresh copies of the head's docs tree. The docs tree is identical to 199955d2's, and the copies had the same edits and the same 900 s cap. Development was not run again: its build and its merge base are unchanged since the fourth recheck, so the comparison is with that run.
- **No new refusals.** The sum-check hook recorded no mismatch in any file.
- **Same exit status as development in all 26.**
- **Spiegelman benchmark:** it now runs, with development's residuals, to the cap (table above). At 199955d2 it was refused at line 621.
- **Other differences:** only timestamps, object ids and timings, plus line numbers in the two examples the PR edits.
- **Annulus convection example:** every timestep in the table matches. The recorded `dt` of the step taken twice differs in its 16th digit (…025.23 or …025.355). Repeat runs of the same build give either value, at 199955d2 too, so this is run-to-run noise, not the change.

## Findings

1. Low: the skip is broader than "units partly unknown".

   A term is skipped when a field without units appears anywhere in it, even where that field cannot affect the term's units:
   - inside a function's argument: `T*exp(U)` is kelvin;
   - as a pure number beside 1: `T*(1 + U)` is kelvin, by the PR's own sum rule.

   So `eta0 + T*exp(U)` passes the setter and solves, where 199955d2 refused it. Development behaves the same, so this does not block the merge.

   A narrower walk would skip a term only when the field without units is a factor or a base of a power, outside any function argument. That would keep these refusals and still accept `C/(2*edot_II)`. This could be a follow-up.

2. Low: `evaluate(Max(T, x*U))` returns 1200 kelvin.

   The value is the `x*U` branch in model units (0.6 × 2), labelled with `T`'s units:
   - development returns a bare 1.2;
   - 199955d2 refused it.

   Development's existing check for a field without units beside factors with units catches `T + x*U` but not this `Max`, on every build. For the case the commit is for, the label is right: `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))` is a viscosity. For an arbitrary branch it is a guess. A note in the docstring, or extending that check to `Min`/`Max`/`Piecewise` branches, would cover it.

3. Low: the `_common_units` docstring still reads "a field without units, is a pure number beside them". It does not say that a term holding one is now left out of the comparison. One sentence would fix it.

4. Low (carried): the four older commit messages with a session code (fourth-recheck finding 3). A squash merge, or a clean squash message, settles it.

New tests:
- `test_a_plastic_branch_with_a_velocity_without_units_is_not_refused` passes here, fails at 199955d2 (refused at the setter), and passes on development. It guards the fix.
- `test_max_with_the_field_above_the_reference_gives_its_value` gives e^0.4 at T = 1400 K, T0 = 1000 K. It passes on all three builds, so it pins the value and is not a regression guard.
- The updated `test_the_mismatch_message_prints_a_negated_constant_as_its_number` passes here and at 199955d2. It fails on development, which does not refuse that sum.

## What was run

- **Builds:**
  - the head: cace7153's `.py` files over a copy of the 199955d2 build (no compiled source changed);
  - 199955d2: that build as is;
  - development at 1e10c63f: the fourth recheck's overlay.
- **Diff read:** 199955d2..cace7153, two files.
- **Serial, head, compared with 199955d2's output:** the fourth recheck's probes for `Min`/`Max`/`Abs`/`Piecewise`, dimensionless constants, strain-rate units, the `test_1059` model (arrays compared bit for bit), reassignment and constant sums, the earlier-fixes probe, and the 40-term cost.
- **Strain rate, velocity without units:** two user-written plastic viscosities at the setter and the solve.
- **New probe, three builds:** sums, `Min`, `Max` and `Piecewise` holding a field without units (mesh and swarm), through `get_units`, evaluate, the setter, the solve, and reassignment at the solve.
- **Parallel, head:** the `Min`/`Max` probe at np 2, np 3, and np 3 with an empty rank; the constant-sum, reassignment-at-solve, setter-and-solve, earlier-fixes and new probes at np 3 with an empty rank.
- **Serial slice, head, the same 82 files:** 719 passed, 7 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed. That is the fourth recheck's 717 plus the 2 new items.
- **np 2, head:** `test_0762` and the parallel `test_0035`: 13 passed.
- **New and changed `test_0760` items:** run on 199955d2 and on development, as above.
- **Examples and docs:** the 26 files at the head, with a 900 s cap.

**Not run:**
- the full suites;
- CI at cace7153 (not pushed);
- `test_1086`: it needs up to 5 ranks, beyond this run's core budget, and the commit does not touch it;
- anything above np 3;
- the Spiegelman benchmark, and `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))`, to completion on any build;
- development's 26 files again (unchanged since the fourth recheck);
- the docs' Markdown code blocks.

The runs shared the machine with other jobs. Logs kept by the reviewer.
````
