### #1295 round 2 at 738b3f9c (local head, to be pushed): ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

## #1295 re-check at 738b3f9c: evaluate and global_evaluate no longer add 1e-18·x·y

**Verdict at 738b3f9c: ready to merge on green from this review.** The second `_unwrap_expressions` pass is back in `petsc_interpolate` and `rbf_evaluate`, and only the added term stays removed. Every case behind finding 1 now gives the right value through both evaluators: serial, at np 2, and at np 3 with a rank that holds no cells. `test_0066` passes in full. The defect stays fixed: small values come back exact on head and wrong on development. Compared with development, the 600-case battery shows nothing beyond the removed term, and the 3 shell cases that broke in round 1 now work. Cost is unchanged. Two passes are enough at every nesting depth I tried. The few failures that remain are the same on development (new finding 1, Low, not this PR's).

### What changed since 4257f042

- `f9b36a33`: after the `zero_matrix` line was removed, the second `expr = _unwrap_expressions(expr, keep_constants=False)` is back under its old conditions. In `petsc_interpolate` that is `mesh is not None`. In `rbf_evaluate` it is `mesh is not None or expr.atoms(BaseScalar)`, which is the old mesh-less branch without its `try/except IndexError`. I read the diff against development's side of the merge (762469b7). The `.pyx` change is now exactly the epsilon term plus the new entry-by-entry shaping in `_lambdify_and_evaluate`.
- `498a68a5`: new tests for `uw.expression` times a scalar field and a vector field, a nested expression, a coordinate expression, `Matrix([[a*T]])`, and the shell's `unit_e_0`, `unit_e_2` and `rRotN`. They cover both evaluators, plus a parallel test on every rank.
- `738b3f9c` and `39304120`: merges of development. They bring no other change to `_function.pyx`.

### Earlier findings, then and now

| finding at 4257f042 | now at 738b3f9c |
|---|---|
| 1. High: the second unwrap was removed along with the term. `global_evaluate` raised for `a*T`, `a*u`, nested `b*T`, `eta*T` and shell `unit_e_0`/`unit_e_2`/`rRotN`. `evaluate` raised for `Matrix([[a*T]])` and `a*u`. `test_0066` ramp cases failed | **Fixed.** All cases are right through both evaluators, at np 1, 2 and 3 (one rank empty). `test_0066`: 31 passed |
| 2. Low: `independent_of_N` still in the shell frames | Unchanged and harmless where it sits. The new test bounds it rather than comparing it. The follow-up is #1303 |
| 3. Low: code reading; the mesh-less `rbf_evaluate` branch went with the unwrap | Branch restored under the same condition. The rest still reads correctly |
| 4. Low: PR body (3.08× vs 2.67×; "confined to shaping") | Corrected in the round-2 body and the test docstring |

### Method

- Head: a fresh worktree at 738b3f9c, built on 4 cores at the start of this re-check. The installed `_function` extension is newer than its `.pyx`, and the installed `.py` files match the sources.
- Base: development's side of the merge is 762469b7. I used an existing clean build at fbef72fa, which differs from 762469b7 only in CI workflow files. Head's and base's installed packages differ only in `_function` (and the build-id file).
- Round 1: the existing clean build at 4257f042, used only to confirm that the new tests fail there.
- Every run printed `underworld3.__file__`. All runs used 4 cores, with at most 4 processes at once, alongside other jobs on the machine. Parallel runs went through the MPI silence supervisor at 60 s. For the empty-rank runs, a shell partition gave the last rank no cells on every mesh (box 121/121/0, annulus 140/140/0, shell 508/515/0).
- Logs kept by the reviewer.

### Results

**Finding 1's cases** against hand values. The box holds T = 1 + x + 2y and u = (x − y, 3x), both P2, so both are exact. The parameters are a = 3, b = 5a and η = 2 exp(−aT). The annulus and shell frames are compared with their closed forms. Tolerance: rel 1e-12 + abs 1e-15.

| case | 4257f042 | development | head |
|---|---|---|---|
| `a*T`, `b*T`, `eta*T`, `vanishing*T`, `global_evaluate` | NameError / SyntaxError | right | right |
| `Matrix([[a*T]])`, `a*u`, `Matrix([[b*T, 1]])`, both evaluators | NameError | right | right |
| shell `unit_e_0`, `unit_e_2`, `rRotN`, `global_evaluate` | NameError / SyntaxError | right | right |
| shell `rRotN*T`, `unit_e_2*T`, both evaluators | SyntaxError | right | right |
| annulus `unit_e_0`, `unit_e_0*T`, `rRotN*T` | right | right | right |
| 3- and 4-deep nesting (c1 = 2a, c2 = c1 + a·x, c3 = c2·c1 + 1, c4 = c3·T): `c3*T`, `c4*u`, `Matrix([[c3*T, c2], [c1*u0, 1]])`, `Matrix([[c3]])*u`, `Matrix([[c3, x]])`, `c3*x` | NameError in 9 of 12 calls | right | right |
| totals, serial (53 calls) | 19 right, 31 raise | 45 right, 5 raise, 2 off | **identical to development** |
| np 2 and np 3 (empty rank), `global_evaluate` on every rank | — | 4 raise per rank | **identical to development**, line for line |

- The lines that differ between head and development are the removed term itself. `vanishing*T` (that is, 1e-18·T) is exact on head, with abs error 4e-34, against 2.7e-19 on development. The third entry of shell `unit_e_2` through `global_evaluate` is exactly 1e-18·x·y·z on head; development doubles it.
- The 5 raises and the 2 values that are off are the same on both builds, and none are this PR's (new finding 1):
  - `global_evaluate` of a bare `uw.expression` (`eta`, `c3`, `c4`) raises.
  - `Matrix([[c4, c3]])` raises in both evaluators, because a mesh variable hidden inside an expression inside a Matrix is not found.
  - The Stokes flux misses my hand value by 7.5e-4 on both builds. It contains a derivative, which the evaluators project. Its values are identical between the builds.

**Is two passes always enough?** Yes, for every depth I tried.
- `_unwrap_expressions` makes no change to a mutable `Matrix` beyond returning it as an `ImmutableDenseMatrix`.
- One pass on an `ImmutableMatrix` unwraps fully. It reaches 4 levels in one go: `ImmutableMatrix([[c4, c3]])` gives `[[(18x + 37)·T, 18x + 37]]`.
- So the second pass only finishes the container, and depth does not matter. Every Matrix case above reached a fixed point at pass 2, including a matrix-valued `uw.expression` (`m1.sym`).
- A loop to a fixed point would not change any result here. The robust fix is to convert to an immutable matrix in `_unwrap_expressions` itself (new finding 1).

**The defect stays fixed** (round 1's small-value probe; T = x + 2y, Ts = 1e-20·(x + 2y); six points; rel 1e-12)

| run | development | head |
|---|---|---|
| np 1, both evaluators (20 checks) | 14 wrong: `1e-19*T` 2.67× at (0.5, 0.5); P2 field at 1e-20 17.7×; mixed 2x2 rel 9e6; `global_evaluate(1e-30*x)` 5e11× | 0 wrong; max rel err 4.0e-16 |
| np 2, own points and all points | 8 wrong per rank | 0 wrong |

**Tests**

- The new expression tests pass on development too, as they should, because development has the second unwrap. So they guard this PR's round-1 regression, and the small-value tests guard the defect.

### New findings

1. Low (not this PR's; the same on development): some expressions with a `uw.expression` still fail in the evaluators. Seen on both builds:
   - `global_evaluate` of a bare `uw.expression`: `global_evaluate(eta, pts)` raises SyntaxError (`\eta`), and `c3` raises NameError.
   - `global_evaluate(Matrix([[a, 2]]))` raises NameError (`a_c`). So does `Matrix([[h]])`, where `h` holds `x` only inside an expression. `evaluate` gives the right value for these.
   - `Matrix([[c4, c3]])`, and `Matrix([[h_T]])` with `h_T = a*T`, raise NameError (`T`) in **both** evaluators. A mesh variable that sits only inside an expression in a mutable Matrix is not found, so the expression goes down the mesh-less path.
   - All of these come from one cause: one `_unwrap_expressions` pass does not unwrap a mutable `Matrix`. The mesh-less `rbf_evaluate` path then skips the second pass when no coordinate symbol is visible at the top level.
   - Suggested follow-up (an issue, not this PR): have `_unwrap_expressions` convert a `MutableDenseMatrix` to an immutable one before expanding. Then one pass is complete, and the evaluators' second call (and its conditions) can go.
2. Low: wording in the tests and the PR body.
   - `tests/test_1295_evaluate_adds_nothing_to_small_values.py` line 109 and the parallel test's docstring say "the parallel review desk's review of #1295". The round-2 body has the same phrase in its heading. "The #1295 review" would read better to someone outside this work.
   - "UNFILED 43" next to #1303 adds nothing once #1303 exists. Development's tests already use "UNFILED n" labels, so this is optional.

### What was run and not run

- Run:
  - finding 1's cases and the 3- and 4-deep nesting probe through both evaluators: serial, np 2, and np 3 with an empty rank, on head, development and 4257f042 (serial)
  - the unwrap-pass probe (mutable and immutable matrices)
  - the small-value probes: serial, np 2, np 3 with an empty rank
  - the 600-case battery, serial
  - cost at 1e6 points, np 1 and np 4
  - the new tests on all three builds (serial; parallel at np 2, 3 and 4)
  - `test_0066` in full
  - 172 serial files
  - 17 parallel evaluate files at np 2 and np 4
- Not run:
  - the units case with #1284 (round 1's `global_evaluate` NameError there came from finding 1, now fixed; not re-run)
  - the 24 extra parallel files from round 1
  - the battery in parallel
  - tier C, examples, the full suite, macOS
````

````markdown
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

## Second re-check of #1310 at 3e9eb52e

**Verdict at 3e9eb52e (the local head to be pushed): changes requested.**

The three findings from the re-check at f2ed5b16 are fixed:
- At np 1, a map that cannot be checked is now refused. The case that gave 1.58 (P2) and 2.53 (DG P1) is refused, with one message.
- A missing or unreadable variable file is an error on every rank at np 1, 2, 3 and 3 with an empty rank. Nothing hangs.
- The test docstring is accurate.

The full matrix gives the same outcome as f2ed5b16 in every case, in all three modes: 4,824 cases, each exact or refused on every rank. The restart driver is exact or refused on every route. The test passes 11 of 11 at np 1, 2, 3 and 3 with an empty rank. The neighbour tests match development.

The new "legacy" verdict reopens a silent wrong read (new finding 1). It covers more files than intended. Every variable file written by `write_timestep(..., petsc_reload=True)` before 2026-09-18 (b25d2bad) keeps its reload data at the file root: `topologies/uw_mesh/dms/uw_mesh/order` is there. The reader looks for the saved order only under `/restart/petsc` or `/uw_checkpoint`, so it finds none and calls such a file "legacy". A legacy file is now read through the map unchecked at every np.

Take a file in that layout, written at np 2 or 3, and read it on a freshly built gmsh mesh. The result is wrong by 1.58 to 1.59 for a P2 field and 2.53 to 2.59 for a DG P1 field. This happens at np 1, 2 and 3, with no error. Development does the same, but f2ed5b16 refused these reads at np ≥ 2.

### The findings from f2ed5b16, then and now

| # | at f2ed5b16 | at 3e9eb52e |
|---|---|---|
| 1. High: at np 1 an unverifiable map was trusted | Fresh gmsh mesh at np 1, file written at np 3, source renamed or set to mode 000 after loading: wrong by 1.58 (P2) and 2.53 (DG P1) through `read_checkpoint`, `load_from_h5_plex_vector` and the payload-only `read_checkpoint` | **Fixed.** Same runs: refused on every route, one message. f2ed5b16, rerun on the same files: still 1.58 / 2.53. At np 2, 3 and 3-empty, with the source renamed or set to mode 000, the result is as before. The writing mesh reads its own files exactly. Every other mesh is refused on every rank. `read_timestep` of a `/fields` file is exact. |
| 2. Low: a missing or unreadable variable file hung a parallel job | Rank 0 raised and the other ranks waited in the `bcast` | **Fixed.** At np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty, with the file missing or set to mode 000 (our own copy, restored afterwards), every rank raises the same error: `RuntimeError: read_checkpoint: cannot open '<file>' (FileNotFoundError: …)` or `(PermissionError: …)`. Nothing hangs. |
| 3. Low: stale test docstring | Described the old `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | **Fixed.** |
| Behaviour change (parallel only at f2ed5b16) | In parallel, a rebuilt mesh whose checkpoint mesh file had been moved was refused. At np 1 it was read, and the read was exact. | It is now refused at every np, as intended (see "What users see differently"). |

### Outcomes that changed from f2ed5b16

| case | f2ed5b16 | 3e9eb52e | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| np 1, source unreadable: fresh gmsh mesh reading a file written at np 3 | wrong, 1.58 / 2.53 | refused | the fix for finding 1 |
| np 1, source unreadable: another mesh built from the same gmsh file in the same process, or a rebuilt copy | exact | refused | the same rule: only the writing mesh's own layout is trusted |
| np 1: rebuilt mesh whose checkpoint mesh file was moved after loading (restart 3→1, `read_checkpoint`, `load_from_h5_plex_vector`, payload `read_checkpoint`) | exact | refused | the same rule; the intended behaviour change |
| missing or unreadable variable file, np ≥ 2 | hang | the same `RuntimeError` on every rank | the fix for finding 2 |
| missing or unreadable variable file, np 1 | `FileNotFoundError` or `PermissionError` | `RuntimeError` naming the cause | new finding 3 |
| **file with no saved order found** (the layout written before 2026-09-18, or a file whose order dataset is absent), np ≥ 2, fresh gmsh mesh or the writing mesh | refused | **wrong, 1.58 to 1.59 / 2.53 to 2.59** | new finding 1 |
| the same files, np ≥ 2, mesh rebuilt from the checkpoint | refused | exact | "legacy" now goes through the map, which is right for a rebuilt mesh |

No other case changed. The full matrix is unchanged in all three modes: 1,608 cases per mode, 0 changed. Files written by development (bf022b14) are unchanged too: 288 cases, the same outcome as at f2ed5b16. Those files keep their order under `/restart/petsc`, so they are not "legacy".

### Runs

- **Build.** 3e9eb52e is one commit on f2ed5b16. It changes only `discretisation_mesh_variables.py` and the test, so it is Python-only. We copied the development build's (bf022b14) installed package and laid 3e9eb52e's `src/underworld3` `.py` files over the copy. That overlay differs from the f2ed5b16 overlay only in `discretisation_mesh_variables.py`. Development and f2ed5b16 ran from the earlier re-check's overlays. Every run printed `underworld3.__file__` and used the intended overlay.
- **Conditions.** MPICH, at most 4 ranks on 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine. Every parallel run went through the repository's MPI supervisor (silence 60 s, or 120 s for pytest) inside an outer timeout.
- **Matrix.** The earlier probe, run in three modes: the default relative cache, an absolute cache, and a relative `outputPath` with every read path relative. Each mode had 26 runs: 10 writes at np 1, 2, 3, 4, 3 with an empty rank, two random layouts and refined meshes, and 16 cross-np reads. Each mode gave 1,608 cases, so 4,824 in all. Every case was exact, or refused with one message on every rank. Every run exited 0. The per-case outcomes are identical to f2ed5b16's.
- **Restart driver** (Poisson, `write_timestep(..., petsc_reload=True)` every 2 steps), default cache. Restarts 2→3 and 3→2 on 12 routes each, and 3→1 on 9 routes:
  - Rebuilt mesh, with `read_checkpoint`, `read_timestep`, the payload-only `read_checkpoint` or `load_from_h5_plex_vector`: step 2 is exact. Step 4 is within 8.5e-15 of the uninterrupted run (1.3e-14 at np 1), for a field of size 0.300.
  - Fresh mesh with `read_timestep`: exact.
  - Fresh mesh with `read_checkpoint`, the payload-only `read_checkpoint` or `load_from_h5_plex_vector`: refused on every rank, one message.
  - New route: the mesh is rebuilt from a copy of the checkpoint mesh file, and the copy is moved after loading. `read_checkpoint`, payload-only `read_checkpoint` and `load_from_h5_plex_vector` are refused at np 1, 2 and 3. `read_timestep` of the `/fields` file is exact, and its step 4 matches the run above. On f2ed5b16 the same route is exact at np 1 and refused at np 2.
- **Unverifiable map** (the earlier harness). Each run used its own new directory and its own `.meshes`. After loading, rank 0 renamed the mesh's source file or set it to mode 000; only the run's own files were touched. Runs at np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty, with P2 and DG P1 fields, 12 reads each, every outcome the same at every np:
  - The writing mesh reads its own `ck` and payload-only files exactly, through `read_checkpoint`, `load_from_h5_plex_vector` and `read_timestep`.
  - Every other read through the map is refused on every rank.
  - `read_timestep` of a `/fields` file is exact.
- **Missing or unreadable variable file** at np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty: the same `RuntimeError` on every rank, and every run exited 0. For the unreadable case, mode 000 was set on a copy the run made, and restored afterwards.
- **Files with no saved order found.** Two harnesses, at np 1, 2 and 3, on development, f2ed5b16 and 3e9eb52e. Reads 3→1, 2→3, 3→2 and 1→3 on a fresh gmsh mesh and on a rebuilt mesh, plus in-process reads on the writing mesh:
  - (a) The reload layout written before 2026-09-18. We replicated `Mesh._write_petsc_reload_file` of b25d2bad^ in a probe: `sectionView` and `localVectorView` at the file root, plus the direct vector under `/uw_checkpoint`. It was applied to the variable files that `write_timestep` writes.
  - (b) Files the run wrote, altered in place: the order dataset deleted, the reload group moved to the file root, or `/restart/petsc` renamed to `/uw_checkpoint` with the order kept.

  Results:

  | reads, file written at np 2 or 3 | development | f2ed5b16 | 3e9eb52e |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | fresh gmsh mesh, read at np 1 | wrong | wrong | **wrong** (1.58 / 2.53) |
  | fresh gmsh mesh, read at np 2 or 3 | wrong | refused | **wrong** (1.58 to 1.59 / 2.53 to 2.59) |
  | rebuilt mesh, `read_checkpoint` | exact | exact at np 1, refused at np ≥ 2 | exact |
  | writing mesh, in-process (b only) | wrong | exact | wrong |
  | files written at np 1 | exact (`read_checkpoint`) | refused at np 3 | exact |
  | `/uw_checkpoint` group with its order kept | wrong on fresh meshes | refused or exact | refused or exact, as f2ed5b16 |

  `read_timestep` of the `/fields` data is exact everywhere.
- **test_1302** (11 cases), run from the source tree with the default relative cache:

  | build | np 1 | np 2 | np 3 | np 3, empty rank |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 3e9eb52e | 11 passed | 11 passed | 11 passed | 11 passed |
  | f2ed5b16, same test file | 9 passed, 2 failed (the two new cases) | hangs in the missing-file case; the supervisor ends it after 120 s | the same | the same |
- **Neighbours,** 3e9eb52e against development (the review's development logs, same build):

  | set | development | 3e9eb52e |
  |---|---|---|
  | Serial, the 18 files (4 workers) | 202 passed, 1 skipped | 202 passed, 1 skipped |
  | Serial, the 6 snapshot and restart files | 50 passed, 1 xfailed | 50 passed, 1 xfailed |
  | Parallel at np 2 and 3: ptest_0762, ptest_0864, test_0005_xdmf_viz_topology_mpi, test_0690, test_0757, test_0790, test_0795 | 17 passed at each np | 17 passed at each np |
  | test_0690 at np 3 with an empty rank | 4 passed (at f2ed5b16) | 4 passed |
  | Parallel at np 2: test_0020 (swarm values), test_1155, test_0079 | 5 passed | 5 passed |

  Under pytest, ptest_0010 is not collected on either build, as in the review.
- **Diff f2ed5b16..3e9eb52e** read in full:
  - `_point_map_verdict` returns "legacy" when the variable file has no saved order, and `None` when the source cannot be read.
  - `unverified = point_map is None`, with no np condition.
  - Rank 0's `h5py.File` open is guarded, and the error is broadcast.
  - The test gets two new cases and a new docstring.

### New findings

**1. High: files in the reload layout written before 2026-09-18 are read unchecked, and wrong on a fresh mesh**

`read_checkpoint` reads the saved order only from `{checkpoint_group}/topologies/uw_mesh/dms/uw_mesh/order`. `checkpoint_group` is set only when `restart/petsc/topologies` or `uw_checkpoint/topologies` exists. Variable files written by `write_timestep(..., petsc_reload=True)` before b25d2bad (2026-09-18) have their `topologies` group at the file root. Their direct vector is under `/uw_checkpoint`, but their topology is not. So `checkpoint_group` is `None`, `saved_order` is `None`, and the verdict is "legacy". At 3e9eb52e, "legacy" is read through the map with no check, at every np.

The order is in those files, at `topologies/uw_mesh/dms/uw_mesh/order`. We wrote the old layout with the b25d2bad^ writer code at np 2 and 3. We then read it on a freshly built `UnstructuredSimplexBox`, with `read_checkpoint` or `load_from_h5_plex_vector`:

| written → read | P2 scalar | DG P1 |
|---|---|---|
| 3 → 1 | **wrong, 1.58** | **wrong, 2.53** |
| 2 → 3 | **wrong, 1.59** | **wrong, 2.59** |
| 3 → 2 | **wrong, 1.58** | **wrong, 2.53** |
| 1 → 3 | exact | exact |

The fields are of size about 2, and there is no error or warning. f2ed5b16 refused the three parallel-written cases at np ≥ 2. Development reads them wrong. So this is not a regression against development, but it gives back the protection f2ed5b16 had. A restart from a checkpoint written by an older release is a likely case: a fresh gmsh mesh reading an old file.

**Fix.** When `checkpoint_group` is `None`, look for the saved order at the root (`topologies/uw_mesh/dms/uw_mesh/order`). Every uw3 reload file seen in this review has one there. Then the verdict is "match" or "mismatch" as for current files:
- the rebuilt mesh stays exact;
- a fresh mesh on a parallel-written file is refused.

For a file that truly has no saved order, nothing can be checked. The safe call is the f2ed5b16 behaviour, at every np: only the writing mesh's own layout is trusted, and every other read is refused, with `read_timestep` named for `/fields` files. This refuses a rebuilt mesh that would have read such a file exactly. If that cost matters, the reader can accept "legacy" only when the mesh's source file is the variable file's companion checkpoint mesh file.

Add a test case: write the old layout at np ≥ 2 (move `restart/petsc/topologies` to the root, as test_0005_xdmf_compat already moves groups), then read on a fresh mesh. Expect a refusal.

**2. Low: the refusal for a moved mesh file does not say the file has gone**

For a rebuilt mesh whose checkpoint mesh file has moved, the message says the numbering "cannot be checked against the file mesh '<path>' was loaded from". It then says to "Rebuild the mesh from the checkpoint written with this file", which the user has already done. The `read_timestep` alternative is named, and it works: the `moved_rt` restart is exact at np 1, 2 and 3. Say why the check failed, and what to do: "the file mesh '<path>' was loaded from no longer exists or cannot be read; restore it, or …". The verdict can carry that reason, because rank 0 already knows whether the file exists.

**3. Low: a missing variable file now raises `RuntimeError`, not `FileNotFoundError`**

Before, at np 1, `read_checkpoint` of a missing or unreadable file raised `FileNotFoundError` or `PermissionError`. It now raises `RuntimeError` on every rank. The cause is in the message. No caller in `src/` catches the old types, but user scripts that test for a missing checkpoint with `except FileNotFoundError` (or `OSError`) will stop catching it. Raise the same exception class on every rank instead, or a `RuntimeError` subclass of `OSError`.

### What users see differently

For restarts at 3e9eb52e, compared with development:
- **Rebuilt mesh** (`uw.discretisation.Mesh('<name>.mesh.<index>.h5')`) with `read_checkpoint`, `load_from_h5_plex_vector` or the payload-only `read_checkpoint`: exact at any np. Development was wrong through `load_from_h5_plex_vector`.
- **Fresh gmsh mesh** with `read_checkpoint`, or `load_from_h5_plex_vector`, on a file written at np ≥ 2 by this branch or by development: refused on every rank. Development read it wrong. The message names the rebuilt mesh, and names `read_timestep` for files that hold `/fields`.
- **Rebuilt mesh whose checkpoint mesh file was later moved or deleted:** refused, now at np 1 as well. `read_timestep` of a `/fields` file still works, and it is exact. With a payload-only file (`create_xdmf=False`) there is no route left except restoring the mesh file. The message should say so (finding 2).
- **Missing or unreadable variable file:** an error on every rank. Development hung in parallel.
- **Checkpoints written before 2026-09-18, read on a fresh mesh:** still wrong, as on development, when the file was written at np ≥ 2 (finding 1).

### Not run

- 3-D meshes, manifold meshes, and meshes built from a DMPlex: their path is unchanged.
- In-place repartition (needs `remesh` and mmg, not in this build).
- np above 4.
- An actual file from a release before 2026-09-18. We wrote the old layout with that commit's writer code, run on this build.
- The b25d2bad^-layout probe and the deleted-order probe were run under the default cache only.
- f2ed5b16 was not rerun on the missing-file case at np 3-empty: it hangs at np 2 and 3 in the test.

Logs kept by the reviewer.
````

### UNFILED 50 (local branch fefd229a, to be pushed as a PR): ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# UNFILED 50: `evaluate_gradient` refuses, on every rank, points given to a rank without cells

**At the branch head fefd229a (on `development` fbef72fa; local, to be pushed as a PR): ready to merge on green from this review.**

## Method

- Read the diff: `gradient_evaluation.py` (one `Allreduce` of two int64s, entered by every rank after the method check and before either method's first collective; same `ValueError` on every rank when any rank with no cells holds points) and the new `tests/parallel/test_1350_...` (provisional number).
- Base build: `development` fbef72fa, a clean install. Head: the same install with the branch's `gradient_evaluation.py` laid over a copy (the only library file changed; checked against the head tree). Every run prints `underworld3.__file__`, and every log shows the tree it was meant to use.
- An empty rank from a test-only shell partition of a gmsh mesh (`UnstructuredSimplexBox`, cellSize 1/8, 162 cells; bands over np − 1 ranks, the last rank empty): cells per rank 162/0 at np 2, 87/75/0 at np 3, 62/49/51/0 at np 4. Fields: `x + 2y` (degree 1, gradient (1, 2)) and `x² + 3y²` (degree 2, gradient (2x, 6y)). Each probe catches the exception on each rank, prints it, then enters a barrier, so a rank-local raise shows up as a per-rank line, not a hang.
- Every parallel run under the MPI supervisor with a 60 s silence bound, on four cores, at most four processes at once. Other jobs on the machine were running.

## Results

**Empty rank, both methods, np 2, 3 and 4 (24 runs per tree):**

| case | `development` fbef72fa | branch head |
|---|---|---|
| points given to the empty rank (every rank given 3) | interpolant: `RuntimeError` (closest-cell search) on the empty rank alone, the others return (1, 2) to 1e-15; projection: no error anywhere, the empty rank returns a gradient 2.0 off | the same `ValueError` on every rank, at every np, both methods ("3 point(s) were given to 1 rank(s) that hold no cells of the mesh ..."); every rank reaches the barrier |
| zero points on the empty rank, own points elsewhere | empty rank (0, 2); others correct (linear: ≤ 1.3e-15; quadratic: interpolant 0.04 to 0.20, projection ≤ 6.7e-6) | identical numbers to `development` |
| points on a rank with cells that it does not hold (another rank's centroids; empty rank given none) | no error, wrong: interpolant max error 0.88 to 2.2, projection 1.1 to 3.7 (gradient up to 6) | unchanged: the same numbers, no error (see finding 2) |

**The new test file, np 2, 3, 4:** head 4 passed on every rank at each np. `development`: the two refusal cases fail (`RuntimeError` on the empty rank, "DID NOT RAISE" elsewhere) and the job hangs after pytest finishes at all three np; the supervisor reports every rank spinning in an `Allreduce`, the empty rank's one from `MPIR_Get_contextid` (a different collective), and kills it (exit 124). The zero-points cases pass on `development`, as the body says.

**Default partition, every rank holding cells (np 1, 2, 3, 4; both methods; both fields; each rank its own 3 centroids, rank 0 also 3 fixed points; 40 per-rank result arrays):** interpolant bitwise identical head vs base everywhere. Projection bitwise identical at np 1 and 2; at np 3 and 4 head differs from base by ≤ 1.3e-15, and two reruns of base differ from base by ≤ 1.8e-15: within the base's own run-to-run round-off (the projection solve), not caused by this change.

**Cost (1e5 points over np 4, default partition, each rank's 25,000 points inside its cells):** the guard alone, median of 200 calls, 9.0 µs (max over ranks). Whole calls: projection 0.276 s per call on both trees (warm); interpolant 1.00 s base, 1.03 s head (within the scatter with other jobs on the machine). The one `Allreduce` is negligible.

**Neighbours (the body's lists):**
- 36 serial files: 442 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed on head and on base, no failures. (One pass of all 36 per tree, the two trees side by side; it reached my 50-minute time limit partway through `test_1078` on both trees, with no failure so far, and the last three files were rerun: 26 passed on each.) The body's count of 462 passed is from a different collection count; outcomes agree.
- 33 parallel files at np 2: identical outcomes head vs base for 31 files (head 284 passed, 24 skipped summed over ranks, including `test_1350`'s 8; base 276, without it). `test_1350`: head passes, base hangs (above). `test_1064_rotated_freeslip_parallel`: on both trees it did not finish under the 60 s silence bound (rank 0 waits silently on a serial child the test spawns; rerun once per tree, same result). CI uses 300 s. This is a limit of this review's supervisor setting, not of the change, and `evaluate_gradient` has no callers in the library (only `compute_clement_gradient_at_nodes` is used elsewhere, and it is not touched).

**The swallow in `_evaluate_gradient_interpolant`** (`except Exception: outarray[i] = gradient_reshaped[0]`): I ran an instrumented copy that records every time the except branch fires, on a 2-D simplex box (random points and the vertices themselves), a quad box, an annulus, a 3-D simplex box and points outside the domain, serially and at np 3 (each rank its own points): about 16,000 points, 0 hits, gradient exact to 2e-14. For the try block to raise, `get_closest_cells` would have to return an invalid cell, and it either returns a valid local cell or raises `RuntimeError` before the loop. I found no in-domain input that reaches it. Finding 3.

## Findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium: none for this change. Already on `development` and not changed here, so not this PR's: a rank with cells that is given points it does not hold gets silently extrapolated gradients from both methods (errors 0.9 to 3.7 above for a gradient up to 6). The new docstring says each rank's points "are answered from that rank's own cells", which is right. One more sentence there would help: a point outside the calling rank's cells is extrapolated, and `global_evaluate` is the path for points another rank owns. The projection method's last step, rank-local `evaluate`, warns about this, but the interpolant does not. Worth an issue if not already open.
3. Low:
   - The swallow above: unreachable in every configuration I tried. It stays a silent fallback with no stated failure mode, which the Charter treats as a defect, so removing it (or letting it raise) is the clean fix, in a separate change.
   - The guard counts a 1-D `coords` as one point (`int(points.size > 0)`). This matches the methods' own `reshape(1, -1)`. A 0-d object array (a quantity wrapper `np.asarray` cannot unpack) would also count as one point on an empty rank and be refused there. I could not make that happen with the documented coordinate forms.
   - The test number 1350 is provisional (the body says so); renumber when the PR number is known.

## Not run

np 5 or more; 3-D meshes with an empty rank (the 3-D case was run only for the swallow check, without an empty rank); `test_1064` to completion; macOS; the full suite; CI (GitHub writes are paused). Logs kept by the reviewer.
````

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# UNFILED 55: test_0812's gradient checks read the whole mesh's extent and every rank answers

**At the branch head 68e6a068 (stacked on #1305's branch at e111957a; local, to be pushed as a PR): ready to merge on green from this review.** Merge #1305 first, then retarget to `development`.

## Method

- Read the diff against e111957a. It touches only `tests/test_0812_poisson_with_units.py` (a shared `_global_extent` helper with `Allreduce` MIN/MAX and `+inf`/`-inf` on a rank with no nodes, used by the BC check and the gradient check; the gradient at the centre is read through `global_evaluate`) and `scripts/test.sh` (one new np 3 line). No library code. It merges cleanly with `development` fbef72fa and with the `evaluate_gradient` fix (UNFILED 50, fefd229a).
- Library: a clean `development` fbef72fa install. The branch's base (233d3c44) is 125 commits older, but the change is test-only, so the library it will merge into is the one to test against. Every run prints `underworld3.__file__`.
- An empty rank from a test-only shell partition of the gmsh mesh: bands over np − 1 ranks, the last rank empty. It applies to `StructuredQuadBox`, which the test uses. The probe below printed the cells per rank: 64/0 at np 2, 32/32/0 at np 3, 24/16/24/0 at np 4 (8 × 8).
- Every parallel run under the MPI supervisor with a 60 s silence bound, on four cores, at most four processes at once. Other jobs on the machine were running.

## Results

**test_0812 at the branch head:**

| layout | serial | np 2 | np 3 | np 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| default partition | 4 passed | 4 passed, every rank | 4 passed, every rank | 4 passed, every rank |
| last rank empty | — | 4 passed, every rank | 4 passed, every rank | 4 passed, every rank |

**#1305's version of the file (e111957a), np 3, last rank empty:** the job hangs and the supervisor kills it (exit 124). Ranks 0 and 1 are in `update_lvec()` (a `globalToLocal`), called from the rank-local `evaluate` in `_check_linear_gradient`. The empty rank has already left that test and is in a barrier inside the next test's `StructuredQuadBox` build. This matches the body's account: the empty rank raised in the test's own extent over zero nodes, and `evaluate` itself did not hang.

**Mutant** (scratch copy, expected gradient 5 % off): both gradient tests fail, and the other two pass, on every rank: serial; np 2, 3 and 4 with the last rank empty; np 3 default. So the check still bites on every rank, the empty one included.

**Is rank-local `uw.function.evaluate` collective-safe with an empty rank?** Yes. A probe at np 2, 3 and 4, with the last rank empty, called `evaluate` on every rank for a degree-2 field (`x² + 3y²`), its x-derivative and a coordinate expression (`x + 2y`). Each was called with four point sets per rank: own (3 own centroids, none on the empty rank), zero everywhere, foreign (a centroid of another rank; the empty rank gets one too), and mixed. That is 12 calls per rank, run once with a barrier after each call and once with no barriers. Every rank completed every call in all six runs, and nothing raised. Each rank got its own local answer: own points exact (field to 9e-16, derivative to 2e-5, coordinate exact), and zero points gave shape (0, 1, 1). For a foreign point, the coordinate expression is exact everywhere. A rank with cells extrapolates the field and derivative (errors 0.02 to 1.2). The empty rank returns 3.9e-21 (effectively zero) for both, with a `RuntimeWarning` that the points are not located on this rank (finding 3). I retract the suggestion in my #1305 review that `evaluate` might not be collective-safe on an empty rank.

**The test.sh line:** I ran the exact new line (`mpirun -n 3 python -m pytest --with-mpi tests/test_0812_poisson_with_units.py` under the supervisor) from the branch tree: `collected 4 items` and `4 passed` on each of the 3 ranks. The line sits in the always-on np 3/np 4 block, `if [ $PARALLEL_RANKS -gt 0 ] && shard_owns 6`. CI calls `scripts/test.sh --p 2 --shard K/N` over the matrix from `--print-shards`, which is `[1,2,3,4,5,6]` on this branch, so shard 6 runs it under mpirun, on the default partition. `bash -n scripts/test.sh`: OK.

**Stack:** `git diff --stat e111957a 68e6a068`: `scripts/test.sh` (+5) and `tests/test_0812_poisson_with_units.py` (+24 −14), nothing else.

## Findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium: none.
3. Low:
   - `_global_extent` hard-codes two dimensions (`np.full(2, ...)`, `np.empty(2)`). Right for this 2-D test; `mesh.dim` would make the helper safe to copy into a 3-D test.
   - Body wording: "a rank-local `evaluate` would extrapolate there". On the empty rank it does not extrapolate: for a point it does not hold, it returns about 0 for a field and its derivative, with a `RuntimeWarning`. This is already on `development` and not this PR's, but "returns 0 with a warning" is the accurate description, and it is the real reason `global_evaluate` is needed there.
   - CI covers the file at np 3 on the default partition only. The empty-rank layout is a local test-only hook, as the body says. The mutant run shows the default np 3 run also catches a wrong gradient.

## Not run

The coverage and deprecated-pattern scripts (the body reports them); np 5 or more; macOS; the full suite; CI (GitHub writes are paused). Logs kept by the reviewer.
````

### #1307 re-check at 1c3834f7 (local head, to be pushed): ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**At 1c3834f7 (local head, to be pushed): ready to merge on green from this review.** The one-line fix to `Mesh.adapt`'s child closes the Medium finding from the 2f7fca41 review. Adapt then fault now works with every cell on one rank, and gives the default partition's slip. Where 2f7fca41 already worked, the answers are bitwise unchanged. The only behaviour change is to plain `adapt` on a base held by one rank, and it is acceptable (below).

## Earlier findings, then and now

- **Medium, adapt then fault with every cell on one rank:** fixed. `_adapt_nested` now passes `_preserve_partition=True` to the child, the same one-line fix measured in the earlier review. There is a new test case for it.
- **Low, `addon_partition._moved_wrap` and the `custom_mg` moved Mesh:** settled for `_moved_wrap`. I read the code and forced the case in one run:
  - `partition_addon` counts the ranks that receive a unit before it redistributes anything.
  - If only one rank would receive one, it refuses on every rank ("the whole add-on would sit on one rank").
  - `_moved_wrap` is called only after that check, and nowhere else.
  - In the run I forced `_peel_balance` to send every unit to rank 0 (np 2, default partition, `faulted_2d(2)`). It refused collectively, and `_moved_wrap` was called 0 times.
  - `custom_mg._colocate_level` is unchanged and still not reached by any case. That is acceptable as a known gap noted by reading.
- **Low, PR-body wording ("second-fault child"):** not checked here. The PR body is not part of the local commit.

## Method

- The change is Python only. I copied the 2f7fca41 overlay, which sits on an install whose compiled sources match the merge base exactly, and laid every `.py` from 1c3834f7 over it. Only `discretisation_mesh.py` differs from the 2f7fca41 overlay.
- I compared three versions: 1c3834f7, 2f7fca41, and the development merge base.
- Every run printed `underworld3.__file__` to confirm which overlay it loaded.
- The layouts are the earlier review's partition hook: every cell on rank K (`onK`), height bands, last rank empty, random, and the default partitioner.
- All runs were on 4 cores, through the MPI supervisor. Other jobs on the machine were running at the same time.

## Runs

**Adapt then fault** (`faulted_2d(2)`: cellSize 0.1, `refinement=1`, two adapt levels, `add_fault`, a shear solve with a frictionless fault):

| np | layout | development | 2f7fca41 | 1c3834f7 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | — | — | — | 1470 cells, 131 pairs, slip 0.159580936 |
| 2 | all on rank 0 | refuses (211) | refuses (678) | 1470/0, tail 242/0, 968/0, 1268/0; 131 pairs; slip 0.1595809403 |
| 2 | all on rank 1 | refuses (211) | refuses (684) | 0/1470, same slip, v/p SHA equal to the rank-0 run |
| 3 | all on rank 0 | — | refuses (895) | 1470/0/0, same slip and SHA |
| 4 | all on rank 0 | — | — | 1470/0/0/0, same slip and SHA |
| 4 | all on rank 3 | — | — | 0/0/0/1470, same slip and SHA |

- **Against np 1:** the one-rank runs match np 1 to 1.6e-8 in velocity and 4.9e-7 in pressure (|p| max 3.49). The nodes are identical. This is within the 1e-6 solver tolerance.
- **Layouts where 2f7fca41 already worked:** 1c3834f7 is bitwise identical to 2f7fca41 in cells per rank on the child and every tail level, pairs, slip, and the v/p SHA. The layouts were np 2 default, band and random; np 3 random and last rank empty; np 4 band.

**Plain `adapt` without a fault** (`adapted_2d(2)` and a shear solve whose exact solution is linear velocity with constant pressure):

| np 2 layout | 2f7fca41 | 1c3834f7 |
|---|---|---|
| default | child 673/687, tail 119/123, 476/492, 599/609 | identical, bitwise |
| all on rank 0, nvb | child redistributed to 683/677; tail stays 242/0, 968/0, 1208/0; velocity error 2.9e-8 | child stays 1360/0 with its tail; velocity error 1.6e-8 |
| all on rank 0, sbr | child 1564/1559, tail on rank 0 | child 3123/0 with its tail |
| all on rank 0, edge_split | child 1440/1420, tail on rank 0 | child 2860/0 with its tail; velocity error 1.9e-11 (2f7fca41: 6.9e-11) |

- **What changes:** with every base cell on one rank, the adapted child used to be spread over the ranks while its multigrid levels stayed on the one rank. Now the child stays on the one rank with them.
- **Load balance:** the solve now runs on one rank for that child, so the work is not spread. I judge this acceptable:
  - The base's own partition put everything there, and the levels were already there on 2f7fca41.
  - The old child was not co-partitioned with its levels, which is the state the add-on code refuses.
  - The code comment has always said "on-rank; no redistribute".
  - On every engine, a distributed base (the default constructor's) is unaffected.
  - The child can be one-rank only when the base was. The pure-Python NVB engine, which is not rank-local, runs only at np 1.
- All solves converged, at np 1 and np 2, on both versions.

**Tests:**

- **New case `test_adapt_then_fault_matches_the_default_partition`:** passes at np 2, 3 and 4 on 1c3834f7 (4 passed each). It fails on 2f7fca41 at np 2 and 3 (1 failed, 3 passed), with the `partition_addon` refusal.
- **Serial neighbours** (the same 107 files, 4 workers) on 1c3834f7: 997 passed, 5 skipped, 1 xpassed, the same as on 2f7fca41 and development.
- **Parallel neighbours at np 2** (the same 58 files) on 1c3834f7: 389 passed, 18 skipped. The outcome of every file is the same as on 2f7fca41.

## Findings

1. High: none.

2. Medium: none.

3. Low:
   - **The new test does not check its own premise:** it records the cells per rank (`held`) only in the assertion message. A one-line `assert sum(1 for c in held if c) == 1` would stop it passing quietly if the hook ever stopped putting every cell on rank 0.
````

### UNFILED 52 (local branch 61209737, to be pushed as a PR): changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at 61209737 (local branch head, to be pushed as the PR): changes requested.** The refusal is right, it is collective, and no common workflow is newly refused. The message needs fixing before merge. It tells users to build the mesh with `qdegree=2p` "which integrates the mass matrix exactly". With PETSc's rules, `qdegree=p` already integrates the mass matrix exactly, on every cell type I measured. A mesh's qdegree sets the quadrature for every solver on that mesh, so following the advice costs up to 7 times more quadrature points per cell for no gain in the mass matrix. This is a one-line change to the message, plus the test regex.

## What users see differently

At the default mesh `qdegree=2`, a projection of degree 3 or higher now raises `ValueError` instead of printing a caution and solving. In practice that means:

- **Direct projections:** `Projection`, `Vector_Projection`, `Tensor_Projection` and `MultiComponent_Projection` onto a P3 or higher field. Run, refused.
- **`solver.tau` on a solver with a P3 unknown:** Poisson with P3 T, or Stokes with P3 velocity. The flux projection takes the unknown's degree. Run, refused.
- **By code reading, not run:**
  - the adjoint's `dual_of` on a P3-velocity Stokes (its scratch field takes u's degree);
  - `LevelSet` with a P3 phi (its gradient projection takes phi's degree);
  - `gradient_evaluation` of a P4 source (it projects to degree p-1 = 3);
  - VE Stokes with P4 velocity (its history is degree p-1 = 3).

On triangles, every case that is now refused was silently wrong before. A P3 projection of sin(3x)·y had a maximum of 1.249 against 0.9986 at qdegree 3, so 25% off. A P3 Poisson `tau` had a maximum of 1.642 against 1.500, and a P3 Stokes `tau` 1.255 against 1.179. The message names the fix.

These are **not refused** (run at the default qdegree 2, base and head give the same output):
- VE Stokes P2/P1 (history P1) and P3/P2 (history P2);
- Stokes P2 `tau`;
- AdvDiffusion (SLCN) with P2 and with P3 T;
- Navier–Stokes SLCN with P2 and with P3 velocity;
- `evaluate` of a derivative of a P3 field (its projector is P1);
- the free-surface P1/P2 projections and the MMPDE metric gradient (degree 1 by default), through their test files.

None of the docs examples is refused. Every degree-3 example outside `WIP/` either builds its mesh with `qdegree=3` or projects nothing above degree 2. I ran `Ex_Convection_Cartesian_ThermoChem.py` (P3 T, `proxy_degree=3` swarm, default qdegree) for 2 steps at np 2: it gives the same output on base and head.

## Method

- **Builds:** base is development fbef72fa, from an existing clean install. Head is 61209737. The change touches `petsc_generic_snes_solvers.pyx`, so I rebuilt the compiled `generic_solvers` module from 61209737 against the same PETSc and laid it, with the changed `.py` files, over a copy of the base install. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`, and every run used the overlay its log names.
- **Machine:** 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine.
- **Mass-matrix spectra:** I assembled each projection's Jacobian with smoothing 0 and took the eigenvalues of the dense matrix. Cells: triangles P1–P4, quadrilaterals P1–P4, tetrahedra P1–P3 and hexahedra P1–P3, scalar and vector, at qdegree p−1, p and 2p. For each I report the zero-eigenvalue count, the condition number and ‖M_q − M_2p‖/‖M_2p‖.
- **Accuracy:** projections of a smooth non-polynomial function at qdegree p−1, p and 2p, compared with qdegree 2p+2 and with the function at the nodes. Run with GMRES+GAMG and with CG+Jacobi, on triangles P2–P4 and tetrahedra P2–P3.
- **Quadrature point counts:** from `PETSc.FE().createDefault(dim, 1, simplex, q)` for q = 0 to 8.
- **Representative workflows:** the cases listed above, base against head, serial.
- **Collective refusal:** the projection and `tau` refusals at np 2, and at np 3 with a shell partition that leaves the last rank with no cells.
- **Neighbour tests:**
  - Serial: 212 files that use projections, VE, SLCN, Navier–Stokes, free surface, MMPDE, level sets or degree-3+ fields, at `-n 4` with tier C excluded, on head. I re-ran failures alone on base and head.
  - Parallel: 91 files of the same kinds under `tests/parallel`, at np 2 on head. I re-ran every non-zero exit on base.

## Results

**Where the mass matrix is singular.** "zero" counts eigenvalues below 1e-12·max.

| cell | degree | qdegree p−1 | qdegree p | ‖M_p − M_2p‖/‖M_2p‖ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| triangles | P2, P3, P4 | 1 zero each (scalar; 2 for the vector) | none, cond 13 / 20 / 32 | 2e-16 to 6e-16 |
| quads | P1–P4 | 7 / 13 / 19 / 25 zero | none | 2e-16 to 1e-15 |
| hexes | P1–P3 | 19 / 61 / 127 zero | none | 3e-16 to 1.4e-15 |
| tets | P2 | 1 zero (3 for the vector) | none, cond 60 | 3e-16 |
| tets | P3 | **none on three meshes**, cond 460–750 (120–140 at q = p) | none | 5e-16 |
| triangles, tets | P1 at q 0 | none on these meshes | none | 1e-16 |

- **Threshold:** "singular when qdegree < degree" holds for triangles, quads, hexes and P2 tets. P3 tets at qdegree 2 (the default) are not singular; their matrix is under-integrated (20% off) but invertible.
- **qdegree = p is exact.** The matrix at qdegree p equals the 2p matrix to round-off on every cell type. This is expected from PETSc's rules. A simplex rule at qdegree q is exact to degree 2q: the triangle point counts 3, 6, 12, 16, 25 for q = 1 to 5 are the degree-2q symmetric rules. A tensor cell uses (q+1)^d Gauss points, exact to degree 2q+1. The mass matrix needs degree 2p.

**Accuracy at qdegree = p.**
- At qdegree p the answer differs from the exact-quadrature answer by less than the projection's own error. Max relative difference against nodal error: triangles P2 3.0e-4 against 2.1e-3, P3 1.1e-5 against 1.6e-4, P4 1.8e-6 against 1.1e-5; tets P2 1.2e-3 against 8.4e-3, P3 4.7e-4 against 1.4e-3. GMRES and CG agree to the digits shown.
- That remaining difference comes from integrating the non-polynomial right-hand side, not from the matrix. The draft table's qdegree = degree column (including the 9e-4 on P2 tets) measures the same thing. It is the quadrature error every other solver on that mesh carries too.
- There is no near-singular trouble at qdegree p: condition numbers there equal the exact ones.
- The test's claim holds: P3 and P4 agree with 2p to 1e-4 with GMRES and with CG.

**Below qdegree p.**
- On triangles the answer is arbitrary: 0.16 to 2.1 off, with the solver reporting convergence. This confirms the defect.
- P3 tets at qdegree 2 are degraded but stable: nodal error 6.1e-3 against 1.4e-3, about P2 accuracy, and a direct P3 projection reached 0.99776 against 0.99745 at qdegree 3. Refusing it is defensible, since the field loses an order, but it was not "arbitrary".

**Collective refusal.** At np 2, and at np 3 with an empty rank, every rank raised the same message, for both the projection and the `Poisson.tau` paths, and each run exited cleanly. No rank hung.

**Neighbour tests.**
- **Serial on head:** 2248 passed, 73 skipped, 10 failed, 1 collection error. No log contains the new refusal.
  - The 10 failures are in `test_1086_mmpde_seam_matches_serial` and `test_1103_facet_slip_matches_serial`. These tests start their own MPI jobs, which stalled under `-n 4` on 4 cores. Run alone at np 2 and 3, they gave 13 passed on base and 13 passed on head.
  - The collection error is a `tests/parallel/ptest_*` script picked up by pytest.
- **New test:** 6 failed and 4 passed on base, 10 passed on head, as the draft says.
- **Parallel at np 2 on head:** no file refused.
  - 9 files exited non-zero, and each gives the same result on base. Four are `ptest_*` scripts that are not pytest files.
  - The other five (test_1063, test_1064, test_1089, test_1095 and test_1152 parallel) run serial references in subprocesses. Each passed what it reached, then hit the 1200 s cap on both base and head.

## Findings

1. High: none.

2. Medium: the message recommends the wrong qdegree. It says "Build the mesh with qdegree=2p, which integrates the mass matrix exactly (qdegree=p is the minimum)". The docstring and the module text of the new test say the same.
   - qdegree = p already integrates the mass matrix exactly (table above).
   - The mesh's qdegree is shared by every solver on the mesh, so 2p over-integrates them all. Points per cell for a P3 field: triangles 33 instead of 12, tets 168 instead of 24, hexes 343 instead of 64.
   - Suggested message: "Build the mesh with qdegree=p (or higher), which integrates its mass matrix exactly". Update the test's `match=rf"qdegree={2 * degree}"` to match.
   - The PR body's table should say that its qdegree = degree column is the right-hand side's quadrature, not the matrix.

3. Medium: this is a behaviour change and needs a CHANGES.md line. Scripts that project to P3+ or read `.tau` from a P3 solver at the default qdegree used to run, silently wrong on triangles, and now stop. The message is clear and names the fix. A CHANGES.md entry should name who is affected: P3+ projections, `.tau` on P3 solvers, `dual_of` on P3 Stokes, P3 level sets, gradients of P4 fields. The branch does not touch CHANGES.md.

4. Low: "singular exactly when qdegree < degree" is not true for P3 tetrahedra at qdegree 2. The docstring, the test module text and the message all say it. There the matrix is invertible and the answer is about P2-accurate. Keeping the refusal is fine, since a P3 field at P2 accuracy is not what the user asked for. But the message's "its mass matrix is singular and the answer is arbitrary" should read "singular or under-integrated" so that it stays true on tets.

## Not run

- Hexahedra P4 and tetrahedra P4 spectra (too large for dense eigenvalues here).
- Deformed (non-affine) quadrilaterals. On those, qdegree p is no longer exact, but the matrix remains invertible.
- The `dual_of`, `LevelSet` P3, P4 `gradient_evaluation` and P4 VE cases, which come from reading the code.
- Tier C tests.
- Parallel runs at np 3 and above beyond the refusal check.
- Docs notebooks under `beginner/` and `WIP/`. All the beginner ones with degree-3 fields set qdegree=3.

Not caused by this change: a P3-velocity Stokes solve at np 3 with an empty rank stalled in my probe on base and on head alike. I have not traced whether the probe or the library is at fault.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1310 third re-check at 1f5ff65c (local head, to be pushed): ready to merge on green

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

## Third re-check of #1310 at 1f5ff65c

**Verdict at 1f5ff65c (the local head to be pushed): ready to merge on green from this review.**

The three findings from the re-check at 3e9eb52e are fixed:
- A reload file in the layout written before 2026-09-18 is now checked, because the reader finds its saved order at the file root. The reads that gave 1.58 to 1.59 (P2) and 2.53 to 2.59 (DG P1) are now refused on every rank, at np 1, 2, 3 and 3 with an empty rank. A rebuilt mesh still reads these files exactly.
- When the mesh's file has gone, the refusal now says so.
- A missing or unreadable variable file raises `FileNotFoundError` or `PermissionError`, the same on every rank. Nothing hangs.

We found no silent wrong read at 1f5ff65c. We ran about 5,980 read cases at np 1 to 4, with probes, the restart driver and the full matrix. Every case was exact, or refused with one message on every rank. On the same old-layout files, 3e9eb52e still gives the wrong values above.

The full matrix gives the same outcome as 3e9eb52e in all three modes. That is 4,824 cases, and none changed. The only outcomes that changed are on old-layout files and files with no saved order. Each of those changes is intended, and they are listed below. test_1302 passes 12 of 12 at np 1, 2, 3 and 3 with an empty rank. The neighbour tests match development.

What is left is two small things: a stale docstring and some wording in the refusal (finding 3).

### The findings from 3e9eb52e, then and now

| # | at 3e9eb52e | at 1f5ff65c |
|---|---|---|
| 1. High: files in the reload layout from before 2026-09-18 were called "legacy" and read unchecked | Fresh gmsh mesh, file written at np 2 or 3: wrong by 1.58 to 1.59 (P2) and 2.53 to 2.59 (DG P1) at np 1, 2 and 3, with no error | **Fixed.** The order is read at the file root. Those reads are now refused on every rank at np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty. The rebuilt mesh is exact. A file written at np 1 and read on a fresh mesh at np 1 or 3 is exact, because the saved order matches the source's order (the check passes; it is not skipped). If a file has no saved order at all, only the writing mesh reads it, in its own layout; every other read is refused at every np. |
| 2. Low: the refusal for a moved mesh file did not say the file had gone | "cannot be checked against the file mesh … was loaded from" | **Fixed.** Now: "the file mesh '…' was loaded from (…) has gone (moved or deleted), so its point numbering cannot be checked against it". There is a small wording nit (finding 3). |
| 3. Low: a missing file raised `RuntimeError` | `RuntimeError` on every rank | **Fixed.** `FileNotFoundError` for a missing file and `PermissionError` for one set to mode 000, at np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty, with the same message on every rank. Other open errors raise `OSError`. |

### Outcomes that changed from 3e9eb52e

We compared every case on the head with the same case at 3e9eb52e: 116 case keys changed. Each one is the fix, or the safe rule for files whose numbering cannot be checked.

| case | 3e9eb52e | 1f5ff65c | keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| Layout from before 2026-09-18 (written with that release's writer code), file written at np 2 or 3, fresh gmsh mesh, `read_checkpoint` or `load_from_h5_plex_vector`, read at np 1, 2 or 3 | **wrong, 1.58 to 1.59 / 2.53 to 2.59** | refused | 12 |
| Files we wrote and then altered, with `restart/petsc/topologies` moved to the root and `restart/` removed; fresh mesh, file written at np 2 or 3 | wrong | refused | 18 |
| The same altered files, read in the writing process at np 2 or 3 by the writing mesh or another mesh built from the same gmsh file | wrong | refused. The writing mesh is refused only because this alteration also removes the direct vector, so `same_layout` has nothing to read. | 16 |
| Order dataset deleted: fresh gmsh mesh, file written at np 2 or 3 | wrong | refused | 18 |
| Order dataset deleted: writing mesh in its own process, np 2 and 3 | wrong | exact (its own layout is trusted) | 12 |
| Order dataset deleted: another mesh from the same gmsh file, np 2 and 3 | wrong | refused | 4 |
| Order dataset deleted: rebuilt mesh (every np), another mesh from the same gmsh file at np 1, and a fresh mesh reading a file written at np 1 | exact | refused | 36 |

The last row is the cost of the safe rule. If a file truly has no saved order, it cannot be checked, so only the writing mesh reads it. Every uw3 reload file we have seen saves an order, in either layout, so this case needs a file someone has edited by hand.

Unchanged: every file with its order kept under `/uw_checkpoint` (refused or exact, as before); every case in the full matrix; every case on the unverifiable-source harness; and the files written by development.

### What users see differently

For restarts at 1f5ff65c, compared with development:
- **Rebuilt mesh** (`uw.discretisation.Mesh('<name>.mesh.<index>.h5')`) with `read_checkpoint`, `load_from_h5_plex_vector` or the payload-only `read_checkpoint`: exact at any np. This includes checkpoints written before 2026-09-18. Development was wrong through `load_from_h5_plex_vector`.
- **Fresh gmsh mesh** with `read_checkpoint` or `load_from_h5_plex_vector`, on a file written at np ≥ 2: refused on every rank. This includes checkpoints written before 2026-09-18. Development read these files wrong. The message names the rebuilt mesh, and names `read_timestep` for files that hold `/fields`; we checked that `read_timestep` is exact on these files. A file written at np 1 is still read exactly on a fresh mesh, because its order checks.
- **Rebuilt mesh whose checkpoint mesh file was later moved or deleted:** refused at every np. The message now says the file has gone. `read_timestep` of a `/fields` file is exact.
- **Missing or unreadable variable file:** `FileNotFoundError` or `PermissionError` on every rank. Development hung in parallel. Scripts that use `except FileNotFoundError` / `except OSError` keep working.
- **A reload file whose order dataset has been removed** (we have not found one written by any release): only the writing mesh reads it. Every other read is refused, including the rebuilt mesh.

### Runs

- **Build.** 1f5ff65c is one commit on 3e9eb52e. It changes `discretisation_mesh_variables.py` and the test, and is Python-only. We copied the development build's (bf022b14) installed package and laid 1f5ff65c's `src/underworld3` `.py` files over the copy. That overlay differs from the 3e9eb52e overlay only in `discretisation_mesh_variables.py`. 3e9eb52e and development ran from the previous re-check's overlays. Every run printed `underworld3.__file__`, and every run used the intended overlay.
- **Conditions.** MPICH, at most 4 ranks on 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine. Every parallel run went through the repository's MPI supervisor (silence 60 s, or 120 s for pytest) inside an outer timeout. Every probe and driver run exited 0.
- **Layout from before 2026-09-18** (the probe that replicates `Mesh._write_petsc_reload_file` of b25d2bad^). We read the previous re-check's files (written at np 1, 2 and 3) on a fresh gmsh mesh and on a rebuilt mesh. Reads 3→1, 2→1, 2→3, 3→2, 1→3, 1→1 and 2→3-empty, on 1f5ff65c and on 3e9eb52e. On 1f5ff65c we also wrote this layout at np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty and read the 3-empty file at np 1.

  | file written at | fresh gmsh, `read_checkpoint` / `load_from_h5_plex_vector` | rebuilt, `read_checkpoint` | `read_timestep` (fields) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | np 2, 3, 3-empty, read at np 1, 2, 3, 3-empty | refused (3e9eb52e: wrong, 1.58 to 1.59 / 2.53 to 2.59) | exact | exact |
  | np 1, read at np 1 or 3 | exact (order matches) | exact | exact |
  | in-process, writing mesh, np 2, 3, 3-empty | refused. The old writer code does not register the write, so the mesh is not recognised as the writer. | n/a | n/a |
  | in-process, writing mesh, np 1 | exact | n/a | n/a |

- **Altered files** (the earlier probe; only the run's own files are altered). Four variants: order dataset deleted; reload group moved to the root; `/restart/petsc` renamed to `/uw_checkpoint` with the order kept; and renamed with the order deleted. Each variant was written at np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty, with in-process reads. Cross-np reads 3→1, 2→1, 2→3, 3→2, 1→3, 2→3-empty and 3-empty→1. With the probe above, 632 cases in all, each exact or refused with one message on every rank:
  - order deleted, with or without the rename: the writing mesh is exact in its own process. Every other `read_checkpoint` and `load_from_h5_plex_vector` is refused ("the file saves no point order"), at every np, rebuilt mesh included. `read_timestep` of `/fields` is exact.
  - reload group at the root: as for the old layout above.
  - `/uw_checkpoint` with the order kept: unchanged from 3e9eb52e.
- **Matrix.** The earlier probe in three modes: the default relative cache, an absolute cache, and a relative `outputPath` with every read path relative. 26 runs per mode, with writes at np 1, 2, 3, 4, 3-empty, two random layouts and refined meshes, plus 16 cross-np reads. 1,608 cases per mode, so 4,824 in all. Every case was exact or refused. Compared case by case with 3e9eb52e: 0 changed.
- **Restart driver** (Poisson, `write_timestep(..., petsc_reload=True)` every 2 steps), default cache. Restarts 2→3 and 3→2 on 12 routes each, and 3→1 on 9 routes, 33 in all. Every outcome is the same as at 3e9eb52e:
  - rebuilt mesh (`read_checkpoint`, `read_timestep`, payload-only `read_checkpoint`, `load_from_h5_plex_vector`): step 2 exact. Step 4 within 8.5e-15 of the uninterrupted run at np 2 and 3, and 1.3e-14 at np 1, for a field of size 0.300.
  - fresh mesh with `read_timestep`: exact.
  - fresh mesh with the other three routes: refused on every rank, one message.
  - mesh file moved after loading: `read_checkpoint`, payload-only `read_checkpoint` and `load_from_h5_plex_vector` are refused at np 1, 2 and 3, with the new "has gone (moved or deleted)" wording. `read_timestep` is exact.
- **Unverifiable source** (the earlier harness: rank 0 renames the mesh's source file, or sets it to mode 000, after loading; only the run's own files are touched). np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty, P2 and DG P1, 12 reads each. The outcomes match 3e9eb52e case by case:
  - the writing mesh reads its own files exactly;
  - every other read through the map is refused on every rank;
  - `read_timestep` of `/fields` is exact.

  The rename case now says "has gone". The mode-000 case keeps the general "cannot be checked" wording.
- **Missing or unreadable variable file,** np 1, 2, 3 and 3-empty: `FileNotFoundError` (missing) and `PermissionError` (mode 000, set on the run's own copy and restored to 600 afterwards). The message is the same on every rank, and nothing hangs.
- **Files written by development (bf022b14)**, read on 1f5ff65c: 2→3, 3→2, 4→1, 1→3, plus refined 3→2 and 3→1. Same outcomes as 3e9eb52e: exact on the rebuilt mesh; on a fresh mesh, refused unless the file was written at np 1.
- **test_1302** (12 cases), run from the source tree with the default relative cache:

  | build | np 1 | np 2 | np 3 | np 3, empty rank |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1f5ff65c | 12 passed | 12 passed | 12 passed | 12 passed |
  | 3e9eb52e, same test file | 11 passed, 1 failed (missing file: `RuntimeError`, not `FileNotFoundError`) | 10 passed, 2 failed (that case, and the old-layout case: the fresh read is not refused) | the same as np 2 | not run |

  The new old-layout case fails on 3e9eb52e at np 2 and 3. At np 1 it passes on both builds, as it should: its file is written at np 1, so the fresh read is exact either way.
- **Neighbours,** 1f5ff65c against development (the review's development logs, same build):

  | set | development | 1f5ff65c |
  |---|---|---|
  | Serial, the 18 files (4 workers) | 202 passed, 1 skipped | 202 passed, 1 skipped |
  | Serial, the 6 snapshot and restart files | 50 passed, 1 xfailed | 50 passed, 1 xfailed |
  | Parallel at np 2 and 3: ptest_0762, ptest_0864, test_0005_xdmf_viz_topology_mpi, test_0690, test_0757, test_0790, test_0795 | 17 passed at each np | 17 passed at each np |
  | test_0690 at np 3 with an empty rank | 4 passed | 4 passed |
  | Parallel at np 2: test_0020 (swarm values), test_1155, test_0079 | 5 passed | 5 passed |
  | ptest_0010 as a script at np 4; ptest_1079 as a script at np 2 | PASS; exit 0 | PASS; exit 0 |

  Under pytest, ptest_0010 is not collected on either build, as in the review.
- **Diff 3e9eb52e..1f5ff65c,** read in full:
  - `_point_map_verdict` returns "no order" and "gone" in place of "legacy" and part of `None`.
  - The saved order is looked for under the checkpoint group, then at the root.
  - `unverified` covers `None`, "gone" and "no order".
  - The refusal has a reason for each verdict.
  - The open error carries its kind, and every rank raises that class.
  - The test gets a stricter missing-file case and the old-layout case.

### New findings

**1. High:** none.

**2. Medium:** none.

**3. Low: a stale docstring, and wording in the refusal for a source that has gone**
- The `_point_map_verdict` docstring still describes `"legacy"` for a file with no order. It does not mention `"no order"` or `"gone"`.
- In the "gone" message, the mesh name is the path itself, so the path appears twice: "the file mesh '<path>' was loaded from (<path>)". The sentence also chains "so … so": "…cannot be checked against it, so the point map may not apply".
- The advice that follows still opens with "Rebuild the mesh from the checkpoint". For the moved-file case it would help to say "restore the file, or rebuild the mesh from it at its new location" first. We did not run the rebuild from the new location, but it should be the ordinary rebuilt-mesh route.

None of this affects which reads are allowed.

### Not run

- 3-D meshes, manifold meshes, and meshes built from a DMPlex: their path is unchanged.
- In-place repartition (needs `remesh` and mmg, not in this build).
- np above 4.
- An actual file from a release before 2026-09-18. We wrote the old layout with that commit's writer code, run on this build.
- The old-layout and altered-file probes were run under the default cache only.
- test_1302 on 3e9eb52e at np 3 with an empty rank.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### UNFILED 52 r2 at 83dbc69a: ready to merge on green from this review

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at 83dbc69a (local branch head, to be pushed as the PR): ready to merge on green from this review.** The message now recommends `qdegree = degree`, and following it gives an accepted projection with an exact mass matrix on all four cell types. CHANGES.md has an entry. The wording is "singular or under-integrated". One wording slip in the CHANGES.md entry is left (finding 3). It is not a blocker.

## Earlier findings, then and now

| # | Then (61209737) | Now (83dbc69a) |
|---|---|---|
| 2. Medium | The message recommended `qdegree=2p`, but `qdegree=p` is already exact. | **Fixed.** It says "Build the mesh with qdegree={degree}, which integrates the mass matrix exactly; a higher qdegree only integrates the right-hand side more closely, at a cost to every solver on the mesh." The docstring and the test module text agree, and the test regex matches the new text. |
| 3. Medium | No CHANGES.md entry. | **Fixed.** The 2026-11-25 entry lists what is newly refused at the default qdegree of 2. The cases found by reading the code are marked as not run. It tells users to build the mesh with qdegree at least the highest projected degree, and says that common workflows at the default degrees are not affected. No session codenames. |
| 4. Low | "Singular exactly when qdegree < degree" is false for P3 tets at q2. | **Fixed.** The message, the docstring and the test text say "singular or under-integrated". The docstring says a P3 tet at qdegree 2 is invertible but about P2-accurate. That case is still refused, with the new wording (below). |

## Runs

- **Build:** I rebuilt the compiled `generic_solvers` module from 83dbc69a against the same PETSc as before (exit 0). I laid it, with `systems/solvers.py` from 83dbc69a (unchanged since 61209737), over a copy of the earlier head overlay. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__` and shows the overlay was used. The base is development fbef72fa, as before.
- **Diff 61209737..83dbc69a:** three files. CHANGES.md (+25), the `.pyx` comment, docstring and message, and the test's module text and regex. No change to the code that decides when to refuse.
- **Following the message (serial):** For each cell type, a projection at qdegree p−1 is refused. I parsed the qdegree the message names, rebuilt the mesh at that qdegree, and solved.

  | cell | degree | refused at | message names | solve | ‖M_q − M_2p‖/‖M_2p‖ | cond | nodal error |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | triangles | P3 | q 2 | qdegree=3 | accepted | 4.3e-16 | 24 | 1.5e-4 |
  | quads | P3 | q 2 | qdegree=3 | accepted | 1.1e-15 | 58 | 1.9e-4 |
  | tets | P3 | q 2 (invertible there; refused by design) | qdegree=3 | accepted | 5.2e-16 | 125 | 1.4e-3 |
  | hexes | P2 | q 1 | qdegree=2 | accepted | 6.8e-16 | 198 | 2.4e-2 (2×2×2 mesh) |

- **Parallel message:** I ran these on head:
  - a P3 projection at np 2;
  - a P3 projection at np 3 with the last rank holding no cells;
  - Poisson P3 `tau` at np 3 with the empty rank;
  - Stokes P3 `tau` at np 2.

  Every rank printed the new message, naming `qdegree=3`. All four runs exited 0 and none hung.
- **test_1052:** 10 passed on head. On development: 6 failed ("DID NOT RAISE") and 4 passed.
- **Representative workflows (serial, default qdegree 2):** I re-ran the earlier review's 14 cases on head. The refusal set is unchanged: P3 projection (2-D and 3-D), Poisson P3 `tau` and Stokes P3 `tau` are refused. VE P2/P3, VE `tau` P2, Stokes `tau` P2, Navier–Stokes P2/P3, AdvDiffusion P2/P3, Poisson `tau` P2 and evaluate of a P3 derivative all run. Their printed values match the earlier head run.
- **Machine:** 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine.

## New findings

1. High: none.

2. Medium: none.

3. Low: the CHANGES.md entry is slightly too narrow and uses internal labels.
   - It lists "`.tau` (the stress projection) on a solver with a P3 velocity". The flux projection takes the unknown's degree, so `.tau` on a Poisson (or other scalar) solver with a P3 unknown is also refused. I ran that case: refused here and in the earlier review. Suggest: "`.tau` (the flux or stress projection) on a solver whose unknown is P3, such as Poisson with a P3 T or Stokes with a P3 velocity".
   - The entry names the classes by their internal `SNES_` names, which match the error message. Users write `uw.systems.Projection` and the others, so a parenthetical would help.
   - It cites "UNFILED 52" and "the review", where other entries cite PR numbers. Suggest the PR number once the PR is opened.

## Not run

- The full serial and parallel neighbour sweeps of the earlier review. This change is to messages and text only, and the representative workflows and test_1052 were re-run.
- Spectra beyond the four follow-the-message cases. The earlier table stands, since nothing that builds the matrix changed.
- The cases found by reading the code (`dual_of`, P3 `LevelSet`, P4 `gradient_evaluation`, P4 VE), as before.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````
