### #1197 r6 at f0a5d897: ready to merge on green from this review

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Sixth recheck at f0a5d897: ready to merge on green from this review.** This head is two commits on cace7153 and has not been pushed yet, so CI has not run on it. The verdict is for f0a5d897 as it stands.

The two commits make a `Min`, `Max` or `Piecewise` unknown when one of its branches has unknown units. They also narrow the skip from the fifth recheck: a field without units now makes a term unknown only through a product, the base of a power, a named expression or a branch. Inside a function argument it does not, so `T*exp(U)` is compared again. A sum is unknown only when all of its model-dependent terms are, and constants do not decide this.

Every viscosity form we tried that a user would reasonably write is still accepted, with development's numbers. The two sums that development accepts and the head now refuses are both genuine kelvin-beside-Pa s mismatches. There is one new Medium finding: a sum and a `Max` give different answers for the same unknown branch. It changes no solved value and does not block the merge.

Only `.py` files changed between cace7153 and f0a5d897: `function/unit_conversion.py`, `units.py` and `tests/test_0760_evaluate_units_contract.py`. So the head was not rebuilt. Its `src/underworld3` `.py` files were laid over a copy of the same 199955d2 build as before. After the overlay, only the two changed source files differ from that build. The baseline is development at the merge base 1e10c63f, using the same overlay as in the fourth and fifth rechecks. Every run printed which `underworld3` it loaded.

## Fifth-recheck findings, then and now

| finding | at cace7153 | at f0a5d897 |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Low: the skip is broader than "units partly unknown" | `T*exp(U)` and `T*(1 + U)` skipped, though both are kelvin | Partly addressed. `T*exp(U)` is compared: `eta0 + T*exp(U)` and `eta0*exp(U) + T` are refused at `get_units`, `evaluate` and the setter, with "... is in kelvin and \eta_0 is in pascal * second. These terms need the same dimension." `T*(1 + U)` is still skipped. The author chose this: in `1 + U` the only model-dependent term is unknown. That is consistent with the new rule, and development accepts it too. Closed |
| 2. Low: `evaluate(Max(T, x*U))` gave 1200 kelvin | yes | Fixed. It now gives a bare 1.2, as development does, and `get_units` gives None. `Min(T, x*U)` and both `Piecewise` forms likewise give a bare 1.1 |
| 3. Low: stale `_common_units` docstring | yes | Fixed. The docstring now covers the unknown term and the unknown `Min`/`Max`/`Piecewise` |
| 4. Low: four older commit messages carry a session code | open | Still open, left for the squash. The two new commits and the test comment carry no session code, no machine name and no path |

## What is now compared or refused, against development

There were 24 viscosity forms and 8 consistency forms. The setup was `T` in K (1100), fields `U` (2) and `C` (0.3) without units, `x` in km (18), and `eta0`, `eta1`, `eta_max` in Pa s. Each form went through `get_units`, `evaluate`, the setter and the solve, on all three builds.

**Newly refused (development sets and solves them):**
- `eta0 + T*exp(U)`: development solves to 2.136692085e-05.
- `eta0*exp(U) + T`: development solves to 9.560371433e-05.

Both are kelvin beside Pa s, so both are genuine mismatches, and the refusal is right. Development's `evaluate` also refuses them, with its message about fields without units. Nothing else is refused that development accepts.

**Still accepted at the setter and the solve, with development's |v|max to 10 digits.** Development also accepts every one of these:
- Composition-weighted: `eta0*(1 - C) + eta1*C`, the same plus `eta_max`, and the swarm-variable version `eta0*(1 - Cs) + eta1*Cs`.
- Arrhenius times composition: `eta0*arr*(1 - C) + eta1*C`.
- Harmonic mean: `1/((1 - C)/eta0 + C/eta1)`.
- Exponential in composition: `eta0*exp(beta*C)` (beta named or a literal 2), `eta0*exp(beta*C) + eta1`, `eta0*exp(beta*C) + eta1*C`, `Min(eta0*exp(beta*C), eta_max)`, `eta0*arr*exp(beta*C) + eta1` and `eta0*exp(beta*Cs) + eta1`.
- `eta0*arr + eta1*U`, `eta0*(x/W + C)`, `eta0*(x/W + C) + eta1` and `eta0*sqrt(C) + eta1`.
- `eta0 + T*(1 + U)`, `eta0 + exp(U)`, `eta0 + x*U*eta1` and `Max(eta0, x*U*eta1)`.

Here `arr = exp(E/(R*T) - E/(R*T0))`. Two forms, `eta0*arr*U` and `eta0*arr*(1 + U)`, set on every build. Their solves stop in the algebraic multigrid setup ("Computed maximum singular value as zero") identically on all three builds. That is a property of the probe, not of this change.

**`evaluate`** still refuses every one of these forms, on all three builds, through development's existing check: "fields without units beside factors with units ... Give the field units". That check is unchanged.

**`get_units` differs from development, for information only.** The setter and the solve are not affected.
- The head gives None where development gives pascal * second for `eta0*(1 - C) + eta1*C`, `eta0*arr*(1 - C) + eta1*C`, the harmonic mean, `eta0*(1 - Cs) + eta1*Cs` and the user-written `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))` with a velocity without units.
- The head gives pascal * second where development gives dimensionless for `Min(eta0*exp(beta*C), eta_max)`.

## Consistency: the sum against `Min`, `Max` and `Piecewise`

These are the same branch `x*U` (or `x*U*eta1`), with `U` a field without units. Results were the same serially and at np 2, where every rank evaluates.

| expression | f0a5d897 | cace7153 | development |
|---|---|---|---|
| `T + x*U` | get_units kelvin; evaluate refused (field without units) | kelvin; refused | kilometer; refused |
| `Max(T, x*U)` | None; evaluate 1.2, bare | kelvin; 1200 kelvin | dimensionless; 1.2, bare |
| `Min(T, x*U)` | None; 1.1, bare | kelvin; 1100 kelvin | dimensionless; 1.1, bare |
| `Piecewise((T, x > W/2), (x*U, True))` | None; 1.1, bare | kelvin; 1100 kelvin | dimensionless; 1.1, bare |
| `Piecewise((T, x > 0.5), (x*U, True))` | None; 1.1, bare | kelvin; 1100 kelvin | dimensionless; 1.1, bare |
| `eta0 + x*U*eta1` | Pa s; refused; set, solves 0.001606931276 | Pa s; refused; same | km Pa s; refused; same |
| `Max(eta0, x*U*eta1)` | None; refused; set, solves 0.001758062066 | Pa s; refused; same | dimensionless; refused; same |
| `Min(eta0, x*U*eta1)` | None; refused | Pa s; refused | dimensionless; refused |

The sum still quietly gives `x*U` the units of `T`: `get_units(T + x*U)` is kelvin. The `Max`, `Min` and `Piecewise` of the same branch are unknown (finding 1). The setter and the solve give the same numbers on all three builds.

## Runs and results otherwise

- **The fifth recheck's serial probes, compared line by line with their cace7153 output:** `Min`/`Max`/`Abs`/`Piecewise`, dimensionless constants, strain-rate units, reassignment and constant sums, the setter-and-solve probe and the earlier-fixes probe. All are unchanged.
- **The `test_1059` model:** its 21 evaluate, projection and Stokes arrays are bit-for-bit cace7153's.
- **The fifth recheck's give-up probe** differs only in the intended places:
  - `eta0 + T*exp(U)` is refused;
  - `Max(T, x*U)` gives a bare 1.2;
  - `get_units` of `Min(eta0, T*U)` and `Piecewise((eta0, x > W/2), (T*U, True))` is now None.
- **Plastic viscosities, velocity without units:**
  - `eta_b + Min(eta, Max(C - rho*g*y/2, 0)/(2*edot_II))` sets and solves in 4 Newton iterations to |v|max 2.75637835, as before.
  - `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))` sets. Its `get_units` is now None, where it was pascal * second. Its solve did not finish within 900 s, as on every earlier build.
- **Spiegelman benchmark, 900 s cap:** no refusal. Its 13 nonlinear residuals match the fifth recheck's line for line (0.00662074 ... 0.00641563). It did not reach the 14th before the cap, which the fifth recheck did. The machine was shared with other jobs and with this review's serial slice.
- **Per-solve check cost:** the check alone takes 4.4 to 4.6 ms, and 22.1 ms after a named value changes (fifth recheck: 4.5 to 4.8 ms and 22.4 to 22.7 ms).
- **Every rank the same:**
  - The `Min`/`Max` probe at np 2, np 3, and np 3 with an empty rank; the constant-sum, reassignment-at-solve, setter-and-solve and earlier-fixes probes at np 3 with an empty rank. Once the ranks are sorted, all match the fifth recheck's output.
  - The give-up probe at np 3 with an empty rank differs from the fifth recheck only in the intended places above.
  - The new probe at np 2 on all three builds, and at np 3 with an empty rank on the head, gives the same verdict on every rank. Its |v|max differ from serial only in the 10th digit.
  - The empty rank's local `evaluate` at a point it does not own prints a different number. This is the probe-side effect seen in earlier rechecks.
- **New tests:**
  - `test_a_max_with_a_branch_of_unknown_units_is_unknown`: passes on the head; fails at cace7153 (kelvin) and on development (dimensionless).
  - `test_a_known_unit_product_with_a_unitless_field_in_a_function_is_compared`: passes on the head; fails at cace7153 and on development, where `evaluate` refuses with the field-without-units message, not "different dimensions".
  - `test_a_regularised_plastic_branch_with_a_velocity_without_units_is_not_refused`: passes on all three builds. It guards against the intermediate commit's regression, not against an earlier pushed head. It has no assertion beyond "the setter does not raise".
- **Serial slice, head, the same 82 files:** 722 passed, 7 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed. That is the fifth recheck's 719 plus the 3 new tests.
- **np 2, head:** `test_0762` and the parallel `test_0035`, 13 passed.
- **Examples and docs:** the 26 files were run at the head from fresh copies of its docs tree, which is identical to cace7153's, with the same edits and the same 900 s cap.
  - No new refusals: the sum-check hook recorded no mismatch in any file.
  - Every file has the same exit status as at cace7153, and so as development's.
  - The last error lines are identical to cace7153's.
  - The annulus convection example differs only in the 16th digit of the step taken twice, the known run-to-run noise.

## Findings

1. Medium: a sum and a `Min`/`Max`/`Piecewise` disagree on the same unknown branch.

   With `U` a field without units, the sum skips the unknown branch and takes the known one's units:
   - `get_units(T + x*U)` is kelvin;
   - `get_units(eta0 + x*U*eta1)` is pascal * second.

   The same branch in a `Max`, `Min` or `Piecewise` makes the whole unknown: `get_units` is None.

   `evaluate` disagrees too:
   - the sum is refused, with development's "give the field units" message;
   - `Max(T, x*U)`, `Min(T, x*U)` and both `Piecewise` forms return a bare model value (1.2, 1.1) with no message. Development behaves the same way, so this is not a regression.

   The rule is that ambiguous input is refused, with a message showing the fix. Under that rule the forms should agree: both unknown, or both refused at `evaluate` with that message. Two changes would do it:
   - extend development's field-without-units check in `evaluate` to `Min`/`Max`/`Piecewise` branches (it already catches the same branch in a sum);
   - have `get_units` of a sum with a skipped term return None, not the known terms' units. The setter would keep comparing the known terms, as now.

   This changes no solved value: the setter and the solve give the same numbers on all three builds. It can be a follow-up. It does not block this PR.

2. Low: `get_units` now returns None for some common viscosities where development returns pascal * second. Examples are `eta0*(1 - C) + eta1*C`, the harmonic mean and the swarm version, with `C` a composition field without units. Adding a constant `eta_max` makes the same sum pascal * second again. This follows from the new rule (every model-dependent term is unknown). It affects only what `get_units` reports, not the setter or the solve. A line in the `get_units` docstring would set the expectation.

3. Low (carried): the four older commit messages with a session code. A squash merge, or a clean squash message, settles it.

## Not run

- the full suites;
- CI at f0a5d897 (not pushed);
- `test_1086`: it needs up to 5 ranks, beyond this run's core budget, and the change does not touch it;
- anything above np 3;
- the Spiegelman benchmark and `Min(eta, C/(2*edot_II))` to completion, on any build;
- development's 26 files again (unchanged since the fourth recheck);
- the docs' Markdown code blocks;
- the description wording (it is edited on GitHub, and nothing has been pushed).

The runs shared the machine with other jobs. Logs kept by the reviewer.
````

````markdown
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

## #1309 re-check at 4fdeee00: natural conditions have no free component; a named component needs a value

**Verdict at 4fdeee00 (local head, to be pushed): ready to merge on green from this review.** Both earlier findings are fixed. Nothing that was accepted and solved before changes, apart from the cases that solved to NaN. Two small wording points remain (finding 3), and neither blocks the merge.

### Earlier findings, then and now

| finding at 8460d6d5 | at 4fdeee00 |
|---|---|
| 2. For a natural condition the message suggested `(None, 0.0)`, and natural `(None, 1.0)` solved to NaN (SNES reason -4) | Fixed. Any free entry in a natural condition is refused when the condition is added, with its own wording ("give 0.0 for no traction on a component, e.g. (0.0, 1.0)"). `(0.0, 1.0)` still solves, bitwise as before. |
| 2. A named component with no value was accepted: natural `(None, None)` with `components=[1]`, and essential `(None, 0.0)` with `components=[0]`, both reason -4 | Fixed. Both are refused when added. The natural one is caught by the natural rule, the essential one by the new "names component(s) [0] in components=, but gives them no value" check. |
| 3. The message printed the converted value (`[oo, oo]`) | Fixed. It prints what the caller wrote: `(None, None)`, `(None,)`, `(0.0, 0.0)`, and for a `sympy.Matrix` argument the Matrix itself. |
| 3. `is_setup = False` was set before the raise | Fixed. It is now set only after a condition is recorded. |

### Runs

- Fresh build of 4fdeee00, since the `.pyx` changed. The compiled module is newer than the source, and every log prints `underworld3.__file__` from the new build. The comparison is with the earlier builds of 8460d6d5 and its merge base with development.
- I read the diff 8460d6d5..4fdeee00 (the `.pyx` and the PR's test).
- **Probe, 41 cases**: the 31 from the first review, the 4 from my follow-up (natural `(0.0, 1.0)`, natural `(None, 1.0)`, and the two named-None cases), and 6 new ones. Each case ran serially, at np 2, and at np 3 with an empty rank (123 runs on the head, plus the 10 new and follow-up cases on 8460d6d5). Outcomes are per rank, with the SHA-1 of each rank's local solution.
  - All 19 cases that constrain at least one component and solve give hashes and SNES reasons identical to 8460d6d5 at np 1, 2 and 3. This holds for every case below:
    - essential `(None, 0.0)` without `components=`, still accepted;
    - `(0.0, None)`, `(None, x*y)`, the quantity case, `Matrix([oo, 0.0])` and 3-D `(None, None, 0.0)`;
    - the deprecated `components=[1]` and `components=1` forms;
    - natural `(0.0, 1.0)`, natural `(0.0, x*y)` and Poisson natural `1.0` (all reason 2);
    - Nitsche, the fault cases, Poisson, projection and AdvDiffusion.
  - Rotated free slip also matched at np 1 this time.
  - The all-free essential cases are refused as before, on every rank; only the wording is new.
  - These cases change from reason -4 to refused at the add, on every rank at np 1, 2 and 3: natural `(None, 1.0)`, natural `Matrix([oo, 1.0])`, natural `(None, None)` with `components=[1]`, essential `(None, None)` with `components=[1]`, and essential `(None, 0.0)` with `components=[0]`.
  - The message is the same on every rank in every refused case.
- **Refused call leaves the solver alone**: solve, then try a refused natural `(None, 1.0)`, then add a valid natural `(0.0, 1.0)`, then solve again.
  - `is_setup` stays True across the refusal and goes False on the valid add.
  - The second solve converges (reason 2). Its per-rank hashes equal those of a fresh solver given the same two conditions, at np 1, 2 and 3 with an empty rank.
  - On 8460d6d5 the same sequence accepted the `(None, 1.0)` condition and ended at reason -4.
- **Who used a natural condition with None**: I scanned for `add_natural_bc` or `add_condition(..., "neumann")` calls with `None` or `oo` in the value. The scan covered every `.py` file, notebook code cells, and `.md`, `.qmd` and `.pyx` text under tests, docs, scripts and src. It found no caller, so no existing test or example is newly refused. The internal callers in src pass expressions.
- **PR test**: 9 of 9 pass on the head. On 8460d6d5, 5 fail: the two natural cases (whose message changed), the two named-component cases, and the message and `is_setup` test. The other 4 pass.
- **Neighbours, serial**: the same 216 files plus the PR test, with 4 workers.

  - The per-test outcomes of the 216 files are identical to 8460d6d5: 1791 entries, 0 differences. The other 9 passes are the PR test.
- **Neighbours, parallel**: the same 66 files under `tests/parallel/`, one at a time at np 2 with `--with-mpi`. Every file has the same outcome as on 8460d6d5, with 0 failures and the same count of passed lines.

### New findings

1. High: none.

2. Medium: none.

3. Low: wording.
   - A natural condition with every component set but `components=[]` (`add_natural_bc((0.0, 0.0), "Left", components=[])`) passes the natural check and reaches the all-free check, which now always says "the essential condition on 'Left' ... e.g. (None, 0.0) to fix only the second". On 8460d6d5 it said "natural". It is still refused on every rank. Only the word, and the advice to use None, are wrong for a natural condition. Possible fixes: keep the kind in that message, or let the natural branch handle `components.size == 0` too.
   - The `add_natural_bc` docstring still says, under `components`, "Use `None` in `conds` for unconstrained components". A natural condition now refuses exactly that. The `add_condition` docstring describes `None` and `sympy.oo` entries for both condition kinds.
   - The natural message gives `(0.0, 1.0)` as its example on a one-component field too (Poisson `(None,)`).

   None of these affects a result.
````

### #1290 (merged a72603b6) np 8/12: no regression found at np 8/12

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**a72603b6 (the merge of #1290 into development), np 8 and 12: no regression found at np 8/12.** Every rank's answer at np 8 and np 12 is bit-identical to development just before the merge, in every layout tested. The widening floor added after the np 1 to 4 review (e9b4366b) fixes finding 1 of that review at these rank counts. Just-outside points now cost 21 to 29% less, and near-seam points 13 to 25% less.

## What was run

- Head: the merge commit a72603b6, whose PR side is ee9e7112 and includes the widening floor. Base: its first parent 2784f8ca. Between the two, only `_function.pyx` and the PR's parallel test differ.
- Both were built fresh. All 12 compiled modules are newer than the build start. `_CELL_WALL_SLAB_FLOOR` is 1e-12 on head and absent on base. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`, and each one names the intended build.
- np 8 ran on 8 dedicated cores and np 12 on 12, under the MPI supervisor. Other jobs on the machine were running on unpinned cores, two of them briefly on the np 12 cores.
- Layouts on gmsh meshes:
  - the default partition;
  - "emptyband": bands over np-1 ranks, so the last rank has no cells (confirmed from the cell counts per rank);
  - "rand8" and "rand12": cells assigned at random.
  The partition hook does not reach StructuredQuadBox, so the quad meshes ran on the default partition in every layout.
- Probes, on both builds:
  - the widening probe: tri, quad and tet boxes of size 1, and quad and tri boxes of size 1e-6, with points pushed 0 to 1,000 widenings, and 1e-14 to 1e-12 absolutely, outward from boundary vertices where two ranks meet;
  - the case probe on tri, quad, tet and sheared-tet boxes, including one rank with no cells that also asks for no points;
  - the sheared-box seam probe;
  - the hand-over count probe;
  - the cost probe.
- The PR's test file at np 8 and 12 on both builds, and at np 1. At np 8 and 12, the neighbouring global_evaluate parallel tests on head: test_0786, test_0960, test_1076 and test_1249.

## Results

Tolerance: 1e-12 absolute on O(1) fields, the probes' threshold. All rank arrays are in fact bit-identical, so the max diff is 0.

| np | layout | probe | rank arrays head = base | max diff | hangs | wall (longest run) |

| 8 | default, emptyband, rand8 | widening, 5 meshes | 1,680 of 1,680 | 0 | none | 7 s |
| 8 | default, emptyband, rand8, empty rank asking nothing | cases, 4 meshes | 2,784 of 2,784 | 0 | none | 35 s |
| 8 | default, emptyband, rand8 | seam | 96 of 96 | 0 | none | 4 s |
| 12 | default, emptyband, rand12 | widening, 5 meshes | 2,520 of 2,520 | 0 | none | 10 s |
| 12 | default, emptyband, rand12, empty rank asking nothing | cases, 4 meshes | 4,224 of 4,224 | 0 | none | 46 s |
| 12 | default, emptyband, rand12 | seam | 144 of 144 | 0 | none | 6 s |

- No point was evaluated on a rank whose cells do not contain it while another rank's cells do. This count is 0 on both builds in every case-probe run.
- The PR's test: 8 passed on every rank at np 1, 8 and 12, on head and on base.
- Neighbouring tests on head: 9, 1, 2 and 4 passed on every rank at np 8 and 12.
- All runs exited 0. None was stopped by the supervisor, and the longest run took 133 s.
````

### UNFILED 62 at tree 990a40b8: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# Bounded orphan-repair query in `barycentric_prolongation` (unfiled 62)

**Verdict at the patched head (tree 990a40b8, local, to be pushed as a PR): changes requested.**

The change itself is sound. Memory is now linear in the orphan count, the CSR is identical to development's wherever there are no exact distance ties, every orphan is claimed, and the transfer only steers multigrid, so the answer does not move. Two tests block a merge on green, though. On this machine both fail at the head and neither failure is a defect in the new builder:

- the new test_0893 hard-codes counts that depend on the platform;
- test_0885 now fails at every rank count, because an existing uninitialised-buffer read in the RBF kd-tree is no longer masked.

Both need fixing before the PR can go green.

## Method

- Base: development fbef72fa, using a clean existing install. Head: 0001 and 0002 applied with `git am` on fbef72fa. The tree is 990a40b8eb28, as expected. `git diff --stat` shows only `custom_mg.py` and the new test, so the head was laid over a copy of the base install as a .py overlay, with no rebuild. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`.
- Mutant: the head overlay with the widening removed. The loop's break condition was replaced by `True`, so k stays at 9.
- Fixtures: 61 coarse and fine clouds, built once and fed to all three builders. They comprise:
  - exact-tie integer grids scaled by 1/32, at offsets of 0, 1/4 and 1/2 cell, with n = 16, 24, 32 and 48 and the fine cloud in the left half (as in the author's probe_ties);
  - regular quad and simplex box meshes at h = 1/16, against a twice-finer vertex grid and against a half-cell-offset (cell-centre) coarse level;
  - the 40 random 2-D and 3-D clouds and the two forced-orphan grids from the author's probe;
  - two crowded clouds with more orphans than fine nodes (30 fine nodes, and 1 fine node).
- CSR comparison: indptr, indices and data, compared exactly. Where they differ, the comparison recovers each build's claims (the rows that differ from the `repair_orphans=False` build), then sorts each orphan into one of three cases: the same fine node, a different fine node at exactly the same distance, or a different distance.
- Reference check: each head result was also checked against a brute-force reference, in which each orphan in turn takes the nearest free fine node and ties go to the lower index.
- Runs were pinned to 12 cores. Parallel runs went through the MPI supervisor (silence 120 s) inside a `timeout`. Other jobs on the machine were running throughout. Logs kept by the reviewer.

## Results

### 1. tests/parallel test_0885 and test_1017_custom_mg_parallel_mpi at np 2, 3 and 4

| build | np 2 | np 3 | np 4 |
|---|---|---|---|
| base | 10 passed | 10 passed | 10 passed |
| head | 9 passed, 1 failed | 9 passed, 1 failed | 9 passed, 1 failed |

The failure is `test_0885::test_each_build_reports_its_own_statistics`, at its second call: `rbf_prolongation` on 4 coarse points raises "Cannot build a 6-point stencil: the kd-tree holds 4 point(s)". It also fails serially on the head (3 of 3 runs) and passes serially on the base (3 of 3). All of test_1017 passes on both. See finding 2 for the cause. It is not in the new code.

### 2. CSR identity, head against base

- **No ties: identical.** This covers:
  - all 40 random clouds;
  - the forced grids (n = 40 with 785 orphans; n = 60 with 1,768 orphans);
  - the quad and simplex mesh fixtures (314 orphans against the twice-finer grid; 0 against the cell-centre level);
  - the grids at offsets 0 and 1/4, at every n;
  - the crowded 30-node cloud (1,536 orphans, 30 claimed).

  The head matched the brute-force reference on all 60 fixtures that both builds completed.
- **The subduction-zone annulus fixture, serially, all calls identical.** At refinement 4 and chain 2 there are 5 calls, with orphans 0, 0, 0, 10 and 38. At refinement 5 and chain 2 there are 6 calls, with orphans 0, 0, 0, 0, 2 and 14. The solves agree: one outer iteration, CONVERGED_ATOL, the same peak memory.
- **Half-cell-offset grids with the fine cloud in the left half (exact four-way ties): they differ.**

  | n | orphans | entries differing | rows | same fine node | other node, equal distance | head farther | base farther |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 16 | 141 | 168 | 84 | 44 | 35 | 17 | 32 |
  | 24 | 321 | 396 | 198 | 90 | 62 | 53 | 83 |
  | 32 | 573 | 754 | 377 | 135 | 128 | 92 | 157 |
  | 48 | 1,295 | 1,720 | 860 | 292 | 268 | 208 | 384 |

  The difference is **not only tie order**. Once an early tie goes the other way, later orphans find a different set of free nodes, and some of them claim nodes at different distances (the last two columns). Each build is a valid greedy outcome. The head's follows its stated rule exactly (the reference check matched on every fixture). The base's depends on cKDTree's internal order. The author measured 184 to 788 entries at n = 16 to 32 on their laptop, and this machine gives 168 to 754 with the same probe, so development's tie order also depends on the platform.
- **Half-offset grids with full fine coverage (public builder, np 1): tie order only.** n = 32 has 144 orphans and 590 entries differing; n = 48 has 338 orphans and 1,373 entries differing. In both, every differing claim is at exactly the same distance (108 and 249 orphans) and none is farther.
- **np 4, the public default called per rank:** each rank's strip of the fine cloud against the whole coarse cloud, the pattern a parallel caller would use. Rank 0's strips differ on the two half-offset grids (352 and 872 entries, partly cascading). Every other rank, the zero-offset grid and the mesh case (around 860 orphans per rank) are identical.
- **The custom-FMG route at np 4 never runs the repair.** All 16 builder calls in check 5 at np 4 had `repair_orphans=False`, so head and base use the same code there.

### 3. test_0893 (the memory test)

| build | result |
|---|---|
| base | fails at the memory cap: peak 54.5 MB against 20 MB |
| head | fails: `assert 1768 == 1770` (orphans_repaired) |
| mutant | fails: `assert 1768 == 1770` |

On this machine the fixture gives 1,768 orphans, nnz 7,264 and 871 empty columns. The test hard-codes 1,770, 7,260 and 877. The head's peak here is 4.2 MB (base 54.5 MB, time 1.7 s against 3.5 s). The cause is that the coarse cloud is a regular 60 x 60 grid. Its Delaunay triangulation is not unique (every square has two valid diagonals), so qhull's choice, and with it the orphan set, varies between platforms (finding 1).

Does it bound the right thing? The tracemalloc cap does catch a k = orphans + 8 regression, with a margin of 2.7 times (54.5 MB against 20 MB), and the head is about 5 times under the cap. tracemalloc sees the query's numpy result arrays, which is where the quadratic term lives. It does not bound time, and it does not see scipy's internal C++ allocations. That is acceptable for this regression. The `orphans_repaired == 1770` assertion cannot catch a repair regression, because that statistic is recorded before the repair runs (finding 4).

### 4. Mutant (k fixed at 9, no widening)

What breaks:

- 46 of 61 fixtures differ from the head.
- On the forced n = 60 grid the mutant claims 148 fine nodes for 1,768 orphans and leaves 1,620 orphans with no claim (expected 0), with nnz 10,504 against 7,264. When all 9 candidates are taken, `argmax` returns a taken node and the earlier claim is overwritten.
- The "every orphan repaired" invariant fails on every crowded and tie fixture.

What does not break:

- All 90 existing serial custom-MG tests pass on the mutant (90 passed, 1 skipped, the same as head and base; only test_0893 fails, on all three builds).
- The parallel tests at np 2 give the same result as the head.
- `orphans_repaired` still reports 1,768.

So the only test that guards the widening is test_0893's nnz and empty-column assertions, and only on a platform where its hard-coded counts hold. That is a coverage gap. A platform-independent version of the test is described in finding 1. It derives the orphans from the `repair_orphans=False` build and checks every claim against the brute-force greedy reference. In the reviewer's scratch copy it passes on the head, fails on the base (54.5 MB) and fails on the mutant (claims differ).

### 5. Custom-FMG solve on an exact-tie fixture

The fixture is a Poisson problem, P2, CG with rtol 1e-10, through `set_custom_fmg`. The fine mesh is a regular simplex box at h = 1/16, and the coarse levels are at h = 1/4 and h = 1/32. The h = 1/32 level is finer than the fine mesh, which gives 1,762 orphans with exact ties on the serial reduced route.

| np | transfer | head its | base its | reason | max abs diff in u (max |u| = 1) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 208 entries differ (104 rows: 1 claim at equal distance, 103 at a different distance) | 11 | 12 | CONVERGED_RTOL both | 3.7e-11 |
| 4 | identical (repair off on every call) | 3 | 3 | CONVERGED_RTOL both | 0 (bitwise) |

At np 1 the difference is below the solver tolerance (rtol 1e-10). The np 4 and np 1 head solutions agree to 3.2e-11. The answer does not move.

### Other checks

- **Termination.** k doubles, capped at n_fine. The loop exits when k == n_fine. Before each orphan, `len(fixes) == n_fine` stops the repair, so at k == n_fine at least one candidate is free, and `argmax` never returns a taken node.
- **More orphans than fine nodes.** This is handled. The crowded 30-node case claims all 30 and leaves 1,506 unclaimed, as the reference expects. Development raised on n_fine = 0 and also on n_fine = 1 (IndexError, because `atleast_2d` turns a k = 1 result into the wrong shape). The head handles both.
- **'Claimed' semantics unchanged.** Orphans are taken in the same order, and each takes the first free candidate in distance order. The boolean `taken` array replaces the old set one for one.
- **Tie handling.** Widening while `d[a] == d[-1]` is the right guard against a k-truncated tie.
- **Cost.** With 21,929 orphans against 5,000 fine nodes in a corner, the head takes 11.7 s and 7.8 MB, against the base's 419 s and 1.76 GB. With 9,744 orphans against 2,000 nodes: 2.6 s and 3.3 MB against 73.6 s and 315 MB.

## Findings

1. High: test_0893's hard-coded counts depend on the platform, so the test fails on this machine at the head. It gets orphans 1,768, nnz 7,264 and 871 empty columns, where the author got 1,770, 7,260 and 877. The coarse cloud is a regular grid with a non-unique Delaunay triangulation. The memory cap itself is sound and catches development's builder (54.5 MB against 20 MB).

   Replace the hard counts with platform-independent checks: the orphans from the `repair_orphans=False` build, a check that each replaced row is a unit injection, and the claims compared with an inline brute-force greedy reference (nearest free node, lower index on ties). A scratch version of this test passes on the head and fails on both the base and the mutant. It is the only test found that catches the widening being removed (check 4). Jittering the coarse grid with a seeded perturbation would also make the triangulation unique, but the reference check is stronger.

2. High: `test_0885::test_each_build_reports_its_own_statistics` fails at the head at np 1, 2, 3 and 4, and passes on development. The cause is not in this patch. `KDTree.find_closest_n_points` (ckdtree.pyx) allocates its result buffers with `np.empty`. When nnn (6) exceeds the tree's point count (4), the unfilled slots hold whatever was last in that heap memory, and the `closest_n >= self.n` guard reads that stale data.

   On development the test passes only because the preceding barycentric call happens to leave small integers in the freed memory. The same RBF call on its own raises on development too. Pre-seeding freed 6-slot buffers with 0 makes it pass, and with 2^40 makes it raise, on both builds. The new builder allocates differently and unmasks the problem.

   The fix belongs in ckdtree.pyx: fill the index buffer with the `self.n` sentinel and the distances with inf before `knnSearch`, or use the count that nanoflann returns. It needs a rebuild and probably its own PR, merged first or alongside. Changing the test's 4-point coarse cloud would only hide a real bug. Until one of these lands, the PR cannot merge on green.

3. Medium: on exact ties, the PR text should not describe the difference as tie order alone. On the half-offset fixtures with the fine cloud in the left half, one tie going the other way changes which nodes later orphans find free. Then 17 to 208 orphans per fixture take a farther node under the head, and 32 to 384 a farther one under the base. Both are valid greedy outcomes, and the solve does not move (check 5). The entry counts also differ by platform (168 to 754 here, 184 to 788 on the author's laptop), because development's tie order came from cKDTree's internal ordering. Please say so in the body, and quote the counts as examples, not as fixed numbers.

4. Low: `stats["orphans_repaired"]` counts the orphans found, not the claims made. It is recorded before the repair and is unchanged even when the mutant leaves 1,620 orphans unclaimed. Either add a claimed count (for example `orphans_claimed`) and assert it equals min(orphans, n_fine), or drop the test assertion that relies on the found count.

5. Low: the commit message mentions the n_fine = 0 fix but not that development also raised on n_fine = 1 (IndexError), which the head fixes. A two-line test for both cases would pin this down.

6. Low: memory is now linear in the orphan count, but time is still a per-orphan Python loop, with widening queries of up to n_fine neighbours in crowded regions (11.7 s for 21,929 orphans against 5,000 fine nodes). That is fine for the serial routes. The parallel builders should keep `repair_orphans=False`, and the docstring of the public default could say that a per-rank caller should pass False.

7. Low (observation, not a request): on the tie fixture, the serial route with the repair on needed 11 to 12 iterations, while the parallel route, where orphans are dropped at install, needed 3. The routes differ in more than the repair, so this is only indicative. It is relevant to the separate proposal to drop the repair on the serial route.

## Not run

- A rebuild of the head. The overlay is valid because only .py files change.
- The author's A/B plugin over its 98 calls. The same 15 test files did pass on the head with the same results as the base (90 passed, 1 skipped).
- The subduction-zone annulus fixture in parallel on the head. The repair is off on the parallel builders, so that code path is unchanged.
- The mutant at np 3 and 4.
- Any run on macOS, where the author's counts came from.

Nothing was pushed or posted.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1312 r2 at bd3a4091: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**At bd3a4091 (local head, to be pushed): changes requested.**

This is the second round. The first review was at f9438460. All six fixes work as described, with the same result on every rank. Finding 1 of the first review (a constant such as `sympy.pi` getting past the refusal) and finding 2 (`add_basal_traction` with a quantity refused on the solver's own fields) are fixed. Findings 3 and 5 are fixed, and finding 4 is mostly fixed. The neighbour tests and the 26 docs examples behave as at f9438460.

Two of the new checks go further than intended, and one should be fixed before merge:

- A plain zero **natural** condition on a degC or degF field is now refused. That is the insulating boundary, `add_natural_bc(0.0, "Top")`, and development accepted it. The offset-unit argument ("0 is not 0 K") applies to the field's value, not to its flux (finding 1).
- On a degC field, the new dimension check for a lone expression fails with a raw pint error when the expression is itself in degC (finding 2).

Both are small changes to the new code.

## Earlier findings, then and now

| # | first review (f9438460) | now (bd3a4091) | run |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 High | `sympy.pi`, `sqrt(2)`, `E` accepted on fields with units, read in model units | **fixed**: refused alone and in tuples, essential and natural, on K, degC, km and m/s fields. Also `2E`, `2pi`, `pi/4+1`, `sin(1)`. `pi - pi` and `cos(pi/2)` are zero and pass. Fields without units, dimensionless fields and models without units are unchanged | p33 (761 cases), new probe section F1 |
| 2 Medium | `add_basal_traction([q 1 MPa, 0])` refused on the solver's fields | **fixed**: records -0.0315576, the same as on passed fields and the negative of `add_natural_bc((q 1 MPa, 0))`. Plain nonzero rows and `[pi, 0]` are still refused | p33b, F2 |
| 3 Medium | plain 0 on degC accepted as 0 K | **fixed for essential conditions**: plain 0, 0.0, -0.0, `Integer(0)`, `Float(0)`, `np.float64(0)` and `Matrix([0])` are refused on degC and degF. `uw.quantity(0, "degC")` records 0.2101 (273.15 K). K, degR, delta_degC, km and dimensionless fields still take a plain 0. **Over-applied to natural conditions** (new finding 1) | p33, F3, p_degc2 |
| 4 Low | lone expression not dimension-checked | **fixed for essential conditions**: a lone expression in km or mm/yr on a K field is refused, and K or degC on K or degC fields is accepted. Still open, as the first review said: a lone `uw.expression` holding a plain 0.5 is accepted as 0.5 in model units. Natural conditions and tuple entries are not checked (as on development) | F5 |
| 5 Low | lone value on a vector field set only x | **fixed**: refused for essential and natural, in every model (new finding 4). The message asks for one entry per component. The `components=` forms are unchanged | F4 |
| 6 Low | rule covers `add_condition` only; level set refuses its own `far_field` float | level set now takes a quantity: `far_field=uw.quantity(10, "km")` on a field in m records 1.0, and a wrong dimension is refused. A plain `far_field=1.0` on a field with units is still refused, and the message names neither `far_field` nor the level set. Nitsche and the rotated datum are as before; the update text now lists them | F6 |
| 7 Low | scaling-off record change undocumented | **documented** in the update text. The behaviour is unchanged: 1300 K records 1300.0, and `evaluate()` still reads 1.69e6 K in that mode | p33b toggle |
| 8 Low | without reference quantities, a lone expression of 5 km gives 5 but a quantity gives 5000 | unchanged (a scalar field is not affected by the lone-vector check) | p33 nounits |
| 9 Low | cosmetic | lone `sympy.oo` on a vector field is now refused by the lone-value check. Lone `np.float32`/`np.int64` still give "Unsupported BC conds". The natural message still prints `<the flux's units>` literally | F4, ADV |
| 10 Low | `test_1064` time limit; 0812/0817 under mpirun | see Runs | parallel list |

## The six fixes, by probe

All refusals are `ValueError`s. They name the condition, the boundary and the field, and, where relevant, the component and the field's units.

1. **Any constant.** Shown above. The message gives the number's value (3.14159) and suggests `uw.quantity(3.14159, "<field units>")`. `sympy.I` and `sympy.zoo` now raise a bare `TypeError: Cannot convert complex to float` from the check (finding 6).
2. **Basal traction.** `[q 1 MPa, 0]`, `[0, q 1 MPa]`, `[q 1 MPa, q -2 MPa]` and the tuple form record -0.0315576 / 0.0631152 on the solver's fields, on passed fields with units and on passed fields without them. On development the solver's fields gave -9.96e7. `[0.5, 0]`, `[3.0, 0]`, `[np.float64(0.5), 0]` and `[pi, 0]` are refused. Mixed `[q 1 MPa, 3.0]` is refused, but the message blames component 0 (the converted quantity, -0.0315576) rather than the plain 3.0 in component 1 (finding 5). Also accepted is `[q 5 km, 0]` (a length as a traction), which records -0.5 (finding 5). `[q, None]` and a pint traction fail as on development.
3. **Offset unit.** Shown above. Without a temperature reference, a plain 0.0 on degC is also refused, which is right (it would be 0 K there too). The message adds "Zero needs none, except on an offset unit such as degC, where 0 is not 0 K". That is accurate for essential conditions only.
4. **Lone value on several components.** Lone 0, 0.0, 0.5, `True`, `np.float64(0)`, a quantity, a `uw.expression`, `sin(y)`, `sympy.oo`, `Integer(0)` and `pi` are refused on the Stokes velocity: solver's fields, passed fields with and without units, essential and natural, and on a `Vector_Projection`. The message is "one value for the field 'V', which has 2 components. Give one entry per component, None for a free one, e.g. (value, None)." `Matrix([0, 0])`, `v.sym` and full tuples are accepted. `components=0` with a lone value and the full-tuple `components=` forms record as before. `components=1` with a lone value is still refused by the #1129 check. A one-entry tuple or list, `(0.0,)` or `[0.0]`, is still accepted on a 2-component field and sets x only (finding 4).
5. **Lone expression dimension.** Shown above. The check also refuses lone symbolic values that have no units or come from the mesh coordinates: `sin(y)` ("is in dimensionless"), `y` and `1 - y` ("is in kilometer") on K or degC fields. The same values as a 1-tuple are accepted (finding 3). On a degC field, a lone expression in degC (another degC field's `sym`) fails with `OffsetUnitCalculusError` (finding 2).
6. **Level set.** A quantity `far_field` on a field in m or km is converted and dimension-checked. A plain number on a field without units or dimensionless is accepted as before. A `far_field` without units on a level-set field without units is accepted unchanged (1.0, 0, `np.float64`, `Integer(1)`). A quantity `far_field` on a field without units is now non-dimensionalised (1 m becomes 1e-4 in a 10 km model), where f9438460 took `float()` of it (1.0). That is consistent with every other boundary value.

**Parallel.** The new parallel probe ran at np 1, at np 2 in horizontal bands (cells 121/121), and at np 3 with an empty rank (121/121/0, shell partition of the gmsh box). It covered constant refusals on the Stokes velocity, lone-value refusals (0.0, a quantity, an expression), a plain basal traction, plain 0 essential and natural on degC, `pi` and a lone 5 km expression on degC, and a plain level-set `far_field` on a field in m. Every rank raised the same message, and nothing was recorded. An allreduce right after reached every rank. The accepted values were the same on every rank: `(q 5 mm/yr, 0)` gives [0.5, 0.0]; a basal `[0, q 0.1 MPa]` gives [0.0, -0.0031558]; `q(1026.85 degC)` gives 1.0; `q(0 degC)` gives 0.2101; a `far_field` of 1 m gives 1e-4. The Stokes solve after the refusals gave a left-wall vx of 0.5 everywhere. The integral of v·v was 1.9156022e-20 at np 1 and 1.9155884e-20 at np 2 and np 3, the same digits as in the first review. The degC Poisson integral was 621.30349 at np 1 and 621.30352 at np 2 and np 3. Field data were read on every rank.

## Adversarial: legitimate inputs

- Plain 0 on K, km, dimensionless and unitless fields: accepted, alone and in tuples, essential and natural.
- `sympy.Integer/Float/Rational` and numpy scalars: refused on K and km (nonzero), accepted unchanged on dimensionless and unitless fields. Same as at f9438460.
- Quantities on degC and degF: `q(1026.85 degC)` and `q(1300 K)` give 1.0, `q(32 degF)` gives 0.2101, and an expression of `q(1026.85 degC)` gives 1.0.
- Mixing `pi` with a quantity: `pi * q(300 K)`, `q(300 K) * pi` and `pi * expression(300 K)` are accepted on K and degC fields and give 0.72498 (942.48 K), the same as `q(300π K)`. On a km field they are refused for dimension.
- A natural zero on a degC field: **refused** (finding 1). The workaround `q(0, "W/m**2")` is accepted (natural conditions are not dimension-checked, so any units pass).
- A lone other field's `sym` on a K or km field is accepted. On degC it fails with a raw pint error (finding 2).
- `(np.array(0.5),)`, a 0-d array in a tuple, still passes the refusal on a K field and records 0.5 in model units (finding 6). This was the same at f9438460.

## Runs

- **Build.** bd3a4091 changes a `.pyx` file, so it was built fresh in its own worktree. The extension is newer than the build start and holds the new names (`components_given`, `offset_unit`). The installed `level_set.py` and `systems/solvers.py` are identical to the commit. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`. f9438460 used the first review's build, and development is the first review's base build (7091a93c).
- **Probes, serial.** The first review's probes (p33 in two modes, p33b, p_bc in two modes, p_degc2, p_dim, p_toggle) were rerun on bd3a4091 and compared with their f9438460 logs. Every difference is one of the six fixes or one of the findings below. Apart from those, the only changes are the longer message text. The new round-2 probe (1,017 cases: the six fixes and the adversarial cases) and a lone-symbolic probe ran on all three builds.
- **Serial neighbours** (the first review's 214 files, at most 4 workers): 2014 passed, 25 skipped, 18 xfailed, 2 xpassed, no failures (70 min). At f9438460 it was 2007 passed, 19 xfailed, 1 xpassed. The 7 extra passes are the new `test_0774` tests. The new xpass is `test_0850::test_closure_complex_expression` (an xfail on `get_units` of MeshVariable symbols), which this PR does not touch.
- **Parallel neighbours** (the first review's 15 files, np 2, file by file): 13 files pass or skip as in the first review (0765, 0766, 0768, 0770, 0774, 0856, 0992, 1065, 1066, 1069 normal, 1086, 1103 pass; 1069 label-on-some-ranks and 1074 skip at np 2, as at f9438460). `test_1064_rotated_freeslip_parallel` failed once on a JIT compile that crashed under load (`mpicc` exit -10) and then hung until the supervisor stopped it. Run alone it gave 7 passed, 2 skipped in 111 s. In the first review it reached the time limit on both builds. At np 3 with an empty rank, 0774, 1069 label-on-some-ranks and 1074 pass.
- **Edited and units tests.** `test_0774` serially: 24 passed. The same file against f9438460's build fails exactly the 7 new tests, and against development the 9 tests that need this PR. Under mpirun at np 2 (0774, 0810, 0812, 0817, 0818, 1450): 45 passed, and the same two serial-by-nature tests fail as in the first review (`test_0812::test_poisson_check_bc_values`, `test_0817::test_poisson_scaling_improves_conditioning`).
- **First review's parallel probe** at np 1, 2 and 3 (empty rank): the same refusals on every rank. v·v is 1.9156022e-20 / 1.9155884e-20 / 1.9155884e-20, as before. Its plain 0 on the degC wall is now refused, as intended, so its degC solve uses one wall only.
- **Docs examples** (the first review's 26, 900 s cap): the exit status and last error line are the same as at f9438460 and on development for all 26. `Ex_Convection_Annulus_Recorded` and tutorials 13, 14 and 16 run to the end. The 97 recorded `add_condition` calls match f9438460's, with none refused. fault_friction made one more call before the cap. fault_friction and the Spiegelman benchmark reach the cap as before. AnnulusFS and Ex_viscousFingering are still missing from the docs tree.
- **Load.** 12 cores, with other jobs on the machine.

## New findings

1. Medium: a plain zero natural condition (zero flux) on a degC or degF field is refused.

   The offset-unit flag is set from the field's units and applied to essential and natural conditions alike. So `add_natural_bc(0.0, "Top")` on a degC temperature (an insulating wall) now fails. Development and f9438460 accepted it, and the `add_natural_bc` docstring shows this exact call. The message says "0 is not 0 K", which is not true of a flux. It also suggests `uw.quantity(0, <the flux's units>)`. The same refusal holds at np 2 and np 3. Applying `offset_unit` only when `c_type == 'dirichlet'` would fix it. A test with a zero flux on a degC field would cover it. This should be fixed before merge: it breaks a common, correct script that worked on development.

2. Medium: on a degC field, a lone degC expression fails with a raw pint error.

   The new lone dimension check computes `1 * uw.units(str(given))`. When the expression's units are degC (for example another degC field's `sym[0, 0]`, or `0.5 *` that), pint raises `OffsetUnitCalculusError: Ambiguous operation with offset unit`. A `uw.expression` of `q(1026.85 degC)` is fine, because it unwraps to kelvin. Using `uw.units(str(given)).dimensionality`, as the quantity check two screens up already does ("the unit itself, not 1 * unit"), would avoid it. This is not a regression against development, which refused every lone expression, but it is new code in this commit.

3. Low: the lone dimension check refuses unitless and coordinate expressions that the tuple form accepts.

   On a K or degC field, a lone `sin(y)` is refused ("is in dimensionless"), and so are `y` and `1 - y` ("is in kilometer", from the mesh coordinates). The 1-tuples `(sin(y),)` and `(1 - y,)` are accepted, as at f9438460. Development refused all lone expressions, so nothing that worked there breaks. Still, a lone value and its 1-tuple now mean different things. Either check both forms or neither. Skipping expressions whose units are dimensionless would treat `sin(y)` like a plain number. That may be wanted, but then the message should say so.

4. Low: the lone-value refusal applies to every model, and a one-entry tuple still sets x only.
   - The check does not depend on units. `add_essential_bc(0.0, "Left")` on a Stokes velocity is now refused in models without reference quantities and on fields without units too. Development took it as x = 0. That is the right outcome, but it is a behaviour change for every user, and the update text should say so.
   - `(0.0,)` and `[0.0]` on a 2-component field are still accepted, and constrain x only. That is the same silent outcome the fix removes for a lone value.

5. Low: the basal-traction pass-through skips the refusal for the whole row, and its mixed-row message points at the wrong entry.
   - A row whose entries are all quantities, zeros or symbolic goes through `add_natural_bc` inside the library's internal context. So `[q 5 km, 0]` (a length as a traction) records -0.5 with no complaint, where f9438460 refused it. `add_natural_bc` does not dimension-check natural conditions either, so this matches that path. But a dimension check on the quantity, against a stress, would be cheap here.
   - `[q 1 MPa, 3.0]` is refused, and the message names component 0, -0.0315576 (the converted quantity), and suggests `uw.quantity(-0.0315576, …)`. The offending entry is the 3.0 in component 1.

6. Low: other values get past the check, or fail in it.
   - `sympy.I` and `sympy.zoo` raise `TypeError: Cannot convert complex to float` from the new `float(val)` (`is_number` is true for them). Before, they were recorded.
   - A 0-d numpy array in a tuple, `(np.array(0.5),)`, is not seen as a plain number and is recorded as 0.5 in model units (as at f9438460).
   - A lone `uw.expression` holding a plain number is still accepted in model units (first review, finding 4).

7. Low: the level set's refusal of a plain `far_field` does not mention `far_field`. The message reads "essential (Dirichlet) condition on 'All_Boundaries': the plain number 1 on the field 'phi'…". It could say that it came from `LevelSetSolver(far_field=…)`, now that a quantity is the fix.

## The update text

It is accurate on the six fixes and on the runs it lists. Corrections:
- "Zero on an offset unit is refused": add "for essential conditions". As built, natural conditions are refused too (finding 1).
- "A lone value on a field of several components is refused": add that this applies in every model, with or without units, and that a one-entry tuple is still taken as x only (finding 4).
- "A lone expression on a field with units must have the field's dimension": unitless and coordinate expressions are refused by this check too, while their 1-tuples are not (finding 3).
- "Fields run unscaled in SI in that mode, so that is the value they read": the field data read 1300, but `evaluate()` reads 1.69e6 K in that mode, on both builds. Saying "the field data read" would be exact.
- "Outside the rule": the basal traction with quantities is now also outside it (finding 5). It could be listed.

## Not run

- The full test suite. The serial run on development. Parallel runs at np 4.
- 3-D fields and tensor-valued conditions. Pressure-field conditions through `add_condition`.
- Solves with the new refusals in time-dependent loops (conditions added after a first solve).
- The docs examples past their 900 s cap (fault_friction and the Spiegelman benchmark), and those that fail on every build before any condition is set.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### UNFILED 60 (patches 0001+0002 on fbef72fa): changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# UNFILED 60: the ribbon and sheet wall refusals give their numbers

**Verdict at the patched head (local, to be pushed as a PR): changes requested.** The messages are right, and every rank prints the same text. But test_0865's hard baseline (reach 0.112) does not hold on this machine: here the same fixture prints 0.107, so the new test fails on the branch. One small change to the test fixes it (finding 1). The two message wordings in finding 2 should also change.

## Method

- Applied the two patches with `git am` on development fbef72fa. The resulting commits are ab7f3bb9 and 3c0ce5c4. They differ from the author's 7fe58f8f and 22fb2614 only in committer metadata. The record gives no tree id, so I compared blobs: place_surface.py is 3c8b9bf6 after the first patch and 16faacda after the second, and the tests are 9d49b934 and 4f7e7288. All four match the patches' index lines.
- The change touches only .py files (place_surface.py and two new tests). I laid the patched place_surface.py over a copy of a clean development install (fbef72fa), so no rebuild was needed. Base runs used the clean install itself. Every log records `underworld3.__file__`, and it shows the overlay for head and the clean install for base.
- Environment: gmsh 4.15.2, PETSc 3.25.3, MPICH. Every parallel run went through the MPI supervisor (`--silence 120`) inside an outer timeout, pinned to 8 cores and sharing the machine with other jobs.

## Results

### 1. Rank identity

I ran the test_0865 fixture (ribbon) and the test_0868 fixture (sheet) under mpirun -n 2 and -n 3. Each rank printed its own full `str(err)` and rank 0 compared the gathered texts.

| fixture | np 2 | np 3 |
|---|---|---|
| ribbon (test_0865) | identical on both ranks, exit 0 | identical on all three ranks, exit 0 |
| sheet (test_0868) | identical on both ranks, exit 0 | identical on all three ranks, exit 0 |

Nothing hung. This matches the code: both refusals are built inside the surgery rank's try block, and the failure string is all-gathered before anyone raises (place_surface.py, the `failures = comm.allgather(failure)` sites after the ribbon fill and the sheet carve). The helpers do no collectives.

### 2. The new tests, development against branch

| test | development | branch |
|---|---|---|
| test_0865 (ribbon) | fails: the message has no numbers (as intended) | **fails**: `assert (0.107, 0.0335) == (0.112, 0.0335)` |
| test_0868 (sheet) | fails: the message has no numbers (as intended) | passes |

On this machine the ribbon fixture prints a reach of 0.107 and clearance x h = 0.0343. The author's record has 0.112 and 0.0348. The wall distance (0.0335) and the touched vertex (0.1467, 0.9892) agree. I get the same numbers at np 1, 2 and 3, so the difference comes from the machine, not the partition. The likeliest cause is a small difference in the gmsh annulus mesh (h at the wall vertex differs by about 1.5 %). The author built on macOS, and this machine runs Linux with gmsh 4.15.2.

### 3. Placement tests and the drivers' retry

Serial, head against base, with the same test lists:

| group | head | base |
|---|---|---|
| 0853, 0865, 0868, 1177 | 25 passed, 1 failed (0865, see 2), 1 skipped | 24 passed, 2 failed (0865, 0868: new tests), 1 skipped |
| 0854, 0856, 1091 | 19 passed | 19 passed |
| 0855, 1097 (band partition), 1022 | 33 passed | 33 passed |
| 1174, 0860, 0862 | 38 passed | 38 passed |

Parallel: ptest_0855 (thin volume) at np 2 passed 8 on both head and base. ptest_0854 (sheet) at np 2 passed 3 on head.

Message matching:
- The campaign drivers' retry (annulus_faults.py and the #893 probe) matches the substring `"domain wall"`. Both new messages keep "reached the domain wall", so the retry still fires.
- `add_fault` in fault_split.py re-raises on `"wall" in str(err)`, and that still holds.
- test_1174's `WALLS` regex (`.*domain wall.*`) passes with the longer sheet message.
- test_0855's two `match="domain wall"` checks pass.

### 4. Following the suggested move

**Ribbon** (test_0865 fixture, clearance 0.7):

| run | top | width | outcome | message's advice |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fixture | 0.9 | 0.1 | refused, outer wall | move about 0.073, or narrow the band |
| move as told | 0.827 | 0.1 | refused, now at the **inner** wall (-0.049, 0.498) | move about 0.068 |
| | 0.85, 0.82 | 0.1 | refused, inner wall | |
| narrow as told | 0.9 | 0.05 | refused, outer wall; the lever flips to clearance | move about 0.044 |
| then move | 0.856 | 0.05 | **placed** | |

Neither move alone places this fixture: at width 0.1 the band is too long for the shell in either direction. Doing both, narrowing as the first message says and then moving by the second message's amount, places it. The message correctly names the inner wall once that is the one reached. But the message itself never says its amount is only a guide; that caveat is in the author's record only (finding 2).

**Sheet** (test_0868 fixture):

| start z | advice | z after the move | outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.80 | 0.0605 | 0.7395 | placed (as the record says) |
| 0.87 | 0.0968 | 0.773 | **refused**: the reach grew from 0.227 to 0.253, next advice 0.0257 |
| 0.773 | 0.0257 | 0.747 | placed |

The record's "conservative" claim holds from 0.80, but not from 0.87, the test's own fixture. There a single move falls short and a second message is needed (finding 2).

### 5. Adversarial read of the diff

- **The numbers are the ones the check used.** In 2-D they are `d_skin` (skin distance), `h_vertex`, `clearance` and `width`, the same arrays and parameters that set `reach_v = max(clearance*h, 0.6*width)` and the ring. In 3-D, `err.drop` is a copy of the cavity at the moment of the refusal, after any growth, and `h_cell` and `0.6*h_cell` are the centroid rule's terms. Units are the mesh's coordinate units, the same as clearance x h and the width.
- **The message is the same on every rank.** It is formatted on the surgery rank only and all-gathered as a string (see 1). No reduction is missing.
- **Could building the message raise?** In 2-D, no: `hit` is non-empty by the guard, so `on_wall` is non-empty. In 3-D, `walls = on_wall & ~on_open` feeds `.min()`, which raises ValueError on an empty array. That ValueError would replace the refusal and drop "domain wall", so the drivers would not retry (finding 3).

## Findings

1. High: test_0865's exact baseline `(reach, wall) == (0.112, 0.0335)` fails on this machine (0.107 here, the same at np 1, 2 and 3). The reach depends on the gmsh mesh to the third figure. test_0868 happens to pass here, but it rests on the same kind of baseline. Its touched vertex and carve terms already differ between np 1 and np 2 (finding 4), so its exact reach is only one mesh change from failing too. Suggested fix for both tests: keep the regex parse, then assert what the fixture means rather than printed digits:
   - `reach > wall`;
   - the wall distance within a band, for example 0.03-0.04 for the ribbon and 0.12-0.14 for the sheet;
   - for the ribbon, the reach at least 0.6 x width;
   - the lever text ("narrow the band" or "refine the mesh near it").

   If an exact figure is wanted, assert only the wall distance, which is set by the fixture's geometry.
2. Medium: the suggested move reads as a single fix, but on both fixtures one move is not enough. For the ribbon, the move pushes the band into the inner wall. For the sheet from z 0.87, the reach grows on the new cells and a second move is needed. The record and PR body call it a guide that errs on the safe side; the message does not. Suggested wording: "move it at least about X further from the wall (a guide: the cavity is re-cut at the new position)". For the ribbon's "narrow the band", also give the width at which 0.6 x width drops to the clearance x h term, as `clearance*h/0.6`. Change the PR body's "errs on the safe side" to say it can take two moves.
3. Low:
   - (a) In `_sheet_wall_refusal`, guard `walls` being empty, and the case where the touched vertex is outside the bounded distance's cutoff (for example a touched vertex that is an outcrop-deletable vertex), so the refusal is never lost to a ValueError or a sentinel distance. For example, fall back to the original `err` text plus whatever numbers are available.
   - (b) The touched vertex, and so the two carve terms, depend on face order, which depends on the partition. At np 1 the sheet message names (0.3997, 0.654, 1) with 0.0861 and 0.113; at np 2 and 3 it names (0.2989, 0.4808, 1) with 0.0874 and 0.111. The reach, the wall distance and the advice are the same, so the outcome is the same; this is noted only so that no test pins those terms.
   - (c) "Nearest wall vertex" is taken over all walls, while the move is "from the wall" the cavity touched. Where two walls are close, these can be different walls.
   - (d) The figures are in mesh coordinate units with no unit named. In a scaled model they are nondimensional.

## Not run

- test_1097_place_sheet_partitioned_addon_parallel at np 2 and 3. It went silent for more than 120 s (in solves and NVB refinement, before or after the placement, not in the changed code), and the supervisor stopped it. Base did the same at np 3, so this is the rule's silence limit, not this change.
- test_1097_outcropping_ribbon_partitioned_parallel skips below np 4, and I did not run it at np 4.
- The campaign drivers themselves were not run. Their retry match was checked by reading the code.
- test_0865 and test_0868 under pytest at np greater than 1. They are serial tests, and the fixtures' messages were compared across ranks directly (check 1).

Logs kept by the reviewer.
````

### UNFILED 65 at e720ccdb: ready to merge on green from this review

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# kd-tree: a slot with no neighbour holds the index n and distance inf (UNFILED 65)

**Verdict at e720ccdb (local, to be pushed as a PR; tests in c4b87c3d, base development fbef72fa): ready to merge on green from this review.**

The fix does what it says. Every slot nanoflann does not fill now holds the index `n` and the distance `inf`, and every path that used to read uninitialised memory now gives the right answer or a clear refusal. On development every reproduction failed, in all 24 parallel proxy runs and in 11 of the 12 new test cases, 3 runs out of 3. On this branch every reproduction is exact against a serial reference. One gap is left, and it is not a regression: `evaluate()` still asks for an explicit `dim + 1` stencil, so it refuses where `rbf_interpolate` now answers (finding 1). It can be fixed in this PR or a follow-up.

**Stacking order.** This change goes first. The UNFILED 62 change goes on top of it, then register row 26. 62's test_0885 is green only with this change applied.

## Method

- **Builds.**
  - Head: e720ccdb, built from a clean worktree.
  - The stack: fbef72fa with e720ccdb merged (a fast-forward), then the five row-26 patches applied with `git am`.
  - The first build attempt ran twice at once in the same worktrees. The build directories from that attempt were set aside, every `.pyx` was touched, and both trees were rebuilt cleanly, one build each.
  - Base: a clean development install at fbef72fa.
  - Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`. One serial base run that showed the head's path was discarded and rerun.
- **Placement.** Runs were pinned to 8 cores. Parallel runs went through the MPI supervisor (silence 120 s) inside a `timeout`. Other jobs on the machine were running throughout. Logs kept by the reviewer.
- **Freed memory.** Every probe seeds freed memory with 2^40 before the call, as the new test does.

## Results

### 1. tests/test_0103_kdtree_more_neighbours_than_points.py, serial, 3 runs each

| build | run 1 | run 2 | run 3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| development | 11 failed, 1 passed | 11 failed, 1 passed | 11 failed, 1 passed |
| head | 12 passed | 12 passed | 12 passed |

On development, the only case that passes is the submesh transfer. In parallel, the file gives 10 passed and 1 skipped on one rank at np 2 and 3. On the other ranks the two-cell case fails (finding 2).

### 2. IndexSwarmVariable nodal proxy on a rank holding 2-4 particles, or fewer than 5 proxy nodes

- **update_type=1.** A simplex box at cellSize 0.1. K = 2, 3 or 4 particles, with materials alternating 0, 1, 0, 1, are all placed on the last rank, so the other ranks are starved and keep their proxy. npoints is 5.
- **update_type=0.** A 1 x NX quad column under a band partition (the partition hook), one cell per rank, so each rank holds 4 proxy nodes. The material is 1 below mid-height.
- **The reference.** The same particles in a serial run, compared at the nodes held only by the populated rank. For update_type=1 there is also an independent inverse-distance average over all K particles.

| case | np | development (3 runs each) | head: max abs difference from serial |
|---|---|---|---|
| update_type=1, K = 2 | 2, 3 | 6 of 6 runs: `IndexError` on the sparse rank. Indices included 135216862245376, 4415347, 7305798900593028717 and 9218868437227405311. The job then aborts on mismatched collectives (MPI_Abort 63, or truncated-message errors) | 0 (independent reference: 0) |
| update_type=1, K = 3 | 2, 3 | 6 of 6: `IndexError` (index 23, 4415338, ...), then abort | 0 (reference: 1.1e-16) |
| update_type=1, K = 4 | 2, 3 | 6 of 6: `IndexError`, then abort | 0 (reference: 2.2e-16) |
| update_type=0, 4 proxy nodes per rank | 2, 3 | 6 of 6: `IndexError` index 9218868437227405311 | 0 |

On the head, every populated rank's level sets lie in [0, 1] and sum to 1 within 2.2e-16. Starved ranks keep their proxy and warn, as designed. Development never returned a silently wrong material in these 24 runs. It raised every time, and the run died.

### 3. Each changed docstring states the default clamp

| function | docstring line |
|---|---|
| `KDTree.find_closest_n_points` | "the slots beyond the points it holds carry the index ``self.n`` (one past the last valid index) and the squared distance ``inf``" (this function has no default; it states the sentinel) |
| `KDTree.query` | "If the tree holds fewer than ``k`` points, the slots beyond them carry the index ``self.n`` and the distance ``inf``" |
| `KDTree.rbf_interpolator_local` | "The default never exceeds the number of points the tree holds; a larger ``nnn`` given explicitly is refused." |
| `KDTree.interpolation_matrix` | "or every point the tree holds if that is fewer; a larger ``nnn`` given explicitly is refused." |
| `KDTree._resolve_nnn` | "The default is the scheme's size or every point the tree holds, whichever is fewer" |
| `KDTree._refuse_short_stencil` (new) | "Refuse a stencil the tree cannot fill" |
| `Mesh._idw_transfer_to_var` | "the 1/d-weighted average of its dim+1 nearest old DOFs (all of them, on a rank that held fewer)" |
| `MeshVariable.rbf_interpolate` | "default: 4 for 3D, 3 for 2D, or every DOF this rank holds if that is fewer). A larger value given explicitly is refused." |
| `IndexSwarmVariable` (class) | "``npoints`` ... A rank holding fewer particles, or fewer proxy nodes, than ``npoints`` uses all of them." |

There are three exceptions, all Low (finding 4):

- `_apply_monotone_limit`: only a comment was added. The docstring still says "the ``mesh.dim + 1`` nearest source DOFs".
- `old_rbf_interpolator_local_from_kdtree` and `_to_kdtree`: their `nnn=4` default is a parameter value, so it is refused rather than clamped. Nothing in `src` calls them.
- The `IndexSwarmVariable._update` branches: comments only.

### 4. test_0885 and the neighbour tests

**test_0885 on the head:**
- np 1: 1 passed, 3 skipped.
- np 2, 3 and 4: 4 passed on every rank.

This is the test that failed in the UNFILED 62 round-1 review.

**Serial neighbour tests.** These are 28 files that use `find_closest_n_points`, `query(k)`, `rbf_interpolate`, `interpolation_matrix`, `IndexSwarmVariable` or `KDTree`, with test_0103 excluded. They were run with xdist, 4 workers.

| build | result |
|---|---|
| head | 331 passed, 3 skipped |
| development | 330 passed, 3 skipped, 1 failed |

The development failure is `test_0102::test_stencil_larger_than_the_cloud_reports_what_went_wrong`, with "DID NOT RAISE". That is the same bug: on development the existing guard catches a 20-point stencil on a 5-point tree only when stale memory happens to hold a large value.

**Parallel neighbour tests at np 2:** test_0682, 0767, 0756, 0775 and 1087. Both builds give 34 passed and 6 skipped on each rank.

**RBF prolongation.** A Poisson problem, P1, rtol 1e-10, `set_custom_fmg(builder="rbf")` over coarse levels of 1 x 1 (4 nodes) and 4 x 4 quads.

| build | np 1 | np 2 | np 3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| development | `CollectiveBuildError` ("Cannot build a 6-point stencil: the kd-tree holds 4 point(s)"), 3 runs of 3 | the same, raised on every rank | the same, on every rank |
| head | 4 its | 4 its | 5 its |

On the head, the np 2 and np 3 solutions match np 1 within 2.1e-13 (max \|T\| 0.5685).

**Degree-0 `rbf_interpolate` with the default stencil.** A 1 x NX column of quads under the band partition, C = the cell-centre height, evaluated at each rank's own centroids.

| layout | development | head |
|---|---|---|
| np 1, 2 cells | not run | exact (1e-12) |
| np 2, 1 + 1 cells | refused on both ranks | exact (0) on every rank |
| np 2, 2 + 1 cells | rank 0 exact, rank 1 refused | exact (0) on every rank |
| np 2, 3 + 2 cells | rank 0 exact, rank 1 refused | exact (0) on every rank |
| np 3, 1 + 1 + 1 cells | refused on every rank | exact (0) on every rank |
| np 3, 1 + 1 + 2 cells | ranks 0 and 1 refused, rank 2 exact | exact (0) on every rank |

On development the refusal is rank-dependent.

### 5. Adversarial read: are the new raises collective-safe?

- **`_refuse_short_stencil` on the custom-MG builders.** The parallel builders reach it only through `_agreed_node_transfer`, which turns any rank's exception into a `CollectiveBuildError` on every rank. This was checked at np 2 and 3 on development, where the old guard fired on one rank and every rank raised. On the head, the default clamp means it does not fire at all. This path is safe.
- **The proxy update, the submesh transfer and the monotone limit.** No new raise; each clamps k.
  - The submesh transfer keeps `max(tree.n, 1)`, so a rank that held no old DOFs still raises `IndexError` on its own. That is development's behaviour, kept deliberately and stated in the comment.
  - The monotone limit returns early on an empty tree.
- **`MeshVariable.rbf_interpolate` with an explicit `nnn`, and `rbf_evaluate`.** These are rank-local refusals inside `evaluate()`, which is collective because it calls `update_lvec` first. Development refused here too, at random. The head refuses every time on the ranks that hold too few DOFs, so the hazard is unchanged, not new. It is reached without the user passing any `nnn` (finding 1).

## Findings

1. Medium: `uw.function.evaluate` still refuses a degree-0 variable on a rank holding `dim` or fewer cells, and the refusal is rank-dependent. `rbf_evaluate` (`_function.pyx`, line 2047) calls `parent.rbf_interpolate(coords, nnn=mesh.dim+1)` with an explicit stencil, so it bypasses the new clamp. That path serves `evaluate(..., rbf=True)`, the exterior-point rung of every `evaluate()`, and the `monotone="pick"` re-evaluation through `evalf=True`.

   Measured on the head:
   - Serial, a 2-cell mesh: `evaluate` at exterior points raises "Cannot build a 3-point stencil: the kd-tree holds 2 point(s) ... Pass nnn at most 2". The user passed no `nnn`.
   - np 2 with 3 + 2 cells: rank 0 returns values and rank 1 raises. Inside a collective call, that run aborts or hangs.

   The PR body lists "also `evaluate`'s fallback path" as fixed. That holds for the unlocated-point rung (line 1859, which uses the default), not for the exterior rung. Dropping `nnn=mesh.dim+1` there gives the same `dim + 1` stencil with the clamp. It is a one-line change in a file this PR already rebuilds. Otherwise, narrow the claim and file it separately.

2. Low: `test_a_cell_variable_on_two_cells_interpolates_with_its_default_stencil` fails at np 2 on one rank and at np 3 on two ranks. The two-cell mesh lands on one rank, and a rank with no cells returns zeros by design (#405), so it reads 0 where 5 is expected. The body reports 10 passed and 1 skipped on both ranks at np 2. Here the partition puts both cells on one rank. Skip it at `uw.mpi.size > 1`, as the proxy case does, or compare only on ranks that hold cells.

3. Low: with `order=1` and a tree of fewer than `dim + 2` points, the default stencil clamps to `n` and is then refused by `linear_exact_weights`: "needs at least dim + 2 = 4 neighbours, got nnn = 3". With one point it is refused by the `nnn=1` guard. Both are clear refusals, but each names an `nnn` the caller never passed. A sentence in the `_resolve_nnn` docstring would cover it ("order=1 on a tree of fewer than dim + 2 points is refused").

4. Low: three docstrings do not state the rule.
   - `_apply_monotone_limit` still says "the ``mesh.dim + 1`` nearest source DOFs"; the clamp is only in a comment.
   - `old_rbf_interpolator_local_from_kdtree` and `old_rbf_interpolator_local_to_kdtree` default to `nnn=4`, which is refused rather than clamped on a smaller tree, against the PR's rule that "defaults take at most the points held". They have no callers in `src`. Either say so in their docstrings, or make the default `None` and route it through `_resolve_nnn`.

## Not run

- Above np 3 for the proxy and RBF probes; test_0885 was run up to np 4.
- The full suite, `level_3`, and macOS.
- The submesh transfer and the monotone limit in parallel. They were covered serially only, by the new test and by test_0760 and test_0768.
- A run where development silently returned a wrong material. In 24 parallel runs and 33 failing serial cases it always raised.

Nothing was pushed or posted.
````
