### Row 26 at cff9a7eb (on 65 + 62): changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# Serial custom-FMG: drop orphan coarse DOFs, do not repair them (register row 26)

**Verdict at cff9a7eb (local, to be pushed): changes requested.**

The change is right. On the serial route the graded fixture's velocity count falls from 13 to 4, which now matches the 4 the parallel route gives at np 2, 3 and 4. The parallel route is untouched: bitwise the same solution as development at np 4. A mutant that restores the repair fails test_0894. Three spot-checked rows of the iteration table reproduce exactly.

One thing blocks merging on green. **test_0894's guard fails on this machine on every build**, because its |v| baseline depends on the platform's mesh (finding 1).

**Stacking order.** UNFILED 65 (the kd-tree fix) goes first, then UNFILED 62, then this change. The local ids, all on fbef72fa:

| step | id |
|---|---|
| UNFILED 65 | e720ccdb |
| 0001 | 453fff79 |
| 0002 | 0dc93ed5 |
| 0003, UNFILED 62 | 2d3b4850 |
| 0004 (the original a5b1b623) | d1969910 |
| 0005, row 26 (the original c0ef3729) | cff9a7eb |

The row-26 change is 0004 and 0005. It touches only `custom_mg._reduced_transfer` and the new test_0894.

## Method

- **Builds.** The stack is a clean build at cff9a7eb. Base is a clean development install at fbef72fa.
- **Overlays.** "62 only" is `custom_mg.py` at 2d3b4850 laid over the UNFILED 65 build. The mutant is `custom_mg.py` at cff9a7eb with `_reduced_transfer` set back to `builder(coarse_coords, fine_coords)`, which restores the repair on the serial route, laid over the stack build.
- **test_0894's fixtures at any rank count.** A probe calls the test's own `_solve` and `_uniform_tail`. It reports `ksp_its`, `vel_its_last` and `pres_its_last`, and |v| over the gathered velocity nodes with duplicates removed, which is the serial test's `np.linalg.norm(v.array)`.
- **Spot check.** The author's recorder plugin was run on three tests from the iteration table, on the stack and on development.
- **Placement.** Runs were pinned to 8 cores. Parallel runs went through the MPI supervisor (silence 120 s) inside a `timeout`. Other jobs on the machine were running throughout. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`. Logs kept by the reviewer. The platform is gmsh 4.15.2 and scipy 1.16.3.

## Results

### 1. test_0894's fixtures, serial against parallel

Each cell gives outer/velocity/pressure iterations, then |v|.

| fixture | build | np 1 | np 2 | np 3 | np 4 |

| graded (3,245 orphans) | development | 1/13/9, 787.2454327417 | 1/4/12, 787.2454327604 | 1/4/11, 787.2454327839 | 1/4/12, 787.2454327721 |

| guard | stack | 1/3/9, 817.0121646338 | 1/4/12 | 1/4/12 | 1/4/12 |

- **Velocity counts.** The serial count now matches parallel (4 at every np). Pressure counts still differ by route, 9 serial against 11-12 parallel. They differ on the no-orphan guard too, so that difference comes from elsewhere, not from the repair.
- **Solutions.** Over np 1-4 they agree within 9.7e-10 (relative to max |v|) on the graded fixture and 6.4e-10 on the guard, with the solver at 1e-8. The stack's serial graded solution differs from development's by 3.6e-10.
- **The parallel route is unchanged.** The stack and development give bitwise identical velocity arrays at np 4 on both fixtures.
- **Not 3.** The body's graded figure is 3 velocity iterations. This machine gives 4. That is inside the test's bound (≤ 4), but at its edge (finding 2).

### 2. Custom-MG parallel tests at np 2, 3 and 4

### 5. Patch 0005 (bounded guard counts)

The guard now asserts outer ≤ 2, velocity ≤ 4 and pressure ≤ 10, against 1/3/9 measured. That is the right form under the testing guide's round-off rule. But it keeps a hard |v| baseline, 816.99052405 at rtol 1e-7, and that is what fails here.

### Serial custom-MG suite (17 files)

| build | result |

## Findings

1. High: test_0894's guard baseline depends on the platform and fails here on every build.
   - **The failure.** `test_no_orphans_no_change` asserts |v| = 816.99052405 to rtol 1e-7. This machine gives 817.0121646338 (relative difference 2.6e-5) on development, 62 only, the stack and the mutant alike. The cause is the gmsh-generated annulus (28,362 velocity nodes here), not the solver: the np 1-4 solutions agree within 6.4e-10. It is the same kind of failure as round 1's test_0893.
   - **A narrow pass.** The graded case's baseline, 787.24535978, passes only narrowly: 787.2454327516 here, a relative difference of 9.3e-8 against an rtol of 1e-7.
   - **The fix.** Replace the absolute norms with comparisons the platform cannot move. For example, compare the solve with the repair against the solve without it, on the same mesh in the same test (the 3.6e-10 difference is in the body), or use an analytic or mesh-independent functional at a tolerance set by the discretisation.

2. Medium: the graded fixture's velocity count is 4 here, at the test's bound (≤ 4), against the 3 in the body and the table. Iterations are reproducible only to round-off (UNFILED 64). A bound with no margin will flake on some platform. Use ≤ 6, which still separates 13 from 3-4, and say in the body that the count is 3 on one machine and 4 on another.

3. Low: the body says this change "carries [UNFILED 62] as its first two commits". It is now three (0001-0003). It should also say that UNFILED 65 merges first, since test_0885 depends on it.

4. Low: commit 0004's message names the #893 fixture by an internal session code. It should describe the fixture instead, for example "the #893 placed-ladder hierarchy (the subduction-zone annulus fixture)".

## Not run

- The author's full 51-file recorder comparison. Three rows plus the changed row were spot-checked.
- The 3-D box and split-ladder fixtures from the body.
- The graded fixture under a non-default partition.
- test_0894 under `mpirun`. The test itself reads `v.array` locally, so it is serial by design; the probe covers np 2-4.
- macOS.

Nothing was pushed or posted.

# UNFILED 60, round 2: the ribbon and sheet wall refusals give their numbers

**Verdict at the patched head (0a9d08fc, local): changes requested, for text only.** The code and tests are right on this machine. Both new tests pass on the branch and fail on development. Every message has the new wording, and at np 2 every rank prints the same text. One claim in the PR body and in the third commit's message needs correcting (finding 2). No code change is needed.

## Method

- Applied patches 0001-0003 with `git am` on development fbef72fa. The commit ids differ from the author's only in committer metadata. The blobs match the patches' index lines: place_surface.py 55b62836, test_0865 d756cda1, test_0868 89707728. So the tip matches 0a9d08fc in content.
- Only .py files change. I laid the patched place_surface.py over a copy of a clean development install (fbef72fa), and ran the new tests from the patched tree. Base runs used the clean install. Every log records `underworld3.__file__`.
- gmsh 4.15.2, PETSc 3.25.3, MPICH. Runs were pinned to 4 cores while other jobs used the rest of the machine. Parallel runs went through the MPI supervisor (`--silence 120`) inside an outer timeout.

## Round 1 findings, then and now

| round 1 | now |
|---|---|
| 1. High: test_0865's exact baseline (0.112) failed here (0.107) | **Fixed.** Both tests now assert relations: reach > wall, the wall distance in a band (0.02-0.05 for the ribbon, 0.13 ± 1e-3 for the sheet), reach ≥ the largest carve term, and the lever text. They pass here (below). |
| 2. Medium: the move read as a single fix; "errs on the safe side" | **Fixed in the messages.** Both now say "move it at least about X further from the wall (a guide: the cavity is re-cut at the new position)". "Errs on the safe side" is gone from the PR body. The ribbon's lever is now stated as a fact: "narrow the band: its width sets the carve down to a width of about 0.0572, where clearance x h takes over". Here clearance x h prints 0.0343, and 0.0343/0.6 = 0.0572, so the figure is right (the author's machine gives 0.058 from 0.0348). |
| 3a. Low: empty wall set or sentinel distance in the sheet refusal | **Fixed.** I called `_sheet_wall_refusal` directly in three cases: every wall vertex on the outcrop; the only wall vertex beyond the 2 x reach cutoff; and an ordinary case. None raised ValueError and none printed "nan". All three kept "domain wall". The first two read "the mesh has no wall vertex off the outcrop" and "no wall vertex off the outcrop is within 0.26 of it", and they drop the move. In 2-D the empty case cannot arise, because both call sites raise only when the ring touches a wall vertex. |
| 3b. Low: partition-dependent terms must not be pinned | **Fixed.** The touched vertex and the carve terms are only used through reach ≥ max(terms). That relation holds at np 1 and np 2 here (sheet: 0.227 ≥ 0.113 and 0.227 ≥ 0.111). |
| 3c. Low: nearest wall vs touched wall | **Addressed by wording.** The message names the touched vertex and "the nearest wall vertex" separately. The move is still reach minus the nearest wall over all walls. See finding 3. |
| 3d. Low: units | **Fixed.** Both messages say "In mesh units". |

## Runs

| | development | branch |
|---|---|---|
| test_0865 (ribbon) | fails twice: the message has no numbers | passes twice |
| test_0868 (sheet) | fails twice: the message has no numbers | passes twice |

| path | steps | placement |

The sheet path matches the PR body exactly. The ribbon figures differ from the author's (0.821/0.743, 0.058/0.864), in line with the gmsh difference already noted. But see finding 2 for the ribbon's move-only path.

**3. Rank identity at np 2** (MPI supervisor): the test_0865 ribbon fixture and the test_0868 sheet fixture. Each rank prints its full `str(err)` and rank 0 compares them. Both runs gave identical text on both ranks, with the new wording ("a guide", "mesh units"), and exited 0 with no hang.

## New findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium: the PR body and the third commit's message say that following the messages takes the ribbon "from 0.9 to 0.821 to 0.743". The second step of that path does not follow the message. At top 0.827 (0.821 on the author's machine) the refusal names the **inner** wall, at (-0.049, 0.498), and says "move it at least about 0.0681 further from the wall". follow.py still moves the top inward, toward that wall. It places at 0.759 only because of how this dipping trace meets the inner wall: 0.80 is still refused at the inner wall, and 0.78 places. Moving away from the named wall, as the message says, gives 0.895, which is refused at the outer wall again. So at width 0.1 the move-only path swings between the two walls. The path that does follow the messages is narrowing first, then moving. Suggested text: "Following the messages converges in two steps: the ribbon narrowed to about 0.058, then moved to about 0.86; the sheet from 0.87 to 0.773 to 0.748. At width 0.1 a move alone does not place the ribbon: the second refusal names the inner wall." Also say that the ribbon figures are from one mesher (0.0572 and 0.859 on Linux). follow.py could move away from the named wall instead of always inward, but it is not part of the change.
3. Low: the ribbon's move is the reach minus the distance to the nearest wall vertex on any wall, but the touched vertex can be on the other wall (as at top 0.827). The message is now explicit about which is which, so I take this as declined. It only affects the size of a figure that is already labelled a guide.

No velocity or pressure sub-solve reported DIVERGED_ITS or reached its cap. The largest velocity sub-solve was 24 iterations (split fault, np 3), and the largest pressure sub-solve 23. Development capped velocity sub-solves in the loop and TI fixtures and in the tests at every np.

### Check 2: which KSPs see A_vv

Read in rotated_bc.py, then inspected at run time (serial and np 2) by Mat handle and `getTransposeNullSpace()`, with `ksp.view()`:

- The velocity sub-KSP's Amat and Pmat are the same Mat (A_vv). It carries the transpose null space (1 vector), and the view shows `has attached transposed null space` on `rotfs1_fieldsplit_vel_`.
- The Schur complement (pressure sub-KSP's Amat, `schurcomplement`): its A00 and Ap00 are the same Mat as A_vv (`getSchurComplementSubMatrices`), so the null space is present there too. The view gives its inner solve as "KSP solver for A00 block viewable with the additional option -rotfs1_fieldsplit_vel_ksp_view". That is the velocity sub-KSP itself (PCFieldSplit reuses it for A00^-1 inside S). The recorder agrees: about 9 velocity applications per outer iteration, one before the Schur solve, one per pressure iteration and one after it.
  - This is harmless, because the mode is discretely divergence-free. |A10 m| / (|A10|_F |m|) = 1.0e-14, so the mode's component of A01 p is zero up to rounding. Whatever A00^-1 returns along m is annihilated by A10. Removing the mode changes S only at rounding, and the answers above bear that out.
- The custom-FMG shell's inner PCMG has A_vv as its operator. Its finest-level smoother KSP (GMRES) also uses A_vv, so its KSPSolve now projects its right-hand side too. That is idempotent: the shell has already projected what it hands the cycle. The cost is one dot product and one axpy per smoother call. Levels 0 (coarse, preonly) and 1 have their own Galerkin operators, with no transpose null space.
- The outer operator Ahat keeps its coupled null space (2 vectors) as before. The pressure Pmat has none. The GAMG fallback (no hierarchy) and the direct-LU route never call `_project_exact_null_modes`, so they are unchanged.
- There is no other caller. `_project_exact_null_modes` is called only from `_solve_rotated_iterative` when a custom prolongation exists.

### Check 3: staleness

The annulus at mu 1e-4, cell 0.1, on one solver, serial and np 2, each step compared against a fresh solver with the same set-up:

| step | admitted modes | A_vv | TNS(A_vv) | vs fresh, serial (v, p) | vs fresh, np 2 (v, p) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| free slip on both walls | 1 | - | 1 vector | - | - |
| (a) `reset.conditions()`, no-slip Lower | 0 | new Mat | none | 0, 0 | 0, 0 |
| (b) the reverse: no-slip to free slip | 1 | new Mat | 1 vector | 0, 0 | 3.8e-15, 9.9e-15 |
| (c) mu 1e-4 to 1e-2, same KSP context (values refreshed in place) | 1 | same Mat | 1 vector | 0, 0 | 0, 0 |
| (d) tolerance 1e-6 to 1e-8 (context rebuilt) | 1 | new Mat | 1 vector | 0, 0 | 7.1e-14, 1.1e-14 |

- Any change to the geometry or the conditions rebuilds the DM, or at least the KSP context. The fieldsplit then extracts a new A_vv, and the null space is attached only if modes are admitted again. No stale null space survives.
- The only path that keeps the null space is an in-place value refresh, as in (c). That is right for any viscous rheology: a rigid rotation has zero strain rate. It has the same lifetime as the shell's own mode set, which already worked this way.

With the one line replaced by `pass`, test_0895's cap test fails serially and at np 2 with `AssertionError: {'pressure': 0, 'velocity': 1}`. Its guard (no-slip, no mode) passes. Development fails the same way at np 1-4.

The earlier probe pass's script is the issue's repro (`Annulus(cellSize=0.4, refinement=2)`, `_rotated_use_lu = True`, tolerance 1e-11), serial. It was run unchanged on both builds, plus a copy that also prints the reason and full-precision residuals.

Row 27 (patched head):

#1058 is still LIVE on the patched head, and bit-identical to development. At mu 1e-4 the loop stalls at 1.026e-8 against its 4e-10 target (1e-11 x rnorm0 40) and does not converge. ksp_reason 4 is KSP_CONVERGED_ITS of the preonly LU, one iteration per increment. mu 1e-6 and 1e-8 still fail with -11 on the cold start. This is expected: the direct-LU route has no custom prolongation and never reaches `_project_exact_null_modes`, so row 27 does not touch it. The `target None` in the extra line only means that this dictionary key does not exist. The target is the issue's 4e-10.

### Findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium: test_0895's LU reference does not converge. `_annulus(..., lu=True)` solves at mu 1e-4 with tolerance 1e-11. That is #1058's case, and on the patched head, at every np, the reference prints `nonlinear rotated free-slip did NOT converge in 3 iterations (rel |F̂| = 7.38e-11 ...)` (serial; 2.06e-11 at np 2). The test compares against it to 1e-9 without checking it.
   - It passes today because the stalled LU answer is close enough on this mesh.
   - The issue records a stalled LU answer 1.6e-8 from the iterative one on a coarser mesh, so the reference could move past the 1e-9 bound without anything failing loudly.
   - Fix: assert `direct._rotated_freeslip_info["converged"]`, with an LU tolerance the route reaches (for example 1e-10), or state in the test that the reference is the stalled LU and widen the bound to match.
3. Low: the `PETSc.NullSpace` wrapper is created inline and never released.
   - The Mat holds its own reference, so nothing is lost. But in parallel petsc4py defers the destruction of a dropped wrapper to the next collective garbage cleanup, the pattern behind #1007. Here that is one velocity-length vector per mode for each context.
   - Fix: keep it in the context (as `nsp` and `cns` are) and destroy it in `_destroy_rotated_ksp_ctx`.
   - Separately, the admission (the Jacobi-scaled ratio against the threshold) is evaluated only when a context is built. An in-place operator refresh keeps both the shell's modes and now the transpose null space. That is correct for viscous rheologies (check 3c), and it predates this change. A comment saying so at the new line would help the next reader.
4. Low: the PR body's TI row reads 2/11/11 before and after. Here, serially, it is 2/11/11 with one cap on development and 2/10/11 with none on row 27. The answer is unchanged (5.0e-14). The body could say "counts unchanged within one velocity iteration".

### Not run

- The 39-file rotated free-slip suite before/after. The serial tables in the handoff were not re-recorded here, and no test outside test_0895/test_1157 was run in parallel.
- A mesh-move staleness case. `mesh.deform` releases a circular wall and the next rotated solve is refused (test_1187). Check 3 used the boundary-condition change, the reverse change, a viscosity refresh and a tolerance change instead.
- Open MPI, and np above 4.
- Wall-clock figures are indicative only: other jobs on the machine shared it.

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# Round 2: kd-tree empty slots (UNFILED 65)

**Verdict at 713c5dae (tests in 682b1209): ready to merge on green from this review.**

Round 1 was at e720ccdb. The round-2 change drops the explicit `nnn=mesh.dim+1` in `rbf_evaluate`, routes the old interpolators' default through `_resolve_nnn`, fixes the two-cell test, and adds docstrings. Every round-1 finding is closed. On a rank holding `dim + 1` or more DOFs, the answers are bit-identical to e720ccdb.

## Round-1 findings, then and now

| # | round 1 (e720ccdb) | now (713c5dae) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Medium | `evaluate()` of a degree-0 variable raised on a rank holding `dim` or fewer cells, through the exterior rung and `rbf=True`; rank-dependent | Closed. Every rank answers in every layout tried (below). None raises. |
| 2 Low | the two-cell test failed on ranks holding no cells at np 2 and 3 | Closed. The test expects zeros on a rank with no cells (#405). 15 passed, 1 skipped on every rank at np 2 and np 3. |
| 3 Low | `order=1` on a small tree gives a refusal naming an `nnn` the caller never passed | Closed by docstring. `_resolve_nnn` now says: "``order=1`` on a tree of fewer than ``dim + 2`` points is refused: the clamped default is then too small for an affine fit, and the refusal names that clamped size (``nnn = n``, or ``nnn=1`` on a one-point tree), not a size the caller passed." |
| 4 Low | three docstrings did not state the rule | Closed. `_apply_monotone_limit`: "Bound an interpolated result by the ``mesh.dim + 1`` nearest source DOFs (or every source DOF the asking rank holds, if that is fewer)". `old_rbf_interpolator_local_from_kdtree` and `_to_kdtree`: "``nnn`` defaults to 4, or every point the tree holds if that is fewer; a larger ``nnn`` given explicitly is refused." Their default is now `None` and goes through `_resolve_nnn`. The new test for them fails on e720ccdb and passes on the head. |

## Runs

- **Build.** 713c5dae was built fresh in a clean worktree, because `_function.pyx` and `ckdtree.pyx` changed. e720ccdb is the round-1 build, unchanged. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`. The runs were pinned to 3 cores, with other jobs on the machine running. Parallel runs went through the MPI supervisor (silence 120 s) inside a `timeout`. Logs kept by the reviewer.
- **Finding 1 probe.** Two degree-0 variables are used: C = 0.1 + x + 2y at the centroids, and K = 5. The probe calls `evaluate` with `rbf=True` at each rank's own centroids, and `evaluate` at 4 points outside the mesh (the exterior rung). Exterior C is checked against an independent numpy inverse-distance average over the rank's min(dim+1, n) DOFs. Freed memory is seeded with 2^40 first. Two layouts are used: a 1 x NX quad column under a band partition, and the new test's 9-cell triangle with given shell counts.

| layout (DOFs per rank) | e720ccdb | 713c5dae |
|---|---|---|
| np 2, band 3 + 2 | rank 0 answers, rank 1 raises "Cannot build a 3-point stencil" in all 4 checks | every rank answers. C at centroids within 4e-12. Exterior C within 4.3e-13 of numpy. K = 5 within 8.9e-16. |
| np 2, band 1 + 1 | both ranks raise | every rank answers; all errors 0 |
| np 2, triangle 7 + 2 | rank 1 raises | every rank answers. Exterior C within 1.1e-13 of numpy. K within 8.9e-16. |
| np 2, triangle 3 + 6 | both answer | both answer |
| np 3, band 1 + 1 + 1 | every rank raises | every rank answers; all errors 0 |
| np 3, band 1 + 1 + 2 | every rank raises | every rank answers, within 2.2e-13 |
| np 3, triangle 5 + 2 + 2 | ranks 1 and 2 raise | every rank answers, within 1.1e-13 |
| np 3, triangle 7 + 1 + 1 | ranks 1 and 2 raise | every rank answers, within 1.1e-13 |

- **Unchanged stencil.** On every rank holding `dim + 1` or more DOFs (6 rank-layouts, 4 quantities each), the head's values are bit-identical to e720ccdb in 24 of 24 comparisons.
- **test_0103 and the new parallel file together.**
  - Serial: 13 passed, 3 skipped. The new file's 3 tests need at least 2 ranks.
  - np 2 and np 3: 15 passed, 1 skipped on every rank.
  - On e720ccdb, serially, the old-interpolator test fails ("1 failed, 12 passed"). At np 2 the new file fails its two `evaluate` tests on rank 1 and passes on rank 0. It ends without a hang.
- **test_0885:** 4 passed on every rank at np 2 and at np 3.
- **Serial neighbour tests.** These are round 1's 28 files, which include test_0102, plus 4 files that use `rbf=True` (test_0504, 0507, 0720, 0784). They ran with xdist, 3 workers, using the head's test files. Both builds give 376 passed, 3 skipped, 1 xpassed.

## Adversarial read of the diff

The round-2 source change removes a raise. It adds none.
- In `rbf_evaluate`, the default `nnn` is `min(dim + 1, n)`, so the explicit-stencil refusal can no longer be reached from `evaluate()`. A rank holding no DOFs returns zeros before the tree is built (#405), which is rank-local.
- The old interpolators now clamp. They are rank-local and have no callers in `src`.
- No new collective is entered or skipped on any branch.
- The new test reads `C.data` and calls `evaluate` on every rank.

## New findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium: none.
3. Low: the new parallel test is built for np 2 to np 5 only. It puts `9 - 2 (size - 1)` cells on rank 0, which is negative from np 6 up, so the shell partition there is invalid. That partition is built on rank 0 only, so the run could fail on one rank. Cap the counts (for example, skip at `uw.mpi.size > 5`), or say in the docstring that the test assumes np 5 or fewer. This was not run above np 3.

## Not run

- Above np 3. Three-dimensional meshes for the `evaluate` rungs.
- The `monotone="pick"` re-evaluation through `evalf=True` on a rank with few cells. It goes through the same `rbf_evaluate` line.
- The full suite, `level_3` and macOS.

Nothing was pushed or posted.

### UNFILED 68 + 70/22 + 72 at the 0003 tip (911a7b16 on 44aa8c53 on d37db66e): changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# UNFILED 68 + 70/22 + 72: add_fault refuses an empty or partial cut; the cut labels its edge across a kink; the cut refuses a flat cell

**Verdict at the 0003 tip (0003 on 0002 on 0001 on development fbef72fa; local, to be pushed as a PR): changes requested.**

In serial, the three commits do what they say, and they are well measured:
- 68 stops silent partial faults;
- the labelling fix makes curved surfaces and fault cuts complete;
- 72 turns the flat cells that the labelling fix exposed into a clear refusal, and never refuses a trace that development places.

In parallel the branch hangs: every 2-D `add_fault` cut and every `add_conforming_surface` that labels a facet hangs at np >= 2 (finding 1, in 0001 and 0002). The fix is two lines, and it was measured here. With it applied, and with the tolerance tightened (findings 2 and 3), this would be ready to merge on green.

Commits, applied with `git am` on fbef72fa:
- 0001 = d37db66e (UNFILED 68).
- 0002 = 44aa8c53 (UNFILED 70/22, plus 68's guard on add_conforming_surface).
- 0003 = 911a7b16 (UNFILED 72).

Every blob matches the patches' index lines: fault_split.py 69bfe90d then bf51588f; line_cut.py 7d8a74cb then e601c005; discretisation_mesh.py 7dcb094d; test_0897 e865dfa8 then c0f93aeb; test_0898 f9f8dfee; test_0899 0a412995. The regenerated 0001 and 0002 that came with 0003 are content-identical to the first copies (only the "[PATCH n/3]" subject prefix differs), so nothing was re-applied. Each finding names its commit.

## Method

- All three commits change Python only. Each build is an overlay of the changed files on a copy of a clean development install at fbef72fa, whose copies of those files match fbef72fa byte for byte. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`.
- Builds:
  - base: development;
  - 68: 0001;
  - 0002 tip;
  - 0003 tip;
  - review fix: a tip with 68's two array reductions in buffer form (finding 1);
  - mutants: 0001 without the refusal call, and 0003 without the flat-cell call;
  - tolerance probe: the 0003 review-fix build, which records the guard's worst distance and three cell statistics on every call. This is a probe only, not a proposal for the code.
- Cores 0-3 only (taskset): at most 4 processes, pytest -n at most 2.
- Every parallel run went through the repository's MPI supervisor (silence 180 s) inside an outer timeout.
- Other jobs were running on the machine (load about 14), so times are indicative.
- Partitions came from a shell-partition hook on gmsh meshes:
  - band: cells by height, rank 0 at the bottom;
  - emptyband: the last rank holds no cell;
  - rand1: random.
- A cell-quality recorder wrapped every `add_fault` call. It logs each returned mesh's minimum area/(longest edge)^2 and minimum angle (min-reduced over ranks), or the refusal. It ran on development, the 0002 tip and the 0003 tip.
- Logs and the CSV (2,571 rows) kept by the reviewer.

## UNFILED 68 (0001)

### (a) test_0897 fails on development and passes on the branch
- Development: 3 failed, 1 passed. The one-cell guard case passes on development, as it should.
- 0001: 4 passed. 0002 tip: 4 passed. 0003 tip: in the suite run, passed.
- Mutant (refusal call removed): 2 failed (the empty and the short cut).
- **np 2: hangs on 0001 and on every tip.** Rank 0 raises `ValueError: The truth value of an array with more than one element is ambiguous`, which is not the refusal. Rank 1 waits in a broadcast inside `_refuse_an_empty_or_partial_cut`, and the supervisor killed the run. Native stacks: rank 0 in `PyMPI_reduce_p2p`/`PMPI_Recv`, rank 1 in `MPIR_Bcast_intra_binomial`.

### (b) The verdict comes from reduced values and is the same on every rank
By reading:
- The decision uses only reduced quantities: a SUM of the facet count, a SUM of [edge-length sum, count], a MIN of the trace-to-chain distances, and 0002's MAX of the in-domain flags.
- No reduction sits under a rank-dependent branch, and both raises depend only on reduced values.

Measured:
- **As written, the MIN reduction hangs.** In mpi4py 4.1.1 the lowercase `comm.allreduce` pickles its operand and applies MIN as `x if x < y else y`. On a NumPy array that comparison raises on the ranks that combine operands; the rest wait in the broadcast.
  - Reproduced by a four-line probe.
  - Reproduced on the check at np 2 (band: rank 0 raises, rank 1 hangs) and at np 4 (rand1: ranks 0-2 raise, rank 3 hangs).
  - The empty cases still agree, because they raise before the MIN.
  - Serial runs are unaffected, because a one-rank allreduce applies no operator.
- **With the review fix** (`comm.Allreduce(MPI.IN_PLACE, d, op=MPI.MIN)`), 11 cases at np 1 and at np 2, 3 and 4 under band, emptyband and rand1 (10 layouts, 110 runs) all AGREE, with byte-identical messages on every rank and no hang. The cases:
  - a hand-labelled chain at y = 2/12, all on rank 0, empty, half and whole;
  - a vertical chain that crosses every rank (e.g. 2/3/2/4 edges per rank at np 4), empty, half and whole;
  - add_fault on test_0897's three traces;
  - a vertical trace with and without the add-on partition. Without it, every rank then raises the existing seam refusal alike.
  - The emptyband layouts include an empty rank.

### (c) The half-cell estimate on graded meshes and on the subduction-zone annulus fixture's traces
Traces: the fixture's fault (the annulus driver's parabola, inset half a cell from the wall), sampled at 0.3, 0.5, 1, 2 and 4 cells, forward and reversed. Meshes:
- a uniform annulus (cell 0.05);
- an annulus adapted toward the fault (x4, edges 0.0078-0.066);
- two boxes graded x20 (a refined blob at one end of a long arc; a refined band across its middle).

Each result was rebuilt with the refusal off, to measure the real gap against the local cell. On 0001:
- One-, two- and four-cell sampling: added, gap 0. **No false refusal.**
- 0.3 cells (uniform annulus): empty, refused.
- 0.5 cells: refused as partial, where development raises "several fragments".
- Graded boxes, coarse sampling: refused, all real gaps (0.54-17 local cells).
- 32 cases in all: 8 true refusals, 22 true adds, 0 false refusals, 0 missed.

At the 0002 and 0003 tips (new labelling), 29 adds and 3 refusals of gaps smaller than half the LOCAL cell (0.30-0.47). All three are at a trace tip on the coarse side of a graded mesh (finding 3). test_1079, test_1092 and test_0559 pass on every build.

### (d) The fault suite
- Serial, the 38-file fault-suite list:
  - development: 417 passed, 1 skipped (43:39);
  - 0002 tip, the 38 + test_0897 + test_0898: 423 passed, 1 skipped;
  - 0003 tip, + test_0899: 426 passed, 1 skipped (43:22).
  - Nothing else changed.
- Parallel fault tests (the 32 tests/parallel files that use add_fault, fault_split or split_fault) at np 2, one fresh supervisor per file: Development and the 0003 review-fix build give identical results, file by file. 30 files pass (161 passed, 3 skipped, the same counts on both builds). Two files were killed by the 180-s silence rule on BOTH builds, with every rank busy in the same long computation, so they are slow rather than hung: test_1097 (4 passed, then `_adapt_nested`) and test_1174_3d (in the rotated free-slip solve). Neither is a regression; both were run alone. The UNFIXED tip was not run through this list: by finding 1, every file there that makes a 2-D cut hangs, as shown on test_0897 and the probes.

### (e) The reductions use raw mpi4py
- The repository's CLAUDE.md: "PETSc handles synchronisation — avoid direct mpi4py." uw.mpi has no reduction helper. The library's buffer helper is `addon_partition._allreduce`: `comm.Allreduce(MPI.IN_PLACE, ...)` with an agreement point for `agreed_build`. Its docstring warns about exactly this: "the pickling form applies the operator to Python objects".
- The sites in `_refuse_an_empty_or_partial_cut`:
  1. `allreduce(int(marked.size), SUM)`: a scalar, so it works. It counts a seam edge once per rank that holds it, which is harmless for "> 0". It should use the helper.
  2. `allreduce(np.array([sum, count]), SUM)`: it works only because `+` on arrays is element-wise. It should use the helper.
  3. `allreduce(d, MIN)`: **hangs.** It must be `Allreduce(IN_PLACE, d, MIN)`, or the helper with op=MIN.
  4. `allreduce(inside, MAX)` (0002): **hangs the same way.** It must be `Allreduce(IN_PLACE, inside, MAX)`.
- 0003's `_refuse_degenerate_cells` uses `allreduce(n_bad, SUM)` on a scalar and `allreduce(worst, MIN)` on a tuple (ratio, size, x, y). Both work, because tuples compare element by element. They are still pickled reductions; prefer the helper (or a MINLOC on the ratio, then a broadcast of the position).

### Mutant
The refusal call removed: test_0897's two add_fault cases fail. The hand-labelled case calls the function directly, so it cannot see this mutant. The add_fault cases do.

## UNFILED 70/22, the labelling fix (0002)

### (f) test_0898
- Development: collection error (`_refuse_an_empty_or_partial_cut` does not exist yet).
- 0001: 2 failed.
  - The arc test fails as stated: "misses part of the trace ... 0.682 from the trace".

On this mesh the hairpin is added rather than refused, so its check does run.

### (g) Hairpin and self-approach: no spurious edge at np 1, 2 and 3
The handoff's `measure_labels.py` on the regular 1/12 box at 1-cell sampling. np 1 ran on the tip; np 2 and np 3 ran on the review fix, because the tip hangs in parallel. Every rank reports identical counts, equal to the handoff's rows:
- add_fault hairpin: raised 3, partial 10, complete 7, spurious 0;
- add_fault approach: partial 12, complete 8, spurious 0;
- add_conforming_surface hairpin and approach: raised 19, partial 1, spurious 0.

### (h) The sphere (test_1079's arcs)
- Cut as add_fault cuts them; the expected chain is the consecutive on-line vertices joined by an edge.
- Cell 0.2 with half-angle pi/6: 6/6 labelled. Cell 0.05 with 0.25: 13/13. Cell 0.1 with 0.25: 6/6. No extra edge under either rule.
- The handoff's sphere probe: 15 edges along 16 on-line vertices (cell 0.05, arc 0.3) and 8 along 9. That is complete and identical under both rules; this is the "16 of 16" in the handoff's count.

### (i) Curved-wall and graded meshes from the curved-walls fixtures
Meshes:
- the 1168 annulus (cell 0.1);
- the same annulus slid "graded2" (theta + 0.1 sin 2 theta);
- the elliptical annulus (1.5 x 1, inner circle 0.5).

Traces, at 2, 1, 0.5 and 0.3 cells:

- a 270-degree arc and a half-ellipse (add_fault's cut, analysed before the split).

Results:

  - arc 48/48 to 39/39;
  - half-ellipse 54/54 to 41/41;
  - ellipse spiral 20/20.
  - The smallest chord/arc of any labelled edge is 0.987, so the 0.5 ratio is far from biting on smooth curves.

  - annulus spiral: 14, 8, 3 and 1 of 19;
  - graded2 spiral: 0 of 22 at 0.3 cells;
  - half-ellipse: 26/41 and 4/41 at 0.5 and 0.3 cells;
  - ellipse spiral: 16, 12, 7 and 3 of 20.
- Refused alike on both builds: the arc at 0.5 and 0.3 cells ("snapping inverted").
- Where the ratio can bite, a single kink inside a cell (add_conforming_surface):
  - 60 and 90 degrees: labelled;
  - 110 degrees at one position: refused as partial (the cut leaves no single edge across it);
  - above about 125 degrees: "an edge crossed more than once";

### (j) add_conforming_surface's guard in parallel
By reading:
- The guard runs on every rank after the cut and the optional repair.
- `points_in_domain` is collective up front (`get_max_radius` comes before any early return), so the call order is safe.
- A point that any rank holds counts as inside (MAX), and points outside get distance 0.
- **The MAX is the pickled reduction, and it hangs** (finding 1), after the MIN, which hangs first.

Measured: a curved surface at y about 0.1, under band, has 11 of its 15 points inside and all of them on rank 0:
- 0002 tip, np 2: rank 0 raises the ambiguous-truth ValueError and rank 1 hangs.
- Review fix, np 2 and np 3: AGREE on every rank for the surface added, for the same surface with a 1.5-cell hole cut from its label (refused, with the message identical to np 1), and for a surface crossing every band.

### Reproducing the handoff's grid
On the 0002 tip, with the old rule plugged back in against the new rule:
- add_conforming_surface, regular box, lines and arcs, 1 cell: old 20 complete / 20 partial, new 40 complete. Matches the handoff's row.
- add_fault, irregular box, lines and arcs, 0.3 cells:
  - old: raised 15, empty 8, partial 11, complete 6. Matches.
  - new: raised 13, partial 7, complete 20, against the handoff's 15 and 18. It repeats exactly here. The difference is most likely platform rounding in snap and split refusals; empty and partial match.
- At the 0003 tip the new row becomes raised 16, partial 6, complete 18. The flat-cell guard refuses the two flat "complete" meshes, and one that was partial.

## UNFILED 72, the flat-cell refusal (0003)

### (a) test_0899
- Development: 2 failed. 0002 tip: 2 failed. 0003 tip: 3 passed.
- Mutant (the guard call removed): 2 failed, 1 passed.

### (b) The guard never refuses a trace that development places with sound cells
The pull-rule grid (480 traces: lines and arcs, and kinks; both boxes; 1, 0.5 and 0.3 cells; every sample pulled), the grid rows, the hairpin and approach fixtures, the curved walls, the graded fixtures, the tolerance cases and the fault suite were all run on development and the 0003 tip:
- **Flat-cell refusals where development returned a mesh: 0.**
- The 26 flat-cell refusals in the grid: development refused all 26.
  - 25 as "the label meets itself at a junction", 1 as "several fragments".
  - The 0002 tip returned 16 of them with a flat cell, refused 5 others as partial, and crashed on 5 in `DMPlexComputeGeometryFVM failed`. That crash becomes a clear message under 0003.
- The 4 in the grid rows: development refused all 4 (junction).
- The one in the fault suite: test_0866's coarse-level replay, which development also refused (junction). The level stays welded with the existing warning, and the test passes.
- The 0003 tip refuses 62 grid traces that development placed. **All 62 are 68's partial-cut refusal, not 72's**: development returned those faults partial. The lowest development ratio among them is 5.7e-5.

### (c) The gap, on this machine's slice
1,764 returned meshes were measured with the flat guard off (development and the 0002 tip) or passing it (0003 tip):
- **Flat:** 22, all at area/longest^2 <= 5.3e-16, 0 degrees.
- **Sound:** the minimum is 5.73e-5, development's kink at 0.3 cells on the regular box (0.008 degrees). Next comes 2.1e-3 (test_1087's moving fault, 0.25 degrees; the same on development and the tip).
- **Nothing between 1e-14 and 5.7e-5.**
- The fixtures that the author's hunt did not include:
  - curved walls: minimum 0.042;
  - graded boxes and the annulus fixture: 0.0068;
  - fault suite: 2.1e-3;
  - grid rows and hairpins: 7.1e-3.
- **1e-9 sits safely in the gap**: six orders of magnitude above the largest flat and 4.8 below the smallest sound mesh. This agrees with the author's ratio files (dev 329 meshes, minimum 5.73e-5; new 732, 22 flat, maximum 5.3e-16).
- A quality threshold would be a separate decision. Development's 0.008-degree cell passes, which is the stated intent.

### (d) Collective
test_0899's trace was run through the cut directly on the partitioned mesh, through add_fault with the add-on partition, and through add_fault without it:
- np 2 (band, rand1) and np 3 (band, rand1): every rank raises the same message (sha1 equal on every rank), no hang.
- Under band at np 3 the flat cells (around (0.483, 0.461)) lie on one rank only.
- Control, a line that places (np 2, review fix): added on every rank.

Message: "the cut leaves 2 flat cell(s) ... the worst at (0.48313, 0.461222), beside the trace's sample at (0.490011, 0.449158); the trace turns 90 degrees at (0.428078, 0.557737) ... Give a sharp turn a vertex of its own (sample the trace at it), reduce its angle, or resample the trace near it about a cell apart."

## Tolerance: mean vs median vs shortest side (0001)
The worst of the 22 lenient passes: kink120_9, the regular 1/12 box, samples 0.3 cells apart, every sample pulled, trace-to-chain 0.568 nominal cells. It was rebuilt with the author's generator (seed 22) and added at the tip:
- **It is visibly incomplete, not offset.**
  - The chain is in two components (four ends).
  - It covers 3.44 of the trace's 5.00 cells.
  - It stops 0.31 cells short of a tip and leaves a 0.58-cell gap at the 120-degree kink.
  - The chain's vertices lie on the trace, but stretches are unlabelled.
  - Slip 0.0906. Its 1-cell twin is refused ("an edge crossed more than once").
- kink120_4 at 1 cell: two components, 3.75 of 5.00 cells, a 1-cell gap at the kink. Slip 0.0914, against 0.0832 for the complete twin (the same straight-armed trace sampled 0.5 cells apart: one component, 5.00 cells): **10 % high**.
- kink120_1 at 0.3 cells: one component, but 4.38 of 5.00 cells long. Its edge across the kink is the chord 0002 admits (chord/arc exactly 0.5 at 120 degrees), 0.54 cells from the corner. Slip 0.0605, against 0.0864 for the complete twin at 1 cell: **30 % low**.
- **The three half-tolerances there, in nominal cells:**
  - mean (as written): 0.569-0.575. The mean includes the diagonals: the mean edge of a right-triangle mesh is 1.14 h.
  - median edge: 0.500.
  - median shortest side: 0.500.
  - Either the median or the shortest side would refuse all 22.
- False refusals with the stricter tolerances:
  - Over the 41-file fault suite on the 0003 tip (220 guard calls that pass, which include test_1079, test_1092 and test_0559): neither the median nor the shortest side would refuse any. The largest worst distance over the median half-cell is 0.18, and over the shortest-side half-cell 0.39.
  - On the graded and annulus fixtures: the same 3 refusals as the mean, no new one.
- **View: tighten the tolerance.** Use the median edge length near the trace, or better the local edge length at the worst point (that also cures finding 3). A gap of half a nominal cell at a kink moves the slip by 10-30 % here: the silent wrong answer 68 exists to stop. The shortest side gives the same number on these meshes, but it is the more fragile statistic near slivers.

## Findings

1. High (0001; and 0002's MAX): **every parallel 2-D add_fault cut, and every parallel add_conforming_surface, that labels a facet hangs.**
   - The cause: `comm.allreduce(d, op=MPI.MIN)` and `comm.allreduce(inside, op=MPI.MAX)` are mpi4py's pickling reductions on NumPy arrays.
   - Fix (measured, 110 runs on 10 layouts at np 1-4, plus (g) and (j) at np 2-3): `comm.Allreduce(MPI.IN_PLACE, d, op=MPI.MIN)` and the same for `inside`, or `addon_partition._allreduce`.
   - Add a tests/parallel test of test_0897's add_fault cases at np 2. The handoff asked for exactly that run, and it is what would have caught this.
2. Medium (0001): on a regular right-triangle mesh "half a cell" is 0.57 nominal cells. The guard passes broken chains (two components, gaps at kinks) and a corner-cut chain whose slip is 30 % low. The median edge would refuse all 22 measured cases and no case in the fault suite (Tolerance section).
3. Medium (0001): the cell size is the count-weighted mean edge in the trace's grown bounding box, so it is not local.
   - On a mesh graded x20 across the trace it came to 0.0149, against a local cell of 0.066 at the gap. That is about 0.11 local cells in the coarse part. Three tip gaps of 0.30-0.47 local cells are refused there, against the stated "half a cell".
   - Refined where the trace is, the cell size is 2-3 times the local cell (0.019 against 0.006-0.008).
   - Use the local edge length near each trace point.
4. Low (0001): test_0897's short case passes the threshold by 5 % (0.05 against 0.0475-0.0476). The threshold moves with the partition, because seam edges are counted per rank that holds them (0.0971-0.0982 against 0.0979 serial). If 2 or 3 is taken, re-derive the fixture well clear of the threshold.
5. Low (0002): add_conforming_surface's refusal gives add_fault's advice ("resample the trace to about one cell apart"). There no sample is pulled, and after 0002 sampling is not what makes a surface partial.
6. Low (0003): the flat-cell message advises "give a sharp turn a vertex of its own (sample the trace at it)" even when the turn is already a sample, as in test_0899, where the 90-degree corner is a control point. Make that advice conditional on the turn not being a sample.
7. Low (0002): test_0898's hairpin test passes under the old rule too. Say in its docstring that it guards against a regression only.
8. Low (0003): `_refuse_degenerate_cells` visits every cell in Python (`getTransitiveClosure` per cell) on every cut. That is fine at these sizes, but it costs seconds on the subduction fixture's 128,000-cell finest level. `cell_areas` is already vectorised, and the longest edge can be computed the same way.
9. Low (0002): the "up to 120 degrees" in `_CHORD_TO_ARC`'s comment holds only where the cut leaves a single edge across the kink. A 110-degree kink at one position was refused as partial (honestly). Reword it as a bound on the rule.

## Not run
- The serial fault suite with the recorder on development was stopped at 68 % (during test_1092's tide test) to free cores. Its 126 recorded meshes match the 0003 tip's sound minima, and the full development suite had already passed (417/1).
- A converged slip reference for the tolerance cases beyond the twin comparison (same geometry, other sampling).
- The parallel fault tests on the unfixed tip, beyond the hangs documented above (each hang costs about 400 s).
- The tolerance variants (median and shortest side) as a refusal in parallel; they were recorded serially only.
- The ptest_* scripts (not collected by pytest); the 3-D sheet path (untouched); GitHub CI (paused).

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### Row 28 / #1058 refusal cut at f917543d: the cliff is not a function of the ratio alone

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict: the cliff is not a function of the ratio alone.** The 1e-9 cut catches every wrong answer measured on the annulus, but it is a proxy for a different test, and it misses in both directions on other geometries. The wrong answers are not the factorisation losing the answer: they are the correct solution plus an arbitrary rigid rotation, left in because the rigid-rotation gauge test (`_mode_satisfies_constraints`, operator test against `_ROTATION_MODE_TOL = 1e-8`) rejects the true null mode once the viscosity is small. That test, not `diag_ratio`, sets the cliff, and in `diag_ratio` terms it moves with the mesh: about 8.5e-10 (annulus, cell 0.4), 7.8e-10 (annulus, cell 0.2), 7.4e-10 to 7.6e-10 (disc), 1.9e-10 (spherical shell). On the shell the cut refuses correct answers over a factor of 5 in viscosity. A 1e9 viscosity contrast at unit reference viscosity is refused with a correct answer, and the remedy the message names cannot lift it.

Change reviewed: f917543d ("#1058: the rotated direct-LU route converges at small viscosity, or refuses"), run from the reviewer's built environment for that commit. Accuracy measure as in the change's own probes: mu * vrms against the mu = 1 solve on the same mesh (Stokes is linear in 1/mu). For a refused case, the accuracy comes from a second solve with the module constant `_ROTATED_LU_MIN_DIAGONAL_RATIO` set to 0 at run time (no source edited). Each row also records the gauge decision (`solver._rotated_gauge_report`), its operator violation `op_viol`, the rigid-rotation content of mu * v, the error with that rotation removed, and MUMPS INFOG(28) (null pivots) after each factorisation. All serial unless stated; 5 cores, other jobs on the machine.

## Mechanism

`op_viol = |J t| / (mean |diag(J_vv)| * |t|)` for the rigid-rotation mode t. The numerator is assembly round-off in rows whose scale does not go with the viscosity (the coupling); the denominator goes as mu. So at fixed mesh `op_viol` is proportional to 1/mu (cell 0.4 annulus: 1.26e-10 at mu 1e-8, 1.26e-8 at mu 1e-10), and once it passes 1e-8 the true null mode is rejected, the gauge is not removed, and the singular LU leaves an arbitrary multiple of the rotation in the answer. In every row of every table:

- accurate rows: `gauge removed True`, `op_viol < 1e-8`;
- wrong rows: `gauge removed False`, `op_viol >= 1e-8`, and the error with the rigid rotation removed is 1e-13 to 2e-8, i.e. the rest of the answer is right (2-D rows; the 3-D rotation split was not computed).

Since `diag_ratio` is also proportional to mu, the cliff in ratio terms is `diag_ratio * op_viol / 1e-8` at any one viscosity, a mesh-dependent constant. Predicted from the mu 1e-8 row and measured from the bracket:

| geometry | cell | predicted cliff ratio | measured bracket (highest wrong, lowest accurate) | 1e-9 inside? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| annulus | 0.4 | 8.54e-10 | 8.47e-10, 8.81e-10 | no: cut refuses accurate rows in (8.8e-10, 1e-9) |
| annulus | 0.2 | 8.13e-10 | 7.48e-10, 8.16e-10 | no: same, (8.2e-10, 1e-9) |
| annulus | 0.1 | 7.73e-10 | not measured (runs stopped; see below) | predicted no, as at 0.4 and 0.2 |
| disc | 0.4 | 7.58e-10 | 6.26e-10, 1.88e-9 (sweep step) | yes, coarse bracket |
| disc | 0.2 | 7.43e-10 | 7.40e-10, 8.64e-10 (gauge); plus a separate failure at 1.23e-9, see finding 2 | no |
| spherical shell (3-D) | 0.3 | 1.91e-10 | 1.77e-10, 2.22e-10 | no: cut refuses accurate rows in (2.2e-10, 1e-9) |

The original two-mesh bracket (6.8e-10 wrong, 1.4e-9 accurate) contained 1e-9 only because the sweep steps were a factor of 3; at finer steps the annulus cliff sits at 7.5e-10 to 8.8e-10 on all three meshes, below the cut, so on the annulus the cut is safe with a margin of about 15%, and conservative. The shell's cliff is 5 times lower.

## Parallel

| case | np | result |
|---|---|---|
| annulus 0.4, mu 1e-10 (refused) | 2 | ValueError on both ranks, both naming set_reference_quantities; no hang; exit 0 under the supervisor |
| annulus 0.4, mu 1e-10 (refused) | 3 | ValueError on all three ranks, same message; no hang |
| shell 0.3, mu 1e-9 and 3e-10 (refused) | 2 | ValueError on both ranks; no hang |
| annulus 0.4, mu 3e-10 (accepted) | 2 | mu vrms 4.790064844862 against serial 4.790064851994, relative 1.5e-9; serial is itself 1.6e-9 from mu = 1; floored target (rel residual 4.9e-5 at np 2, 7.2e-6 serial). Matches. np 2 retried the workspace to 5000 where serial factored at 1000. |
| annulus 0.4, mu 1.5e-10 (accepted) | 2 | err 5.2e-11 against mu = 1 (serial 1.9e-10). |

With the cut disabled, the wrong answers are different at each np (annulus 0.4, mu 1e-10: err 0.14 serial, 0.79 at np 2, 0.19 at np 3): the rotation multiple is arbitrary, as expected for an unremoved null mode.

## Workspace retry (item 5)

The ×5 retry fires: on the annulus at cells 0.2 and 0.1, lu_workspace is 5000 at mu 1e-8 and below (cell 0.2 also at 1e-9 and 1e-10; but 1000 at mu 8e-11, so it is not monotone in mu), and on the disc at cell 0.2 from mu 1e-8. At np 2 on cell 0.4 it fired at mu 3e-10 where serial did not. It never went past 5000 and never reached the 25000 cap in any run. At cell 0.1 it fired at mu 1e-8 and 1e-9 (5000), and those solves took about 5 minutes each against 5 s at mu = 1.

## Findings

1. High: the cut tests a proxy, and the real cliff is the gauge test. Every wrong answer measured is the right answer plus a rigid rotation, left in because `_mode_satisfies_constraints` rejects the true rotation mode when `op_viol >= _ROTATION_MODE_TOL (1e-8)`. `op_viol` divides a viscosity-independent round-off by the mean velocity diagonal, so it grows as 1/mu, and the cliff in `diag_ratio` terms is mesh- and geometry-dependent (8.5e-10 to 7.4e-10 in 2-D, 1.9e-10 on the shell). The cut sits above all measured cliffs, so it has not let a gauge-wrong answer through, but on the shell it refuses correct answers for mu from about 4.3e-10 to 2.3e-9 (ratio 1.9e-10 to 1e-9), and nothing guarantees a finer or differently shaped mesh keeps its cliff below 1e-9. Suggest making the operator test scale-free (compare `|J t|` against the round-off of the rows it is taken over, e.g. `eps * |J|_inf * |t|` times a margin, or the velocity rows against the velocity diagonal and the pressure rows against the coupling), then re-measuring; on this evidence that may remove the need for a refusal on these cases altogether, since the error with the rotation removed stays at 1e-13 to 2e-8 down to mu 3e-12 on the disc. If a refusal stays, refuse on the condition that actually fails (a declared rotation whose constraint test passes but whose operator test does not, on the LU route) rather than on `diag_ratio`.

2. High: a genuine viscosity contrast is refused with a correct answer, and the named remedy cannot help. A 1e9 contrast at unit outer viscosity (annulus cell 0.4) gives `diag_ratio` 1.1e-10 at every scale (mu 1, 1e3, 1e6), the answers agree across scales to 3e-10 to 8e-10, and the gauge is removed (`op_viol` 2.6e-16). Non-dimensionalising with set_reference_quantities does not change the ratio, so the user has no way past the refusal. A 1e8 contrast passes at 1.1e-9, i.e. just. The commit names this ("a real viscosity contrast this large is refused too"), but the message tells the user something that will not work. At minimum the message should distinguish a small overall viscosity from a large contrast.

3. Medium: an accepted case above the cut fails outright, and only warns. Disc, cell 0.2, mu 1e-10 (`diag_ratio` 1.23e-9, accepted): the LU updates do not reduce the residual (22.6 to 17.6, line search halves then stalls), the solve ends `converged False` with a WARNING and returns, and the fields hold an iterate 25% off. Deterministic (reproduced twice, and with the floor removed), and isolated: mu 2e-10, 1.2e-10, 9e-11 and 7e-11 on the same mesh are right to 4e-13. So the cut does not mark where the factorisation itself stops working; this failure is a different one from the gauge cliff, and it is not monotone in the ratio. It is at least loud. Whether the route should raise rather than warn here (as it does for a hard linear failure) is a separate question, but a user who trusts the refusal to cover the bad region will not expect it.

4. Medium: the floored target loosens as 1/mu. 100 * eps * |A|_inf * |u| with |u| ~ 1/mu and |A| dominated by the coupling lets accepted rows report converged at relative residuals up to 1.1e-3 (disc 0.4, mu 1e-9; answer still 3.4e-9 off) and refused-but-disabled rows up to 2.9e-2. The answers in the accepted rows are right, so the floor is not wrong, but `converged True` at 1e-3 is a weaker statement than users will read into it; recording `target_floored` helps, and the floored target itself is worth putting in info.

5. Low: on the annulus the cut has about 15% margin over the measured cliff (cut 1e-9 against cliffs 8.5e-10, 8.1e-10, and a predicted 7.7e-10 at cell 0.1); the original bracket rows (2.0e-9 / 6.8e-10 and 1.4e-9 / 4.1e-10) were a factor-of-3 sweep step, not the cliff. The code comment should quote the refined brackets if the cut stays.

6. Low: MUMPS null-pivot detection (ICNTL(24)) reported 0 or 1 null pivots throughout, in accurate and wrong rows alike, so INFOG(28) is not a usable refusal signal here.

## Not run

- The cell 0.1 annulus below mu 1e-9: the cliff there is predicted (7.7e-10), not bracketed. Annulus cells finer than 0.1, and the shell at a second resolution.
- The 3-D rotation split (error with the three rotations removed) on the shell; the gauge report stands in for it.
- A fix for finding 1: the scale-free gauge test was not implemented or measured.
- Parallel runs of the disc and of the contrast cases; np 4 and above.
- The iterative (non-LU) rotated route at the same viscosities.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

One new check goes further than intended. It is the only reason for "changes requested", and the fix is small:

It also refuses a sympy zero such as `sympy.Integer(0)` or `S.Zero`, and the message calls it an ambiguous plain number. Development and bd3a4091 accepted all of these (finding 1).

## Round-2 findings, then and now

A plain 0 essential condition on degC/degF is still refused. A nonzero plain flux is still refused. | 3 Low | lone `sin(y)`/`y`/`1 - y` refused, their 1-tuples accepted | **fixed**: a lone value and its 1-tuple now get the same result. `sin(y)` is accepted in both forms. `y`, `1 - y` and `q(300 K)*(1 - y)` are refused in both forms on K and degC fields. The check is skipped on dimensionless fields (new finding 2) | R3 |

This holds with and without reference quantities, for essential and natural conditions, on the solver's fields, on passed fields with and without units, and on a `Vector_Projection`. `(0.0, None)`, `Matrix([0, 0])` and the `components=` forms are unchanged. The update text says this applies in every model | R4 |

The new check is too strict on other rows (new finding 1) | R5, p_r3c |
| 6 Low | `I`/`zoo` raised a bare `TypeError`; `(np.array(0.5),)` passed | **fixed**: `I`, `zoo`, `1+I` and `nan` are refused with "is not a real number", alone and in tuples, essential and natural. `(np.array(0.5),)` is refused as a plain number, and `(np.array(0.0),)` is accepted. Still open, as before: a `uw.expression` holding a plain 0.5 is taken in model units (new finding 3) | R6 |

The five update-text corrections from round 2 are all in the text.

## Runs

- **Build.** 9adc2c9a changes a `.pyx` file, so it was built fresh in its own worktree. The extension is newer than the build start. The installed `level_set.py` and `systems/solvers.py` are identical to the commit. The comparison builds are bd3a4091 (round 2's build), f9438460 and development 7091a93c (both from the first review). Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`.
- **Round-2 probes on head vs bd3a4091.**
  - The 1,017-case probe with reference quantities: 108 cases differ.
    - Intended: 32 natural zeros on degC/degF now accepted; 18 complex constants and `nan` now refused as not real; 2 `(np.array(0.5),)` now refused; 14 short tuples and lone values on vector fields now refused; 3 `[q 5 km, 0]` basal rows now refused; 5 plain basal rows on passed fields now refused; 14 tuple expressions of the wrong dimension now refused (such as `(expr 5 km,)` on a K field); 2 `(pi*y, 0)` on a velocity now refused; 2 lone `sin(x)` on degC now accepted.
    - Not intended: 3 basal `[sympy.Integer(0), 0]` rows now refused (finding 1). 9 lone expressions of another dimension on a dimensionless field and 1 level-set `far_field` expression on a dimensionless field are now accepted (finding 2).
    - Also among the 108: 3 lone dimensionless expressions on K, km and degC fields are now accepted in model units, as the new rule says (finding 3).
  - Without reference quantities: 8 differ, all short tuples on vector fields, now refused.
  - The lone-symbolic probe: 6 of 40 differ, all intended (round-2 findings 1 to 3).
- **New round-3 probes,** on head, bd3a4091 and development.
  - A 635-case probe on the seven findings, the short-value rule in both models, and coordinate datums on velocity fields. 155 cases differ from bd3a4091. All are the fixes above, apart from finding 1 and finding 2.
  - A 46-case probe on common coordinate datums in a model with reference quantities. These are accepted: `T0 + dT*(1 - y/H)`, `T*erf(y/H)`, `T*(1 + 0.1*cos(pi*x/H))`, `q(300 K) + y*q(100 K/km)`, a `Piecewise` of K expressions, and on a velocity `V*(x/L)*(1 - x/L)`, `V*sin(pi*x)` and `q*tanh(x/L)`. These are refused for dimension: `1 - y`, `x*(1 - x)`, `V*x` and `0.5*x`.
  - A 48-case probe on basal-traction rows (finding 1).
- **The test conversions.** The `test_0033` lid in its old form (`alpha*x*(1 - x)`) on bd3a4091 and in its new form on both builds gives the same results, with and without reference quantities. The non-dimensional datum is the same at four points. The velocity data match (sum of |u| 32.64253808664). J is 1.194284606189e-02 and dJ/dalpha is 6.167265095190e-03, the same in every digit. On head, the old form is refused in a model with reference quantities ("is in kilometer ** 2, and the field … is in meter / second"). `test_0035` at np 2: head 4 passed. bd3a4091 with its own (old) file: 4 passed. The old file on head: 2 failed (both units cases, refused for dimension) and 2 passed.

Run alone it gave 7 passed, 2 skipped, exit 0, in 110 s on head and in 111 s on bd3a4091. In round 2 it failed once under load and passed alone, so this is not a regression.

The coordinate-dependent essential datums the examples record are in models without reference quantities (the Richards and SolNL examples). The Spiegelman benchmark's velocity is an expression in mm/yr and passes. In the nine examples that fail before or apart from their conditions on every build, the conditions were read: they are quantities, components forms or penalty natural conditions. None is a coordinate datum on a field with units.
- **Load.** 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine.

## New findings

1. Medium: `add_basal_traction` refuses a Matrix row and sympy zeros in a model with reference quantities.

   The new per-entry check uses `entries = list(conds) if isinstance(conds, (list, tuple)) else [conds]`. So a `sympy.Matrix` is one entry. It is not a quantity, not `sympy.Basic` (a mutable Matrix), and not `== 0`, so it is refused as a plain number. The message reads "component 0: the plain number Matrix([[0], [0]]) … e.g. uw.quantity(Matrix([[0], [0]]), "MPa")". This happens on the solver's fields and on passed fields. Refused on head, accepted on development and bd3a4091:
   - `Matrix([0, 0])` and `Matrix([[0, 0]])`;
   - `Matrix([sin(pi*x), 0])`, a symbolic traction;
   - `[sympy.Integer(0), 0]`, `[S.Zero, S.Zero]` and `[sympy.Float(0.0), 0]`, where zero needs no units;
   - `[q 1 MPa, sympy.Integer(0)]` on passed fields. On the solver's fields it is refused too, but there bd3a4091 also refused it (with the converted quantity named), and development recorded a wrongly scaled -9.96e7.

   `ImmutableMatrix([0, 0])` is accepted. A quantity without units, `[q 1 MPa, uw.quantity(0.5)]` or `[uw.quantity(0), 0]`, fails with `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'dimensionality'`. Models without reference quantities are not affected.

   A traction is often written as a Matrix (for example `-sigma_n * n`), so a correct call that worked on development now fails. The fix would be to flatten a `MatrixBase` into its entries and to treat a sympy number with `is_zero` as zero. A quantity without units should be refused with a message, not an `AttributeError`. A test with `Matrix([0, 0])` and `[sympy.Integer(0), q 1 MPa]` in a units model would cover it.

   Related, and probably intended: a plain row such as `[0.5, 0]` is now refused on a passed velocity without units in a units model, where development and bd3a4091 took it as -0.5. `add_natural_bc` accepts plain numbers on such a field. The update text could say that the basal check does not depend on the field's units.

2. Low: the symbolic dimension check is skipped on dimensionless fields.

   The check runs only when the field's units have a dimension. On a dimensionless field, a lone expression of 300 K, 5 km or 5 mm/yr is now accepted, where bd3a4091 refused it. So is `pi*expr(300 K)`. 300 K is recorded as 0.2308 (300/1300). The tuple form was already accepted at bd3a4091. A level-set `far_field=uw.expression(q 1 m)` on a dimensionless field gives 1e-4. Yet the bare quantity `q(300 K)` is refused on the same field ("has the dimension [temperature], and the field … is in dimensionless"), and so is a `far_field` of `q(1 m)`. An expression with a dimension, on a field that has none, could be refused the same way.

3. Low: a symbolic value with no units is taken in model units.

   This is the documented rule ("one with no units, or dimensionless, is taken"). But it lets the plain-number ambiguity through inside an expression. On a K field with a 1300 K reference, `1300*sin(pi*y)`, `exp(-y)`, a `uw.expression` holding a plain 0.5, and an `expr(q 0.5 dimensionless)` are all read as multiples of 1300 K. Also, a one-element 1-d array in a tuple, `(np.array([0.5]),)`, passes as 0.5 in model units (as at bd3a4091). It is worth a sentence in the update text, "taken in model units", rather than more code here.

4. Low: the tuple dimension check is a behaviour change for existing scripts.

   In a model with reference quantities, the mesh coordinates carry the length units. A coordinate profile in a tuple was accepted on development and bd3a4091 and read in model units. On head it is refused. Examples: `(x*(1 - x), 0)` or `(0.5*x, 0)` on a velocity, and `(1 - y,)` on a temperature. Refusing it is right, and `test_0033`/`test_0035` show the conversion (one model velocity times the profile in one model length). The update text lists `y` and `1 - y` on a K field, but not that tuples which worked before now fail. Saying so, as the text does for the short-value rule, would help users.

5. Low: more values than components are accepted (not new).

   `(0.0, 0.0, 0.0)` on a 2-component velocity is recorded with components [0, 1, 2], on head, bd3a4091 and development. The new "fewer values" check could also refuse "more values" with the same message.

## The update text

It is accurate on the round-3 changes and on the runs it lists: `test_0774` 30 passed, `test_0035` 4 passed at np 2, and `test_0033`'s datum unchanged. Corrections:
- The basal-traction item ("checks each quantity against a stress itself, and refuses a plain entry naming it") holds for lists. As built, it also refuses a Matrix row and sympy zeros (finding 1). Once that is fixed, the sentence stands. It could add that the check applies on fields with or without units.
- "A symbolic value is dimension-checked … One with no units, or dimensionless, is taken": add "in model units" and "on a field whose units have a dimension" (findings 2 and 3). Add that a coordinate profile such as `x*(1 - x)` on a velocity, accepted before, is now refused (finding 4).
- "A complex constant (`sympy.I`, `zoo`) is refused as not real": `nan` is refused the same way.
- The heading names 39a4dc3d. The branch head is 9adc2c9a, which holds the test conversion, so the heading could name both.

## Not run

- The full test suite (363 of the test files ran serially). The serial run on bd3a4091 (round 2's 214-file result was used). Parallel runs at np 4.
- 3-D fields and tensor-valued conditions. Pressure-field conditions through `add_condition`.
- Conditions added after a first solve in time-dependent loops.
- The docs examples past their 900 s cap (fault_friction and the Spiegelman benchmark), and those that fail on every build before any condition is set. AnnulusFS and Ex_viscousFingering are still missing from the docs tree.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
