### #1304 r at 5a79b59f: changes requested; UNFILED 51 (bdf01c2a): ready to merge on green from this review, after #1304

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

## #1304 at 5a79b59f

**Verdict at 5a79b59f (local head, to be pushed): changes requested.** The nested-expression finding is fixed for a named expression nested 1, 2 or 3 deep. Through both entry points it gives the direct expression's value exactly, and it builds bitwise the same Annulus, simplex box and structured box as the quantity, with reference quantities and without. One mixed form still goes wrong without reference quantities: a nested expression in arithmetic with a quantity, such as `nested + uw.quantity(10, "km")`. It becomes a sympy `Add`, takes the `unwrap` path and gives the km magnitude. An Annulus built from it has outer radius 6380 and inner radius 3.48e6, silently. Development refused that input with TypeError, so this is still the earlier finding's failure mode, in a narrower form (new finding 2). Everything else re-ran with only intended differences.

### Earlier findings, then and now

| at 3d9ea0be | at 5a79b59f |
|---|---|
| 1. Medium: the body says tutorial 12 stops on a unit error that #1293 fixes | The library side is unchanged, as expected. The notebook fix is a separate docs commit (section 2 below). The PR body still carries the #1293 line, and it can be corrected when the body is next edited. |
| 2. Medium: a nested named expression takes a different path from a direct one; without reference quantities it gives the km magnitude and a silently wrong Annulus | Fixed for the named form at depths 1-3, through both entry points, in all three units modes (table below). Not fixed for a nested expression inside arithmetic with a quantity or another expression (new finding 2). |
| 3. Low: dimensionally inconsistent derivative sums are accepted | Unchanged (earlier behaviour). |
| 4. Low: the derivative arithmetic is incomplete (`M * s.diff(x)`, `**`, `@`, `1/`) | Unchanged. |
| 5. Low: the two entry points disagree on a bare `UnitAwareDerivativeMatrix` | Unchanged. |
| 6. Low: bare Symbol and number pass-through; Matrix return type | Unchanged. |
| 7. Low: tutorial 15 is not repeatable on one rank | Not re-checked. |
| 8. Low: the Annulus test used `approx` on radii | Fixed. The test now uses `np.array_equal` on `_coords`, and the new nested test does the same. |

### Runs

- **Builds.** The change is Python only, and the merge base is unchanged (development da35d141). The 5a79b59f build is the earlier review's 3d9ea0be overlay (the merge-base compiled install with `.py` laid over it), with the two changed files replaced. Every installed `.py` matches 5a79b59f byte for byte, and the 3d9ea0be overlay was re-checked against 3d9ea0be. Every run printed `underworld3.__file__`. All runs were pinned to 4 cores, alongside other jobs on the machine. Logs kept by the reviewer.
- **Earlier probes, re-run on 3d9ea0be and 5a79b59f.** The 3d9ea0be re-run reproduces the earlier logs line for line. On 5a79b59f the derivative-arithmetic probes, the derivative solves and the bitwise projection checks are unchanged. In the `non_dimensionalise` and mesh probes, only the nested rows changed, and each change is the intended one:
  - `uw.scaling` of a nested quantity expression now gives 6.369999999999857 (as the direct expression and the quantity do) with reference quantities, and 6370000.0 without them (it was 6.37 and 6370);
  - the nested-radius Annulus now has the quantity Annulus's coordinate hash.
- **Nested-expression probe (new).** A value was wrapped in a named expression and that expression nested 1, 2 and 3 deep, and the result was compared with the direct expression through `uw.non_dimensionalise` and `uw.scaling.non_dimensionalise`. Values: 6370 km, 3000 K, 5 cm/yr, 1e21 Pa s, 2.5, `sympy.Float(2.5)` and a dimensionless quantity. The three modes were reference quantities, quantities without reference quantities, and no units.

  | mode | rows where depth 1-3 differ from direct: 3d9ea0be | 5a79b59f |
  |---|---|---|
  | reference quantities | 4 (`uw.scaling` gave 6.37 against 6.369999999999857, and so on) | 0 |
  | no reference quantities | 4 (`uw.scaling` gave 6370 against 6370000.0, 3000 against 3000.0, 5 against 0.05, 1e21 against 3.16e28) | 0 |
  | no units | 0 | 0 |

  - A field expression with a nested denominator (`T / nested(ΔT)`, depth 0-3, and an outer expression nested 2 deep) gives `0.5*T` at every depth, on both commits.
  - `get_units` gives km at every depth. The `uw.dimensionalise` round trip gives 6370 km at every depth. The `uw.scaling` round trip gives 6370 km at every depth; on 3d9ea0be it gave 6370.000000000144 km with reference quantities and 6.37 km without.
  - Changing the innermost expression's value is followed: the nested form then reads 3.48.
  - Mixed forms: `2*nested`, `quantity*nested` and `nested/x` match the direct forms on 5a79b59f. `nested + quantity` and `expression(nested + quantity)` do not (new finding 2).
- **Meshes from nested lengths (new), by coordinate hash.** On 5a79b59f, all of these hash the same as the quantity mesh, in both modes, with and without reference quantities:
  - an Annulus with the outer radius, or all three lengths, nested at depth 0-3;
  - an UnstructuredSimplexBox with its lengths nested at depth 0-3;
  - a StructuredQuadBox nested at depth 3.

  On 3d9ea0be the nested meshes differed in the last bits with reference quantities. Without them they were wrong: outer radius 3.48e6 with inner radius 6370, or a 2000-wide box in place of a 2e6-wide one.
- **Solves.** A Poisson problem, with reference quantities (Q = 3e-13 K/s, κ = 1e-6 m²/s, 1300 K top) and with plain numbers. Its source term was:
  - the quantity;
  - the direct expression;
  - the expression nested 1, 2 and 3 deep;
  - `nested*2/2`;
  - `2*nested3 - direct`.

  On both commits, every expression form gives a temperature field bitwise equal to the direct expression's. The source term goes through the symbolic path, which never had the nesting problem. Two observations:
  - A Dirichlet BC from a `uw.expression`, direct or nested, is refused by `add_dirichlet_bc` on both commits: "Unknown quantity type: UWexpression" with reference quantities, and "Unsupported BC conds" without. So there is no expression BC path to compare, and this is earlier behaviour.
  - With reference quantities, the quantity source term and the expression source term give different fields (T max 1.3 against 1.3066). This is `Poisson.f`'s quantity branch, which ignores the time scale. It is older than this PR and is reported in the #1306 re-check.
- **np 2.** The earlier np 2 probe gives the same hash on both ranks, and the expression-radius Annulus is bitwise the quantity one on each rank's local coordinates, as before. The source-term solve at np 2 gives every expression form bitwise equal to the direct one on each rank, and the BC refusal is the same on both ranks.
- **Tests.**
  - The PR's tests (`test_0776`, `test_0777`): 11 passed serially, and 11 passed on each rank at np 2.
  - Against 3d9ea0be, the new nested test fails with reference quantities. Without them it did not finish within a 600 s cap, because the mesh it asks for has its radii the wrong way round.
  - Serial bands `test_05*` to `test_08*`, plus `test_0004`, `test_0023` and `test_1064`, 4 xdist workers: 1689 passed, 0 failed, 23 skipped, 19 xfailed, 1 xpassed. At 3d9ea0be it was 1687 passed; the difference is the two new test cases.
  - Parallel units files at np 2, all passed on both ranks, with the same counts as before: `test_0770` 2, `test_0992` 4, `test_0785` 16 + 1 skipped, `test_0750` 13.

### New findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium: **without reference quantities, a nested expression in arithmetic still takes the `unwrap` path and gives its own-unit magnitude.** With `a = uw.expression("a", uw.quantity(6370, "km"))`, `n = uw.expression("n", a)` and `b = uw.expression("b", uw.quantity(10, "km"))`:

   | input | type | `uw.scaling.non_dimensionalise` | Annulus with inner radius 3480 km |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | `a + uw.quantity(10, "km")` | UWexpression | 6380000.0 | r 3.48e6 to 6.38e6 |
   | `n + uw.quantity(10, "km")` | sympy Add | 6380 | r 6380 to 3.48e6 (outer and inner swapped in size) |
   | `n + b` | sympy Add | 6380 | |
   | `2*n` | UWexpression | 12740000.0 | |

   - On development da35d141 the two `Add` rows raised TypeError ("Cannot convert expression to float"), so this is a refusal that became a silently wrong mesh. 3d9ea0be behaves as 5a79b59f does.
   - With reference quantities the `Add` rows give 6.38 against 6.379999999999857, which is harmless.
   - The asymmetry comes from expression arithmetic: a direct expression folds `a + quantity` into a new named expression holding a quantity, which then takes the quantity path. A nested one gives a plain `Add`.
   - Suggest one of:
     - in `uw.scaling.non_dimensionalise`, convert each quantity-holding atom of a symbolic expression to SI before `unwrap` when there are no reference quantities;
     - refuse such an expression there, as development did;
     - have nested expressions fold like direct ones in `__add__`.

   A test row for `Annulus(radiusOuter=nested + uw.quantity(10, "km"))` against the quantity would cover it.
3. Low: none new.

### Not run

- np 3 and above, and the full `scripts/test.sh --parallel-only` run: the four units files and the PR's tests at np 2 instead.
- Test bands outside 05-08, apart from the three extra files.
- The 26 docs units files were not re-run. The new commit changes only the nested-expression branch of the two entry points, and the earlier sweep's files use no nested expressions.
- Tutorial 15 repeatability.

## UNFILED 51 (tutorial 12) at bdf01c2a, on top of #1304

**Verdict: ready to merge on green from this review, after #1304.** The change is docs only: one file, `docs/beginner/tutorials/12-Units_System.ipynb`, 8 lines added and 2 removed. It does two things:
- `(temperature/velocity[0]).units` becomes `uw.get_units(temperature / velocity[0])`, with a two-line comment;
- the cell that shows an incompatible sum failing gets the `raises-exception` tag.

With #1304 underneath, the notebook executes to its end.

### Base

- The commit sits on its own branch, `docs/tutorial-12-units-of-an-expression`, based on development fbef72fa, not on #1304. It needs #1304, which is not in development yet.
- The notebook is byte-identical at fbef72fa, da35d141 and 5a79b59f. So a local cherry-pick onto 5a79b59f applied cleanly, with no conflicts, giving a local commit with the same one-file diff.
- Merge order: #1304 first, then this branch, rebased or merged with development.

### Runs

Tutorial 12 ran in three ways, each with a 900 s cap:
- executed as a notebook (`jupyter nbconvert --execute`, which honours `raises-exception`), with one cell added in front to print `underworld3.__file__`;
- converted to a script and run under `ipython`;
- converted to a script and run under plain `python`.

| build and notebook | notebook execute | ipython script | plain python |
|---|---|---|---|
| #1304 (5a79b59f), notebook without 51 | exit 1: AttributeError at `(temperature/velocity[0]).units`, "'MutableDenseMatrix' object has no attribute 'units'" | exit 1, same cell | exit 1 at the `%%timeit` cell (`get_ipython` undefined) |
| #1304 + 51 | **exit 0**, all cells run; the intended DimensionalityError is recorded in the tagged cell | exit 1 at the intended DimensionalityError in the last code cell (that is, it ran to the end) | exit 1 at `%%timeit` |
| development fbef72fa, notebook without 51 | exit 1: TypeError at `uw.non_dimensionalise(temperature / velocity[0])`, "Cannot non-dimensionalise object of type MutableDenseMatrix" | exit 1, same cell | exit 1 at `%%timeit` |
| development + 51 (the branch as pushed) | exit 1, same TypeError, one cell earlier than the changed one | exit 1, same | exit 1 at `%%timeit` |

On #1304 + 51, the units the cells print:
- `uw.get_units(temperature / velocity[0])` gives `second * kelvin / meter`, which is K/(m/s), with velocity in m/s;
- `temperature.diff(x).units` gives `kelvin / kilometer`;
- the derivative cell prints `dT/dy units = kelvin / kilometer`.

So the branch needs #1304: on development alone the notebook stops at the cell before the one this commit changes.

### Findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium: none.
3. Low:
   - The plain-script form stops at the notebook's `%%timeit` cell on every build. This is as before, and it is not a problem for a notebook, but a docs runner that converts notebooks to plain scripts will count tutorial 12 as failing.
   - Merge order matters: merged alone into development, the notebook still fails, one cell earlier. The branch description should say that it depends on #1304.

### Not run

- The rendered docs build.
````

### #1306 r at 2e0c6176: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at 2e0c6176 (local head, to be pushed): changes requested.** The earlier finding is fixed. `Poisson.f` now takes a raw pint quantity and records the same value as the `uw.quantity` form. Solves are bitwise identical, serially in all three units modes and at np 2. The fold-in also changed one thing that worked on development, which is why changes are requested. A raw *dimensionless* pint quantity given as the source term of the other solvers (Darcy, AdvDiffusion, AdvDiffusionSLCN, Diffusion, NavierStokesSLCN, the composed Eulerian AdvDiffusion) used to be accepted as 2.0. It is now refused with `SympifyError` (finding 2.1). The fix is small. A second finding is older than this PR but now applies to raw pint too: `Poisson.f` given a quantity ignores the quantity's units (finding 2.2).

## Earlier findings, then and now

| at 75cb427b | at 2e0c6176 |
|---|---|
| 2. Medium: `Poisson.f` refused `1e-12 * uw.units("K/s")` (DimensionalityError) and took `uw.quantity(1e-12, "K/s")` | Fixed. The raw form records the same value as the quantity in all three modes (`-6.67e-16` with scaling on, `-1e-12` with it off and with no reference quantities). The new `test_a_source_term_takes_a_raw_quantity[Poisson]` covers it. |
| 3. Low: the `not isinstance(value, UWQuantity)` guard in `from_raw_pint` never fires | Unchanged. Harmless. `from_raw_pint` now calls the `quantity` factory, which returns `UWQuantity(value, units)` unchanged, so no value changes. |
| 3. Low: a numpy-array quantity as a body force works raw but not as `uw.quantity` | Unchanged (outside this diff). |
| 3. Low: the thermal convection tutorial prints stray units on `Ra` | Unchanged (outside this diff). |

## What changed since 75cb427b

One commit: `from_raw_pint(value)` at the top of seven `f` setters: Poisson, Darcy, AdvDiffusion, Diffusion and NavierStokes in `solvers.py`, and the composed AdvDiffusion in `advection_diffusion_eulerian.py`. `from_raw_pint` now builds through `quantity(...)` rather than `UWQuantity(...)`. One new parametrised test in `test_0778`.

## Runs

- **Builds.** Python-only change. The head build is the earlier review's merge-base install (development 657d984e, built fresh for that review) with the 2e0c6176 `.py` laid over it. Every installed `.py` matches 2e0c6176 byte for byte. The "old" build is the earlier review's 75cb427b overlay, checked the same way against 75cb427b. Every run printed `underworld3.__file__`. All runs were pinned to 4 cores, alongside other jobs on the machine. Logs kept by the reviewer.
- **Earlier probes, re-run on 75cb427b and 2e0c6176.** These are the parameter, expression, body-force, entry-point, NavierStokes `rho`/`penalty`/body-force and no-units-variable probes, in the three units modes (reference quantities with scaling on, with scaling off, none). The 75cb427b re-run reproduces the earlier review's logs line for line. On 2e0c6176 the only changes are intended:
  - `poisson.f.raw` now records the quantity form's value (three modes, and the no-units-variable probe);
  - `advdiff.f.raw` (SLCN) now fails with the quantity form's `SympifyError` instead of `DimensionalityError`.
- **Source-term probe (new).** The same heating rate, raw and as `uw.quantity`, went into each of the seven `f` setters, in K/s, in K/Myr, with the wrong dimension (Pa) and dimensionless, in the three modes, serially and at np 2.
  - Raw equals `uw.quantity` in every row on 2e0c6176: 28 of 28 per mode, and 28 of 28 at np 2. On 75cb427b, every raw row with units raised `DimensionalityError`.
  - Only Poisson takes a quantity at all. The other six refuse both forms with the same `SympifyError: <value> [<units>]` (for example `SympifyError: 1.0 [pascal]`), which is what the commit message says.
  - At np 2 both ranks gave the same result in every row: same values, same refusals, same messages.
- **Wrong dimension.** The six non-Poisson setters refuse a Pa source in both forms with the same message on every rank. `Poisson.f` does not refuse it, in either form: `uw.quantity(1.0, "Pa")` and `1.0 * uw.units.Pa` both record `1/1500` with scaling on, and 1.0 otherwise, on every rank. This matches the merge base for the quantity form (finding 2.2).
- **Solves.** A Poisson problem (diffusivity raw or `uw.quantity`, `f = 0.3 K/s` raw or `uw.quantity`, quantity BCs) gave bitwise identical temperature fields for raw, quantity, and raw-`f`-only inputs:
  - serially in all three modes;
  - at np 2 with scaling on and with no reference quantities, on each rank.

  On 75cb427b the raw forms raised. The earlier Stokes + Poisson solve scripts (`solve.py`, `solve_b.py`) give the same hashes on both commits and at np 2, as the earlier review recorded.
- **Tests.**
  - `test_0778_raw_pint_quantities.py`: 7 passed serially, and 7 passed on each rank at np 2. The two new cases fail on 75cb427b, as intended.
  - Serial bands `test_00*` and `test_05*` to `test_08*`, 4 xdist workers: 2395 passed, 0 failed, 25 skipped, 19 xfailed, 1 xpassed. At 75cb427b it was 2392 passed and 1 failed, the `test_0849` colormap test that depends on worker scheduling; it did not recur this time.
  - Parallel units files at np 2, all passed on both ranks, with the same counts as before: `test_0750` 13, `test_0755` 11, `test_0770` 2, `test_0774` 7, `test_0785` 16 + 1 skipped, `test_0992` 4.

## New findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium:
   1. **A raw dimensionless quantity as a non-Poisson source term is now refused (regression from development).** On development 657d984e and on 75cb427b, `solver.f = 2.0 * uw.units.dimensionless` was recorded as `Matrix([[2.0]])` by Darcy, AdvDiffusion, AdvDiffusionSLCN, Diffusion, NavierStokesSLCN and the composed AdvDiffusion, in all three modes. On 2e0c6176 it raises `SympifyError: 2.0 [dimensionless]`, because `from_raw_pint` turns it into a `UWQuantity`, which these setters cannot sympify. A dimensionless pint quantity is what a ratio of two raw quantities gives, so this can reach working scripts. The same conversion changes `Poisson.f` with scaling on, from 2.0 to 0.00133 (2/1500, divided by the temperature scale); this now equals the `uw.quantity(2.0, "dimensionless")` form. Suggest: either leave a dimensionless quantity as its magnitude in these six setters, or have them take a `UWQuantity` the way `Poisson.f` does. Add a dimensionless row to `test_a_source_term_takes_a_raw_quantity`.
   2. **Older than this PR: `Poisson.f` given a quantity ignores the quantity's units.** This holds on the merge base and on both commits, and raw pint now takes the same path. The setter takes `float(value.value)`, the magnitude in whatever units it was given. With scaling on, it divides that magnitude by the unknown's scale only. With reference quantities set (1000 km, 5 cm/yr, 1500 K), the same heating rate gives:

      | `f` | recorded | T max |
      |---|---|---|
      | `uw.quantity(0.3, "K/s")` | 2e-4 | 1.07 |
      | `uw.quantity(9.47e12, "K/Myr")` (same rate) | 6.3e9 | 1.25e12 |
      | `uw.expression("H", uw.quantity(0.3, "K/s"))` | (symbolic) | 2.5e13 |
      | `uw.expression` of the K/Myr form | (symbolic) | 2.5e13 |

      Only the expression route converts units and includes the time scale. The two quantity forms disagree with it, and with each other, by up to 13 orders of magnitude. A Pa source is accepted without a word. This PR is not the cause and does not make it worse for `uw.quantity` users, but it deserves its own issue: the quantity branch of `Poisson.f` should go through `uw.non_dimensionalise`, as the expression route does.
3. Low:
   - `test_a_source_term_takes_a_raw_quantity[AdvDiffusion]` passes when both forms raise the same exception type, so it checks consistency, not that the source term works. That fits the commit message. A comment saying that AdvDiffusion takes no quantity in either form would stop a later reader from taking the pass for support.

## Not run

- np 3 and above.
- The full `scripts/test.sh --parallel-only` sweep: only the parallel units files listed above.
- Notebook tutorials. The thermal convection example was not re-run, because this commit touches only `f` setters and `from_raw_pint`, and the example sets no source term.
````

### #1274 at e3691d1c: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at e3691d1c: changes requested.** The test change is right, and I could reproduce every claim in the PR body. The odd-count case now runs and catches a mutant that development's version of the test misses. The broadcast directory fixes the HDF5 failure under mpirun. One small change is still needed: nothing in `scripts/test.sh` runs this file under mpirun, so CI never runs the parallel half of the PR (finding 1). Adding one line fixes that.

## Method

- Test-only PR: one file, `tests/test_0768_manifold_read_timestep.py`, +19/−7. I read it against its merge base with development (c20d352b). I ran it against a clean development install at fbef72fa, the current tip, using the test file from the branch and, for comparison, the one from development. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`, and every log shows the installed development package.
- To test the guard, I copied the installed package and changed one line in each copy, giving two mutants of `discretisation_mesh_variables.py`:
  - **Mutant A** reverts the saved-cloud fix from 3755d4ff: `_saved_cloud_stats(data_file, self.mesh.dim)` instead of `cdim`. This is the reshape that the docstring says raised on an odd count.
  - **Mutant B** reverts the #870 fix in `read_timestep` from 6ea892d8: `cdim = self.mesh.dim`.
- Parallel runs went through `scripts/mpi_supervisor.py --silence 120`, on 4 cores, with other jobs on the machine. I also checked `scripts/test.sh --print-shards` and `scripts/check_test_coverage.py` on the branch, and a `git merge-tree` against fbef72fa.

## Results

**Node counts on this machine.** I checked these at np 1 to 4. The global count is the same at every np. The per-rank stratum counts are not.

| cell size | 0.4 | 0.3 | 0.28 | 0.35 | 0.27 | 0.45 | 0.31 | 0.25 | 0.32 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| global nodes | 162 | 192 | **227** | 162 | **229** | 162 | **197** | 272 | **161** |

The odd case picks 0.28 (227 nodes). The table matches the PR body.

**test_0768, per rank:**

| | development's test | this branch's test |
|---|---|---|
| serial | 1 passed, 1 skipped ("no candidate cell size gives a node count of parity 1") | 2 passed |
| np 2 | 2 failed on each rank (HDF5 `H5Fcreate`/`H5Fflush`, each rank writing its own `tmp_path`) | 2 passed on each rank |
| np 3 | hangs in the parity selection; the supervisor killed it at 120 s of silence | 2 passed on each rank |
| np 4 | 2 failed on each rank (HDF5) | 2 passed on each rank |

The np 3 hang on development is worth noting. Its parity test used the rank-local stratum count, which includes ghosts. At 0.28 that count is odd on one rank and even on the others (87, 92, 93). The supervisor's stacks show rank 1 inside the next `SphericalManifold()` while ranks 0 and 2 are already in `_set_vec()`. So the switch to the global count is needed, not cosmetic: without it the mesh choice enters a collective on some ranks only.

**Repeats.** I ran the branch's test 5 times at np 2 and 5 times at np 3. All 10 runs passed with 2 passed on every rank, and no run hung.

Only rank 0's directory receives files (`m870.mesh.00000.h5`, `.xdmf`, `m870.mesh.T870.00000.h5`); the other ranks' `tmp_path` directories stay empty. Several ranks do share the `.h5` files, but they write them through PETSc's collective HDF5 viewer, and only rank 0 writes the XDMF. I found no file written independently by several ranks. pytest's normal retention removed the numbered directories of earlier runs; only the last run's directories remained. The broadcast is the same pattern already used in `test_0003_save_load.py` and the `test_0005_xdmf_*` files.

**Mutants.** I ran these with `-x`.

| | serial, branch test | np 2, branch test | np 3, branch test | serial, development test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mutant A (saved-cloud reshape by `dim`) | **fails**: [1] `ValueError: cannot reshape array of size 681 into shape (2)` | **fails**: [0] `RuntimeError` (the #640 coincident-DOF refusal, from scrambled rows) | **fails**: [1] raises on rank 0 alone, the other ranks hang, supervisor kill | **passes**: 1 passed, 1 skipped |
| Mutant B (#870 reshape by `dim`) | **fails**: [0] `coordinateArray must have shape (n, cdim)` | **fails** | **fails** | fails |

So the odd case does what the PR intends: it is the only case that catches Mutant A, serially and at np 3, and development's version of the test misses Mutant A completely.

**Neighbours.** I ran `test_0003_save_load`, `test_0005_xdmf_compat`, `test_0005_xdmf_manifold_and_checkpoint`, `test_0767_swarm_after_a_mover`, both `test_0768_*` files, `test_0782_mesh_coordinates_are_degree_one` and `parallel/test_0757_swarm_read_timestep_mpi` in one pytest process:

- **Serial:** the branch gives 65 passed, 2 skipped; development gives 64 passed, 3 skipped. The difference is the odd case.
- **np 2:** the branch gives 61 passed, 3 failed, 3 errors on each rank; development gives 59 passed, 5 failed, 3 errors. The two extra failures on development are the two test_0768 cases. The other failures are the same on both sides: setup errors in `test_0005_xdmf_manifold_and_checkpoint`, two `test_0782` assertions, and `test_0767[nodes]`. They are in files that `test.sh` runs serially only, and this PR does not touch them.

**Wiring and tiers.** On both sides the file runs serially only, through `batch 2 tests/test_05*py tests/test_06*py tests/test_07*py` (shard 2). `--print-shards` gives `[1,2,3,4,5,6]`, and the coverage check passes: "all 496 test files are reachable". The markers `level_1`, `tier_b` are unchanged and follow CLAUDE.md, where a proven bug-fix test starts at tier B.

**Merge.** `git merge-tree` against fbef72fa is clean. Development has changed `scripts/test.sh` since the merge base, but this PR does not touch that file.

## Findings

1. Medium: the mpirun half of the PR is not run by anything. `scripts/test.sh` runs mpirun on `tests/parallel/test_*.py` and on named files. `tests/test_0768_manifold_read_timestep.py` is neither, so CI only ever runs it serially. A regression back to per-rank directories, or back to a rank-local parity, would go unnoticed. The fix is one line, following the precedent of `tests/test_1184_general_pressure_split.py` in the always-on block (shard 6). Run it at np 3, because np 3 is where development's rank-local parity hung and where Mutant A shows up as a rank-0-only raise:
   ```
   OMPI_MCA_rmaps_base_oversubscribe=1 $SUPERVISE mpirun -n 3 python -m pytest --with-mpi \
     tests/test_0768_manifold_read_timestep.py || status=1
   ```
   It takes about 3 s, and it passed 5 times out of 5 at np 3 here. If the authors would rather not wire it, the PR body and the module docstring should stop presenting the mpirun run as a tested property.
2. Low: the odd case can still skip without notice on another machine. All nine candidates are counts measured on this workstation; the earlier commit 3755d4ff measured 195 and 164 nodes elsewhere. On a host where all nine come out even, the case that guards Mutant A turns back into a skip, which is how this PR's problem went unnoticed in the first place. One option is to make the parity-1 skip fail when an environment flag set by CI is present. Another is to build the odd count from a mesh whose node count does not depend on gmsh. At a minimum, the docstring should say that the counts are from one host.
3. Low: `_global_node_count` creates a new global vector on every call and never destroys it: up to nine meshes plus the assertion. `mesh.dm.getCoordinates().getSize() // mesh.cdim` reads the existing global coordinate vector, gives the same number without allocating, or the new vector could be destroyed after use.

## Not run

- CI, because GitHub writes are paused.
- The full `scripts/test.sh` shard 2 serial batch, and the full test suite. Only the neighbour set above was run.
- np above 4, and multi-node runs. A broadcast `tmp_path` assumes every rank sees rank 0's temporary directory, which holds on one node, the same as for the existing `test_0003` and `test_0005` files.
- Other hosts' gmsh, so I could not check whether the odd case runs on CI's runner (finding 2).

Logs kept by the reviewer.
````
