### UNFILED 53 at f83d4465: ready to merge on green from this review

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at f83d4465 (local, to be pushed as a PR): ready to merge on green from this review.** The neighbour bands completed afterwards (addendum).

This is the fix for the defect an earlier review of #1306 reported as finding 2.2: `Poisson.f` given a quantity ignored the quantity's units and the time scale, so the same heating rate in K/s and in K/Myr gave answers 13 orders of magnitude apart, and only the `uw.expression` route was right. On f83d4465 a quantity source term is converted by its own dimension in every `f` setter. With scaling on, all seven setters record the hand value for K/s, K/Myr, W/m³, a dimensionless quantity, and the raw pint form of each, and every solve with a quantity source matches the same solve with the source as an expression (bitwise in K/s and K/Myr on one rank and at np 2, within 4e-15 otherwise), on every rank at np 1, 2 and 3.

## Method

- **Builds.** Python-only change. The base and head runs use a clean install of development fbef72fa, the branch's base, with each tree's `.py` files laid over a copy of it. Every overlaid `.py` file matches its tree byte for byte. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`. Three variants: base fbef72fa, head f83d4465, and fbef72fa with this branch and #1306 (2e0c6176) merged. All runs were pinned to 4 cores, alongside other jobs on the machine. Logs kept by the reviewer.
- **Recorded values.** The same source term as `uw.quantity`, as raw pint and as `uw.expression(name, uw.quantity(...))`, into each of the seven `f` setters: Poisson, Darcy, AdvDiffusion, AdvDiffusionSLCN, Diffusion, NavierStokesSLCN and the composed Eulerian AdvDiffusion. Five sources: 3e-14 K/s, the same rate in K/Myr, 1e-6 W/m³, 1 Pa (the wrong dimension) and 2 (dimensionless). The value the solver reads (`unwrap(f, keep_constants=False)`) is compared with a hand value computed with pint alone: SI divided by the scales from 1000 km, 5 cm/yr, 1e21 Pa s and 1500 K with scaling on, and the SI magnitude otherwise. Three modes: reference quantities with scaling on, with scaling off, and no reference quantities. Also a bare number and an `np.float64` in every setter.
- **Solves.** A steady Poisson problem on a 1000 km square (Q2, 8 × 8, Dirichlet 300 K top and 1600 K bottom, diffusivity 1e-6 m²/s, source 3e-14 K/s, whose quadratic part peaks at 3750 K), with the source as `uw.quantity` and raw pint in K/s and in K/Myr, as an expression in each, and as 9e-8 W/m³ with a 3 W/m/K conductivity, which is the same problem. Each field is compared with the expression-K/s field and with the analytic solution. Then one 1 Myr step of Diffusion and of AdvDiffusionSLCN (V = 0, zero walls) with the same source forms. Without reference quantities, a plain SI float as well. Three modes, serially; scaling on at np 2 and np 3; no reference quantities at np 2.
- **Wrong dimension in parallel.** The recorded-value probe at np 2 and np 3, with the last rank holding no cells (a shell partition hook), every rank reporting its own result and whether the ranks agree.
- **Merge with #1306** (raw pint, 2e0c6176). Merged locally with this branch into fbef72fa, and every probe re-run on the merge.
- **Docs and tutorials.** Searched `docs/` and `tests/` for every `f` set from a quantity. Two set one: tutorial 13 (`13-Scaling-problems-with-physical-units.ipynb`, `poisson_nd.f = uw.quantity(2.0, "K/m**2")`) and `tests/test_0817_poisson_nd.py` (`uw.quantity(10.0, "kelvin")`). The developer guides `HOW-TO-WRITE-UW3-SCRIPTS.md` and `UW3-SCRIPT-WRITING-CHEAT-SHEET.md` show `poisson.f = uw.quantity(2.0, "K")` in snippets. Tutorial 13 was executed on the base, the head and the merge, and its outputs compared cell by cell.
- **Tests.** The new `test_0787_source_term_quantities.py` on the base and the head; with it, `test_0817_poisson_nd.py` and `test_0812_poisson_with_units.py` (the Poisson units tests) on all three variants, and #1306's `test_0778_raw_pint_quantities.py` on the merge.

## Results

- **Recorded values, scaling on.** On the head all 105 rows (7 setters × 5 sources × 3 forms) equal the hand value to 1e-12, on every rank at np 1, 2 and 3. On the base, 42 of 105: the expression form, and the raw dimensionless form. Poisson's quantity form recorded the magnitude divided by 1500 (2e-17 for 3e-14 K/s, where 1.26e-2 is right; 6.3e-4 for the K/Myr form). Darcy, AdvDiffusion, AdvDiffusionSLCN, Diffusion, NavierStokesSLCN and the composed AdvDiffusion refused both quantity forms (`SympifyError`, or `DimensionalityError` for raw pint).
- **Recorded values, scaling off and no reference quantities.** On the head, 98 of 105 rows equal the SI value. The 7 others are the expression form of the K/Myr source, in every setter, which records 0.947 (the magnitude in K/Myr) where 3e-14 is the SI value. That is the expression route, which this PR does not touch, and it is the same on the base (finding 3.1). The quantity and raw forms are right in every row.
- **Solves, scaling on.**

  | source | base T max (K) | head T max (K) |
  |---|---|---|
  | `uw.quantity` K/s | 1600 (source lost) | 4722.667479 |
  | `uw.quantity` K/Myr | 1600 (source lost) | 4722.667479 |
  | raw K/s, raw K/Myr | DimensionalityError | 4722.667479 |
  | `uw.quantity` W/m³ with conductivity | 1600 (source lost) | 4722.667479 |
  | expression, K/s or K/Myr or W/m³ | 4722.667479 | 4722.667479 |

  On the head every Poisson field is bitwise identical to the expression-K/s field, except the conductivity form, which agrees to 4e-15. All of them match the analytic solution to 1.4e-5. The Diffusion and SLCN steps are bitwise identical across all six source forms on the head (T max 1.2092 K and 1.2263 K); on the base the four quantity forms raised.
- **Solves without reference quantities.** On the head every quantity form, in K/s, K/Myr and W/m³, is bitwise identical to the plain SI float, and Poisson matches the analytic solution to 1.4e-5. The expression form in K/Myr is off by 2.5e13 (finding 3.1). On the base the Poisson quantity form in K/Myr was off by the same factor.
- **Parallel.** With scaling on, at np 2 and at np 3, every rank records the hand value in all 105 rows, with the last rank holding no cells (checked: 0 local cells on that rank, 42 or 20 and 22 on the others). The Poisson, Diffusion and SLCN solves give the same agreement as serially, on every rank: bitwise at np 2, and within 1.4e-15 of the expression field at np 3. T max moves by up to 6e-6 between np 1, 2 and 3, the same for every source form, which is within the solver's tolerance. Without reference quantities at np 2, the same as serially. On the base at np 2, both ranks gave the same values and the same refusals.
- **Wrong dimension.** A Pa source is not refused, by any setter, in any form, on any rank (finding 2.1). Every setter records 1 Pa as 6.3e-7, its value in model units, the same as the expression route. At np 2 and np 3 with an empty rank, every rank records the same value. The one refusal this setter family has, Poisson's of a nonzero bare number in a units model, gives the same message on every rank.
- **Merge with #1306.** One textual conflict, in the composed Eulerian AdvDiffusion `f` setter, where both branches replaced the same line. The resolution keeps both: `from_raw_pint`, then `_source_term_in_model_units`. In `solvers.py` the merge is clean, and each setter then converts raw pint twice (once in `from_raw_pint`, once in the helper), with the same result. Every probe gives the same output on the merge as on the head, in all three modes and at np 2 and 3. The two PRs overlap in purpose, as the PR body says, and the helper here makes #1306's `from_raw_pint` calls in these setters redundant.
- **Docs and tutorials.** No answer changes. Tutorial 13 runs to the end (21 code cells, no error) on the base, the head and the merge, and every Poisson and comparison output is identical; the only differing outputs are file paths in warnings and pyvista widget addresses. Its reference scales are 1 m and 1 K, so K/m² divided by the field's scale (the base) and by its own scales (the head) are both 2. `test_0817` sets kelvin on a kelvin field, where the two conversions agree too. The guide snippets (kelvin on a kelvin field) are unaffected for the same reason.
- **Tests.** `test_0787`: 5 failed on the base, 5 passed on the head, as the PR body says. `test_0817` + `test_0812`: 8 passed on the base; with `test_0787`, 13 passed on the head; with `test_0787` and `test_0778`, 20 passed on the merge. The bands below were not completed.
- **Other changes of behaviour, all intended.** A dimensionless quantity or a quantity without units in `Poisson.f` with scaling on is now its value (2), where the base divided it by the field's scale (0.00133); the other setters already gave 2. `uw.quantity(0, "K/s")` is now taken by Diffusion, which refused it on the base.

## Findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium:
   1. **A source of the wrong dimension is accepted without a word.** `poisson.f = uw.quantity(1.0, "Pa")` records 6.3e-7 and solves, in every setter and every form, on every rank. The expression route does the same, so this PR does not make it worse, but the setter now knows the quantity's dimension and could check it. For the time-dependent solvers (AdvDiffusion, AdvDiffusionSLCN, Diffusion and the composed AdvDiffusion) the source must have the unknown's dimension per time, so a check is easy. For Poisson and Darcy it depends on the constitutive parameter, so a check would belong at solve time. A follow-up issue is enough; it need not hold this PR.
   2. **The new tests use sources that are dimensionally inconsistent with their equations.** `test_a_source_quantity_is_recorded_by_its_own_dimension` sets K/m² on Poisson, Darcy and AdvDiffusion, and the solve test sets K/(m² s) on a Poisson problem with diffusivity in m²/s, where K/s is the consistent source. They check the conversion, and the conversion is right, but as examples they teach the wrong units, and a later dimension check (2.1) would refuse them. Suggest K/s with the m²/s diffusivity in the solve test, and K/s for AdvDiffusion. The same holds for the PR body's headline example (`poisson.f = uw.quantity(2, "K/m**2")` is consistent only with a dimensionless diffusivity) and for tutorial 13's comment "for ∇²T = Q, where T is in [K], Q must be in [K/m²]", next to a diffusivity in m²/s.
3. Low:
   1. **Outside this PR: the expression route ignores a source's units when scaling is off or there are no reference quantities.** `uw.expression("H", uw.quantity(0.946728, "K/Myr"))` is read as 0.946728, not 3e-14, so the field is 2.5e13 times too hot. This PR's quantity route is the right one in that row. Worth its own issue.
   2. **Outside this PR: scaling off with reference quantities does not run in SI.** With `use_nondimensional_scaling(False)` after `set_reference_quantities`, the Poisson field's stored maximum is 1.0667 (1600 K / 1500 K), so the boundary values are in model units while the helper's source (and the diffusivity) is SI; the heating is lost (T max 1600 K, where 4722.7 K is right), on the base and the head, by every route. The helper's docstring says fields run in SI in that mode; they do not, here. This mode needs a decision of its own; the helper does what the expression route does.
   3. **Outside this PR: one SLCN step depends on the units mode.** The same 1 Myr AdvDiffusionSLCN step with V = 0 gives T max 1.2263 K with scaling on and 78.49 K with no reference quantities (Diffusion gives 1.2092 K in both), on the base and the head, by every source route including a plain SI float. Seen in passing; worth an issue.
   4. **Raw pint is converted twice after #1306 merges.** Harmless; whichever lands second can drop one of the two.
   5. **`np.float64` and `np.int64` sources are not refused by Poisson** in a units model where a Python `float` is; `np.int64(3)` records 3. Unchanged by this PR.

## Not run

- **The neighbour bands were started and stopped before they finished, with no result:** `test_07*`, `test_08*` and every serial test file that sets `f`, on the head and the merge (4 xdist workers); and the parallel units and solver files at np 2 (`test_0750`, `test_0755`, `test_0770` units mesh move, `test_0774`, `test_0785`, `test_0992`, `test_0768` natural BC, `test_0787` field dual, `test_1065` boundary flux). These must be green before merging.

- The full serial suite, and the full `scripts/test.sh --parallel-only` sweep: only the files listed above.
- np 4 and above.
- Notebooks other than tutorial 13: no other docs example or tutorial sets `f` from a quantity (searched across `docs/` for `.f =` with `quantity`, `uw.units` or a pint unit, and for heating-rate units).

## Addendum: the neighbour bands, completed

The bands listed under Not run were re-run to completion on the same builds:
- Serial: the `test_07*`/`test_08*` bands plus every test file that sets `f` gave 1817 passed, 27 skipped, 18 xfailed, 1 xpassed, 0 failed on f83d4465, and the same counts on the merge with #1306.
- Parallel at np 2: the nine units and solver files (`test_0750`, `test_0755`, `test_0768` (natural BC), `test_0770`, `test_0774`, `test_0785`, `test_0787`, `test_0992`, `test_1065`) all pass on both the head and the merge, with the same counts.

So the verdict above holds without the qualification.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### UNFILED 47 at c9b92c56: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at c9b92c56 (local, to be pushed as a PR): changes requested.** The fix does what it says. `uw.function.derivative(expr, x, evaluate=False)` now returns a `UWDerivativeExpression` with `.sym` and `.doit()`, and `doit()` equals the evaluated derivative. On the base, the deferred form was a bare sympy `Derivative` whose `doit()` silently gave 0 for any `uw.expression`. Changes are requested for one small regression the wrapper brings in: `doit()` no longer takes sympy's hints, so `b.doit(deep=True)` and `(b + 1).doit(deep=True)` raise `TypeError` (finding 2.1). Two other findings are worth fixing at the same time, because tutorial 3, which this PR is for, shows them: `b.sym.doit()` is 0, and the tutorial's own `b.doit()` cell prints 0 (findings 2.2 and 2.3).

## What the PR fixes

From the PR body. `uw.function.derivative(expr, x, evaluate=False)` returned a bare sympy `Derivative`, which has no `.sym`, for anything with a `.diff` method, and so for every `uw.expression`. It built a `UWDerivativeExpression` only for other objects, and that class was itself broken: its name was the expression's LaTeX, which the expression registry already held, so the constructor handed back the expression being differentiated. Tutorial 3's `b.sym` and `b.doit()` cells failed.

## Method

- **Base and staleness.** The branch's base is development c1691428. Development (fbef72fa) is 43 commits ahead. Neither file this branch changes (`function/__init__.py`, `function/expressions.py`) changed in between. The branch merges into fbef72fa cleanly, and with #1304 (5a79b59f) as well.
- **Builds.** Python-only change. The base and head runs use a clean install of c1691428, with the branch's `.py` files laid over a copy of it. The merged runs use a clean install of fbef72fa, with the merged trees' `.py` files laid over a copy. Every overlaid `.py` file matches its tree byte for byte. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`. Six variants: base c1691428, head c9b92c56, development fbef72fa, development + this branch, development + #1304, development + both. All runs were pinned to 4 cores, alongside other jobs on the machine. Logs kept by the reviewer.
- **Probe.** The deferred derivative of five expressions: a `uw.expression` of a field and a coordinate, in x and in y; a `uw.expression` of a plain symbol; a plain sympy expression; a field symbol times a coordinate; and tutorial 3's form, the derivative with respect to another `uw.expression`. For each one: the type, `.sym`, `doit()` against `evaluate=True`, `unwrap`, `.sym.doit()`, whether two deferred derivatives of the same thing are equal, `evaluate` at points, `b + 1`, `2 * b`, a chained derivative, `(b + 1).doit()`, `b.doit(deep=True)`, `uw.non_dimensionalise(b)` and `uw.get_units(b)`. Also a vector variable, a Matrix expression, a projection of a deferred derivative, and registry growth over 500 calls. Run without and with reference quantities, in all six variants.
- **Tutorial 3** (`3-Symbolic_Forms.ipynb`), executed in all six variants.
- **Probe at np 2.** The same probe on head and development + both, every rank reporting its own result.
- **Tests.** The new `test_0779_deferred_derivative.py` on the base and the head.

## Results

- **The fix.**

  | | base c1691428 | head c9b92c56 |
  |---|---|---|
  | type of `derivative(eta, x, evaluate=False)` | `Derivative` | `UWDerivativeExpression` |
  | `.sym` | AttributeError | `Derivative(eta, x)` |
  | `doit()` equals `evaluate=True` | no (0) | yes, for all five expressions |
  | `(b + 1).doit()` | 1 | `2*x*s + 2*s_{,0}*x + 1` (correct) |
  | `evaluate(b.doit())` at two points | `[0, 0]` | equals the evaluated derivative |
  | `UWDerivativeExpression(eta, x)` | TypeError | builds, and `doit()` is right |
  | vector variable | TypeError | a Matrix of wrappers |

  On the base, every deferred derivative of a `uw.expression` evaluated to 0 without a word. The head fixes that.
- **Unchanged by this PR.** A Matrix expression keeps sympy's elementwise form (`ArrayDerivative`) on both. A deferred derivative cannot be evaluated or compiled on either: `uw.function.evaluate(b, ...)` and a projection of `b` both raise sympy's printer error for `Derivative`, on the base and on the head. `uw.get_units(b)` is `dimensionless` on both, where the evaluated derivative of the same expression has kelvin. 500 deferred derivatives leave the registry the same size.
- **Development and merges.** Development fbef72fa behaves as the base in every probe row. Development + this branch behaves as the head in every row. Adding #1304 changes one row, `uw.non_dimensionalise(b)`: with #1304 it is `Derivative(<the unwrapped expression>, x)`; without, `Derivative(eta, x)` (see finding 3.4). The probe is otherwise identical with and without reference quantities.
- **Tutorial 3.** On development + both PRs, all 40 code cells run without an error. On the head (and on development + this branch) the notebook stops only at cell 9, `s.diff(x) + s.diff(y)`, which needs #1304. On the base, on development and on development + #1304 it stops at cell 9 and/or at cell 29, `b.sym` (AttributeError). Between development + #1304 and development + both, the only output changes are the deferred-derivative cells 27 and 29 (and object ids). Cell 30, `b.doit()`, prints 0 in every variant (finding 2.3). Pyvista's off-screen warning in cell 31 is the same everywhere.
- **Probe at np 2.** Both ranks give the same result in every row but the point evaluations of a field's gradient, which differ between the ranks for the evaluated derivative and for `b.doit()` alike (local `evaluate` near the partition; outside this PR). On every rank `b.doit()` equals the evaluated derivative, and the projection of `b` raises the same printer error on both ranks, with no hang.
- **Tests.** `test_0779`: 3 failed on the base, 3 passed on the head, as the PR body says. The neighbour runs below were not completed.

## Findings

1. High: none.
2. Medium:
   1. **`UWDerivativeExpression.doit()` does not take sympy's hints.** sympy calls `doit(**hints)` on every argument of an expression it is asked to `doit` with hints. On the head, `b.doit(deep=True)` and `(b + 1).doit(deep=True)` raise `TypeError: UWDerivativeExpression.doit() got an unexpected keyword argument 'deep'`. On the base the same calls ran (and gave 0). Suggest `def doit(self, **hints)`, and a test row with `deep=True`.
   2. **`b.sym.doit()` is 0.** `.sym` is `sympy.Derivative(eta, x)` with `eta` a symbol, so sympy treats it as a constant: `b.sym.doit()`, `sympy.simplify(b.sym)` and `sympy.diff(b, x)` all give 0, silently. Only `b.doit()` is right. Tutorial 3 shows `b.sym` in one cell and `b.doit()` in the next, so a reader is likely to try `b.sym.doit()`. Suggest either making `.sym` the derivative of the unwrapped expression (`Derivative(unwrap(expr), x)`, whose `doit()` is right), or saying in the docstring that `.sym` is for display and `b.doit()` is the way to evaluate it.
   3. **Tutorial 3's `b.doit()` cell prints 0.** It runs now, as the PR says. But `b` is the derivative of `viscosity_eff = Min(eta_0, tau_y / (2 edot))` with respect to `eta_0`, a `uw.expression` holding 1. `doit()` goes through `uw.function.derivative`, whose `evaluate=True` path unwraps with `keep_constants=False`; that replaces `eta_0` by 1 before differentiating, so the result is 0. Cell 25 (`viscosity_eff.sym.diff(viscosity)`) gives the right answer, `Heaviside(-eta_0 + tau_y/(2 edot))`, and cell 26 (`derivative(viscosity_eff, viscosity)`) gives 0 on the base too. So the cause is older than this PR, but the PR now routes `doit()` through it and points users at the cell. Suggest that a derivative with respect to a `uw.expression` keep that expression as a symbol while differentiating, or refuse it. If that is for another PR, the tutorial text should not present 0 as the answer.
3. Low:
   1. **Two deferred derivatives of the same expression are not equal.** Each call makes a new uniquely named symbol, so `derivative(eta, x, evaluate=False) == derivative(eta, x, evaluate=False)` is False and their difference does not simplify to 0. On the base they were equal. The test `test_two_deferred_derivatives_are_distinct` checks different variables only. If repeated calls should agree, give the name a deterministic key (expression and variables) and look it up; if not, a sentence in the docstring.
   2. **The developer guide's table** (`docs/developer/UW3_Developers_MathematicalObjects.md`, the row for `uw.function.derivative(expr, x, evaluate=False)`) lists it as the same as `expr.diff(x, evaluate=False)`. The two now return different types (a wrapper and a sympy `Derivative`).
   3. **`uw.get_units` of a deferred derivative is `dimensionless`**, on the base and the head, where the evaluated derivative has kelvin. The wrapper could carry the units of the evaluated form. Outside this diff.
   4. **With #1304:** `uw.non_dimensionalise(b)` returns an unevaluated `Derivative` of the unwrapped expression. Without #1304 it returns `Derivative(eta, x)`, whose `doit()` is 0 (finding 2.2 again). Neither is the model-unit value of the derivative. Worth one line in whichever PR lands second.

## Not run

- **Not run in this pass:** the 72 serial test files that use `.diff`, `gradient`, `UWDerivativeExpression`, `uw.function.derivative` or `evaluate=False`, on the head and on development + both; #1304's `test_0776` and `test_0777` on the merge; and the parallel files touching derivatives at np 2 (`test_0035`, `test_0785`, `test_0786` global evaluate, `test_0787` field dual, `test_0999`, `test_1064`, `test_1066`, `test_1082`, `test_1132`, `test_1176`). They are needed before a green verdict after the changes.

- np 3 and above.
- The full `scripts/test.sh --parallel-only` sweep: only the parallel files listed above.
- The other docs examples that take derivatives (`docs/examples/adjoint/sinker_transcript/inverse_sinker_transcript.py`) use `evaluate=True` only, a path this PR does not change, so they were not run. `docs/examples/WIP/developer_tools/SOpt.py` defers the derivative of a Matrix, which keeps sympy's form, and is work in progress.

## Addendum: the neighbour runs, completed

The runs listed under Not run were re-run to completion:
- Serial: the 72 derivative-heavy test files gave 825 passed, 3 skipped, 4 xfailed, 0 failed on c9b92c56, and 828 passed, 0 failed on development with this branch and #1304 merged (the merged tree collects 3 more matching tests).
- Parallel at np 2: ten files (`test_0035`, `test_0785`, `test_0786`, `test_0787`, `test_0999`, `test_1064`, `test_1066`, `test_1082`, `test_1132`, `test_1176`) pass on both. `test_1064` on the head reported 7 passed, 2 skipped and then did not exit before the supervisor's limit; on the merge it exited 0 with the same counts. That file has stalled at exit the same way in earlier reviews, independent of the branch.

The verdict above is unchanged.

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### labels70-22 r1 (0004 23f0f0b4) on 68 + 70/22 + 72: changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# Round 1 re-check: UNFILED 68 + 70/22 + 72, with 0004 (23f0f0b4)

**Verdict at the 0004 tip (0004 on 0003 on 0002 on 0001 on development fbef72fa): changes requested.**

The parallel hang is gone. I found no library hang in any parallel run: 180 probe runs over round 0's 10 layouts, plus the tolerance, moved-mesh and surface runs. Every rank reaches the same verdict, with a byte-identical message. The coordinate-keyed piece count agrees with a count by global point number in every parallel run on an unmoved mesh, and on meshes moved by all four sanctioned movers. Its two preconditions are in finding 2. The graded-mesh false refusals (round 0, finding 3) are fixed. The author's claim about development holds: every trace the piece check refuses was in pieces, or refused, on development.

What still blocks merging is the tolerance itself, which was round 0's finding 2. The new "local median" does not measure the local cell. At the very points where the chain leaves the trace it grows to 1.1-1.5 cells, and beside the cut's own short edges it shrinks to 0.06-0.08 cells. The result:
- 14 of round 0's 22 lenient passes are still added;
- 15 traces that the 0003 tip refused are now added, three of them with a tip 0.625 cells short;
- round 0's kink120_1 (slip 30 % low) is added, not refused.

The fix is small and is sketched under finding 1: measured on the 23 tolerance cases, the base mesh's own local edge length refuses every case more than half a cell off the trace.

Commits, applied with `git am` on fbef72fa:
- 0001-0003 are tree-identical to round 0 (trees 06b4084c, be3a066f and e58e22c7, the same as round 0's worktree). The patch files differ only in the "[PATCH n/4]" subject prefix.
- 0004 changes `fault_split.py` (133 lines), `line_cut.py` (38) and test_0897 (15), +141/-45 in all:
  - in-place `Allreduce` for the distances (MIN) and the inside mask (MAX);
  - a new `_local_cell_size`: the median length of the edges whose midpoints lie within 3x the point's nearest-midpoint distance, with the MIN and the lengths gathered over the ranks;
  - the tolerance becomes half that size, point by point;
  - a global piece count for add_fault: every rank's labelled edges are gathered and joined at end points keyed by coordinates, deferring to the split when a chain vertex touches a Plus/Minus label;
  - surface advice for add_conforming_surface;
  - 72's loop vectorised through `closure_points`, and its message reworded;
  - the `_CHORD_TO_ARC` comment reworded;
  - test_0897 gains a hand-labelled chain in two pieces.

## Method
- Python only. The build is an overlay of the three changed files (fault_split.py, line_cut.py, discretisation_mesh.py) on a copy of the clean development install. The overlay's other files match fbef72fa byte for byte. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`. Development is the clean install itself.
- Four cores only (taskset), at most 4 processes, pytest -n 4.
- Every parallel run went through the repository's MPI supervisor (silence 180 s) inside an outer timeout.
- Partitions come from round 0's shell-partition hook on the gmsh meshes: band, emptyband (the last rank holds no cell), rand1. Other jobs were running on the machine, so times are indicative.
- A probe-only wrapper ran before the real check on every call. On the dm the check receives, it recomputes:
  - the pieces keyed by coordinates (the patch's rule) and by global point number (through the point SF), and the number of ranks that hold chain edges;
  - the worst excess d/h, and the local h at the worst point;
  - an SF check that compares every shared vertex's coordinates on the ghost rank with the owner's, bit for bit.
- Logs kept by the reviewer.

## Round-0 findings then and now
1. High, the hang (0001, 0002): **fixed.** Both array reductions are now in place. See (a).
2. Medium, the tolerance was too lenient (0001): **not fixed. It is looser at tips and corners.** See (b) and new finding 1.
3. Medium, the cell size was not local on graded meshes (0001): **fixed.** The three false refusals of gaps 0.30-0.47 local cells are now added. In both parts of the x20 mesh the patch's h at the worst point is within 0-6 % of an independent six-nearest-edge median. See (b).
4. Low, test_0897's short case sat 5 % from the threshold: **changed, not as described.** The README says the short case "is now caught by the partition-independent piece count". Measured at np 1-4 on all 10 layouts, the short case's chain is in ONE piece, and the distance test catches it. It does so at a point 0.06 cells off the chain where the local h has collapsed to 0.081 cells (excess 0.74). The real 0.6-cell gap at the tip would also refuse (excess 0.546, h 1.10 cells). See new finding 3.
5. Low, add_conforming_surface's advice: **fixed** ("Cells there are about ...; refine the mesh along the surface, or smooth the trace there").
6. Low, 72's message asked for a sample at an existing one: **fixed.** The advice is now general.
7. Low, test_0898's hairpin docstring: **unchanged** (0004 does not touch test_0898).
8. Low, 72's per-cell Python loop: **fixed**, vectorised through `closure_points`. test_0899 passes; see (e).
9. Low, the 120-degree comment: **fixed.**
- Round 0 also asked for a tests/parallel test of test_0897's add_fault cases. **None was added** (new finding 4).

## (a) Parallel: np 1-4 across round 0's 10 layouts
Layouts: np 1; and np 2, 3 and 4 under band, emptyband and rand1.

The probe has 18 cases, run on each layout:
- round 0's 11: hand-labelled chains on one rank and across every rank, empty, half and whole; test_0897's three add_fault traces; a vertical trace with and without the add-on partition;
- new hand-labelled chains with a one-edge gap, on one rank (`d_one_gap`) and across every rank (`d_all_gap`);
- add_fault with `across_seams=True` for the vertical trace, a horizontal one at y = 0.52 and a diagonal one;
- the horizontal and diagonal traces with the default add-on partition.

Results:
- **180 runs: all exit 0, and every case AGREES on every rank (sha1 of the message equal). No hang.**
- Messages are also identical across layouts wherever the outcome is the same, h values included.
- **Coordinate-keyed pieces equal the global-number pieces in all 180.** The shared-vertex SF check found 0 mismatches.
- **A seam-crossing chain counts as ONE.**
  - `d_all_whole` held edges on 2, 3 or 4 ranks and was accepted everywhere.
  - `af_vert_as` (across_seams) on 2-4 ranks: 1 piece, added under band and emptyband at every np.
  - The default add-on partition gathers the chain onto one rank: 1 piece, added.
  - `af_vert_np` (partition=False) crosses 2-4 ranks and counts as 1 piece. It then reaches the existing seam refusal, the same on every rank.
  - Where across_seams is refused (rand1, or a band seam running along the trace), the refusal is fault_split's existing "a fault facet lies ON the partition seam", after the piece check has passed, and it agrees on every rank.
- **The two-pieces cases** (`d_one_gap`, `d_all_gap`, the gap 0.4 local cells, so the distance test passes) are refused as "in 2 pieces" on all 10 layouts, with one message, including the gap at a band seam.
- **Empty rank:** the emptyband layouts at np 2, 3 and 4 behave exactly as the others.
- **A chain wholly on one rank:** `d_one_*` and the add-on-partitioned add_fault cases.
- **add_conforming_surface's MAX:** round 0's (j) probe at np 1, 2 and 3 under band. The three cases (low, low_part, cross) AGREE, and the messages are identical across np.
- **Grep of the 0004 diff** for lowercase collectives:
  - `allreduce(int(marked.size), SUM)`: a scalar. Fine.
  - `allreduce(int(on_slit), MAX)`: a scalar. Fine.
  - `allgather(mine)`: a list of NumPy arrays, pickled. No operator is applied, so it is correct. It costs one pickled list of dense-point arrays per rank: fine at these sizes, worth a buffer form if traces grow to thousands of samples.
  - `allgather(local)`: a list of coordinate-tuple pairs. Correct.
  - 72's `allreduce(n_bad, SUM)`: a scalar. Fine.
  - 72's `allreduce(worst, MIN)`: a tuple of scalars, compared element by element. Correct.
  - **No NumPy array goes through a pickled reduction any more.**

## (b) Tolerance
### The 22 cases
The 22 are 19 under the every-sample pull and 3 under light+sharp, rebuilt with pull22's generator (seed 22). Four were added to the set:
- the three 0.625-cell tip gaps below;
- the author's kink120_1, on the irregular box at 1 cell.

| outcome at the 0004 tip | cases |
|---|---|
| refused by distance | 3 (box_irr 0.5: la4, kink90_3, kink120_2) |
| refused "in 2 pieces" | 5 (box_irr 0.5 kink150_1; box_reg 1.0 kink120_4, every-sample and sharp; box_reg 0.3 kink120_9, both) |
| **added** | **14** |

The 14 added cases were measured with round 0's tolerance probe. Slip is the sheared-box frictionless fault, against the twin sampled 1 cell apart:
- **7 stop 0.5 cells short of a tip** (one component, 4.5 of 5.0 cells or 5.5 of 6.0):
  - box_irr 0.5: la10, kink10_4, kink10_5, kink30_8, kink90_8;
  - box_reg 0.5: la4, kink90_5.
  - Slip against the twin: kink10_4 -16 %, la10 -8 %, la4 -8 %, kink10_5 -6 %, the rest -1 to -4 %.
- **7 cut a corner** (one component, 4.12-4.38 of 5.0 cells):
  - box_reg 0.5: kink90_3, kink90_8, kink120_2 (every-sample and sharp);
  - box_reg 0.3: kink90_3, kink90_8, kink120_1.
  - Slip: **kink120_2 -33 %** (0.0641 against 0.0961); **kink120_1 -30 %** (0.0605 against 0.0864); kink90_8 at 0.3 -9 %; the others ±4 %.

**kink120_1:**
- Round 0's case (regular box, 0.3 cells) is **added**, not refused. It is one piece (7 edges); its worst point is 0.541 cells from the chain, against a local h of 1.50 cells there (excess 0.36).
- The author's case (irregular box, 1 cell) is refused "in 2 pieces", as the README says.

In every added case, the local h at the gap is 1.0-1.5 nominal cells. At the corners it is 1.13-1.50, so half of it is 0.56-0.75 cells.

### Partition independence
All 26 cases at np 1, 2 and 3 (default partition): **the same message on every rank and at every np (26/26), h values included.** The gathered median works as intended.

### The 480-trace pull grid (kinks and lines/arcs, both boxes, 1, 0.5 and 0.3 cells)

| | 0003 tip | 0004 tip |
|---|---|---|
| returned | 287 | 279 |
| trace-to-chain > 0.5 cells | 19 | 21 |
| of which > 0.500001 cells | 5 (max 0.568) | 7 (max **0.625**) |

Moves between the two tips:
- 23 traces that 0003 added are refused at 0004: 18 by pieces, 5 by distance.
- **15 that 0003 refused are added at 0004.** Among them:
  - box_reg 0.3 kink10_6, box_irr 0.3 kink30_8 and box_irr 0.3 kink90_7, all with a tip **0.625 cells short**. Slip: -12 %, -1 % and -16 % (kink90_7 also cuts its corner, 4.19 of 5 cells);
  - box_reg 0.3 la17, 0.6 cells short;
  - eleven at exactly 0.5 cells.
- **No returned mesh has its F chain in more than one piece.**

### No false refusal
- The subduction-zone annulus fixture's fault traces (fault inset half a cell), forward and reversed, on the uniform annulus and the annulus adapted x4 toward the fault:
  - at 1, 2 and 4 cells: all added;
  - at 0.3 and 0.5 cells: also added. On the uniform annulus at 0.5 cells this includes the forward trace that round 0's tips refused (gap 0.43 local cells).
- The x20 graded boxes (blob and band): every case that development can cut is added, gaps 0-0.47 local cells. **0 false refusals, 0 missed partials**, classed against an independent local cell (the median of the 6 nearest edges at the worst point).
- test_1079, test_1092 and test_0559: in the suite, see (e).

### x20 graded mesh: is it local in both parts?
Yes for the gaps that matter. At the former false refusals the patch's h at the worst point is:
- 0.0520, against 0.0504 independent (blob 0.3, reversed);
- 0.0552, against 0.0552 (blob 0.5, reversed);
- 0.0618, against 0.0586 (uniform annulus 0.5).

In the refined parts its minimum falls below the cell:
- 0.0010 on the adapted annulus, against an independent minimum of 0.0037;
- 0.0067, against 0.0119, on the blob.

That is the cut's own short edges (finding 3). It is harmless wherever the chain lies on the trace (d = 0).

## (c) Development's pieces (dbg_split2.py and dev_pieces.jsonl)
- `dbg_split2.py` on development and at the tip, identical output on both:
  - kink120_1 (irregular box, 1 cell): F in 2 pieces, 7 edges; FPlus and FMinus 1 piece each, ending 2.165 cells short of the trace;
  - kink120_4: 2 pieces on every side;
  - kink150_4: F in 2 pieces; Plus and Minus 1.25 cells short.
- **All 360 entries of dev_pieces.jsonl were reproduced on development** (6 meshes x spacings, 60 kink traces each): outcome and piece count identical in 360/360, including the 95 refused. 265 returned; 101 in pieces (99 in 2, 2 in 3), as the README says.
- At the tip, on the same 360:
  - **37 refused "in N pieces". Development returned 33 of them in pieces** (2 or 3) **and refused the other 4.** None was a one-piece development fault.
  - 32 refused by distance: development returned 16 one-piece partial, 15 in pieces, and refused 1.
  - The author's claim holds.
  - Of development's 101 faults in pieces, the tip refuses 48 and adds 53, every one as a single chain (the labelling fix joins them). 9 of the 53 are more than half a nominal cell off the trace; see (b).

## (d) The coordinate join on a moved mesh, np 2 and 3
The probe moves a regular 1/12 box under band, checks the moved mesh with the SF check, then runs add_fault (vertical and diagonal traces, default partition and across_seams). Movers:
- `mesh.deform` by a function of the local coordinates (test_1173's shift);
- `mesh.deform` by a degree-2 MeshVariable displacement, and by a degree-1 one, each evaluated with `uw.function.evaluate` at each rank's local vertices, ghosts included;
- `node_redistribution` (test_1173's metric);
- `smooth_mesh_interior`;
- `mesh.deform` with a rank-dependent last-bits change (x (1 + 1e-13 rank)).

The field and smooth movers also ran on an irregular box under rand1 at np 3, with 308 shared vertices.

Results:
- **After every sanctioned move, ghost == owner bit for bit:** 0 mismatches of 13-308 shared vertices.
  - This includes the deliberately rank-dependent deform. `mesh.deform` re-synchronises the ghosts from the owners.
  - So the field-evaluated deform cannot make identity fail.
- On every cut dm: coordinate pieces = global-number pieces = 1, with the across_seams chain on 2 or 3 ranks. All AGREE.
- The verdicts that are refusals are fault_split's existing seam refusal (diagonal across a band seam).
- **Only by writing the local coordinate vector directly** (`dm.setCoordinatesLocal`, rank-dependent, no scatter; not a sanctioned path) could I make ghosts lag owners: 11 of 13 shared vertices off by up to 2e-15.
  - Even then the add_fault chains stayed 1 piece. Their seam vertices are pulled or snapped onto the trace, which sets them identically on every rank.
  - The SF check of the cut dm showed 9-20 mismatched shared vertices elsewhere.
  - A hand-labelled chain along the base mesh's own edges at x = 0.5, crossing every band, checked directly (no pull, no snap, no cut):
    - on that raw-written mesh it is **refused as "in 2 pieces" at np 2 and "in 3 pieces" at np 3**, where the global-number count is 1;
    - after the sanctioned deform, and after the rank-dependent deform, it is accepted as one piece at np 2 and 3.
  - So the join is exact for every sanctioned mover tested, and it depends on a precondition that `mesh.deform` maintains but nothing in the check asserts (new finding 2).
- Development control: the across_seams diagonal on the deformed mesh is refused by the same seam refusal on development, so that refusal is pre-existing.

## (e) Suites
- The 41-file fault, conforming-surface and line-cut suite at the 0004 tip, with -n 4 and test_1092's tide test deselected, as in round 0: **426 passed, 1 skipped** (36:49). This is identical to round 0's 0003 tip on the same list (426/1). Development's 417/1 on the 38 files it can collect is round 0's run, on the same install.
  - The author's 431 includes the deselected test and the files of the author's own list.
  - test_1079, test_1092 (without the tide test) and test_0559 pass.
- The probe wrapper recorded all 225 guard calls in the suite:
  - the largest excess among the calls that pass is 0.033 (test_1079): no near miss, so no false-refusal risk in the suite;
  - pieces by coordinates = pieces by point number in 222 of 225.
  - The 3 that differ are test_1174's second fault crossing an earlier split fault: coordinates count 1 piece, point numbers count 2. The slit's duplicated vertices share coordinates, so the coordinate key joins the chain across the slit. Those tests pass: the deferral to the split and its touching-junction refusal handle them (new finding 2).
- test_0897 (4 tests, including the new two-pieces case), test_0899 (3) and test_0898 (2), serially at the tip: 9 passed.
- Development, test_0897 and test_0899: 5 failed, 2 passed. The failures are test_0897's empty, short and hand-labelled cases and test_0899's two refusals; the passes are the whole cut and the trace that places. As expected.
- test_0897 under mpirun at np 2 and np 3, as a serial test file: "test_a_whole_cut_is_added" fails on the ranks that hold no fault after the add-on partition (`getStratumSize > 0` is rank-local). At np 3, "test_the_check_on_chains_labelled_by_hand" fails on the ranks that hold none of the hand-labelled edges, and the remaining rank waits in the check's collective until the supervisor ends the run. These are rank-local assertions in a serial test, not a library fault; the library side is (a). It is why a tests/parallel version is needed (finding 4).

## New findings
1. Medium (0004; blocks, as round 0's finding 2 did): **the "local median" is not the local cell. It inflates exactly where the chain leaves the trace.** `_local_cell_size` takes the median of the edges whose midpoints lie within 3x the nearest-midpoint distance, so the ball grows with the gap.
   - At a tip gap or a corner chord the nearest midpoint is about half a cell away. The ball then reaches the diagonals and the long chord, and the median comes to 1.1-1.5 cells. Half of that is 0.56-0.75 cells.
   - Measured:
     - 14 of the 22 still added;
     - kink120_1 and kink120_2 added with slip 30-33 % low;
     - 15 traces that 0003 refused are now added, three with a 0.625-cell tip gap (slip up to -16 %);
     - the grid's lenient passes went from 19 (max 0.568) to 21 (max 0.625).
   - The piece check is sound and catches what it was built for (two-component chains). It does not touch the one-piece corner cuts and short tips.
   - Suggested fix: measure the cell on something that does not depend on the gap. For example:
     - the median of a fixed number of nearest edges (k = 6) on the BASE mesh, before the pull and the cut;
     - or the median edge of the base cells containing the point.
   - Measured on the 23 cases (the 19, kink120_1 on the irregular box, and the three 0.625 tips), probe only, with the same chain and distances:
     - **base mesh, 6 nearest edges: refuses 20 of 23.** That is every case more than half a cell off the trace, kink120_1 (0.541) and the three 0.625 tips among them, with h at the worst point 0.97-1.01 cells.
       - The 3 it passes sit at exactly 0.500 cells: box_reg 0.5 la4, kink90_8 and kink120_2. kink120_2's slip is still 33 % low.
       - So the threshold itself (half a cell, strictly) also wants a look. A corner chord exactly half a cell from the corner is not a good fault.
     - **6 nearest edges on the cut mesh: refuses only 5.** The cut's own edges inflate it too (h 1.05-1.39 at the gaps).
   - Not measured: either estimator over (b)'s no-false-refusal set and the suite.
2. Low (0004): **the coordinate key is identity only under two unstated preconditions, and the code comment claims more** ("this is identity, not a tolerance").
   - (i) Ghost coordinates must equal the owners'. `mesh.deform` and the movers keep this (d), but a raw `setCoordinatesLocal` breaks it, and a whole chain is then refused "in 2/3 pieces".
   - (ii) Distinct vertices must not share coordinates. An earlier split fault's duplicated vertices do, so the key joins a chain across a slit (test_1174: 1 piece by coordinates, 2 by point number).
   - That second case is what the Plus/Minus deferral exists for. But the deferral is broad: any chain vertex touching any label ending in "Plus" or "Minus" skips the piece check, including a chain in pieces for another reason that merely touches an earlier fault, or a user label so named.
   - Keying the join by global point number (the point SF, as this review's probe does) removes both preconditions, and would let the deferral be limited to pieces that actually meet at a slit.
3. Low (0004): **beside the cut's own short edges the local h collapses to 0.06-0.08 cells.** In test_0897's short case:
   - 53 of 220 trace points have h below half a cell;
   - the message names a point 0.06 cells off the chain, not the 0.6-cell tip gap;
   - it advises "resample the trace to about one cell (0.00674) apart", a twelfth of the mesh cell.
   - The same estimator change as finding 1 would cure it. Meanwhile, compute the advice's cell size from the base mesh.
4. Low (0004): **there is still no tests/parallel test.** Round 0 asked for test_0897's add_fault cases at np 2 (the run that would have caught the hang).
   - test_0897 as written cannot serve: its assertions are rank-local, it fails at np 2, and at np 3 one rank waits in a collective (see (e)).
   - A parallel test should assert on reduced quantities: the global facet count, and the same message on every rank.
5. Low (0004): the README's line for test_0897's short case ("now caught by the partition-independent piece count") does not match the measurement. It is one piece and the distance test refuses it. Correct the README. Better still, make the two-pieces hand-labelled case the test of the piece count, which it already is.

## Not run
- A converged slip reference beyond the twin comparison.
- The alternative tolerance estimators as a refusal over the suite and the fixtures. Finding 1's sketch was measured on the 23 tolerance cases only.
- The deferral to the slit (finding 2) on a crafted second fault that is in pieces for another reason and touches the first.
- The parallel fault-test files (round 0's 32) at the 0004 tip.
- The ptest_* scripts, the 3-D sheet path, and GitHub CI (paused).

Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code
````

### #1058 / row 28 bracket re-run at 975ed339 (= 31d23ae9 code): refusal-cut problem fixed; the new test_1156 pin test fails at np 2/3

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict: the refusal-cut problem is fixed. Every row of the sweep converges with the rotation admitted, and the error is 1.6e-10 or smaller (5.1e-9 on the contrast case). Nothing is refused, and nothing raises except the solves forced not to converge. All six earlier findings are fixed or moot. One test fails: the branch's new pin test in test_1156 fails at np 2 and np 3 ([10, 7] and [10, 7, 7] calls).**

Commits checked: 975ed339, with 306f9bf2, 79be7633 and 975ed339 on top of f917543d, run from the reviewer's built environment for that head; the installed `rotated_bc.py` is byte-identical to the commit's. During the runs the branch was rewritten to 31d23ae9: 975ed339 was split into 70cb0a4b (the pin fix, plus a new test in test_1156) and 31d23ae9. The source difference between 975ed339 and 31d23ae9 is a two-line comment, so the sweep applies to both heads. The tests ran from the 31d23ae9 test files (test_1058 is unchanged between the two; test_1156 has the new test). The sweep and the accuracy measure are the same as in the first review: mu * vrms against the mu = 1 solve on the same mesh, plus the rigid-rotation split in 2-D. Each row records:
- whether the solve converged or raised;
- the gauge decision and `op_viol_rows` (velocity and pressure parts);
- the final velocity and pressure block residuals against `convergence_target` and `pressure_target`;
- `lu_workspace`;
- the per-increment `lu_backward_errors` and `lu_linear_residuals`.

All runs used 4 cores, alongside other jobs on the machine.

## Sweep (serial)

The ranges are over all rows except mu = 1. Fv/Tv and Fp/Tp are the final block residuals against their targets. "bwd err" is the normwise backward error of each increment's solve.

The `op_viol_rows` parts do not move with the viscosity (velocity about 1.9e-16, pressure about 2e-17, from mu 1 to 1e-12), which is the scale-free gauge test working as intended. ## Previous findings, re-judged

1. High (the cut tested a proxy; the real cliff was the gauge test): **fixed.** The diag_ratio refusal is gone, and `_operator_violation` measures the velocity rows against the velocity diagonal and the pressure rows against |B|. The true rotation is admitted at every viscosity tested, down to 1e-12, on all four geometries. `diag_ratio` is still recorded (down to 4.4e-13) and used for nothing.
2. High (a 1e9 contrast was refused with a right answer): **fixed.** It converges at outer mu 1, 1e3, 1e6 and 1e-3, and the scales agree to 2.7e-10 to 5.1e-9.
3. Medium (disc 0.2, mu 1e-10 accepted, unconverged, warned, 25% off): **fixed.** It now converges in one increment to 3.4e-13 (with ICNTL(10) = 2 refinement), and at np 2 and 3 to the same answer. An LU solve that does not converge now raises on every rank with the fields not written. I did not find a case that raised naturally, so the raise path was checked with a capped increment count.
4. Medium (the floored target loosened as 1/mu): **fixed for the velocity rows.** Tv is 1e-9 * |F(0)| at every small mu, and the residuals reached it. The pressure target still goes as 1/mu, by design, since it is relative to |B| |u|; see note 2 below.
5. Low (the code comment should quote the finer brackets): **moot.** The refusal and its constant are removed.
6. Low (INFOG(28) is not a refusal signal): **moot.** Nothing uses it. The new `lu_backward_errors` are 1e-18 to 8e-12 in every row, and their docstring correctly says they do not separate right solves from wrong ones.

## New notes

1. Medium: the new pin test fails at np 2 and 3. `test_the_pins_right_hand_side_row_is_zeroed_on_every_rank` counts every `_zero_rows_local` call per rank, and the owning rank makes 3 more than the others. The extra calls are the residual's `if lu_pin is not None: _zero_rows_local(Fh, [lu_pin])` in `rotated_residual`, and `lu_pin` is None on the ranks that do not own the row. One call is made per residual evaluation, 3 in this test. The increment's `bhat` call, which the commit fixed, is made on every rank. As written, the residual site is harmless: `Fh` is a fresh duplicate, and no norm of it is taken before the zeroing. That is why no sweep run desynced. Still, it is the same owner-only pattern the fix removed, and the test (correctly, by its docstring) counts it. Either call it on every rank there too (`[] if lu_pin is None else [lu_pin]`), which is cheap and keeps the invariant the test states, or narrow the test to the `bhat` site. As it stands the branch's own parallel test is red.
2. Low: the pressure target never binds in these runs. Fp / Tp is 3.3e-5 at most, usually 1e-7 to 1e-9. Tp = 1e-9 * |B|_inf * |u_v| mixes an infinity-norm row sum with a 2-norm over every velocity dof, and grows as 1/mu (annulus 0.4: 4e-5 at mu 1, 9e5 at mu 1e-12). A pressure-row residual far worse than the ones measured here would still pass. That has not mattered for accuracy in any row, but the test is weaker than its name suggests. Comparing |F_p| against eps-scaled |B| |u| in matching norms, or against the pressure rows' own |B u| term sizes, would make it bite.
3. Low: the targets are taken from the current iterate before the rigid rotation is removed, so they carry that arbitrary rotation. Tp is not monotone in mu: annulus 0.4 gives 9.2e4 at mu 1e-11 but 2.3e4 at 3e-12, and disc 0.2 gives 2.6e3 at mu 1e-10 between 1.3e3 and 1.5e3 at its neighbours. Only the pressure target is affected here, because the velocity target sits at its 1e-9 minimum. It would bite through the eps * |K| |u| term if that term were the active one.

## Not run

- Annulus cell 0.1 (about 5 minutes per small-mu solve at the earlier head) and the disc at cell 0.4.
- np 4 and above; the disc and contrast cases in parallel beyond those listed.
- A case that fails to converge without a capped increment count; none was found in the sweep.
- The iterative (non-LU) rotated route.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

### Combined test cleanup 9d1482d4 + d944fa2f (UNFILED 57 r2 + sweep): ready to merge on green from this review

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Ready to merge on green from this review: the combined cleanup, 9d1482d4 + d944fa2f, on development fbef72fa.** Both commits are text only, they combine without conflict, the tests/ directory is clear of session codes and host names apart from the known false positives, and every test has the same outcome before and after.

## What was checked

- **9d1482d4** (branch docs/tests-no-session-or-host-names): 62 files under tests/, one commit on fbef72fa. It is the 104 edits listed in its edit script. One of them, in test_0001_sphere_refinement_folds_parallel.py, rewraps a sentence over two lines, so the diff shows 105 lines.
- **d944fa2f** (branch tests/no-desk-names-in-docstrings): round 2 of the UNFILED 57 docstring change, on 89c2cd08. Together the two commits touch 6 files. These six are not among the 62.

### Text only

I wrote my own AST comparison. For each changed file it parses base and head, drops docstrings (module, class and function), replaces every remaining string constant with a placeholder and compares the dumps. It also lists each non-docstring string constant whose value differs.

- 9d1482d4: 62 of 62 files are identical under this comparison, and 19 of them have identical full ASTs (comment-only edits). This matches the author's astdiff.txt.
- d944fa2f against fbef72fa: 6 of 6 identical. Its second commit against the first (89c2cd08 to d944fa2f): 2 of 2 identical.
- Exactly one string constant changes across the whole cleanup. It is a dictionary key in tests/test_1134_fac_cover_gate_structure.py:32, "Linux x86_64 (<cluster host name>), PETSc 3.25.0" becomes "Linux x86_64 cluster, PETSc 3.25.0". The dictionary, `BAND_SUBDOMAIN`, is used twice, both at line 54-55. One use is `gate["subdomain_rows"] in BAND_SUBDOMAIN.values()`. The other is the assertion message, which formats the whole dict into its text. So the key cannot change whether the test passes. A failure message would show the new key text, and that is all. The author's statement holds.
- No added line is longer than 100 characters.

### Combining them

I made a new worktree at fbef72fa and cherry-picked 9d1482d4, 89c2cd08 and d944fa2f in that order. There were no conflicts. The two changes touch disjoint files (no overlap between the 62 and the 6). `git merge-tree` of the two branch heads gives the same tree as the cherry-picked result. The combined change is 68 files, 116 lines in and 116 out.

### Search of tests/ on the combined tree

I searched every file under tests/ for session codes (the hub, worker, review and cluster codes, case-sensitive with word boundaries), "dev hub", "review desk", "desk", "worker session", the three host names, and local filesystem paths (mount points, user home directories, per-session scratch directories). The search ran line by line, and again on each file's text joined into one line with comment markers stripped, so a phrase split across a line break is caught too. Nine lines in six files match, all known false positives:

- a matrix coefficient name in test_0023:80;
- MeshVariable names in ptest_001:16, test_0700:96, 101 and 106, and test_0836:314;
- "desk" in its everyday sense in serial_reference.py:47;
- a made-up traceback path in test_0054:51 and 55.

Nothing else matched, and no phrase is split across a line break.

### Test runs

All 68 changed files ran serially (no MPI; pytest -n 4, four cores). They used a clean, non-editable development install at fbef72fa. I ran the base tree's test files and then the combined tree's.

- **Collection:** 439 tests from each tree, the same ids. Without the six round-2 files there are 411, which is the author's count for the 62 files.
- **Outcomes:** identical test by test (439 of 439). On both trees: 333 passed, 104 skipped (the parallel tests, which need --with-mpi), 1 xfailed, 1 failed.
  - The one failure is test_1064_constrained_spherical_shell_response.py::test_default_constrained_matches_zhong_velocity_response. It hit the 1200 s per-test timeout I set, on both trees. It is a level_3, slow, tier_c test, and its file has no edit beyond a comment. It is the same on both sides and has nothing to do with this change.
  - My first pass ran without the compiler on PATH, so 71 tests that compile kernels failed the same way on both sides. I reran those 71 on both trees with the toolchain on PATH. The counts above combine the two passes.
- No parallel runs: the AST check shows that no code changed, and round 1 already ran the round-2 files at np 2.

Logs kept by the reviewer.

### Round-2 fixes

The #1290 review recorded that, on the PR at 120aff92, these points fell to the face-tolerance path and were up to 0.12 off at np 2, 3 and 4. The review's np 8 recheck says the same. ## Findings

2. Medium: none.

3. Low:

This was carried over from round 1: two parentheses sit back to back. "from a macOS run on a 12-core Apple M4 (velocity unchanged to 4e-11)" reads better. About 16 of them are matrix variable names in petsc_generic_snes_solvers.pyx, which leaves about 28 real lines: session codes and host names in comments and docstrings, in model.py, mpi.py, agreed_build.py, custom_mg.py, fault_contact.py, fault_mg.py, rotated_bc.py, free_surface.py, discretisation_mesh.py and the solver .pyx. docs/ has 185 matching lines in 60 files. The rest are real session codes and host names in the changelog, the developer guides, the design notes and the subsystem pages. These are out of scope here. ### labels70-22 r2 (0005 a00f18d2, 0006 fb02df80): changes requested

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

# Round 2 re-check: UNFILED 68 + 70/22 + 72, with 0005 (a00f18d2) and 0006 (fb02df80)

**Verdict at the 0006 tip (0006 on 0005 on 0004 on 0003 on 0002 on 0001 on development fbef72fa): changes requested.**

One parallel defect, new in 0006, with a one-line fix (new finding 1). The chain-end check counts each vertex's degree over the gathered edges without removing duplicates. An edge on a partition seam is held by both ranks, so it is counted twice, and a stray end on such an edge is not seen. The result:
- a hand-labelled chain whose end is not a tip is refused serially and accepted on 8 of 9 parallel layouts;
- the same chain inside one band is accepted under two random partitions.

The ranks always agree with each other, and nothing hangs, but the answer moves with the partition. In add_fault's own paths I could not reach it: fault_split's existing seam refusals stop every chain that lies along a seam. The check itself is not partition-independent, though, and the fix is trivial.

Everything else on the parallel side is clean:
- 0005's base-mesh cell size and vertex-number join;
- 0006's tip pull;
- the new parallel test at np 2-4, including an empty rank;
- 0 disagreements and 0 hangs across round 1's layouts, the moved meshes, the slit cases, tips on seams and shared tips;
- the vertex-number join equals the SF count in every recorded check: 366 probe calls and 452 suite calls.

0006 also removes the tip shortfalls exactly as the author says: 0 added faults stop short of a tip on the 480-trace grid. The threshold's knife-edge (finding 2) is down to two corner cuts.

Commits, applied with `git am` on fbef72fa in two fresh worktrees (one at 0005, one at 0006):
- 0001-0004 are tree-identical to round 1 (trees 06b4084c, be3a066f, e58e22c7 and e8cf7655). 0005 is the same tree (ae6f785e) in both worktrees.
- 0005: `fault_split.py` +86/-40, `discretisation_mesh.py` 1 line, new `tests/parallel/test_0897_add_fault_refusal_agrees_across_ranks_parallel.py` (101 lines).
- 0006: `fault_split.py`, the parallel test and test_0897; +126/-40.
  - the tips are pulled last and labelled `UW_FaultTip` by exact coordinates;
  - the chain's degree-1 ends must carry that label, or add_fault refuses.
- Python only. The builds are overlays (one for 0005, one for 0006) of the three library files on a copy of the clean development install. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`.

## Method
- Three cores (taskset), at most 3 processes; the np 4 runs oversubscribe them. pytest -n 3.
- Every parallel run went through the repository's MPI supervisor (silence 180 s), inside an outer timeout.
- The partitions come from round 0's shell-partition hook: band, emptyband (the last rank holds no cell) and rand1.
- Round 1's probe wrapper recomputes, on the dm each check receives:
  - the pieces by coordinates, by SF global number and by `getVertexNumbering`;
  - the deferral flag;
  - the excess against the patch's `_base_cell_size`;
  - the SF ghost-against-owner check.
- Logs kept by the reviewer.

## Round-1 findings then and now
1. Medium, the "local median" inflated at tips and corners (0004): **fixed in 0005.** h is the median of the 6 nearest base-mesh edges. At the 23 cases' worst points it is 0.968-1.015 nominal cells, against 1.1-1.5 at 0004.
2. Low, the coordinate key's preconditions (0004): **fixed in 0005** (join by vertex number).

   - The broad deferral is unchanged (finding 4).
3. Low, h collapsed beside the cut's short edges (0004): **fixed in 0005.** The short case's message names the 0.6-cell tip and advises resampling to about one cell (0.0833). Since 0006 that trace is split whole.
4. Low, no tests/parallel test (0004): **fixed in 0005**, extended in 0006; see (a). Its gaps are finding 5.
5. Low, the README line on the short case: **fixed in 0005.**
- Round 0's item 7 (test_0898's hairpin docstring) is still untouched; it was not asked for again.

## Results
### (a) The tests for the tip fix and the parallel test
**Serially**, the 0006 test_0897, test_0898 and test_0899:

**The 0005 version of the parallel test:**

- 0004: 4 passed;
- development: 3 of 4 fail on every rank;

**Reduced quantities:** every outcome goes through `comm.allgather`, and the test asserts agreement before it asserts on the value. No rank-local count is asserted.

### (b) Parallel
**0005, round 1's probes.**

**0006, the end check:** 16 cases (`t6_probe`), np 1, and np 2-4 under band, emptyband and rand1. All 10 runs exit 0, with **0 DISAGREE** (message sha) and no hang.

- **Tip exactly on a seam vertex** (held by 2 or 3 ranks under band and rand1; by 1 under emptyband at np 2):

|---|---|---|

  - With tips labelled at both true ends it is accepted everywhere.
  - This is new finding 1.

**Grep of 0005 and 0006:**
- `allgather(mine)` (a tuple of NumPy arrays, 0005; a dict of ints to bools, 0006) and `allgather(local)` (integer pairs): pickled gathers with no operator. Correct.
- No NumPy array goes through a lowercase reduction.

- Every raise depends on reduced or gathered values. The defect is in what is gathered, not where.

### (c) UW_FaultTip (0006)
- **After add_fault** (np 1 and 2): `UW_FaultTip` is on every rank of the returned mesh, on exactly the two tip vertices. `UW_HostCell` is not; add_fault removes it after use.
- **Checkpoint:** `write_timestep(..., petsc_reload=True)` at np 1 and at np 2 both succeed. The label is in the file (`labels/UW_FaultTip/1/indices`).

### (d) Tolerance and the grid claim
**0005.**
- The 23 round-1 cases: 16 refused by distance, 4 "in 2 pieces", 3 added. The 3 are box_reg 0.5 la4, kink90_8 and kink120_2, at excess 0.49999999999913-0.49999999999971. The shas at np 3 equal np 1.

### (e) Suites
- The 41-file fault, conforming-surface and line-cut suite, serially with -n 3:
  - **0005: 426 passed, 1 skipped** (41 min), the same count as rounds 0 and 1;
  - **0006: 427 passed, 1 skipped** (42 min). The one more is test_0897's new case.

## New findings
1. Medium (0006; blocks): **the chain-end check is partition-dependent.**
   - `degree` is counted over `comm.allgather(local)`, and every rank that holds an edge contributes it. A seam edge is held by two ranks, so its ends get degree 2, and a stray end on such an edge is never seen.
   - Measured: a hand-labelled chain with a stray end is refused at np 1 and accepted on 8 of 9 parallel layouts where it lies on a seam. Inside one band it is accepted under rand1 at np 3 and 4.
   - The piece count beside it is not affected, since a union-find ignores repeats.
   - Fix: deduplicate before counting. For example, take `{tuple(sorted(e)) for edges in comm.allgather(local) for e in edges}` once, and use it for both the union-find and the degrees.
   - Add the seam-line stray-end chain to the parallel test. It fails today at np 2.
2. Medium (0005; reduced by 0006): **the threshold sits on a common value, so the verdict there is round-off.**
   - At 0005, 17 grid faults sat within 1e-6 of 0.5, the one-spacing tip shortfall at half-cell sampling; 11 were added and 6 refused.
   - 0006 removes the tip cases. Two corner cuts remain at 0.4999999999997-0.4999999999999, both added with slip 25-33 % low.
   - Partition-independent (np 1 = np 3 on all 23 cases), but machine-dependent.
   - Compare with an explicit margin (`excess > _COVERAGE_FRACTION * (1 + 1e-6)`, or a fraction a little under 0.5 for the corners) and say which in the docstring.
3. Low (0006): **`UW_FaultTip` is left on the returned mesh and written into checkpoints, and it holds only the last fault's tips.**
   - `UW_HostCell` is removed after use.
   - `_mark_tip_vertices` replaces the label for each fault, so in a two-fault call, or after a second add_fault, it marks the last fault's tips only.
   - Nothing measured trips on it: reload, read_timestep, a second add_fault, and 50 restart tests.
   - Remove it after the check, as `UW_HostCell` is, or document it as a per-call scratch label.
4. Low (0005, carried from round 1; 0006 inherits it): **the Plus/Minus deferral skips the piece count, and since 0006 also the end check**, whenever a chain vertex touches any label ending in "Plus" or "Minus".
   - A 3-piece chain across a slit is accepted.
   - So is a 2-piece chain on a plain box with a user label `UserPlus`.
   - Limit the deferral to pieces that meet at a slit, now that pieces are counted by vertex number.
5. Low (0005/0006, the parallel test):
   - Its add_fault cases always run on one rank (after the add-on partition).
   - Its "across the seams" chain crosses a seam only under the default partitioner, which nothing asserts.
   - The `tip_shared` case asserts only "added", so it passes on 0005, which adds that trace half a cell short.
   - Assert, reduced, that the chain ends on the tip vertices; that more than one rank holds the hand chain at `size > 1`; and finding 1's stray end on a seam.
   - `_outcome` catches only ValueError. A RuntimeError on one rank leaves the others in `allgather`, so catch Exception.
6. Low (0005): the message reads "more than 0.5 of the local cell side"; "half" read better.

## Not run
- Slip on the 0006 grid (GEOM only; the slip figures quoted are the author's records), and a converged slip reference.
- Round 1's 3 light+sharp tolerance cases.
- The parallel fault-test files (round 0's 32) and the other ptest_* scripts at 0006; the 3-D sheet path.

````markdown
Review from the Underworld development team with AI support from Claude Code (H1: review and parallel test runs on a 96-thread Linux workstation (Intel Xeon Gold 6240R, AVX-512; MPICH))
(H1 is the session that reviews pull requests and runs the parallel tests.)

**Verdict at cdfc791c (local head, to be pushed): ready to merge on green from this review.** All three findings from the first round are fixed. `scripts/test.sh` now runs the file at np 3 in shard 6, and there it passes on every rank and still catches Mutant A. The odd case fails under `CI` when no cell size gives the parity it needs, and skips otherwise. `_global_node_count` gives the same counts as before. The two notes below are wording and need not hold up the merge.

## Earlier findings, then and now

| | at e3691d1c | at cdfc791c |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Medium: nothing ran the file under mpirun | `test.sh` ran it serially only | **Fixed.** One np 3 line in the always-on shard 6 block, right after `test_1184`. A dry run of shard 6 calls it (see Runs). |
| 2. Low: the odd case could skip unnoticed on another host | it was a plain skip | **Fixed.** With no candidate of the parity asked for, the case calls `pytest.fail` when `CI` is set and `pytest.skip` otherwise. The message says the counts come from one host. |
| 3. Low: `_global_node_count` made a global vector on every call and never freed it | `createGlobalVector()` | **Fixed.** It now uses `mesh.dm.getCoordinates().getSize() // mesh.cdim`, which reads the DM's own vector and gives the same numbers. |

## Runs

These ran on the same clean development install as round 1 (fbef72fa), with the test file from cdfc791c. Every log prints `underworld3.__file__`. Parallel runs went through `scripts/mpi_supervisor.py --silence 120` on 3 cores, with other jobs on the machine.

- **Diff `e3691d1c..cdfc791c`.** Two files changed, +17/−3: the `test.sh` line with its comment, and the test file (`import os`, the counting function, and the fail-or-skip branch). `bash -n scripts/test.sh` passes. `--print-shards` still gives `[1,2,3,4,5,6]`. `scripts/check_test_coverage.py` reports "all 496 test files are reachable". `git merge-tree` against fbef72fa (development's tip as last fetched) is clean.
- **Wiring.** I put a stand-in `mpirun` on `PATH` that only records its arguments, then ran `scripts/test.sh --p 2 --shard 6/6 --parallel-only`. It exited 0 and made 28 mpirun calls. Call 23 is `-n 4 ... tests/test_1184_general_pressure_split.py` and call 24 is `-n 3 python -m pytest --with-mpi tests/test_0768_manifold_read_timestep.py`. So the new line sits in the shard 6 block and runs whenever `--p` is given to that shard.
- **test_0768 on the head.** Serial: 2 passed. At np 3, with the exact `test.sh` line under the supervisor: 2 passed on each of the 3 ranks, in about 5 s wall time with the usage-metrics post turned off.
- **The CI branch.** With `CI=true`, the head's file passes 2 of 2, both serially and at np 3, because both parities are found here. To force the no-candidate case, I made a scratch copy whose list keeps only the five even cell sizes (0.4, 0.3, 0.35, 0.45, 0.25):
  - with `CI=true`, serially: parity 0 passes and parity 1 **fails** with `Failed: no candidate cell size gives a node count of parity 1 on this host (...)`;
  - without `CI`, serially: 1 passed, 1 **skipped**, with the same message;
  - with `CI=true` at np 3: parity 1 fails on all three ranks, and no rank hangs. The choice depends only on the global count, so every rank takes the same branch.
- **Node counts.** The new `_global_node_count`, the old one (vector created and then destroyed), and a second call of the new one all agree on every rank at np 1, 2 and 3: 162, 192, **227**, 162, **229**, 162, **197**, 272, **161** for the nine candidates in order. This matches round 1's table, which round 1 also measured at np 4.
- **Mutant A** (round 1's copy, which reshapes the saved cloud by `dim`), with the head's test and `-x`:
  - serial: **fails**, `ValueError: cannot reshape array of size 681 into shape (2)`;
  - np 3, with the `test.sh` line: **fails**. Rank 0 raises the same `ValueError`. The other two ranks wait in `_saved_cloud_stats()`. The supervisor reports "no rank moved in 20 s: this is stuck, not slow" and kills the run, which exits non-zero. Round 1 saw the same behaviour. In `test.sh` this sets `status=1` through the supervisor's 300 s silence limit.
- **Update text.** I checked each claim in the update against the runs above, and each one holds. It gives about 8 s for the np 3 run. I measured about 5 s with the metrics post turned off, and `test.sh` turns it on (`UW_NO_USAGE_METRICS=0`), so 8 s is plausible for a `test.sh` run.

## New findings

1. Low: the message says "the counts above are from one host". That text appears in the test report, where nothing is "above" it; the counts are in the docstring of `_sphere_with_node_parity`. Something like "(the candidate counts in the docstring were measured on one host; gmsh differs)" would read better.
2. Low: `os.environ.get("CI")` treats any non-empty value as set, so `CI=false` or `CI=0` would also turn the skip into a failure. GitHub Actions always sets `CI=true`, so nothing changes in practice. If the authors want local runs to be able to opt out, they could compare the value with `"true"`.

## Not run

- CI, because GitHub writes are paused.
- The whole of `scripts/test.sh` shard 6. I ran its mpirun calls only as a dry run, and ran the new line on its own.
- np 4 and higher for the counting probe: this re-check had three cores. Round 1's np 4 counts used the old function, and at np 1 to 3 the new function gives exactly the same numbers.
